# Legatum rules reference (code-verified)

Facts below are cited to file (and usually function), read 2026-10-02 and audited against `master` on 2026-11-02 (city strike and city-shields-garrison and Celts concealment updated 2026-10-03; Influence removed and diplomacy cut to Peace, Alliance or War on 2026-10-03; Byzantium salvage updated 2026-10-04). Purpose: stop
recommendations that assume Civ 6 defaults this game doesn't have. If a fact isn't in the cited
file, say so rather than guessing.

## Save compatibility

**Save compatibility (2026-11-01).** Every engine save carries `save_version = crates/core/src/lib.rs::RULES_VERSION` (currently 50; 50 = the Fable review batch (Sam, 2026-11-22): a rival's original capital scores 1 VP only while held (`scoring::capitals_held`, `Event::CapitalLost`; `Player::capitals_taken` is removed from the save and `PlayerPublic::capitals_taken` now lists the capitals held now), Alliance upkeep is waived at War with a team close to winning (`diplomacy::upkeep_waived_by`, `scoring::near_victory`), the off-by-default experiment `no_purchase_under_threat`, and seeded draws for same-turn tile buys, era firsts and exact world-wonder ties (`rng::seeded_order`), the Celts' sacked capital kept for good (`Player::capitals_sacked`, `VpSource::CapitalSacked`), and World Ages as the only era scoring with three peaceful cards per Age (the `objectives_deck` switch and the era-objective arm removed; a removed field in an old file is ignored); a version-49 save or tape is refused; 49 = skipped technologies (user, 2026-11-22): a tech with no benefit for a faction starts in its known set, no save-shape change beyond that, a version-48 save is refused; see "Skipped technologies" below; 48 = Egypt's Nile (designer, 2026-11-21): doubled Food, no Production, surplus Food builds every item and `IndustryProject::Grow` grows the city; the save shape gains only the `Grow` project value, a version-47 save is refused; 47 = held shots at fallen cities and charges that go on after their target dies (Sam, 2026-11-21; §4 "Held shots at a fallen city" and "Pursuit"), no save-shape change, a version-46 save is refused; 46 = cities can buy a tile with Gold again (designer, 2026-11-20): `Order::BuyTile`, `Event::TileBought`, the city view's `buyable_tiles`, no save-shape change, a version-45 save is refused; 45 = every empire starts with its capital founded on its start hex, no starting Settler (designer, 2026-11-17), no save-shape change, a version-44 save is refused; 44 = Roman veterans found cities without Gold or price escalation (designer, 2026-11-16), no save-shape change, a version-43 save is refused; 43 = each Production building in a German city costs 1 Food and the Ruhr Foundry's own Food cost is removed (designer, 2026-11-15), no save-shape change, a version-42 save is refused; 42 = Rome enters with veteran military foundations, the Legionary and Castrum, and no faction goal; no migration, a version-41 save is refused; 41 = Egypt joins with Food-funded ordinary buildings, the Medjay and Royal Granary, no faction goal, and a save-shape change (designer, 2026-11-15; §2 "Egypt"); no migration, a version-40 save is refused; 40 = World Age Veterans card replaces Demobilise (designer, 2026-11-15; §6 "Experimental World Ages"): `Objective::Veterans`, `FightStats::promotions` (additive, omitted at zero), the `ClaimObjective` action, `Family::Spend` and the Demobilise card are removed (a saved `ClaimObjective` order or Demobilise card is refused in a save; `wasm::tape::Tape::from_json` drops a tape's `ClaimObjective` Action order on load instead, so old tapes still replay), no migration, a version-39 save is refused; 39 = supported defence (designer, 2026-11-15; §4 "Supported defence"): what gets past a city's HP is shared by its garrison and the owner's units ordered into it, each of them adds a counterattack, and the backfill is the supporter that soaks most; no save-shape change, a version-38 save is refused; 38 = the Panzer uses 1 Oil like the Tank (designer, 2026-11-15: "there are no other units that require double supply"): `data::PANZER_SUPPLY`, `resources::unit_slots`/`item_slots` and `SupplyUser::Extra` are removed (a save holding an `extra` supply record is refused by version), no migration, a version-37 save is refused; 37 = the capitals victory (designer, 2026-11-15; `scoring::capital_stands`, `holds_own_capital`): a team wins when it is the only one still holding an original capital of its own, no migration, a version-36 save is refused; 36 = aircraft reconnaissance (designer, 2026-11-15; §Eras "The air layer"): every aircraft gives the Biplane's 4-tile sight (`data::AIRCRAFT_SIGHT`) and a new `Order::AirRecon` plus `GameState::air_recons` (additive, defaults empty), no migration, a version-35 save is refused; 35 = the Modern era (designer, 2026-11-14; spec `docs/design/ninth-era-proposal-2026-11-14.md`): a ninth era between the World War era (the old Modern, renamed in display only) and the Future, five new technologies, five new units (Mechanised Infantry, Main Battle Tank, Rocket Artillery, Jet Fighter, Jet Bomber), the Supermarket and the Air Defence building (`BuildingKind::Supermarket`, `AirDefence`), `CityMemory::has_air_defence`, `CityView::has_air_defence` and `BattleForecast::air_defence` (all default when absent), the era curve gaining a 20% step (`ERA_STEP_PERCENT`, `DESIGN_MELEE_LINE`) and the Future's costs and strengths one step up, no migration, a version-34 save is refused; 34 = the six faction special units and the seller's-version hire rule (designer, 2026-11-14; §8 "Special units") and the Celts keeping a city they founded when they retake it (designer, 2026-11-14; no sack, `CityView::founder` added): new `FactionRule`s, a new `SupplyUser::Extra`, `company::Offer::unavailable`, no migration, a version-33 save is refused like any other; 33 = the Machine Age (designer, 2026-11-14): an eighth era between Industrial and Modern, five new Modern technologies, four new units (Trench Infantry, Howitzer, Early Tank, Biplane), the Stock Exchange, the Biplane's Fighter-or-Bomber upgrade choice (`Order::UpgradeAircraft::to`), and the rules-31 +30% on the Modern costs taken back off; no migration, a version-32 save is refused; 32 = every land military unit can auto-explore (designer, 2026-11-14), no save-shape change; 31 = the 2026-11-13 batch (designer): every empire's Gold is public (`PlayerPublic::gold`), the occupation lock and city freeze are the rule in every game (the lobby switch and `RuleExperiments::occupation_lock` are gone), a captured city loses half its population (rounded down, min 1; none when it returns to its founder), the Tribute and Monument World Age cards are cut (`BuildingKind::TriumphalArch` removed, `FightStats::monuments` removed), and the five Modern technology costs rise about 30%; 30 = Peace clears a captured city's occupation record (user, 2026-11-11); 29 = the three crowns are cut as a VP source and removed from the save (user, 2026-11-11); 28 = the post-8.9.31 additions: World Ages draw one card per Age and a locked occupation unit is held automatically at end of turn (user, 2026-11-11); 27 = aircraft earn XP and promote (user, 2026-11-10), the occupation lock experiment (off by default), World Ages (`objectives=deck` redesigned, `BuildingKind::TriumphalArch`, `CityRazed.prize/walled`, new `FightStats` counters), relief units chain into an assaulted city instead of bouncing (designer, 2026-11-11, Bukhara T67), rival assaults on one city are decided by force instead of bouncing (designer, 2026-11-11, Lucerne), and overflow past a layer of a city's defence is per hit, only the killing hit's unused share, judged against the unit type it reaches (designer, 2026-11-11); 26 = the mounted bonus applies only in melee, never to a mounted unit that is the target of a shot (designer, 2026-11-10); 25 = same-era resource reveals (Niter by Gunpowder, Coal by Industrialization, Oil by Combustion, Silicon by Autonomous Systems), 2026-11-08; 24 = balanced founding sites, a map-generation change (tapes regenerate their map from the seed), 2026-11-07; 23 = Sparta's desertion removed, no replacement, rule-switch bit 43 vacant, 2026-11-07; 22 = Germany's first-building discount removed, 2026-11-07; 21 = a lost wonder race stops the city (`City.wonder_lost`), 2026-11-07; 20 = pairwise Peace, 2026-11-07; 19 = six faction-rule cuts (Sparta retire and heal, Celtic sack and unit, Mongol Peace borders, no wonder buying), 2026-11-07; 18 = era objective capture accounting and Celtic sack evidence, 2026-11-07; 17 = Athens Greece Captive, 2026-11-07; 16 = calls to arms respect every guaranteed Peace, simultaneous betrayals charge both sides, aircraft pay no Gold upkeep, unpaid Companies mutiny with Barbarians Off, 2026-11-07; 15 = city assault forecast matches resolution (Walls in the city-fire forecast, the split counterattack, garrison overflow defence), 2026-11-07; 14 = Walls at 100 Production and the Scriptorium cut, 2026-11-06; 13 = Germany Forge-slot Foundry and double supply, 2026-11-05; 12 = the Celtic sack simplified, 2026-11-03; 11 = the Celts rework and rebase at any distance, both 2026-11-02). `wasm::json::parse_state` checks that integer before decoding state; a missing or different version is refused. The app reports the saved Playtest label when available and asks the player to start a new game (`app/src/saveErrors.ts`, `app/src/screens/TitleScreen.tsx`). Saving and loading a current game preserves the state; neither path edits an existing save file. A save with the right version but broken contents is refused by `core/src/validation.rs::check_state` (reached from `wasm::json::parse_state`), whose messages begin "The save is damaged: "; `app/src/saveErrors.ts::loadErrorMessage` shows the player one fixed sentence (file kept, choose another or start a new game) and logs the detail to the console, and `parseSaveFile` does the same for a chosen file that is not a Legatum save or has an unsupported envelope version. Any rule or save-shape change increments the one core constant; there are no save migrations.

**Pre-rules-25 scenario positions (2026-11-08).** Rules 25 moved four resource reveals one tech later, so a position cut before it could show an empire with a resource it could no longer see. `crates/sim/src/scenario_reveal.rs::migrate` grants the new reveal tech (Gunpowder, Industrialization, Combustion, Autonomous Systems) to any empire that already had the old one (Education, Metallurgy, Industrialization, Electricity), and `scenario_cut.rs::run` applies the same repair to saves and extracted states recorded before version 25 (state loads through `parse_state_for_rehash`). The one-off `legatum-sim scenario-reveal-migrate` repaired the 14 Lab cards and 12 war cases; their baselines were re-recorded on Hard.

**Capital VP compatibility (2026-11-01; rules 50 removed `Player::capitals_taken`, an older save's field is ignored).** `PlayerPublic::capitals_taken`, `CityView::original_capital` and `original_capital_of` default when absent, so a save/tape from the current rules version loads without inventing past capital captures. This additive change was made without a rules-version bump at the designer's explicit request for old saves to load; the Future-era tech tree then raised `RULES_VERSION` to 9, so a version-8 save no longer loads at all. The walled-garrison line-of-sight change (train-1102) raised it to 10, and rebase at any distance (2026-11-02) raised it to 11.

## New-game faction setup

In the app lobby, explicit faction choices are kept and every Random seat is resolved in player
seat order from factions no seat has chosen or already received. The seeded draw is deterministic;
once all factions are in use, a repeated draw is limited to the least-used factions
(`app/src/lobby.ts::resolveFactions`, `app/src/gameSetup.ts::factionChoiceIndex`). Core
`GameSetup::all_ai` seats eight distinct factions from the original nine-faction pool, rotating the omitted faction with the seed; Egypt is selected explicitly in the app or test setups so established seeded tapes do not change
(`crates/core/src/state.rs::GameSetup::all_ai`).

New app lobbies default every AI seat to **Hard**. The all-AI level and mode-specific seat overrides
are stored under the release-independent `legatum.lobby.difficulty` key; Normal remains an explicit choice. The game view
exposes each seat's recorded difficulty and Search (Training) flag, shown in Diplomacy and the victory race
and score ledger (`app/src/lobby.ts::newLobby,rememberDifficulty,buildSetup`,
`crates/core/src/view.rs::public_view`, `app/src/ui/DiplomacyPanel.tsx`, `app/src/ui/VpTrack.tsx`,
`app/src/ui/ScoreLedger.tsx`).

**AI levels (2026-11-21):** players are offered **Easy, Normal and Hard**, and all three plan only
from the seat's own `PlayerView`. **Training is a research tool, not a level a player can choose:**
its battle study reads the true state, including units the seat cannot see, so no production build
lists it in any picker (solo lobby, per-seat list, online staging). A development build (`npm run dev`)
offers it with `?training=1` in the URL (remembered under `localStorage['legatum.dev.training']`;
`?training=0` forgets it; `app/src/lobby.ts::trainingSwitch,offeredLevels`), and there its
description says it uses the true positions of nearby units. With the switch off, a stored lobby
preference of `Training` or the older `HardSearch`, and a lobby or online room whose seats carry the
`search` flag, read as Hard and start a game without the flag (`levelOf`, `levelFields`,
`readDifficultyPreference`, `app/src/staging.ts`). Nothing changed in the core, the save shape or
the hashes: a save or tape recorded with Training still loads, is labelled Training in Diplomacy
and the race, and keeps running its battle study. The online host relays the `search` flag unchanged
(`crates/wasm/src/host/staging.rs`); it offers no picker of its own.

**Training (2026-11-07):** replaced "Hard + Search" and is saved as the same `PlayerSetup::search`
flag on a Hard AI seat (no rules change, no save-shape change). A Training seat
plays plain Hard (`crates/wasm/src/session.rs::make_brain`), except in its battles: each turn its
orders are computed at the start of the turn (`Session::pending_ai_orders`), and every battle in
scope (an enemy city with its army within 2, its own city with an enemy army within 2, or armies
within 2 of each other; at most 3 per seat, every own unit within 3 of the centre searched) is cut
into an arena from the **true state** (the teacher may see everything) and searched by coordinate
descent over every legal per-unit order (hold, rest or heal, every move tile in reach, every
ranged and melee target; melee under 10% to win and moves away from the fight other than one
retreat tile are pruned, except that a melee charge on an enemy city is never pruned: the shots may
zero the city first, then the charge walks in, `training.rs` `SUICIDE` and `city_at`, 2026-11-08), restarted from Hard's orders and the lab's storm, focus and wait,
scored by rollouts of up to 20 turns (Hard continues for the seat; the enemy alternates Hard and a
sortie policy; common dice for every candidate), `crates/ai/src/training.rs`. The searched orders
replace Hard's only for the battle's units; nothing else changes, and the human seat is never
searched live (his battle positions are logged with his orders instead). The search runs while
the human plans (natively on all cores but one in the desktop app, `app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`
`training_start`; in WASM slices in the browser) and is capped at 15 s a turn
(`localStorage['legatum.training.capMs']`); at the cap the best orders found so far are played.
The UI: a top-bar chip while it runs, a progress card if End Turn has to wait, and a turn-report
line on what changed (`app/src/training.ts`, `app/src/ui/Training.tsx`). Every searched battle,
every human battle position and each one's realised outcome are logged as JSON lines to
`<app data>/training/<archive game id>.jsonl` (schema 1, with the arena embedded so any line can be
searched again: `legatum-sim --retrain LOG`); a Training game's archive is marked `mode: training`
(`crates/wasm/src/history.rs::Metadata`). Headless: `legatum-sim --training DIR` (every Hard seat),
`combat_lab --training-compare OUT`, `war_suite --brain training`; summarise with
`tools/sim/training-report.py`.

In a new solo free-for-all lobby, Standard starts six empires (you and five AI seats); Small starts four and Large eight (`app/src/lobby.ts::MAP_SIZES,newLobby`). Changing map size resets the count to that size's default while every AI seat still has a default name and Random faction. Once any AI seat has a custom name or chosen faction, an in-range count stays as chosen; an out-of-range count is adjusted without removing that customised seat (`app/src/lobby.ts::setMapSize`).

## Calendar (display only, designer 2026-11-14)

The UI shows a calendar year beside the turn number ("Turn 81 · 1130 AD" in the top bar, the turn report header and the Resume and Continue buttons), never in place of it. The year is a pure function of the turn number: no game state, no save shape, no RULES_VERSION bump, and no rule reads it. The anchors (turn to year, BC negative, no year 0, linear interpolation in integer maths, one year per turn after turn 300) are in `crates/core/src/calendar.json`; `crates/core/src/calendar.rs` interpolates (`calendar_year`, `calendar_label`) and the app reads the labels exported to `app/src/rules.generated.json` (`calendarLabels`, refreshed with `UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules`), so there is one implementation. The target is a game ending between turn 250 and 300 (the turn limit is 300). The sim report's "Tech pace against the calendar" table sets the median year each tech is learned beside `calendar::tech_real_year` (Flight 1903, Armoured Warfare 1916) as a pacing gauge for the designers.

## 1. Turn structure and orders

**Simultaneous turns**: all players submit orders for the same state; `resolve_turn` applies them
together (`crates/core/src/rules/mod.rs::resolve_turn`). No "active player."

**Order enum**, one per unit/city/research slot per turn (`crates/core/src/orders.rs`):
diplomacy (`DeclareWar{against,breach}`, `BreakTreaty` = leave an Alliance,
`ProposeTreaty`, `RespondToProposal`, `WithdrawProposal`); units (`SetAutomation`, `SetAutoFire`, `MoveUnit{to}`,
`MoveUnitPath{to,path,stop_on_block}`, `SleepUnit{until_healed}`, `SkipUnit`,
`FoundCity`, `DisbandUnit`, `RangedAttack`, `UpgradeUnit`, `UpgradeSiege`); aircraft (`AirStrike`, `AirRecon`, `Rebase`,
`UpgradeAircraft`: Eras and technologies, "The air layer"); city (`SetProduction`, `SetProductionQueue`, `BuyItem`, `BuyTile{tile}`, `HireCompany`
(Byzantium only), `Raze`, `SetCityAutomation`); research (`SetResearch`, `SetResearchQueue`,
`SetScienceConversion` after the last technology);
and **one-time empire actions** through a single variant, `Action { action }` (`orders.rs::Action`,
serde tag `"action"`: `{"type":"Action","action":{"action":"Sakoku","close":true}}`): `Swarm` (the Aztecs' New Fire),
`Sakoku{close}` (Japan closes or reopens the country), `Broadcast`,
`ChooseFreeTechs{techs}`, `RetireVeteran{unit}` (a unit action: several veterans may retire in one turn,
each once), and `CallTheWild` (`ClaimObjective`, the Demobilise card's order, was removed in rules 40); later goal increments add their variants here, never a new order shape.
Checks are `rules/actions.rs::validate_action` (run by `validate_orders`: each action once per turn;
retirements beyond what completes the goal are refused), resolution is `apply_actions` right after the
city orders (spending free picks first), and `PlayerView.actions` (`ActionsView`: `swarm`, `sakoku`, `broadcast`,
`free_techs`, `retire`, `wild`) says what is available, why not, and previews it.

**One order per unit per turn.** A unit is marked `acted` after its order; a second order for it is
rejected. The UI never offers a multi-step per-unit queue. A unit that attacked stays where it fought (a melee winner that kills advances into the
defender's tile, §4) and needs new orders next turn (`rules/units.rs::validate_unit_order`: "already has an order this turn (one per unit)"; Guide combat entry, `app/src/ui/GuideContent.tsx`).
**Idle orders: two, `SleepUnit` (with an optional `until_healed`, below) and `SkipUnit` (user, 2026-10-20; before that `HoldUnit`, `SleepUnit`, `SkipUnit`, `HealUnit` and `WakeUnit`; recorded use in his 24 games: Hold 221, Skip 265, Heal 98).** Player-facing names: military units say **Fortify**; civilians (including Scouts, Settlers, Military Engineers and Heirs) say **Wait**. Both names use the same `SleepUnit` order and F key. `SkipUnit` remains **Skip this turn** (Space).
`SkipUnit` does nothing this turn: it clears the destination, automation and any Sleep, and the unit asks for orders again next turn. `SleepUnit` is the one
standing order for every unit: it clears the destination and automation, keeps the unit where it is and out of the order cycle until it is woken, given another order,
or a visible military unit of a team at War newly comes into sight within its movement radius (`units::wake_threatened_sleepers`, once per newly arrived enemy, so
re-sleeping beside an enemy already there holds; an `UnitAwakened` event and `needs_orders`). Healing and fortification are not orders: any unit that neither
moved, fought nor was ordered to act rests (`units::heal_and_fortify`: +3 fortify a turn to +6, heal 20 at home, 10 in the field, nothing in closed foreign
land), asleep or not, so a sleeper heals and fortifies and stays asleep at full HP.
Old Fortify never woke for enemies; a Sleeping garrison now does (an at-war military unit newly in sight within its move radius, `units::sleeping_threats`, which asks `rules::is_threat`: **barbarians never wake a Celtic sleeper**, 2026-11-03). **Every order wakes:** any new unit order replaces the standing one. **Hold fire:** core never fires
a unit on its own, only on an explicit `RangedAttack`, so a sleeping (or skipped, or unordered) ranged unit does not shoot; the AI turns its stay-put `SkipUnit`
into a shot when a target is in range (`military.rs::fire_from_holds`) and never sleeps a ranged unit it wants to fire. Recorded tapes with the previous idle order names still deserialize through the aliases in `orders.rs` and `types.rs`.
**Heal (user, 2026-11-01; "heals the unit until it's healed then brings them back into the action queue. i find that I 'f' them and then i forget they exist")** is `SleepUnit { until_healed: true }`, not a third order: the wire shape adds a flag (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Not::not")]`), so a plain Sleep serializes byte for byte as before and old tapes (which wrote `HealUnit`, read as plain `SleepUnit` by the serde alias and `wasm/src/tape.rs`) replay unchanged. It sets `StandingOrder::Heal` and rests and wakes for a new enemy exactly like Sleep (`is_sleeping` counts both); right after `heal_and_fortify`, `units::wake_healed` clears the order the turn HP is full, sets `needs_orders` and emits `Event::UnitHealed {unit, owner, unit_kind, at}` (owner's team only, `event_visibility.rs`). The order is refused (`units::check_heal`, in validation and again at resolution) at full HP ("already at full health") and where the unit heals nothing (closed foreign land). A Heal unit stranded in closed land by a border change is not woken (it just keeps resting for 0). The change was additive when it landed (a v8 save had no Heal state); `RULES_VERSION` is now 9, and a save that holds a Heal order cannot load in an older build.
**Auto-explore (`SetAutomation`, `Automation::Explore`) is open to every land military unit as well as the Scout (designer, 2026-11-14, rules 32; before that Scout only).** `data::can_auto_explore` (Scout or a land military unit; Settlers, Heirs and Military Engineers never, aircraft never since they live in hangars, and there are no ships) is the one test, used by `units.rs::check_automation` ("only Scouts and military units can auto-explore") and by `legatum_ai::explore_step`. The route planner is the same for every kind and uses only the fogged `PlayerView` (client-side, `wasm::explore_step`; the Hard AI never calls it): known land only, never into or beside tiles a known enemy army or firing city can strike, never through a tile held by another military unit, and never across a border closed to the unit (`passage_allowed`: foreign land is closed at Peace for every kind, open at War, to an Alliance and across a Courier Hall holder's land). At War it therefore walks into enemy land but stops the moment a known army or firing city threatens it; inside a rival's land when Peace is signed it routes out first. A unit below 100 HP, or threatened, rests in safe ground (heals in place or walks home), and below 60 HP (`EXPLORE_MIN_HP`) or when threatened it stops the automation and asks for orders; with nothing reachable left to explore it stops with `Complete` and returns to the needs-orders queue. A unit held by the occupation lock refuses the order like any move. UI: Auto-explore (`E`) is the Scout's primary; every other military unit keeps Fortify as its idle order and shows Auto-explore beside Move (never primary), in the compact and the full unit panel, hidden (like Move) while the unit garrisons a city under the occupation lock.
UI: the unit card has Fortify for military units and Wait for civilians (the primary for units that do not lead with Auto-explore or Found City, `F`), **Heal (`H`) for a damaged unit** (hidden at full HP; off with its reason under the row where nothing heals; its tooltip gives the rate and turns to full from `resting_heal`) and Skip this turn (`Space`). A healing unit wears the `mark-heal` badge on the map and "Healing" in its status, and when it wakes the queue label reads "healed to full, needs orders" (`turnFlow.ts`, from the last resolution's `UnitHealed` event); `J` is gone.

**Moving onto a hostile unit/city IS the attack** — one fused step, no "attack-then-move"
(`rules/movement.rs`, G18: moves_left/fortify zeroed after a fight). Ranged units "cannot shoot and
move in the same turn" (Guide ranged entry, `app/src/ui/GuideContent.tsx`). **A unit with both attacks (the Mongol Keshig, §8)
chooses each turn:** a `RangedAttack` from where it stands (it does not move that turn) or a move onto
the enemy (a charge). Any military unit may make the move-onto attack, but only a unit that
`data::attacks_in_melee` (the melee line, mounted units including the Keshig, Scouts) can capture or
reinforce a city; a shooter that steps onto a hostile city is bounced (`rules/movement.rs`,
`RANGED_CAPTURE`).

**Resolution phases** (`rules/mod.rs::resolve_turn`): 0) surprise wars at plain Peace take effect now; 1)
barbarian planning, unit orders, then city orders, then empire actions; 2) ranged attacks incl. aircraft strikes and city-strike bombardment (`air::resolve_ranged_phase`; a ground unit's shot at a fallen, empty hostile city is held for phase 3, §4 "Held shots at a fallen city");
3) movement rounds with combat (loops until nobody advances; inside a round a unit entering its own besieged, ungarrisoned city resolves first, see "Reinforcing your own city goes first" in §4); 4) barbarian camp clearing; 5) city
founding; 6) elimination, heal/fortify, then Heal-order wake-ups (`units::wake_healed`); 7) city production/growth/Gold/upkeep, Company expiry, research, borders, roads (`roads::extend`, `extend_roadworks`) and the strategic-supply update; 8)
scoring/victory, the diplomacy boundary (treaties, calls to arms, Peace), turn advances.

**Movement**: a unit can always take ≥1 step even if it can't afford full terrain cost. `MoveUnit`
is a persistent multi-turn goto; `MoveUnitPath` is a confirmed path re-validated each round. Meeting
engagements (two hostiles into the same tile, or a swap) get no terrain/fortify bonus either side.
Two hostile military claimants entering a tile held only by a hostile civilian meet as on empty
ground. The survivor advances and captures the civilian once; a city or military holder still
defends its tile (`rules/movement.rs::movement_round`, meeting claimants and capture pass).
At 0 HP a hostile city can be captured by a melee claimant when its garrison steps out in the
same round. The departing garrison is a movement dependency; if it remains, the claimant stays
out. The owner's own units stepping in that round are its supporters instead (§4, "Supported defence"). Among friendly claimants, only one that can advance is selected to capture, with another
eligible claimant taking over in the same round if the preferred one is blocked
(`rules/movement.rs::movement_round`, `team_captors` and dependency pass).
No named zone-of-control, but pathing routes around hostile units (`rules/movement.rs::next_step`).
Peaceful foreign cities block routes; a unit
cannot stop on that city (`rules/movement.rs::next_step,movement_round`,
`app/src/path.ts::previewRoute`, `app/src/map/reach.ts::reachTiles`).
An allied city is transit-only: a unit may cross its tile during a route but may not end a move
there. Crossing the city and reaching the first tile beyond it is one atomic move; if the unit
cannot pay for the exit this turn, it stays outside the city or takes another route (`rules/movement.rs::next_step`,
`movement_round`). An order targeting one is refused with “Allied units can pass through Bern but can't stop
there.” A unit already in an allied city must move out on its next move. Order validation and the
scenario state validator mirror this (`rules/units.rs::allied_city_destination`,
`scenario::validate_state`). AI destination selection, client path preview apply the
same endpoint rule while leaving the city traversable en route (`proxy_v7.rs::go`, `diplomacy.ts`,
`path.ts::previewRoute`, `map/reach.ts::reachTiles`). Allied same-class units are likewise transit-only;
`movement.rs::stationary_friend` and `movement_round` hop across their tiles atomically.

**Automatic ranged garrison fire (2026-10-31, S13).** `Order::SetAutoFire` stores
`StandingOrder::AutoFire` on a ranged unit in one of its own cities (`rules/units.rs`).
Each `Session::step` chooses a target from the owner's visible view using the scripted AI's
`Planner::best_shot` (`legatum_ai::automatic_garrison_target`) and records the resulting ranged
order for deterministic replay. The shot goes through ordinary ranged validation and resolution;
when no legal hostile target exists, the unit holds. **A Celtic garrison skips barbarians** (`Planner::best_shot_automatic`, `PlayerView::is_threat`, 2026-11-03: barbarians never attack the Celts, so only an explicit `RangedAttack` shoots one). This standing order never moves, declares War
or spends Gold. A move, wait, skip or upgrade replaces it; a manual ranged shot chooses that turn’s target while the setting persists. The client unit card controls the toggle
(`ui/UnitPanel.tsx`, `commands.ts`); `orders.ts::unitsNeedingOrders` excludes it from Return.

**Roads (2026-10-07; laid by the Courier Hall, §8, and by any empire's Roadworks, §3).** `Tile::road` (a link mask, bit d = neighbour d, `roads::NODE` = road tile) and `Map::road_hub`. **Entering a road tile costs 1 move whatever its terrain** (`Map::move_cost`, `TileView::move_cost`, client path preview), and **a unit that starts its turn on a road tile has +1 move** (`rules::units::reset_turn`; `moves_left` can exceed the unit's max). Roads never grant passage: `passage_allowed` is unchanged, so Peace borders stay closed except in a Hall holder's land. Every cost consumer reads the same function: engine steps, `movement_path`, the AI's `Ctx` cost table and route searches, `settlement`, forecasts, and the client's preview, reach lens and movement lens. `path::find_path_by_step` on a map with roads scales costs by 256 and takes 1 off each road tile, so equal-cost routes prefer roads (a map without roads searches exactly as before, so old tapes are unchanged). `path::path_turns` gives the +1 to a unit that starts a later turn on a road. The view exposes `TileView.road` on every explored tile, always current (public). Client preview: `app/src/data.ts::tileMoveCost`, `app/src/path.ts` (`findPath` road tie-break, `pathTurns`, `planTurns`, `roadMoves`).

**Passing through your own units (2026-10-21, Civ 6; replaces "Friends on the route", 2026-10-03, playtest-6.2 "The Last Bridge").** Still one unit per class per tile at the end of every movement round, but a unit may *pass through* a same-class friend of its team, or its own garrisoned city (the garrison is the friend), during its move; it may not *end* its move on one. User: "it should be reasonable for this unit to be able to walk through the city and through my unit to get to the other side." A friend **stays** this round when it is parked (no goto, no moves, stopped) or attacking in place (goto is an adjacent enemy unit or city, `movement.rs::attacking_in_place`); that includes a friend out of moves for now that still has a destination. One costing chooses every land route (`crates/core/src/path.rs::route_around_friends`), used by resolution (`movement.rs::next_step`, confirmed `MoveUnitPath` steps included), the AI's movement preflight (`crates/ai/src/movement.rs::movement_conflict`) and the client preview (`app/src/path.ts:routeAroundFriends,previewRoute`). Rules: (1) **A hop.** When a unit's route starts across friends that stay, it crosses the whole run in one atomic move and lands on the first tile beyond (`path::hop`, `movement.rs::movement_round`): it pays every tile it crossed, `UnitMoved` goes from its tile to the landing, and no round ever ends with it on a friend. The landing goes through the usual claim rules (unit-id order, reservations, bounces), so two units hopping to one tile still take turns. (2) **Stopping short.** A hop it cannot pay for this round (`total > moves_left`, but it fits full moves) waits for next turn on the last free tile it passed, keeping its destination. A hop with no landing is the plain step into the friend, which fails as before with MoveBlocked "blocked by P0's Soldier, which did not move" (or "...which stayed to attack"; `movement.rs::stayed_reason`, the client says "your Soldier", `app/src/format.ts`): the goto is the friend's tile (the unit comes up beside it), an enemy or neutral unit or city holds the landing (an attack cannot start from two tiles away, so such a route goes round the friend instead), or the crossing costs more than the unit's full moves (a friend on hills plus the tile beyond for a 2-move Soldier; two friends in a row). Planning never picks such a crossing when another route exists (`path::crossing_faults`: that run's first tile is a wall for the search); with none, the plain terrain route is kept and the unit waits behind the friend (a choke). (3) **Waiting friends (2026-10-09, playtest 8.6 "units moved backwards").** A friend that stays only for now (out of moves, same team, still has a goto) whose crossing cannot be made is waited for, not circled: the unit walks round only if the way round takes fewer turns than waiting behind it (`route_around_friends`'s `waiting` argument; resolver `next_step` and AI preflight). Parked friends and attackers staying put are walls for such a crossing. (4) A friend with a move out this round is followable (friendly chains move together) but risky, so a route avoiding every such tile wins whenever it arrives in as few turns (`path::path_turns`, which treats a hop as one atomic move that waits a turn when it does not fit). (5) A route through friends that stay wins over a way round unless the way round arrives in fewer turns. (6) A tile a lower-id friend only passes through this turn is reserved for one movement round, not walled for the turn. A follower may request it and wait one round under the same-owner claim rule, keeping its moves; when that route arrives no later in turns than going round, it follows the friend. A tile where the friend ends its move or stays to attack remains a wall for that round of route planning, and a shared final destination still uses the ordinary claim and bounce rules. The preview shows no warning for a crossing it can make; it says "Moves through this tile right behind your Soldier." (neutral ink, `isCalmRouteNote`) for a transit reservation, "Your Soldier is using the direct path this turn, so this unit takes another route." or "Direct path blocked by your Soldier, which stays put this turn: taking a longer route." when it goes round, "Path blocked by your Soldier, which stops on this tile this turn: this unit waits behind it." when the friend will stop in a choke, and warns "Path may be blocked by your Soldier: it must move out first." for a friend that must leave, "Path blocked by your Soldier, which stays put this turn: this unit waits behind it." for a crossing that cannot be made, and "Your Soldier stays on that tile: this unit can't end its move on it and stops beside it." for a destination held by a friend (`path.ts:routeRiskText`); `RoutePreview.crossed` lists the friends passed through so no turn marker lands on one (`planTurns(..., crossable)`). The preview card headline gives the turns to arrive and, for a route much longer than the straight line, says it bends round terrain or units (`path.ts:routeTurnsText`). Ties break on hex order. Several units ordered onto one city declare in unit-id order; shared final tiles still use the normal claim and bounce rules, while units can follow through earlier claimed transit tiles. The client preview only reserves the first pending step for a lower-id unit: a later unit's speculative route or an earlier unit's future step cannot preempt this mover (`app/src/path.ts::previewRoute`). Reissuing a confirmed move recalculates its path against current orders (`app/src/commands.ts::moveSelected`). Allied same-class units are parked friends for crossing; they and allied cities are transit-only (`movement.rs::stationary_friend`, `app/src/path.ts::previewRoute`).
**Routes avoid known enemy reach (player previews only, 2026-10-11, p881).** A route plotted by the player (the hover preview and the order `moveSelected` queues, both `previewRoute(..., { avoidEnemyReach: true })`; the AI's `previewPath` calls leave it off) prefers, among routes that take the same number of turns, steps that no visible enemy military unit can strike (`app/src/enemyReach.ts`). Model: a unit that steps away is hit where it stood (G21), every unit takes one step per round, so the j-th tile of this turn's route (where the unit stands at the start of round j+1) is dangerous when an enemy with at least j+1 moves can be next to it within j steps (the turn's last tile: within moves-1 steps); ranged shots and city fire land before movement and cannot be dodged by routing, and the destination is the player's choice and never counted. `path.ts::saferRoute` re-plans with the dangerous tiles pruned (up to four times), keeps the original when nothing as short exists, and sets `danger` ('leaving' when the first step is exposed, else 'way'); the preview shows "Enemies can catch this unit leaving." / "...on the way." A route to an enemy's tile (an attack) is never rerouted, and a confirmed `MoveUnitPath` is only checked, never replaced. The core's own pathing (`path.rs`) is unchanged: it follows the submitted path.
All factions, including Mongols, route around occupied enemy tiles and take parting hits when struck while leaving; moving civilians remain pinned by a hostile military unit entering their tile (`rules/movement.rs::next_step,movement_round`). A confirmed route through a friend is legal (`units.rs::check_known_path`).

**UI**: click/drag to move; clicking an enemy at Peace opens a "Declare War?" dialog, never a silent
attack; ranged click-to-target with range check; Sleep/Found/Disband/Upgrade/Skip
each issue one order. Production/research queues (city-panel code, not inspected): Shift-click
queues a technology (Guide, Controls: "Research: click / Shift+click", `app/src/ui/GuideContent.tsx`). **One purchase per city per
turn**, an item (`economy.rs::buy_blocker`, `City::last_purchase_turn`).

## 2. Game start

Every empire starts with a population-1 capital founded on its balanced start hex, its centre and first ring claimed, and no production selected. A Scout and Soldier stand on the first two passable neighbors in hex order; no faction, including Rome, starts with a Settler (`crates/core/src/game.rs::try_new_game`, `rules/cities.rs::found_city_at`). The capital gets the ordinary founding name, capital and original-capital flags, Palace yield, and a turn-1 `Chronicle::Founded` entry. It does not train a Settler or raise its price. Extra `rule_experiments.start_settlers` Settlers are placed around the capital. `blank_game` remains empty for tests. Mapgen balances the sites with `balance_founding_sites`. **Every empire starts with a treasury of `data::START_GOLD` = 60 Gold** (`game.rs::try_new_game`; `blank_game`, used by tests, still starts at 0; `rules.generated.json` `startGold`). It is below Swiss interest's first step (100 Gold) and far under the 5,000-Gold hoard goal.

**Turn-1 legality**: an item needs its tech prereq met (`economy.rs::production_blocker`). At turn 1 no techs are
known, so the already-founded capital can choose Scout, Spearman (`Soldier`), Archer (`Ranger`), Horseman or the Granary (+2 Food, 50 Production); a
**Settler needs population 2** (below). A "Workshop turn 1" opening is impossible — Workshop
needs Bronze Working.

## Eras and technologies (era overhaul, phase A1, 2026-10-10)

**Nine eras** replace the old four (`crates/core/src/eras.rs`): Ancient, Classical, Medieval, Renaissance, Industrial, **Machine Age** (added 2026-11-14, designer: "the tech jump to modern is too big. tanks + flight is OP"; spec `docs/design/machine-age-proposal-2026-11-14.md`), **World War** (the era first called Modern; display rename only, 2026-11-14), **Modern** (1950 to 2010, added 2026-11-14 after World War; spec `docs/design/ninth-era-proposal-2026-11-14.md`), Future. Five technologies each (45 in all; the Future era is a *living data set* and may grow: the table in `eras::TECHS` is the single source, with a note on how to update it). The old 32-technology tree (Recovery, Consolidation, Integration, Inheritance) is gone: Salvage Protocols, Closed Habitats, Nutrient Synthesis, Precision Industry, Planetary Accord and Legacy Integration were deleted outright; the rest map to the nearest new technology when an old save loads (`TechId::from_name`; saves written with old names load, a plan from the ungated tree is repaired by `cities::normalize_research` the first turn it resolves).

- **The strict gate (user, 2026-10-10).** An empire may research only unknown technologies of its **current era** (`eras::current_era`: the first era that still has an unknown technology; `economy::tech_available`). There are **no prerequisites inside an era**, so any of the five can come first. `SetResearch` and `SetResearchQueue` are refused outside the current era ("X is in the Y era: finish the Z era first"); a queue holds only current-era technologies, no duplicates. Finishing the era's last technology enters the next era **that turn**: `eras::learn` (the only way a technology is learned, also for the Athens' free picks) emits `Event::EraEntered { player, era (the era entered, 1-based), first }`, public to every viewer; `first` marks the first empire into that era (`GameState::era_firsts`, shown in `ResearchView.era_firsts`; two empires finishing on the same turn: the lower player id is first). Nothing is granted on entry: the unlocks and the open techs are the reward. The research queue is empty after an era-up, so the player (or the AI, next planning pass) chooses the new era's order; stored Science carries over. The phase C full-screen era-up takeover hooks on `EraEntered`.
- **Public era.** `PlayerPublic.era` (**1-based**: 1 Ancient .. 6 Machine Age .. 7 World War .. 8 Modern .. 9 Future, the VP numbering; `ResearchView.era`, `TechOption.era`, the `eras::*` functions and `era_firsts` keys are 0-based) for every player in the public view. **Stable API for other systems (VP v2, AI, UI):** `eras::ERA_COUNT` (9), `eras::ERA_NAMES`, `eras::era_name(era)`, `eras::current_era(&techs)`, `eras::era_of(&state, player)` / `GameState::era_of(player)`, `eras::era_of_tech`, `eras::era_techs(era)`, `eras::tech_def(tech)`. The Future era is the last era and is never finished into another; an empire that knows all 45 stays in era 8.
- **Cost.** `economy::tech_cost` = the listed cost (the table below), less **tech diffusion** (10% per other living empire that knows it, capped at 50%). Byzantium uses the same research rule and builds an ordinary Library. A closed country (Sakoku) gets no diffusion. The breakdown names each line. Nothing the empire does changes a technology's price.
- **No boosts (user, 2026-10-17).** Every technology once had a boost earned from play (a Civ 6 Eureka: "Own 3 cities", "Build a Walls", 40% off). They were removed entirely (core: the conditions, `Player::boosts`/`tallies`, `Event::BoostEarned`, `TechOption.boost`; AI: the research order's boost patience; the app). The user: "just things that happen randomly and not things that affect strategic decision making". Tech costs were lowered to stand in for the average discount boosts gave, era by era, so era entry turns held (see Pacing).
- **After the last technology (designer decision 2026-10-31).** Once `eras::all_researched` is true at the start of a turn, `SetScienceConversion` chooses Gold, Food or Production empire-wide (default Gold; changeable every turn). Gold adds floor(total empire Science / 2) to the treasury; Food or Production adds floor(each city's own Science / 2) in that city, with its own yield breakdown line (`economy.rs::yields_for_item`, `player_income`; `rules/cities.rs::process_research` stops accruing Science toward research). Stored in `Player::science_conversion` (default Gold). It awards no VP. The research panel forecasts all three choices (`app/src/ui/TechPanel.tsx`).
- **Forecast.** `ResearchView.schedule` gives each queued technology's cost, turns from now and arrival turn at the current Science income, in queue order (pillar 5).
- **Unit unlocks (phase A2, built).** Every technology that used to announce a unit now unlocks it (table below); the placeholder `CardEffect::Soon` is gone. `data::unit_class(kind)` (`UnitClass` Melee/Ranged/Mounted/Air) is read by combat (`combat::class_bonus`) and `data::unit_era_tier(kind)` by the AI's quality ranking.
- **Pacing (Standard, Hard + Search, 4 players, 512 games):** see `docs/sim-results/era-overhaul/no-boosts.md` (median winner enters Classical..Future at T27/63/103/140/174/204 in 4-player games, within a turn or two of the games before boosts were removed (T27/63/103/140/173/200)). Costs are the `cost` field of `eras::TECHS`. Rules 31 (designer, 2026-11-13) had raised the five Modern costs about 30% because Modern was the shortest era in his tapes; rules 33 (designer, 2026-11-14) took that back off, since the Machine Age now does that job: the five technologies that moved down into the Machine Age cost their pre-31 prices again (388 to 422, era total 2,020), and each new Modern technology costs the geometric midpoint of the Machine Age and Future technology in the same slot (sqrt(388 x 654) = 504 .. sqrt(422 x 720) = 551, era total 2,633). There is no cost multiplier in code: the table is the price. The era-entry turns above are pre-Machine-Age sim medians for Industrial and later; recalibrate with the sim's "Era pacing" report (`telemetry.era_entries`).

| Era | Tech (identifier) | Cost (Science) | Unlocks |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Ancient | Irrigation | 24 | **+1 Food in every city** (the Granary needs no tech) |
| Ancient | Bronze Working | 26 | Workshop |
| Ancient | Masonry | 30 | Walls (Production only); Pyramids (world wonder); Quarries +1 Production (Deep Quarry) |
| Ancient | Currency | 32 | Market |
| Ancient | Writing | 38 | Library |
| Classical | Mathematics | 48 | Phalangite (Spearmen upgrade to it); units can buy **Siege** (siege tier 1: ignores Walls; Industrial and later units have it built in) |
| Classical | Engineering | 48 | Roadworks (every faction); Composite Archer (Archers upgrade to it) |
| Classical | Iron Working | 56 | Reveals Iron; Forge; Mine upgrade (Deep Mine); Cataphract (Horsemen upgrade to it) |
| Classical | Code of Laws (`CodeOfLaws`) | 62 | **Every existing city claims its best available ring-2 tile on research; later cities do so on founding** |
| Classical | Philosophy | 68 | Great Library (world wonder); +1 Science in every city |
| Medieval | Feudalism | 120 | Pikeman (`Trooper`; Phalangites upgrade to it); Mill |
| Medieval | Machinery | 126 | Crossbowman (`Marksman`; Composite Archers upgrade to it); Hagia Sophia (world wonder); +1 Production per Workshop family |
| Medieval | Chivalry | 132 | Knight (Cataphracts upgrade to it); Castle (Walls tier 2, Production only); War Shrine (Sparta) |
| Medieval | Education | 136 | University |
| Medieval | Crop Rotation | 140 | Farm/Pasture upgrade (Irrigated Farm, Ranch); +1 Food per Granary family |
| Renaissance | Gunpowder | 216 | Musketman (`AssaultTrooper`; Pikemen upgrade to it); Arquebusier (Crossbowmen upgrade to it); Niter visible |
| Renaissance | Metallurgy | 224 | Cuirassier (Knights upgrade to it); Siege reaches Star Forts (tier 3). Machinery (Medieval) is tier 2 (Castles) |
| Renaissance | Military Engineering | 230 | Star Fort (Walls tier 3, Production only); **unit upgrades cost half** |
| Renaissance | Banking | 238 | Bank; Grand Bazaar (world wonder) |
| Renaissance | Printing Press | 248 | **+1 Science per Library and +1 Gold per Market** |
| Industrial | Rifling | 344 | Rifleman (Musketmen upgrade to it). Every Industrial or later land unit has **Siege built in** (ignores Walls, Castles and Star Forts; `data::built_in_siege`) |
| Industrial | Military Science | 352 | Cavalry (Cuirassiers upgrade to it; Siege built in); land units cost 10% less Production |
| Industrial | Ballistics (`Cannonry`) | 356 | Field Cannon (Arquebusiers upgrade to it; Siege built in); Royal Arsenal (world wonder) |
| Industrial | Industrialization | 368 | Factory; Coal visible |
| Industrial | Railways | 376 | **A unit that starts its turn on a road gets +2 moves (was +1)**; Roadworks link cities within 12 hexes (was 8); Mines +1 Production |
| Machine Age | Replaceable Parts | 388 | Trench Infantry (Riflemen upgrade to it); Factories +2 Production |
| Machine Age | Combustion | 394 | Early Tank (`Landship`; Cavalry upgrade to it); Oil Wells +1 Gold; Oil visible |
| Machine Age | Steel | 402 | Howitzer (Field Cannons upgrade to it); Eiffel Tower (world wonder); Howitzers, Artillery and Combat Drones fire indirectly over terrain at targets their team sees (`data::indirect_fire`, `los::ranged_clear_with_tech`) |
| Machine Age | Flight | 414 | Biplane (aircraft, based in cities) |
| Machine Age | Electricity | 422 | Stock Exchange |
| World War | Mass Mobilisation (`MassMobilisation`) | 504 | Infantry (Trench Infantry upgrades to it) |
| World War | Rocketry | 514 | Artillery (Howitzers upgrade to it) |
| World War | Armoured Warfare (`ArmouredWarfare`) | 525 | Tank (Early Tanks upgrade to it) |
| World War | Air Power (`AirPower`) | 539 | Fighter and Bomber; each Biplane upgrades to either, the player's choice |
| World War | Electronics | 551 | Research Lab |
| Modern | Mechanised Warfare (`MechanisedWarfare`) | 654 | Mechanised Infantry (Infantry upgrades to it; 3 moves) |
| Modern | Composite Armour (`CompositeArmour`) | 670 | Main Battle Tank (Tanks upgrade to it) |
| Modern | Guided Missiles (`GuidedMissiles`) | 686 | Rocket Artillery (Artillery upgrades to it; range 3, indirect fire); Air Defence (city building) |
| Modern | Jet Engines (`JetEngines`) | 702 | Jet Fighter and Jet Bomber (Fighter and Bomber upgrade to them) |
| Modern | Green Revolution (`GreenRevolution`) | 720 | Supermarket (the Food line's third building) |
| Future | Autonomous Systems | 840 | Combat Drone (Rocket Artillery upgrades to it); Autonomous Armour (Main Battle Tanks upgrade to it); Silicon visible |
| Future | Robotics | 862 | Hunter-Killer, a four-legged armed ground robot (Mechanised Infantry upgrades to it) |
| Future | Fusion Power | 884 | +5 Production in every city; Factory and Research Lab no longer use Coal/Oil; ITER Fusion Reactor (world wonder) |
| Future | Stealth Technology | 906 | Stealth Fighter (Jet Fighter upgrade, strike 7, guard 5), Stealth Bomber (Jet Bomber upgrade, strike 10, only Stealth Fighters intercept); both use Silicon; hangars hold 3 |
| Future | Recursive AI (`RecursiveAi`) | 930 | Reveal the whole map to the team (not unit sight); +20% Production in every city |

**Indirect fire and built-in Siege (2026-10-31).** Steel lets Howitzers (2026-11-14), Artillery and Combat Drones shoot through Forest, Hills and Mountains, but the target must be visible to their team. This applies to order validation and combat forecasting (`rules::battle::ranged_target`, `forecast::forecast_attack_mode`); shots remain range 2 and cannot capture a city. Both kinds already receive full built-in Siege from the Industrial rule (`data::built_in_siege`, `siege_cover`): they ignore Walls, Castles and Star Forts without buying Siege or using Iron for it. Hard uses the same indirect-fire check for target selection and siege positioning, prices visible enemy gun fire with actual wall cover, and places a melee screen beside a positioned gun (`ai/tactics.rs`, `military.rs`, `siege.rs`).

Display names use the Rust identifiers in orders and the wire format: Laboratory = **Library**, College = **University**, Clearinghouse = **Bank**, Hydroponics = **Mill**, MachineShop = **Forge**, Autoworks = **Factory**, InferenceCore = **Research Lab**, StockExchange = **Stock Exchange**, RelayUplink = **Great Library**, PlanetaryFoundry = **Royal Arsenal**, WorldExchange = **Grand Bazaar**. `Citadel` was renamed `Castle`  and `StarFort` was added (Walls tiers 2 and 3); `Arcology` is deleted. Walls tiers add up: Walls +10, Castle +5, Star Fort +5 city strength (`combat::city_defense_with_fort`, `fort` 0/1/2; the city fires with any tier); the Mongols build none of the three. **Railways:** `rules::units::reset_turn` gives a unit that starts its turn on a road `RAILWAY_MOVES` (2) extra moves for an empire that knows Railways, else `ROAD_MOVES` (1); app route previews use the same bonus (`app/src/path.ts::roadMoves`, `crossingFaults`).

**Units/buildings** (the listed Production cost, `data::unit_stats` / `building_stats`).. The identifiers keep their pre-overhaul spelling in orders and the wire format and the display names are historical (`data::unit_name`): Soldier = **Spearman**, Ranger = **Archer**, Trooper = **Pikeman**, Marksman = **Crossbowman**, AssaultTrooper = **Musketman**. The unit data is `data::unit_stats` and is exported to the app with the rules (`app/src/rules.generated.json`: `unitStats`, `factionUnitStats`, `standardUnitStats`, `factionBuildingStats`, `standardBuildingStats`, `terrainYields`, `cityGaps`, `unitNames`, `unitOrder`, `classBonusPercent`, `mountedStrengthPercent`, `mountedCityPenaltyPercent`).

**Three land classes and the triangle** (`data::unit_class`, `combat::class_bonus`; user decision 2026-10-10, "just 3 types"; normal melee is the spear line): the **melee line** beats **mounted**, **ranged** beats the melee line (by range, no bonus), **mounted** beats ranged. The **Military Engineer** is a civilian, not a class (§4, "Walls and the Military Engineer"). **A unit gains `combat::CLASS_BONUS_PERCENT` (40%) of its own base strength** (its faction's `unit_stats_for` strength, rounded down: `class_bonus_strength`) against the class it beats, whether it attacks or defends, in melee or in a meeting engagement: *Melee line against mounted (+40%)* (melee line unit vs a mounted unit) and *Mounted against ranged (+40%)* (mounted unit vs a ranged unit, **in melee only (designer, 2026-11-10; it was also given under fire):** when the mounted unit charges, or the two meet or fight in melee; a mounted unit that is the target of a shot, a Keshig's included, gets nothing, so a Field Cannon (65) shooting Cavalry (79) on open ground is forecast 16 damage, not 4; `combat::fight_bonus_of`, rules version 26). A percentage (designer, 2026-10-31, replacing the flat +18 of 2026-10-10), so the bonus remains 40% of the unit’s strength while both fighters are before the World War era, but later era steps can outweigh it: Spearman +8, Phalangite +10, Pikeman +12, Musketman +17, Rifleman +24; Horseman +10, Cataphract +13, Knight +16, Cuirassier +22, Cavalry +31. The bonus is its own breakdown line, label stating the percentage (`combat::MELEE_VS_MOUNTED_LABEL`, `MOUNTED_VS_RANGED_LABEL`), in every fight (`rules/battle.rs::plan_fight_on`, `combat::add_fight_class_bonus_rules` with the unit's faction) and forecast (`forecast.rs::forecast_attack_mode`), projected to `app/src/combat.ts::classBonus` through `Game.combat_projection`. The kind-only `combat::class_bonus` is the bonus in melee (a charge or meeting); `fight_class_bonus` (no faction edge) serves the AI's estimates of a fight and takes the shot into account; `class_bonus_for` / `fight_class_bonus_for` take the faction. **Mounted units are stronger than their era's melee line and cost more (designer, 2026-10-31, the Civ 6 way: its Horseman 36 against the Spearman's 25; "i basically never buy things from that class until there are tanks"):** every horse's (Horseman to Cavalry, `data::is_horse_mounted`) design strength is `data::MOUNTED_STRENGTH_PERCENT` (130%) of its era's design melee line (`mounted_design_strength`), so a spear line with its bonus is a few points ahead of a rider (Spearman 28 against the Horseman's 26), an even-to-good trade per Production rather than the old massacre (a Horseman charging a Spearman dealt 13 and took 67; now 28 and 32). **Riders storm cities badly (2026-10-31, chosen in the combat lab to keep city capture the melee line's job; the -33% itself decided by the designer on 2026-10-31):** a horse (`data::is_horse_mounted`; 2026-11-14, it was "a pre-Modern mounted unit") charging a city loses `combat::MOUNTED_CITY_PENALTY_PERCENT` (33%) of its base strength, the breakdown line *Mounted against a city (-33%)* (`combat::add_mounted_city_penalty`, in the fight and the forecast): Horseman 26 - 8 = 18, Cataphract 33 - 10, Knight 42 - 13, Cuirassier 57 - 18, Cavalry 79 - 26, a little below the era's melee line; shots (the Keshig's bow), and armour (the Machine Age Landship, the Tank, the Autonomous Armour) pay nothing. Validation: `combat_scenarios --mounted` (`crates/sim/examples/combat_scenarios/mounted.rs`) and a `combat_lab` subset, `docs/sim-results/unit-classes/mounted.md`. Mounted units have 4 moves (`data::MOUNTED_MOVES`; Germany ordinary, Mongols +1 as for every unit); **the Machine Age Landship has 3** (`data::LANDSHIP_MOVES`, the proposal's "armour is slow"), **the Tank has 5 and the Autonomous Armour 6** (`data::TANK_MOVES`, `data::AUTONOMOUS_ARMOUR_MOVES`; user 2026-10-19, the 6 on the correction that the Future armour must not be slower than the Tank). **The triangle ends at the Modern era (user, 2026-10-20; this replaced the open-ground rule of 2026-10-19, which let the melee line keep its bonus against armour only in a city, Forest or Hills):** a class bonus applies only when *both* units are from before the Modern era (`combat::TRIANGLE_END_ERA_TIER` 6, the era tier of the Infantry, Artillery and Tank; the Machine Age, tier 5, is inside the triangle, so Trench Infantry gets +40% against a Landship and a Landship +40% charging a Howitzer; `combat::triangle_applies`, used by `class_bonus`, `fight_class_bonus` and `add_fight_class_bonus_rules`, projected to the app through `Game.combat_projection`). So an Infantry against a Cavalry or a Tank gets no *Melee line against mounted*, a Tank against an Artillery or Cavalry against an Artillery gets no *Mounted against ranged*, and the forecast simply has no line; a Rifleman against a Cavalry, or a Cavalry against a Field Cannon, still does. From the Modern era units differ by strength, cost and moves only. Unit cards for Modern and Future units say so (`unlockCardModel.unitMatchup`), and the Guide has one sentence. The bonus grows with the unit's strength until Modern, while later era steps can outgrow its advantage (the last step is +50%).

**Outrider (corrected 2026-10-31).** Any melee unit may order an attack on a visible horse (Horseman to Cavalry, `data::is_horse_mounted`; 2026-11-14, it was "pre-Modern mounted unit") from a reachable distance. If the attacker began beyond adjacency, it walks toward the horse's turn-start tile without following it. A horse that stays is attacked; one that rides away escapes, and the order ends with a MoveBlocked event. An attacker that began adjacent still pins the horse and strikes it if it steps away (G21). Ranged attacks, city strikes and mounted attacks are unchanged. There is no Spearman or Pikeman exception. Armour (the Machine Age Landship, the Tank, the Main Battle Tank, the Autonomous Armour) has no Outrider (`combat.rs::outrider_blocks`, `rules/movement.rs::start_pursuits`, `aim_pursuits`, `finish_pursuits`, `forecast.rs::forecast_attack_mode`).

**Future technology rules (2026-11-01).** `Robotics` unlocks the Hunter-Killer, an armed four-legged autonomous melee unit; `HumanoidRobot` is a serde alias. `StealthTechnology` replaces Quantum Computing at the same cost and position (`QuantumComputing` is a save/tape alias). Fighters upgrade to Stealth Fighters (strike 7, guard 5) and Bombers to Stealth Bombers (strike 10, +48 against city HP); both take Silicon. Only a Stealth Fighter may intercept a Stealth Bomber (`rules/air.rs`); aircraft upgrade in a hangar with `UpgradeAircraft`, paying Gold and requiring spare Silicon. `FusionPower` adds +5 Production in each city and releases Factory and Research Lab tiers from Coal/Oil (`resources::building_need_for`). `RecursiveAi` gives +20% Production in each city (of the Production after Fusion's +5 and faction bonuses, before natural-wonder bonuses) as a separate breakdown line, and once, when it is learned, records every map tile as explored with public city, improvement and border memory for the research's team (`eras::learn`, `visibility::reveal_all_to_team`); it gives no new sight of units or combat targets, and later changes to the map stay under ordinary fog.

**One unit per class in every era (user revision, 2026-10-10; this replaced the lead's two-era-generations idea).** Each of the three classes has its own unit in each of the nine eras, unlocked by a technology of that era (Ancient: from the start; Classical: Mathematics, Engineering, Iron Working; Medieval: Feudalism, Machinery, Chivalry; Renaissance: Gunpowder (Musketman, Arquebusier), Metallurgy (Cuirassier); Industrial: Rifling, Ballistics, Military Science; Machine Age: Replaceable Parts, Steel, Combustion; World War: Mass Mobilisation, Rocketry, Armoured Warfare; Modern: Mechanised Warfare, Guided Missiles, Composite Armour; Future: Robotics, Autonomous Systems), with historical names (the Future list is a living data set). **The strength curve (user, 2026-10-18, playtest 8.9.4: "each tech era is a big jump", "the difference between a tank and a second-era pikeman should be vast"; this replaced the even steps of 2026-10-10).** **The step grows (user, 2026-10-19, replacing the flat 30%).** Each era makes the melee line stronger than the last by a step that is 5 points bigger each time, `data::ERA_STEP_PERCENT` = 25, 30, 35, 40, 45, 50%; the Machine Age (2026-11-14) splits the 45% Industrial-to-Modern step into 21% and 20% (1.21 x 1.20 = 1.45), so the table was 25, 30, 35, 40, 21, 20, 50%, and the Modern era (2026-11-14) adds a 20% step before the Future's: `data::ERA_STEP_PERCENT` = 25, 30, 35, 40, 21, 20, 20, 50%, `data::MELEE_LINE` = 20, 25, 32, 44, 61, 74, 89, 107, 161 (exact products rounded to the nearest, a .5 rounding down), so the steps are +5, +7, +12, +17, +13, +15, +18, +54: every World War strength is what it was before the Machine Age, and the Future, one era step on, is about 20% above what it was (Hunter-Killer 161, was 134). Every other strength is a **design number** (the old straight-line value: Knight 32, Field Cannon 47/36, Walls 10) that fixes how the unit compares with the melee line of its own era, then rides the curve: `data::era_scaled(design, era) = design * MELEE_LINE[era] / DESIGN_MELEE_LINE[era]` (`DESIGN_MELEE_LINE` = the old 20 + 6 per era), rounded half up (`DESIGN_MELEE_LINE` = 20, 26, 32, 38, 44, **49**, 50, **50**, 56 (the Modern era takes the World War era's 50, so each Modern unit keeps its World War line's design numbers and stands one 20% step above it: 107/89 = 1.20): the Machine Age's 49 lies between its neighbours and is the one value that puts its units on the proposal's numbers, a factor of 74/49 = 1.51; the proposal guessed "about 1.39"). So same-era balance between the classes is untouched, and only the gap between eras grows. `unit_stats` returns the scaled numbers (`design_stats` the design ones); the era of a unit is the era of its technology (`unit_era_tier`). Faction edges are design numbers too and are added before scaling (Byzantium +4, Celts -2 melee and +3 ranged on the archery line, the Keshig's bow 18), as are the siege bonuses vs city HP (`siege_city_bonus`: the Bomber, Jet Bomber and Stealth Bomber 20 design), so the Keshig and the Byzantium Legionary keep their edge over what they replace. Terrain, fortify, wounds and the Hoplite phalanx stay flat; the class bonus is a percentage of the unit's strength (40%, 2026-10-31), while both fighters are before Modern; later era steps can outweigh a counter. Cost still rises by a fixed ratio (melee 1.38, mounted 1.36, ranged 1.27 from a dearer base of 66). Results on the accelerating curve (mean rolls, open ground, `crates/sim/examples/era_duels.rs`): same era, the first striker wins narrowly (melee kills melee in 4 attacks with 10 HP left); one era ahead, the newer melee unit wins in three attacks with 51 to 56 HP left up to the Medieval era and in two with 81 to 90 HP left from the Renaissance on; two eras ahead it wins in two attacks with 81 to 91 HP left (one attack from the Modern era on); three eras ahead in one or two, and from the Industrial era on a unit two eras ahead kills anything in one blow. With the triangle ended at the Modern era (2026-10-20) a same-era Tank still beats an Infantry (3 attacks, 51 HP left; the Infantry attacking dies after dealing back 29) on strength alone, and a Tank kills a Rifleman, an era behind, in one attack. The old validation below was for the straight line and is kept for the same-class numbers at the start of the game. Validation with the scenario simulator (`combat_scenarios --eras`, four of the newer unit against the same Production of the older, Human policy, `resolve_tactical_turn`; `docs/sim-results/unit-classes/eras.txt`): the same class one era ahead wins 61 to 70% (melee), 61 to 67% (mounted) and 61 to 64% (ranged; 43% for the Combat Drone); two eras ahead 81 to 91% (melee), 71 to 82% (mounted) and about 50% (ranged: unanswered fire lets an older shooting swarm keep pace); three or more eras 94 to 98% (melee), 88 to 98% (mounted) and 57 to 65% (ranged). The Hard policy (the scripted AI on both sides) plays these duels timidly and reports far lower numbers (newer units 0 to 20%); read the Human column. Earlier scenario duels found a counter-class unit one era behind could win in early eras; their Future-era cavalry result conflicts with the current Modern-era triangle cutoff and is not a current-rule claim. No composition beat every archetype at 60%+ in those duels (`responses.txt`). Every existing unit can **upgrade straight to the newest researched unit of its line for Gold** (`upgrades_to`: `UPGRADE_COST_PERCENT` (200%) of the cost difference, with a breakdown in the unit panel and on the research card; the final unit must have spare supply for its resource; every skipped step is charged), so an army is not stranded by an era change; the AI upgrades idle units when the Gold and its runway allow (`military.rs::hold_or_upgrade`). The faction rules read the class, and a faction that cannot build a class (the Aztecs and horse-mounted units) is one more line in `data::faction_unavailable_reason`, as Mongols's no-Engineers rule is.

Spearman, Archer and Horseman are available from turn 1, so the triangle exists from the start. Upgrades follow `upgrades_to` through every researched step in one order (summed Gold price, `rules/units.rs::upgrade_plan`, `check_upgrade`; **the one branching upgrade (user, 2026-11-14):** a Biplane's first step is the player's choice of Fighter or Bomber (`data::upgrade_choices`, `upgrade_plan_via`, `Order::UpgradeAircraft { plane, to }`, `to` None = the default Fighter), then the chain goes on along that line (a Bomber to a Stealth Bomber once known); the Air wing shows one Upgrade row per line with its price, `AircraftView::upgrade_options`; final supply only, `resources::unit_upgrade_need_to`; every land military unit from the Classical era on uses 1 of its era's resource while it exists, so its builds and upgrades need a spare one, and an upgrade switches to the new era's resource: §3 "Each era is defined by its resource", `resources::unit_need`, `upgrade_need`). Faction rules read the class: the Sparta melee line ignores wound penalties and Oathbearers replace Spearmen; the Byzantium Pikeman is the Tagmatic Soldier, and it and every later melee-line unit get +4 design strength (era-scaled: +4 to +10) and +10 Production (`data::unit_stats_for`); the Mongols Cataphract is the Keshig, a mounted unit with a bow (§8); Mongols raise no Walls line and buy no Siege before the Medieval era (`data::MONGOL_WALLS_ERA` 2); Germany has ordinary unit movement. The AI chooses its mix by value: `crates/ai/src/composition.rs` (ladders, era quality) and `production.rs::composition_bonus` (what a class is worth next to the enemy army it can see, a balance term when it sees none, and a rush bonus for mounted).

**The air layer (Machine Age to Future, with the Modern era's jets and Air Defence; `rules/air.rs`, `data::is_aircraft`).** **The Biplane** (Flight, Machine Age, 2026-11-14; user: "they should be able to attack too, but smaller radius and not overwhelming damage") is the first aircraft: strike strength 60 (design 40) within `data::BIPLANE_STRIKE_RANGE` = 3 of its city, no city bonus and **half damage to a city's HP** (`data::BIPLANE_CITY_DAMAGE_PERCENT` 50 via `data::city_hp_damage_percent` and `combat::city_damage_percent`, applied in `battle::plan_fight_on`, `city_force` and the forecast, which reports it as `BattleForecast::city_hp_percent`); it guards like a Fighter (`data::is_fighter` is true for it) within `data::BIPLANE_GUARD_RANGE` = 2 (`fighter_guard_radius`); and **every aircraft gives sight** (user 2026-11-15: a Bomber could not see its targets; before RULES_VERSION 36 only the Biplane did): its team sees every tile within `data::AIRCRAFT_SIGHT` = 4 of its city, terrain ignored, as native sight (`visibility::add_air_sight` over every `data::is_aircraft` kind, so it also makes contact), which is what a Howitzer's indirect fire and a strike's visible-target check need. It uses 1 Oil. With Air Power it upgrades to a Fighter or a Bomber, the player's choice. Fighter and Bomber (class Air) are built like units (Air Power, World War era; the Modern era's Jet Fighter and Jet Bomber, Jet Engines, are the same with one more tile of reach) but complete into a city's **hangar** (`GameState::aircraft`, `state::Aircraft {id, kind, city, hp, xp}`, ids from the unit id space; at most `data::AIR_BASE_CAP` = 2 per city, or `STEALTH_AIR_BASE_CAP` = 3 for an owner who knows Stealth Technology, enforced by `spawn_tile` and the placement/buy blockers: "The hangar is full"). They never stand on a map tile: they are not garrison (`rules::is_military` is false for aircraft), cannot be targeted on a tile, never capture, use 1 Oil supply (Biplane, Fighter, Bomber, Jet Fighter, Jet Bomber) or 1 Silicon supply (Stealth) while based but pay no Gold upkeep (`data::pays_upkeep`), earn XP and promotions like ground units (see Promotions; RULES_VERSION 27, user 2026-11-10), appear with private HP, supply, level and XP only to their own team (`PlayerView::aircraft`: `level`, `xp`, `xp_next`); a rival who can currently see their base sees aircraft kinds in `air_sightings`, and sighted aircraft and reported air battles leave `air_contacts` without exposing hidden hangars, and are destroyed with their city when it is captured or razed (`air::lose_city_aircraft`, a `UnitDestroyed` event each). Four orders, one per aircraft per turn (`Order::AirStrike {plane, target}`, `Order::AirRecon {plane, target}`, `Order::Rebase {plane, to}`, `Order::UpgradeAircraft {plane}`; validation in `air::validate_orders`, errors name the rule): any aircraft can **strike** a visible enemy unit or city (the same target choice as a ranged shot, `battle::target_at`, so a city with HP absorbs it) within its strike range from its city: a **Bomber** `BOMBER_RANGE` = 8 (user 2026-10-19; it was 5) with effective strike strength 114 and +36 against city HP (unscaled 64 and 20 in `siege_city_bonus`, before era combat scaling), a **Jet Bomber** `JET_BOMBER_RANGE` = 9 with strike strength 137 and +43 against city HP, a **Fighter** `FIGHTER_STRIKE_RANGE` = 5 (was 3; its guard radius `FIGHTER_RANGE` stays 4) with effective strike strength 103 (unscaled 58) and no city bonus, a **Jet Fighter** `JET_FIGHTER_STRIKE_RANGE` = 6 (guard radius `FIGHTER_RANGE` 4) with strike strength 124 (user 2026-10-17: "let fighters strike, but with more limited range"; `unit_stats` `range`/`ranged`, so `validate_strike`, `forecast_air_strike` and the AI read one table). **Recon** (`Order::AirRecon {plane, target}`, designer 2026-11-15: "both steps on bomber recon"; `air::validate_recon`, `air::settle_recon`, `visibility::add_recon_sight`): the aircraft spends its one order this turn to scout any tile on the map from 1 to its own strike range from its city (`unit_stats(kind).range`: Biplane 3, Fighter 5, Bomber 8, jets 6 and 9, Stealth 7 and 10), seen or not, war or not. The tiles within `data::RECON_RADIUS` = 2 of the target (19 tiles), terrain ignored, are in the owner's team's native sight for **the next turn only**: `settle_recon` records an `AirRecon {owner, target, turn}` as the last step of the ranged phase (after strikes, rebases and upgrades, so nothing changes this turn's fire or movement), and `add_recon_sight` applies it only while `state.turn == turn + 1` (the planning turn, its strike validation, its movement rounds and its end-of-turn exploration), after which `settle_recon` drops it at the next ranged phase. It is live sight of those tiles on that turn, not a snapshot, so units that moved in show; it explores the terrain for good (native sight) and can make contact like any native sight; allies share it as they share any sight, a rival team sees nothing of it. Refusals name the rule: not an aircraft ("A Scout is not an aircraft: only aircraft can scout from the air"), not yours, off the map, "pick a tile away from <city>", "target is out of range: N tiles from <city>, range R", "already has an order this turn (one per aircraft)" (so a Recon and a strike for one aircraft cannot both stand; the first listed wins, the second is refused). A Fighter that recons still guards (like a rebasing Fighter; `used_fighters` is filled only by valid strikes). The AI has no Recon yet and reads only its own `PlayerView`. App: the Recon button on the aircraft card (key A, `AirWing.tsx`) lights every on-map tile in range (`airWing.ts::reconTargets`), a click on the map orders it (`MapCanvas.tsx`), Esc closes the picker. A strike resolves in the ranged phase with no retaliation (a temporary map unit with the aircraft's own id stands in its city for the stage, so the existing fight, event and damage code runs unchanged, then returns to the hangar with its HP). **Aircraft ignore line of sight** (user 2026-10-18: a Bomber "blocked by line of sight" over a Forest): `los::ranged_clear` is true for class Air as for Siege, so a strike needs only the target within range of its city and visible to its team (`battle::check_visible`). A Fighter or Bomber can strike at an existing War **or a War declared this turn** (decision D7): `rules::validate_orders` uses `units::declared_wars` and checks air orders against the same projected state as ranged orders (a surprise war from a valid `DeclareWar` in the list, `diplomacy::surprise_war`), and `resolve_turn` opens surprise wars before the ranged phase, so the strike resolves the same turn as a ground attack does; a War that only starts next turn does not open it. A **Fighter on guard** intercepts: for each strike in aircraft-id order (a Bomber's or a Fighter's), the lowest-id eligible enemy Fighter (at war with the striker's owner, not yet used this turn) whose city is within its guard radius (4 for Fighter, 5 for Stealth Fighter) of the target tile meets the striker in a melee exchange instead; a Stealth Bomber can only be intercepted by a Stealth Fighter (`air::STAGE_AIR`; the strike does not land, both take damage). **Air Defence (Guided Missiles, Modern era; `BuildingKind::AirDefence`, 320 Production, no yield, no hangar slot; designer, 2026-11-14):** a strike on the tile of a hostile city that has one is met the same way, before any Fighter, once per Air Defence per turn (`air::air_defence_at`): a temporary Jet Fighter (`air::AIR_DEFENCE_INTERCEPTOR`, full HP, the Jet Fighter's own strength and the city tile's terrain, no fortification) takes the Fighter's place in the same melee duel, the strike does not land and the striker takes the damage; the Air Defence takes none and the temporary unit never survives the phase (not in the hangar, not on the map after). It guards its own city's tile only, not the tiles around it. A Stealth Bomber passes it exactly as it passes an ordinary Fighter. The strike forecast shows it: `BattleForecast::air_defence` (`AirDefenceForecast`: the interceptor's and striker's strengths, the expected damage to the striking aircraft and its chance to be lost) when the attacked enemy city is seen with an Air Defence (`CityView::has_air_defence`, kept in `CityMemory`), none for a Stealth Bomber. The battle event names the interceptor as a Jet Fighter (placeholder wording until the Modern era's art pass). **A Fighter with a valid strike order this turn has used its one order and cannot intercept** (`resolve_ranged_phase` validates strikes before marking Fighters used); a Fighter whose strike fails validation, one with no order, or one rebasing, still intercepts. A **rebase** moves the aircraft to any other own city with room, at any distance (user 2026-11-02: "it just makes the logistics of moving them around easier"; `data::REBASE_RANGE` = 10 is gone, and the only checks left are own city, not the current base, and hangar room); duplicate orders do not reserve extra hangar slots (`air::validate_orders`). Rebases apply sequentially in aircraft id order, so two aircraft cannot swap between full hangars.
