It resolves after the ranged phase (it cannot strike that turn; `Event::AircraftRebased`). `forecast_attack_mode` forecasts a hangar plane's strike (`forecast_air_strike`, Bomber or Fighter; a Fighter's interception is not predicted, rival hangars are hidden, but an Air Defence's is, since the building is public). AI: `crates/ai/src/air.rs` (a Bomber or Stealth Bomber strikes the best target in range every turn, scored by forecast damage times the target's cost; a Fighter strikes or stays on guard (gene `fighter_strike`, default 1; 0 keeps it always on guard): it guards while a rival at war in the Flight era or later has a city within its era’s longest strike plus this Fighter’s guard radius (Biplane 3 for a Machine Age rival, Bomber 8 from Air Power's era, Jet Bomber 9 from Jet Engines', 10 for the Future era; `air.rs::bombers_likely`), unless a strike is worth more than `GUARD_VALUE`; cities base a Bomber when a war is within reach (a Biplane, valued below it, when a war is within the Biplane's reach), and a Fighter to cover Bombers, one aircraft per city; a Biplane upgrades along its default line (Fighter) when the Gold allows (`hold_or_upgrade`, which asks for a spare of the new form's resource only when it changes); `air_rebase` defaults on and moves idle planes to strictly better own hangars (every own city is a candidate, by value) or out of endangered bases). App: the city panel's Air wing (`ui/AirWing.tsx`, states in `airWing.ts`: ordered, ready, guard, idle); every aircraft with no order for the turn is an `air` decision ("needs orders") in the Return queue and the dock count (`turnFlow.ts`, `airWing.aircraftNeedingOrders`), with a target in reach or not (user 2026-10-18: a bought Bomber never came up): its card offers Strike (targets with forecasts; a team at Peace appears as "declare War and strike", `commands.promptWar`, and a War queued this turn makes its targets strikeable at once), "Stay on guard" (Fighter) / "Hold" (answers it for the turn, `store.airHeld`), Rebase, and Sleep (`store.sleepAir`, `Hold.asleep`: asks nothing until an enemy is in strike range), Fighters answered "stay on guard" or asleep are listed in This Turn (`ui/AirWatch.tsx`), the map rings the cover while the city is selected (`map/airOverlay.ts`: Biplane guard 2 and strike 3; Bomber strike 8, Fighter guard 4 and strike 5; Jet Bomber strike 9, Jet Fighter guard 4 and strike 6; Stealth Bomber strike 10, Stealth Fighter guard 5 and strike 7) and a finished aircraft gets a card stating its job. Not built: aircraft have no map art, banner badge or far-zoom mark (they are not on the map), and no Fighter patrol of its own units.
| Building | Cost | Yield | Requires |
|---|---|---|---|
| Brood Hall (displayed Rice Storehouse) | 50 | +2 Food; **Unavailable** (no people has it: "No people can build the Rice Storehouse yet"; yields nothing for India) | — |
| Granary | 50 | +2 Food | — (turn 1) |
| Workshop | 60 | +2 Production | Bronze Working |
| Market | 60 | +3 Gold | Currency |
| Revenue Office (India's Market) | 60 | +3 Gold; non-capital city remits half its gross Gold to the treasury | Currency |
| Imperial Diwan (India) | 300 | Held goal: its city holder scores 2 VP; build once every owned city has a Revenue Office | Currency |
| Walls | 100 (rule experiment `walls-cost=N` overrides this base price; the Swiss Redoubt adds 20, so it costs 120 by default) | +10 city strength (more each era); **the city fires** (only walled cities do, §4) | Masonry |
| Library (`Laboratory`) | 80 | +3 Science | Writing |
| Roadworks | 140 (`data::ROADWORKS_COST`) | +1 Gold, flat; roads | Engineering. Any faction, buyable with Gold like any building, no per-empire limit. A one-time build that "just turns on": `roads::extend_roadworks` (every resolution, after the Hall's `extend`) links each Roadworks city to every city of any empire within `data::ROADWORKS_RANGE` = 8 hexes, or `RAILWAYS_ROADWORKS_RANGE` = 12 after its owner researches Railways that its road network does not reach yet, by the same layout as the Hall's roads (`roads::connect`, rooted at the Roadworks city; another network's road tiles are walked over for free and merged). A city founded or captured within that range later is linked the next resolution. The Gold is the building's flat yield, not per connection (the Hall's roads give none). Roads persist when the building's city is razed or captured; a captor's Roadworks keeps linking. Rivals use the roads: mobility for both sides, no border opened. AI production priority is described in §10; the frozen proxy AIs never build it |
| War Shrine (Sparta) | 160 | military trained here starts one promotion level higher | Chivalry |
| Bank (`Clearinghouse`) | 200 | +4 Gold | Banking |
| Mill (`Hydroponics`) | 200 | +3 Food | Feudalism |
| University (`College`) | 240 | +4 Science | Education |
| Castle (was Citadel) | 240 | +5 city strength (Walls tier 2; still fires) | Chivalry |
| Star Fort | 320 | +5 more city strength (Walls tier 3) | Military Engineering |
| Forge (`MachineShop`) | 320 | +3 Production; uses 1 Iron (idle without it) | Iron Working |
| Stock Exchange (`StockExchange`) | 360 | +6 Gold (the Gold line's third tier, after Market and Bank); population 8; uses no resource (it keeps the Bank's Niter) | Electricity |
| Research Lab (`InferenceCore`) | 360 | +6 Science | Electronics |
| Factory (`Autoworks`) | 440 | +5 Production; adds 1 Coal and keeps the Forge's Iron (idle without either) | Industrialization |
| Pyramids (Masonry, Ancient) | 240 | +1 Production | World wonder, +2 held VP |
| Great Library (`RelayUplink`, Philosophy, Classical) | 600 | — | World wonder, +2 held VP; needs a Library in its city |
| Hagia Sophia (Machinery, Medieval) | 400 | +2 Science | World wonder, +2 held VP |
| Grand Bazaar (`WorldExchange`, Banking, Renaissance) | 600 | — | World wonder, +2 held VP; needs two Banks, including one in its city |
| Royal Arsenal (`PlanetaryFoundry`, Ballistics, Industrial) | 600 | — | World wonder, +2 held VP; needs a Factory in its city |
| Eiffel Tower (Steel, Machine Age) | 720 | +3 Gold | World wonder, +2 held VP |
| ITER Fusion Reactor (Fusion Power, Future) | 900 | +4 Science | World wonder, +2 held VP |

**World wonder race (2026-10-31).** One named wonder unlocks per era (except the World War era since 2026-11-14: the Eiffel Tower moved with Steel into the Machine Age, and its Hoover Dam is increment 2 of the Machine Age, not built; the Modern era's Apollo Program is increment 2 of the Modern era, not built; so there are seven), and each can complete only once worldwide, even after capture or destruction. Each empire may complete one; a captured wonder does not use the captor's completion. The first city to finish wins; if several finish on the same turn (rules 50), the one with more overflow Production (stored plus this turn's Production minus the price) wins and an exact tie is a seeded draw (`rules/cities.rs::wonder_race_order`, `purpose::WONDER_RACE`): only the racing cities swap places in the production phase's city-id order, and the losers are handled by `resolve_wonder_race` as before. Every other city actively building, queuing or banking that wonder loses that order and receives floor(banked Production / 2) Gold, reported to its owner (`data::GREAT_WORKS`, `economy::building_check_at_era`, `rules/cities.rs::resolve_wonder_race`, `Event::WonderRaceLost`). **A city that was actively building it stops for its owner's choice (2026-11-07, Thun T92 in 8.9.26):** the race is recorded on the city (`City.wonder_lost`: winner, wonder, Production, Gold; shown as `CityDetail.wonder_lost`), and its queue does not start in that turn's production phase whichever city resolves first (a loser resolving after the winner holds its turn's Production as unassigned, up to the bank cap). Any production or automation order clears it; otherwise its next production phase clears it and the queue resumes. The app makes it a "Choose production" decision ("India completed the Hagia Sophia first: Thun stopped building it. You got 15 Gold back, half the 30 Production Thun had put into it. Choose what Thun builds next."), with its waiting queue and a one-click "Resume Mill"; the same line in the report and an "A wonder race lost" moment card, never a message about the next queue item (`app/src/wonderRace.ts`). `WonderRaceLost.active` tells a city that was building it from one that only had it queued or banked ("…so it was taken out of Basel's queue"). A met rival's wonder build, progress and Production per turn are public (`projects::great_work_views`), so the build card lists rival builders with their pace and a city building the same wonder warns with the fastest rival's turns left. The original three have no additional yield effect; the four new landmarks give era-scaled city yields (+1 Production, +2 Science, +3 Gold, +4 Science) (`data::building_stats`). Masonry fits the Pyramids' stone construction, Machinery carries Hagia Sophia away from Education's Niter reveal, Steel is the Machine Age metal-frame engineering slot for [the Eiffel Tower's actual puddled-iron construction](https://www.toureiffel.paris/en/news/history-and-culture/15-essential-things-know-about-eiffel-tower), and Fusion Power fits [ITER's real experimental reactor under construction](https://www.iter.org/building-iter) (`data::building_stats`, `data::building_name`).

**Wonder tiles and held VP (2026-10-24; permanent territory 2026-10-31).** World wonders and the Mongols' Yam Relay Station are queued as `ProductionItem::Wonder {building,tile}`. Choose any land tile owned by the building city within its three-hex work radius, excluding its centre, a natural wonder, or another constructed wonder; terrain gives no prerequisite. The site is stored on the order and completed tile (`Tile::landmark`, public in `TileView`). Completion clears any improvement and replaces the tile's Food, Production, Gold and Science with the building's effect. A completed world wonder site is permanent territory of its city: `rules/cities.rs::settle_territory` removes it from population claims, and population loss cannot release it. Capturing the city transfers its sites; razing or a Celtic sack destroys them. A completed world wonder's +2 VP is a held `VpSource::GreatWork {of}`, handled by `scoring::check_great_work` with public lost/completed events and the ordinary ten-turn victory hold. Completion still uses up the builder's one world wonder; capturing one does not use up the captor's choice. The Hall's goal and Peace passage follow its tile owner. AI chooses low-yield, safer interior sites, values the held VP in capture targets, and guards wonder cities with Walls and a garrison.

Buildings form upgrade families (`data::building_predecessor`: Granary→Mill; Workshop→Forge→Factory; Market→Bank→Stock Exchange; Walls→Castle→Star Fort; Library→University→Research Lab); a higher tier satisfies the lower
tier's requirement. Some gate on population (Forge 4, University/Bank 5,
Research Lab 8, Stock Exchange 8, Factory 6).

**Game length:** there is one pace and the data tables hold its real prices and turn counts (no game-speed setting, scale or `Quick`). No default turn limit (`None` = play until victory); an optional limit ends on
highest VP. The game's expected length is `data::EXPECTED_GAME_TURNS` = 300 (forecast horizon and AI pacing); `data::FIRST_THIRD_TURNS` = 100.

**Research-cost experiment (off by default):** simulator-only `--rules-experiment tech-cost-percent=N`
sets research costs to N% after diffusion and optional city-research adjustments. Valid values are 1–100; unset preserves ordinary costs. See
`crates/core/src/experiments.rs::RuleExperiments::tech_cost_percent` and `economy.rs::tech_cost`.


**Occupation lock (the rule in every game since rules 31, designer 2026-11-13; proposed 2026-11-09, built 2026-11-11 as an experiment):** there is no switch any more, and no lobby or simulator option. `battle::capture_city` records the civ it took the city from in `City::occupied_from` (none for a city its captor's team is retaking, none for barbarians). `rules::occupation::lock`: a military unit (aircraft excluded) inside a city its owner holds with a record cannot leave while its owner's team is at war with that civ and that civ is alive; Peace or its elimination releases it, and clears the record for good (`occupation::clear_released`, run every turn boundary after diplomacy resolves and before `hold_locked_units`, so a Peace is always seen before a later declaration): a later war with that civ neither locks the city's units nor freezes it (user, 2026-11-11, Almaliq); otherwise the record stays until the city changes hands. The lock is the city's, so a replacement unit that moves in is locked too. Locked units can still fortify, heal, skip and shoot (`RangedAttack`, auto-fire); `MoveUnit`/`MoveUnitPath`/`SetAutomation` and `DisbandUnit` are refused at validation ("<Unit> is holding <city> down and cannot leave until Peace with <people>") and again at resolution; a standing route is dropped each movement round, so a melee sortie cannot start either. Upgrades happen in place and stay allowed. The player views carry `CityView::occupation_lock` (the civ a unit of yours would be locked against by ending a move there, including by capturing a hostile city) and `UnitView::occupation_lock`; the tile card warns before entry or capture, the attack forecast repeats that warning whenever the city can be captured this turn (`forecastModel.lockWarning`, shown while the capture chance is above 0), and a locked unit is the city's garrison, held automatically: at the end of every turn `occupation::hold_locked_units` gives a locked unit with no standing order `Sleep` (fortify) if melee or `AutoFire` if ranged (the session then fires at the best visible target each turn, so the lock never costs a shot), clears any route or attack target it carried, and clears `needs_orders`, so it is never in the Return/needs-orders cycle or a pending decision and end-turn never asks about it (Heal is kept until healed, then it is held again). Locked units never wake for approaching enemies. What a locked unit still may do: fortify, heal, skip, `RangedAttack` and auto-fire, upgrade in place; a melee attack out of the city is a move and is refused. Nothing happens if a locked unit dies except that the next unit to move in is locked too (no revolt). Its map token wears a padlock badge (`mark-lock`, top-left, both zooms); its tile card says "Garrison of <city> · held here until Peace with <people>"; its unit card leads with "Garrisoning <city>: holding the city down until Peace with <people>." and a muted line "<City>'s people will not accept your rule while <people> still fights. This unit cannot leave or disband.", and offers no Move, Disband, Fortify or Skip. After Peace the unit stays fortified in the core, but the app asks for it once (designer, 2026-11-15; app only, no rules change): the turn the lock lifts on a unit of yours (locked in the view before the turn, not in the view now: `app/src/releasedGarrison.ts`), it is a pending decision in the Return queue labelled "<City> is secure, needs orders", with one turn-report line ("<Unit>: <City> is secure. Needs orders."); Fortify, Skip this turn or any other order answers it, and a unit still under the lock, one fortified by choice or a teammate's is never asked. A game loaded on the release turn has no earlier view, so it shows no prompt. A right-click or hotkey move is refused at the click (`commands.ts::lockedRefusal`) and never queued. A frozen city is never a pending decision (the wasm `city_automation_orders` skips it, `idleCitiesNeedingProduction` excludes it), its map banner shows "Held" with the Fortify icon instead of the red "Build", its tile card and city panel say "Occupied: <city> builds and buys nothing until Peace with <people>", and the city panel replaces the build list with that note (the existing queue stays visible). **City freeze (designer decision 2026-11-11, same switch):** while `occupied_from` is set, its owner's team is at War with that civ and that civ is alive (`occupation::city_freeze`, the unit lock's condition), the city makes no Production (`process_cities` skips `produce`, so nothing is banked and the yield breakdown shows "Occupied: builds nothing until Peace with <people>") and refuses every purchase and Company hire ("Occupied: <city> builds and buys nothing until Peace with <people>", through `economy::buy_check` and `company::blocker`, so validation and resolution agree; the freeze is checked before resistance, so a city that is both gives the Occupied reason, the one that outlasts resistance). Food, Gold, Science and growth continue. Queue changes are still allowed. Capture resistance (`City::resistance`, `CAPTURE_RESISTANCE_TURNS`) is stronger and independent: it zeroes every yield, stops growth and border growth, blocks buys ("is resisting occupation") and ticks down; the freeze starts counting only from the same capture and lasts until Peace or elimination, so the two overlap for the first turns and the freeze outlasts resistance. `CityView::occupation_lock` on an own city is exactly the freeze, and the city panel says so. Peace needs both teams to agree, so a loser that refuses keeps the unit lock and the freeze in place until it is eliminated. Once Peace is made the record is gone: the former owner cannot re-freeze the city by declaring war again. AI: `drop_leaving_orders_of_locked_units` and `drop_purchases_in_frozen_cities` remove refused orders; nothing else is adapted (no valuation changes; the Peace logic `wants_peace` does not know about the lock).

**Faction-rule ablation (off by default; designer, 2026-11-05):** `RuleExperiments::faction_rules_off` (`experiments.rs`; `legatum-sim --rule-off germany_ruhr_production,germany_ruhr_food,germany_double_supply`) switches individual faction strengths, drawbacks and goals off, so a census can test that each one moves its faction's win share (a strength switched off should lower it, a drawback switched off should raise it). `FactionRule` has one variant per active rule (60 across eleven factions, 2026-11-21: names are snake_case, the faction first, `swiss_` for Switzerland; each faction's last rule is its goal except Egypt and Rome, which have no goal in increment 1, and Egypt's `egypt_nile` (rules 48) switches the Nile off for the earlier `egypt_construction` rule; `<faction>_goal` scores no VP and refuses its goal action while off). Retired bit positions 3, 22, 25, 31 and 42 stay vacant; old experimental save masks naming those rules load with the retired names ignored. The set is a `Copy` bit mask (`FactionRuleSet`, ordered by declaration, saved as a sorted list of names and absent when empty, so saves, tapes and pins are unchanged). Engine sites read it through the `*_rules` twins of the data functions (`data::building_stats_for_rules`, `unit_stats_for_rules`, `unit_moves_for_rules`, `available_to_player_rules`, `min_city_gap_rules`, `levy_cap_rules`, `terrain_defense_rules`, `ignores_wounds_rules`, `is_hoplite_rules`; `combat::*_rules`; `promotion::level_for_rules`; `goals::switched_off`/`spec_in`) and the `RuleExperiments` methods that wrap them; the old names are the same functions with the set empty. The AI reads the same switched numbers (its building, unit, cost, upkeep, movement and goal-VP lookups go through the view's `rule_experiments`; `Planner::rule_on` gates faction-flavoured valuations), so it never plans around a rule that is off. Where one rule in the table is really two mechanisms the switches are split: the Celts' burning of captured cities and what the sack carries off (`celts_no_keep`, `celts_sack`: with `celts_no_keep` off the Celts keep cities and no sack happens). `sparta_veterans` is the start level, the +50% XP and the sixth level together; `sparta_kill_heal` is the kill heal and the capture heal. `tools/sim/ablate.sh <faction>` runs the paired cloud census and writes `docs/sim-results/faction-ablation/<faction>-<date>/README.md` (win share control against off, the paired difference with its 95% interval, when the rule first applies, and a verdict: pays, dead, backwards or inconclusive).

**City/Settler rules**: production floors at 3 before buildings/penalties. Growth needs
`30 + 16×(pop-1)` Food (`data::GROWTH_BASE`, `GROWTH_PER_POP`); **the first
growth (pop 1 → 2) costs `FIRST_GROWTH` = 65 Food** (retuned 2026-10-05 for
population is territory: ring one is worked from founding, about +10 Food a turn, so a new city
reaches population 2 and its first claimed tile around turn 7; it was 10 Food when a pop-1 city
worked one tile; `data.rs::growth_threshold`,
`economy.rs::growth_threshold`, breakdown line "First growth").
**A Settler needs city pop ≥2 to be chosen, queued, continued or bought** (as in Civ 6; user
decision 2026-10-03, `economy.rs::production_blocker`, `buy_blocker`), and consumes 1 pop on
completion. A pop-1 city sees it greyed out with the reason (`view.rs::CityDetail.unavailable`).
A queued Settler that can't start is dropped from the queue with the reason; an active Settler
whose city falls to pop 1 is banked (§3, Production progress) and the city asks for a new
choice. Cost starts at 70, +50/trained Settler; every Sparta Settler, the first included, pays the +50% non-military premium after escalation and before the opening ramp (`economy.rs::sparta_nonmilitary_premium`); **an empire's first Settler costs `data::FIRST_SETTLER_PERCENT` of that price: 100% since 2026-10-05** (it was 65% to hold the opening near turn 18; population is territory made the opening faster, so the discount went; the constant and its zero-valued quote line stay; `economy.rs::production_cost`).
New cities must be **more than 3 hexes** from every existing city, including allies'; India may found cities **3 hexes apart** (`data::min_city_gap`, `rules/cities.rs::check_found_city`).
The Settle lens, settler preview, Found City blocker and AI site scoring use the same city-spacing result as founding resolution even when a city center is unexplored. `view.rs::player_view_cached` exposes only explored, otherwise-foundable tiles inside the real city gap as `PlayerView.city_gap_blocked`; it does not reveal a hidden city's center, owner or name. `settlement.rs::known_legal_site`, `site_blocker` and `SiteBasics::known_legal_site` consume that mask. A hidden-city blocker says "Too close to another city to found one here"; terrain, borders, units and site quality remain fog-limited. The gap is 2 hexes for India and 3 for every other faction (`data::min_city_gap`).

**Population-is-territory retune (2026-10-05).** Working every ring-one tile from founding made the opening much faster (a 48-game Hard Standard probe, seed 9100, 60 turns: first Settler median T11, second city T17). Retuned to hit the first-Settler-T18 and second-city-T25 targets: the first Settler costs full price (the 65% discount is gone) and the first growth costs 65 Food. Result (48 Hard games, seed 9100, 60 turns): first Settler T18, second city T24 at Standard. Levers tried, all 48-game Standard probes: capital +0 Production and 65% gave T12/T19; capital +0 and 100% gave T16/T23 at 30 Food, T18/T24 at 45 Food ; capital +2 and 100% gave T14/T20 at 30 Food, T17/T23 at 60, T19/T25 at 75 and T20/T26 at 90 (65 chosen, the capital's +2 kept so a poor start still makes 5). Start Production now spans 5 to 14 (all of ring one is worked), so terrain matters more than before. No balance sims ran (the AI is not yet competent).

**Default pacing (2026-10-22).** The capital gets +2 Production (like Civ 6's Palace). There is no opening ramp: until 2026-10-22 every Production price phased in from half the listed price on turn 0 to the full price on turn 100 (`economy::ramp_price`, the old speed surcharge); it was removed with the game-speed layer ("delete any leftover machinery", user 2026-10-21) and the early prices were retuned instead so the opening holds: the Settler costs 70 (+50 per Settler trained) and the early buildings and improvements cost what the ramp made them cost in the first third (Granary and Brood Hall 50, Workshop and Market 60, Library 80, Walls 100 (raised 2026-11-06), Farm/Pasture/Quarry 30, Mine 40); units are at their listed prices (a Spearman is 80 from turn 1, 40 before). 8-player Hard, 400 games, seed 5200001: first Settler median T18 and second city T25 before and after (`docs/sim-results/2026-10-22-opening-ramp-removal.md`); the games run about 8% shorter (309 to 285 turns on average) because the cheap early buildings stay cheap. City strike is default, but since 2026-10-03 only walled cities fire (§4). Older notes in this file about T16/T24 or T21/T29 describe earlier tunings; the numbers above are the measured ones. Individual factions can be later than these aggregate medians. See `docs/sim-results/2026-10-04-production-banking-validation.md` for per-faction measurements of the earlier tuning. Verify exact constants in crates/core/src/data.rs and economy.rs.

## 3. Economy

**City building automation (2026-10-31).** A human may set each owned city's saved
`City.build_automation` to Balanced, Science, Gold or Military with
`Order::SetCityAutomation`. This is separate from the city's
tile claim focus. At Ready the client sends its own fogged `PlayerView` to WASM and
`ai::city_automation_choice_with_pipeline`, which values available production using Hard's chooser.
A focus builds its own kind first (2026-11-01, after a Military capital built a Granary and a Farm): any available
research building (Science), economy building (Gold) or combat unit (Military) outranks every other option, each with
a value of at least 1 even when Hard would see no use for it, and other items fill in only when none of that kind can
start (for Military, when no unit's upkeep can be funded: `troop_unfunded`). Science and Gold exclude military units altogether,
even when a city has no garrison or a nearby enemy (`ai/production.rs::automated_choice`). Focused modes leave Settlers to Balanced,
which uses Hard's normal choice. With no active build, the choice becomes a `SetProduction` order. If the
current build is forecast to finish this turn and the queue is empty, the choice is
queued behind it with `SetProductionQueue`. An existing player queue takes precedence.
If the chooser finds no build for a city that needs a choice, Ready stops before turn resolution
and puts that city in the normal Choose production queue. The city keeps automation on; a manual
production order handles this turn. When WASM is loaded, the city warns before Ready from the
same chooser result. The WASM plan returns `orders` and `unchosen_cities`; a later chooser change
can attach a per-city reason there (`wasm/src/lib.rs::city_automation_orders_json`,
`app/src/automation.ts::cityAutomationPreview`, `app/src/turnFlow.ts::pendingDecisions`).
An active Industry project has no completion; automation replaces it at Ready. Egypt's Grow project (rules 48) never completes either.
Changing or stopping automation keeps the active item and replaces or removes an
unstarted item that automation itself queued; manual queue edits replace that marker
(`City.auto_queued`).
No automation path issues `BuyItem`, changes tile claims or reads `GameState` to plan.
The focus and last automated choice are private city detail; capture clears both
(`core/state.rs::City`, `core/view.rs::city_detail`, `core/rules/battle.rs::capture_city`,
`wasm/src/lib.rs::city_automation_orders_json`, `ai/production.rs::city_automation_choice`).

| Yield | Sources |
|---|---|
| Food | City center (2) + terrain + Wheat/Cattle; grows population; German cities lose 1 per Production building on its own stat line (including retained upgrade tiers and the Pyramids) |
| Production | City center (1) + terrain + Stone/Iron/Coal; floor of 3; Workshop family |
| Gold | City center (1) + Coast/Copper/Oil + Market family; Switzerland +1 per 2 population "tolls" and treasury interest; buys items |
| Science | Capital +2 base; other cities +pop/2; Library family; cuts research cost. Every faction's city Science accrues (the Byzantium's did not before 2026-10-10). |

There are four yields. **Influence no longer exists** (removed 2026-10-03, user decision: fewer
currencies, every trade-off priced in Gold). Saves from another rules version do not load.

**Extreme-value bounds (2026-10-31):** The treasury stops accruing at 100,000,000 Gold
(`validation::MAX_GOLD`, `rules/cities.rs::pay_gold`, `validation::credit_gold`). A city stops
growing at population 1,000; stored Food, Production, and Science stop at 1,000,000
(`validation::MAX_POPULATION`, `validation::MAX_STORED`). Save loading rejects negative or
out-of-range amounts with the affected field named (`validation::check_state`). These bounds
leave ordinary game pacing unchanged while keeping long-run arithmetic within range.

**Population is territory** (user decision 2026-10-04): a city works **every** tile it owns within
`CITY_WORK_RADIUS` = 3 hexes (`economy.rs::worked_tiles`), so there is no tile-worker assignment and
no citizen allocation. A new city owns its centre plus ring one (unowned tiles only), all worked from
founding. Population above 1 is territory: see Borders below. Yields don't depend on the build any
more; the active build only steers which tile the *next claim* takes (`auto_focus`).
**Upkeep**: 2 free military units/empire; each extra costs 1 Gold/turn (3 for Switzerland).
**Byzantine thin levies** count upkeep-paying land units except hired Companies, plus aircraft based in owned cities (`economy.rs::levy_count`, `production_check`). Aircraft count toward the levy cap but pay no Gold upkeep and use none of the two free upkeep slots (`economy.rs::upkeep_unit_count`, `upkeep_breakdown`, `forecast/cities.rs::forecast_cities`, `view.rs::unit_upkeep_preview`); Scouts, Settlers and the Heir count toward neither. The public `PlayerPublic.levy` drives the Armed counter and production blockers.
**Scouts and civilians (Settlers) never pay upkeep and never use a free slot, in any faction
(user decision 2026-10-06, after a Switzerland playtest where a Ranger, a Soldier and a ruin's free
Scout bankrupted a 0 Gold treasury on turn 6; Civ 6 Scouts are free too)**
(`data.rs::pays_upkeep`, used by `economy.rs::upkeep_breakdown`, the bankruptcy victim
`rules/cities.rs::bankruptcy_victim`, the forecast and the build-card preview `view.rs::unit_upkeep_preview`,
which is absent for a Scout so its card reads "No upkeep"). The scripted
AI counts only `pays_upkeep` units in its upkeep, runway and disband maths (`ai/production.rs::upkeep_unit_count`).
Each active Alliance adds 1 Gold/turn per owned city (minimum 1) in a game under the calls-to-arms rules (§5; `economy.rs::treaty_upkeep`, a "Treaty upkeep: Alliance with X" line in the Gold breakdown), except on a turn the Alliance is at War with a team close to winning (below, §5 "Alliance upkeep waiver"). No technology discounts military upkeep any more (Strategic Logistics was removed; Military Engineering halves unit upgrade Gold instead). A negative treasury can force a disband
(`crates/core/src/data.rs::paid_military_upkeep`, `economy.rs::upkeep_breakdown`,
`rules/cities.rs::pay_gold`). The bankruptcy forecast includes upkeep (`economy.rs::player_income`,
`forecast/cities.rs`).

**Production progress is banked per item** (Civ 6 style; user decision 2026-10-03, branch
claude/production-banking). `City.production_stored` is the active unit's or building's
progress. Switching away banks it in `City.production_banked` (one entry per item); the newly
chosen item starts from its own bank (0 if none), and switching back resumes it. A banked
Settler's progress goes to the next Settler that city builds. You can't move progress from one
item to another (`state.rs::City::switch_production`, `production_for`; used by
`rules/cities.rs::apply_order`, `advance_queue`, `defer_blocked_head`, `produce`).
- **Unassigned Production** (overflow from a completion, idle banking up to the idle cap,
  Production held while a queue waits) sits in `production_stored` while no unit or building
  is active, and joins whatever starts next, together with that item's own bank. So
  **overflow still carries into the next build** (at most one completion per city per turn).
  Germany projects never hold or bank progress.
- **Buying** never adds progress (purchases are separate full-price copies, since dd1fc7ea; the
  old `City.bought` record is always empty, so there is no gold-bought progress to forfeit).
  Buying a building that already has progress keeps it like overflow: if it was the active
  build, its progress joins the next queued item; if it was banked, the bank joins the current
  build (`rules/cities.rs::apply_order`, BuyItem).
- **Stale banks are dropped** at the start of the city's production phase: a building already
  standing (or a higher tier of its family), or a world wonder once the empire has completed its
  one (`rules/cities.rs::bank_is_stale`, `prune_stale_banks`). Units never become obsolete
  here (`production_blocker` checks only tech and the Settler's population), so a unit's bank
  never goes stale and nothing needs to carry to an upgrade.
- **A lost world wonder race** stops the city for a choice (§World wonder race); its queue waits
  one production phase, then resumes.
- **The active build becoming impossible** (a Settler's city fell to pop 1, a world wonder lost
  its support) banks its progress and reports "X can no longer produce Y: reason; its N
  Production stays banked"; the city asks for a new choice (`rules/cities.rs::produce`).
- **Capture clears** the city's banks with its production (`rules/battle.rs::capture_city`).
- **No decay**: banked progress keeps its value indefinitely (Settler escalation still applies
  to the item's remaining cost).
- View: `ProductionOption.production_stored` (progress if selected now),
  `ProductionOption.production_banked`, `CityDetail.production_banked`,
  `CityDetail.unavailable` (`view.rs::city_detail`).

**Faction-useless items are hidden** (user decision 2026-10-03): master's
`data.rs::available_to_faction(faction, item)` is the one shared rule (production choices, the
tech tree, the AI through its options, `app/src/data.ts::availableToFaction`).
`data.rs::faction_unavailable_reason(item, faction)` wraps it for validation and greyed-out
text ("Only Germany cities can run industry projects", "Only Egyptian cities can choose Grow", "Workshops, Forges and Factories do nothing for Egypt, which has no Production", otherwise "Does nothing for the X"),
checked first in `economy.rs::production_blocker`; such items are not listed as unavailable,
just absent. Faction replacements are the same item under the faction's name
(`data.rs::building_name_for`, TS `factionItemName`).

**Buying** (`BuyItem`; tiles are `BuyTile`, below): 200% of Production cost in Gold (175% Switzerland), **one purchase per city
per turn**. Nobody may buy world wonders; they must be built with Production (`economy.rs::is_unbuyable`; the Mongols' Yam Relay Station is not a world wonder and can be bought). **Walls, Castle, Star Fort, and the Swiss Alpine Redoubt cannot be bought with Gold; every Walls-line building must be built with Production.** This blocks only `BuyItem`: Production can be banked while another item is built, and a half-built Walls-line building can be finished by switching production back to it. The city panel and production list show “Walls must be built with Production; they can't be bought.” on the disabled Buy control. The UI displays the core's quote and blocker. Strategic-resource cost still applies to legal buys; bought units act next turn (`crates/core/src/economy.rs::buy_cost_for`, `buy_quote`, `rules/cities.rs::complete_item`).
**Experiment `no_purchase_under_threat` (off by default; Sam, 2026-11-22, via the Fable review; to be judged on his Swiss and Mongol games; rules 50).** `RuleExperiments::no_purchase_under_threat`, CLI `--rules-experiment no-purchase-under-threat`: a city cannot buy a military unit (`data::unit_stats(kind).military`, aircraft included) while an at-War military unit stands within `economy::THREAT_BUY_RADIUS` (2) tiles of the city centre (`economy::enemy_near_city`, with `rules::is_threat`, so barbarians count except against the Celts). `buy_check` returns `BuyBlock::UnderThreat` and "<City> can't buy military units while an enemy unit is within 2 tiles", so validation refuses the order and the production list's disabled Buy shows that reason; buildings, tiles and Companies are unaffected. Off, it changes nothing. There is no lobby switch (only World Ages has one); the Guide adds a paragraph only when a game has it on.
Swiss interest adds one Gold per 100 Gold held at the economy phase and has **no maximum**
(user, 2026-10-17: the old cap of 5 is gone; interest is floor(treasury / 100) a turn, so a hoard
compounds at about 1% a turn). It is a line of its own in the Gold breakdown ("Switzerland:
interest") and **decadence cuts it** like any other income (user, 2026-10-17): the empire's rate on the
gross interest, to the nearest Gold, shown as its own line "Decadence −N% of interest: luxury" next to
the gross line when decadence is enabled. It is still not a city yield, so it does not count toward Power. Forecast and
resolution use the same current treasury and the same rate (`economy.rs::swiss_interest_gross`,
`swiss_interest_cut`, `swiss_interest` = gross minus cut, `player_income`,
`rules/cities.rs::process_cities`). A captured Swiss city pays the captor current treasury ×
city population / total owned population, doubled for the capital, capped at the whole treasury;
the city view exposes this public bounty (`economy.rs::swiss_capture_bounty`,
`rules/battle.rs::capture_city`, `view.rs::player_view`).

**Borders: population is territory** (user decisions 2026-10-04; `rules/cities.rs`). The old per-city
border timer is gone. Each city holds `grown_tiles` = population − 1 tiles claimed by growth, oldest
first (`City::grown_tiles`; the founding ring is not counted).

- **Claim order, "nearest first, prioritizing quality"** (`next_claim`): the next growth claims a
  tile of the *current ring*, the smallest hex distance from the city among its claim candidates
  (unowned tiles touching its territory, no reach cap). Every claimable tile of a ring goes before
  any tile of the next. Within a ring the best `claim_score` wins: the tile's yields by the city's
  focus (`economy::tile_score` of `owner_tile_yields`, whether or not it will be worked) plus
  `CLAIM_PULL_WEIGHT` (4) × `resources::border_pull` toward known unclaimed Iron/Coal. Ties go to the
  lowest hex (q, then r). Tiles beyond 3 hexes are claimed the same way. They are **unworked** and
  hatched only if no other city of the same empire can work them after the end-of-turn handoff below.
- **Settling** (`settle_territory`, run for every city in city-id order by `process_borders`, and
  at once for a ruin's population gift): claims until `grown_tiles` = population − 1, one tile per
  population, as many per turn as it takes; a city with no free candidate catches up later. Losing
  population (a Settler leaving, starvation, capture) releases tiles one at a time
  (`next_release`): the worst-scoring *unworked* grown tile (beyond 3 hexes; strategic deposits
  score +100 to keep), else the most recent claim. Event
  `TileReleased{city,owner,tile}` beside `TileClaimed`.
- **Same-empire handoff** (`reassign_workable_territory`, at the end of `process_borders`): after every city has settled its claims, a tile beyond its owning city's 3-hex work radius passes to the nearest same-empire city within 3 hexes (lower city id breaks equal-distance ties). A tile already within its city's radius stays put; land never passes between empires this way. The old city's growth-claim record and `tiles_claimed` counter lose a transferred claim; the recipient gets existing land, not another growth claim. `TilesReassigned` groups the transferred tiles by old and new city for the turn report. The map hatches only tiles no own city can work; the claim preview and tile card name a pending recipient where there is one.
- **Founding** (`found_city`): the centre always becomes the new city's (it takes the tile from
  another own city's `grown_tiles` if need be); ring one takes only unowned tiles.
- **What became of the old border-growth sources** (user, 2026-10-05, after seeing the blanket
  "everything becomes Food"): India keeps Food (+1 in every Indian city,
  `data.rs::INDIA_NETWORK_FOOD`); the **Monument and Assembly are cut everywhere**; the Embassy and Academy are removed; **Code of Laws** gives every existing city its best available ring-2 tile when learned (`eras::learn`, `rules/cities.rs::claim_code_of_laws_tile`) and later cities the same on founding. This permanent claim is not one of the grown tiles, so population loss never releases it and it does not raise `tiles_claimed`; **Irrigation** is +1 Food in every city and
  the **Granary** (+2 Food) needs no tech. India Food is an ordinary yield, so its percentages apply.

**Territory sources:** borders grow through population, conquest and a Gold purchase. Population growth claims the next unowned tile touching the city's territory using the claim order above; conquest transfers existing territory with the captured city; a city can also buy a tile (below). The view carries `next_claim: ClaimPreview{tile,yields,worked}`, `territory`, and for the owner `tile_base_price`, `buyable_tiles: [BuyableTile{tile,ring,price}]` and `buy_tile_blocker` (`view.rs::CityDetail`, `buyable_tiles`).

**Buying a tile** (`Order::BuyTile{city,tile}`; designer, 2026-11-20, rules 46; `rules/cities.rs::check_order`, `apply_order`, `buy_tile_check_among`, `tile_price`; it existed until rules 19 and returned as a rebuild, not a revert). Legal when the tile is explored by the buyer, unowned, touches the city's territory (a claim candidate) and lies within `CITY_WORK_RADIUS` (3) of the city; tiles may be bought out of ring order, and farther tiles come only from growth (`buy_candidates`). It uses the city's **one purchase per turn**, shared with `BuyItem` and `HireCompany` (`City::last_purchase_turn`), and is refused wherever those are: the same slot, the occupation freeze and resistance checks (`buy_tile_slot_check`, mirroring `economy::buy_check`). Price in Gold: `(TILE_GOLD_BASE 40 + TILE_GOLD_PER_TILE 20 x city.tiles_claimed) x ring percent / 100`, one division rounding down, with ring percent 100 / 125 / 150 for rings 1 / 2 / 3 (`TILE_RING_STEP_PERCENT` 25; `data.rs`). `tiles_claimed` counts every tile gained since founding, grown or bought, so each purchase raises the next price. There is **no Code of Laws discount** (Code of Laws only gives a new city its best ring-2 tile). Applying it pays the Gold, sets `last_purchase_turn`, gives the tile to the city (`claim_tile`, `tiles_claimed += 1`) and emits `Event::TileBought{city,owner,tile,gold}`, visible like `TileClaimed` (`event_visibility.rs`). A bought tile is **not** a growth claim: it is not in `grown_tiles`, population loss never releases it, and it does not use up a growth claim (settling still claims one tile per population above 1). It resolves with `BuyItem` in `apply_city_orders`, before movement; if two cities of one empire order the same tile in one turn, the first in its order list gets it and the second is refused as "already owned" (validation of one empire's own list sees this too). **Two empires bidding on one tile the same turn (rules 50, seeded):** `rules/cities.rs::defer_contested_tile_buys` draws the order the bids are tried in (`rng::seeded_order` with `purpose::TILE_CONTEST`, turn and tile), so the lower seat no longer always wins; the winner's order keeps its place, and each loser's order is tried after every empire's city orders, where it is refused as "already owned" (its Gold untouched) unless the winner's own order failed. AI seats never buy tiles yet: a decision view (`ViewDetail::Decisions`) leaves `buyable_tiles` empty and `buy_tile_blocker` None, and city automation never buys one. The app opens the shared tile-placement mode from the city panel's "Buy a tile" button: the map lights each buyable tile with its price, and a click on an affordable one queues the order; a click on one the treasury cannot pay is refused in place (the mode stays open). The button is disabled while an item or company purchase is queued in the city ("Cancel the Settler purchase first"), and a queued tile refuses an item the same way: neither replaces the other (core refuses whichever comes second; Guide, "Borders").

**Resources** (`resources.rs::Resource`): Wheat/Cattle/Stone/Copper are Bonus (always visible; Stone on
Grassland, Plains or Hills; Copper on Hills with +2 Gold when improved). Five Strategic resources begin
with Iron in the Classical era (`stats(r).revealed_by`; hidden until then):

| Era | Resource | Terrain | Tile bonus (improved) | Revealed by | Users (1 each while it exists) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ancient | None (Copper is bonus) | Hills | Copper: +2 Gold | Copper always visible | No supply users; Market and Workshop are free |
| Classical | Iron | Hills, Tundra | +1 Production | Iron Working | Phalangite, Composite Archer, Cataphract (Keshig), Forge, Siege |
| Medieval | Iron (continues) | | | | Pikeman (Tagmatic Soldier), Crossbowman, Knight, University |
| Renaissance | Niter | Plains, Desert, Grassland | +1 Food +1 Production | Gunpowder | Musketman, Arquebusier, Cuirassier, Bank |
| Industrial | Coal | Hills, Forest | +2 Production | Industrialization | Rifleman, Field Cannon, Cavalry, Factory |
| Machine Age | Oil | Desert, Tundra, Plains | +1 Production +1 Gold | Combustion | Trench Infantry, Howitzer, Early Tank, Biplane (the Stock Exchange uses nothing) |
| World War | Oil (continues; user, 2026-11-14: "it's ok if oil covers 2 eras") | | | | Infantry, Artillery, Tank, Fighter, Bomber, Research Lab |
| Modern | Oil (continues; a placeholder, so Oil covers 3 eras: the ninth era's resource was not specified) | | | | Mechanised Infantry, Rocket Artillery, Main Battle Tank, Jet Fighter, Jet Bomber (the Supermarket and the Air Defence use nothing) |
| Future | Silicon | Desert, Hills | +1 Production +1 Science | Autonomous Systems | Hunter-Killer, Combat Drone, Autonomous Armour, Stealth Fighter, Stealth Bomber |
**Strategic supply begins in the Classical era** (user, 2026-10-25; `resources::ERA_RESOURCES`,
`era_resource` returns `None` for Ancient): an era's new land units and its key building use 1 of that era's
resource from Classical onward. `unit_need`: every land military unit (`data::unit_class` Melee/Ranged/Mounted) whose
`unit_era_tier` is Classical or later uses `era_resource(tier)`; the turn-1 units (Spearman, Archer,
Horseman) and civilians need nothing. Biplane, Fighter and Bomber use 1 Oil each while in a hangar; Stealth Fighter and Stealth Bomber use 1 Silicon each. Faction unique
units share their kind's need. **An upgrade switches resource** (`upgrade_need` is the target's need when
it differs): a Pikeman upgrading to a Musketman frees 1 Iron and needs 1 spare Niter, and its supply record
restarts (`since` = that turn, so it is the newest Niter user). `building_need`: the key building of each
era (`SUPPLY_TIERS`: Forge, University, Bank, Factory, Research Lab) uses its tech's era resource; the Machine Age's Stock Exchange is not one of them (lead's call, 2026-11-14: the era shares the World War era's Oil with its units and aircraft; the Modern era's Supermarket and Air Defence use nothing either);
a higher tier keeps its lower tiers' needs (a Bank city uses Niter, a Factory city Iron + Coal, a
Stock Exchange city Niter, a Research Lab city Iron + Oil). Free: Market, Workshop, Library, Granary, Brood Hall, Walls/Castle/Star Fort, Mill,
Roadworks, War Shrine, world wonders, faction landmarks. Siege (the Gold upgrade) uses 1 Iron in every era it can be bought, on top of the unit's own need (a Siege Pikeman uses 2 Iron, a Siege Musketman 1 Niter and 1 Iron; lead's pick, 2026-10-23); Industrial and later units have Siege built in and use no Iron for it (designer, 2026-10-28). `current_users` includes aircraft in city hangars; each Biplane, Fighter and Bomber uses 1 Oil, and each Stealth Fighter and Stealth Bomber uses 1 Silicon. The AI scores aircraft, ground units and buildings against the same scarce Oil. Its supply value counts Oil that would restore an idle plane's 25% strength penalty, or support an aircraft that the air production scorer values at a Flight-era city, within the empire's hangar capacity. This value enters Oil Well, settlement and capture choices. A new aircraft's production score pays for the lab or land upgrade its Oil slot would displace, at the existing industry and wartime strength weights (`ai/supply_value.rs::demand`, `aircraft_oil_opportunity`; `ai/production.rs::item_base_value`). Search and Opening brains forward aircraft orders from the scripted policy after their ground plan (`air_wrapper_orders`, default 1).
For a rival city with Oil, the AI's capture score also counts visible rival aircraft that would lose strength if that Oil vanished (`ai/supply_value.rs::rival_loss`).
**Each era's resource is revealed by a technology of the era it is used in** (user, 2026-11-07, "that makes it feel like more of a race"; rules version 25): Iron by Iron Working (Classical), Niter by Gunpowder (Renaissance), Coal by Industrialization (Industrial), Oil by Combustion (Machine Age; Oil is also Modern's resource), Silicon by Autonomous Systems (Future). Until then an era's units and building cannot be started ("Needs Niter: Gunpowder reveals Niter on the map") and its deposits give nothing. Learning the unit's tech and the reveal are separate choices inside the era (Rifling for Riflemen, Industrialization for Coal), so the order of research is the race. Hard adds gene `reveal_tech_bonus` to the revealing technology's research value. A newly learned reveal makes known deposits appear on the map with an ink moment when Map animations are On. `ResourcesRevealed` drives one card per revealed resource with the count of explored deposits within five hexes of the player's cities and the count inside their borders. Tech cards show "Reveals Iron on the map" with its medallion. The supply panel names each resource's users in the current era.
**Strategic supply, not a stockpile** (user, 2026-10-19, "Strategic resources become a core reason for war";
increment 1, the Production line). Nothing is stored and nothing is paid:
- **Supply:** each known deposit on a tile the empire owns supplies `SUPPLY_PER_DEPOSIT` = 2 of its resource, +`SUPPLY_PER_MINE` = 1 with the appropriate improvement or a city built on it (`resources::deposits`, `supplied`). Germany gets twice both amounts: 4 unimproved, 6 improved (`resources::deposit_supply`, `mine_supply_gain`), unless its `GermanyDoubleSupply` ablation switch is off. The supply chips, tile cards and improvement build cards use these owner-specific amounts. Worked or not, inside the work radius or not.
- **Users:** every building tier or unit that needs a resource uses 1 for as long as it exists
  (`building_need`, `unit_need`, `current_users`; table above): e.g. the **Forge** (`MachineShop`) uses 1 Iron; the
  **Factory** (`Autoworks`) adds 1 Coal and keeps the Forge's Iron (a Factory city holds the Forge tier, so it
  uses 1 Iron + 1 Coal); the **Pikeman** (`Trooper`) uses 1 Iron. The Workshop and Library, the Food line
  (Granary, Mill), the Walls line and every other item need nothing. The Bank Silver / University Copper mapping of 2026-10-19 is superseded by the era rule.
- **Starting needs spare supply:** setting production to, buying, or upgrading into a user needs
  `spare` ≥ 1 = supplied − in use − **held**, where builds in progress in *other* cities hold 1 each until
  they finish (`reserved`; the city's own active build doesn't block itself, and replacing it frees its
  hold). Queue heads wait ("…skipped queued Pikeman: No Iron to spare…; kept in queue"); validation runs
  orders in list order, so the second of two Forges with one spare Iron is refused. Upgrades take supply
  in list order before city orders (`rules/mod.rs::validate_orders`, which builds the after-upgrade state
  for the city orders). A Factory also needs this city's Forge working (`start_check`). Wording
  (`shortage_text`): "No Iron to spare: your deposits supply 6 and 5 are in use, plus 1 held for the Forge in
  Bern. Win another Iron deposit, or build a Mine on one (+1 Iron)"; none at all: "Needs 1 Iron: you have no
  Iron yet. Bring an Iron deposit inside your borders"; unrevealed: "Needs Iron: Iron Working reveals Iron on
  the map". Upgrades: "upgrading to Pikeman: no Iron to spare: …".
  The top-bar supply alert is red only for an active shortage or a build/upgrade currently blocked by supply; an upgrade whose technology is still unknown does not make a 0/0 chip red.
- **Short of supply, the newest users go idle** (`update_supply`, run once at the end of resolution, after
  borders and the ResourcesRevealed announcement): each empire keeps `Player::supply`, one `SupplyRecord` per
  user (`SupplyUser::Building{city, building}` or `Unit{unit}`, sorted) with `since` = the turn the empire
  first had it (built, upgraded into or **captured**: a captured Forge is new to its captor) and `idle`. Per
  resource, users sorted by (since, user; on the same turn buildings before units) beyond the supply are idle
  until supply returns at a later count. The record is stored state, so mid-turn changes (a capture, a Mine)
  take effect at the end of the turn.
  - **An idle building tier yields nothing:** its normal yield line stays and a second line cancels it,
    "Forge idle: no Iron" −3 Production (`economy.rs::yields_base`, `resources::idle_tier`; a Factory is idle
    for Coal, or for Iron while its Forge is). Decadence then cuts the net. The yields cache keys on the idle
    tiers.
  - **An idle unit fights `UNSUPPLIED_STRENGTH_PERCENT` = 25% weaker:** a breakdown line "No Iron supply"
    −25% of its base strength (of its ranged strength when it shoots) in every fight: attack, defence, a
    meeting, a city's shot at it, and every forecast (`resources::add_unsupplied`, `rules/battle.rs`,
    `forecast.rs`, `forecast/assault.rs`). Chosen as the simplest visible penalty (lead, 2026-10-20): one
    strength line the forecast already shows, the same rule for any future unit that needs Oil, and the unit
    stays useful (a flat percent, not "can't attack"). The AI's own tactical estimates (attack,
    defence, ranged and `power` of a visible unit) subtract it for units the view shows as `unsupplied`
    (`crates/ai/src/supply.rs::attack_total` and friends, 2026-10-21); the frozen proxy brains and the app's
    `aftermath.ts`, `siege.ts` and `unitEra.ts` don't.
  - **An idle building doesn't count toward a Patrons card** (2026-10-21, `objectives::Objective::progress`):
    "a Forge in N cities" counts a city only while its Forge tier isn't idle (`resources::idle_tier`).
- **Events** (team-private): `ResourcesRevealed{player, resource, city, sources, supply}` (field renamed from
  `per_turn`)  and `SupplyShortage{player, resource, idle, supplied, used}` whenever
  the idle count changes (idle 0: covered again).
- **View:** `PlayerView.supply: Vec<SupplyView{resource, revealed_by, revealed, supplied: Breakdown (one line
  per deposit, "Iron near Lugano, with a Mine" 3), used: Breakdown ("Forge in Lugano" 1, "Pikeman ×3" 3), idle:
  [labels, newest first], held: ["Forge in Bern"], spare}>`; `ProductionOption.supply_need` and
  `resource_blocker`; `UnitView.upgrade_need` (own) and `unsupplied` (any visible unit, so a rival's forecast
  is right); `CityDetail.idle_buildings: [{building, resource}]`. The top bar shows "Iron 5 / 6" (used /
  supplied) with the breakdown tooltip; numbers come from the view only. Until the current era's resource is revealed (`resources.rs` era resource; `revealed` false in `SupplyView`), the bar shows a muted chip "Niter: revealed by Gunpowder" instead (Coal by Industrialization, Oil by Combustion, Silicon by Autonomous Systems), with a tooltip of the units and key building that resource feeds that era (`app/src/ui/SupplyChips.tsx::chipSupplies`, `resources.ts::resourceUsersThisEra`). Only the current era's unrevealed resource gets one.
- **Map (2026-10-22):** every start gets a Copper deposit 2 hexes out and an Iron deposit 3-5 hexes out (each
  nearer to it than to any other start) and 2 bonus resources exactly 2 hexes out (ring 1 stays bare, see Balanced founding sites); Niter, Coal, Oil and Silicon sit
  only in the borderlands (at least 4 hexes from every start, at most 1.5x farther from the nearest rival start
  than the nearest start, at most 3 per nearest start, 3 apart); extra Copper and Iron by land share
  (`placement.rs`, `LAND_TILES_PER_DEPOSIT` 45: about 15 of each a Standard map). Census (32 Hard games,
  2026-10-22, `docs/sim-results/era-resources/`): a typical empire (4-5 cities, land army 6-9) supplies a
  median 2-3 of its era's resource against 4-9 wanted, and about 90% of empires want more than they supply at
  every snapshot T50-T250; about 36 deposits a game change hands, all with a captured city. Before the era rule: On the Standard 48x32 map with 8 empires
  (`crates/sim/examples/supply_census.rs`, 64 Hard games, 2026-10-20): 11 Iron and 10 Coal deposits a map,
  1.4 Iron and 1.25 Coal nearest each start (median 1 each, at most 4 Iron); empires of 8-12 cities hold a
  median 4-6 Iron supply, so they can't put a Forge in every city without expanding or fighting.
- Census numbers with the supply-aware AI are in `docs/learnings.md` (2026-10-20, "Strategic supply").

**Tile improvements** (user decision 2026-10-04, playtest 8; `crates/core/src/improvements.rs`). There
are no Builder units: a city *builds an improvement as a production item* that names one tile
(`ProductionItem::Improvement { improvement, tile }`, in the same `SetProduction` / queue / `BuyItem`
orders as any item; the picker names it "Mine, Iron · 2 tiles NE").
- **A resource yields its bonus only once its tile is improved** (`Tile::improved`, `improvements::tile_resource_yields`;
  a city centre on a resource counts as improved). Before that the tile is plain land, and its bonus
  is not in the city's "Resources" line. The city breakdown names each improved resource with its
  improvement ("Resources: Wheat (Farm), Iron (Mine)").
- **Seven improvements cover the nine resources** (`kind_for`): Farm (Wheat), Pasture (Cattle), Quarry
  (Stone), Mine (Copper, Iron, Coal), Saltpetre Works (Niter), Oil Well (Oil), Quartz Mine (Silicon). Only resource tiles can be improved. Cost (with the Sparta non-military +50%): Farm, Pasture and Quarry 30,
  Mine, Saltpetre Works, Oil Well and Quartz Mine 40 Production (`base_cost`); buyable like any item (200% / 175% Gold), one purchase per city per turn.
- **Eligibility** (`improvements::blocker`, shared by validation, the picker, the AI and queue upkeep):
  the tile belongs to *this* city and lies within the 3-hex work radius (tiles claimed beyond it only block
  rivals, and the city centre can't be targeted), its resource is *known* to the owner (a hidden strategic
  deposit can't be targeted before its revealing technology), the improvement matches the resource, it
  isn't already improved. An item whose tile stops
  qualifying ("can no longer produce…") stops like any impossible build, and its progress stays banked.
- **Upgrades are automatic** (no re-building): the owner's technology decides the form
  (`upgrade_tech`): Crop Rotation turns Farm into Irrigated Farm and Pasture into Ranch;
  Masonry turns Quarry into Deep Quarry; Iron Working turns Mine, Saltpetre Works and Quartz Mine into their Deep forms (`upgrade_tech`); Combustion upgrades the Oil Well (+1 Gold). Railways adds another Production to Mines. An upgraded improvement adds
  one more of the resource's main yield (`upgrade_yields`: Food for Wheat, Cattle and Niter, Production for
  Stone, Iron, Coal and Silicon, Gold for Copper and Oil). The tech cards list it as an effect line.
- **Population loss keeps the released tile's improvement** (`rules/cities.rs::settle_territory`; training a Settler can release an improved tile). Razing and founding a city on an improved centre clear it; capture passes improved land to the captor intact. **Supply:** owning a known deposit is enough to supply 2; a Mine on Iron or
  Coal adds +1 supply (+2 for Germany) as well as the tile's yield.
- **Territory interaction:** claim scoring and release keep valuing a known resource at its improved
  worth (`economy::owner_tile_potential`), so claims still lean toward resources; the claim preview
  and the worked yields show only what the tile yields now. Improvements are banked per item like
  buildings (`City::holds_progress`, `switch_production`).
- **View:** `TileView::improvement` (kind, and `upgraded` on the viewer's own tiles only; remembered
  for fogged tiles through `TileMemory::improved`), `ProductionOption::improvement` (tile, resource,
  gain, and the upgrade still to come) and `CityDetail::options` lists every eligible tile. (Egypt's city view omits Quarry, see the Egypt Nile entry.)
- **AI** (`crates/ai/src/production.rs::improvement_value`): an improvement is valued by the yield it adds
  (Food 4, Production 3, Gold 2, Science 3; the upgrade counts half) over the 30-turn planning horizon
  against its cost, scaled by `knobs.economy`, and ranked with Settlers and units by the usual
  time-discounted production score; it waits while a city is threatened. WASM behavior checks in `app/src/test/improvements.wasm.test.ts`; core tests in `crates/core/src/improvements/tests.rs`.
- Not built: a delegation tech that auto-queues improvements (follow-up, per pillar 3).

## 4. Combat

**Formula**: `base_damage = round(30·e^((own_str−other_str)/25))` from the table `combat::DAMAGE_TABLE` (the strength gap is clamped to ±50, so base damage runs from 4 to 222), × an independent roll per side in
**80–120%**, attacker first. Strength = base + promotion + terrain defense (defender only) +
fortify (defender only) − wounded penalty (missing-HP/10 units, /20 cities, unless faction
`ignores_wounds`). `UNIT_HP=100`, `CITY_HP=200`.

**Melee**: stepping onto a hostile tile is the attack; both sides can take damage. **Meeting**
engagements (both sides moving into the same tile) get no terrain/fortify bonus to either side. A
unit that fought ends its turn — no further move or re-attack.

**Pursuit, catching a retreat, and advancing on a kill** (verified 2026-10-11 against the p881 horseman report). A melee order onto a visible hostile unit's tile is an attack on that *unit* (`rules/units.rs::explicit_attack_target`, `Unit::attack_target`): normally each movement round the pursuer's goto is re-aimed at where the target stands now (`rules/movement.rs`, `struct Pursuit`). **A target that dies before movement** (ranged fire in phase 2) passes the attack to the next visible hostile unit still on its tile, military before civilian, then the lowest id (`movement.rs::next_target_on`, Sam 2026-11-21, RULES_VERSION 47: his Keshig's Horseman target fell to another Keshig's shot and the Settler on the same tile walked away); with nothing hostile left there the order ends with `MoveBlocked` "its target was destroyed before it could attack" (`TARGET_GONE`; before rules 47 it ended silently). Outrider is the exception: distant melee stays aimed at a pre-Modern horse's turn-start tile and stops if the horse rides away (`rules/movement.rs::aim_pursuits`, `finish_pursuits`). Every unit takes one step per round, whatever its moves, so a fast unit outruns a slow pursuer only once the pursuer has run out of moves in an ordinary pursuit. A unit attacked in melee while stepping away takes the hit where it stands without fortification (terrain counts) and completes its step if it survives (G21, the movement.rs module doc); the class bonus still applies. A melee winner that kills its defender advances into the defender's tile (module doc step 5), Civ 6 style; there is no "attack without moving in" for melee or mounted units, so a kill can leave the attacker next to the target's screen and within two tiles of a walled city. The core forecast (`forecast.rs::forecast_attack_mode`) describes only the fight and this turn's city shot from where the attacker starts; the app's forecast card adds the **"after the attack" line** for a melee attack that can win against a unit (no rule change, view-only, `app/src/aftermath.ts::attackAftermath`, shown by `ui/ForecastCard.tsx`): at the defender's tile, unfortified, with the winner's HP after the fight (an Sparta winner at 100), it lists every known enemy walled city within two tiles, regardless of terrain blocking the shot (`forecast_city_strike` mirrored: the city's public `strength` against the winner's defense) and every visible enemy military unit that can shoot it from where it stands or reach it in one turn (, the Threat lens's reach; fogged units are never counted), with expected damage at average rolls including the class bonus, applied in turn order (city, ranged, melee) so each wound weakens the next defense. The risk word is Safe (nothing can reach it), Exposed (it survives the expected total) or Likely lost (the expected total is at least its HP); Likely lost, or Exposed with half its HP gone, goes in the card's headline. The kill chance assumes a fortified defender stays fortified, and the card says so ("Assumes the Archer stays fortified (+6 Defense)"): a ranged defender that fires first loses the bonus (`rules/battle.rs`: an attacker's fortify is cleared), so the card also gives the kill chance without it. The p881 T30 example of a Spearman chasing and striking a retreating Horseman from beyond turn-start adjacency described the former rule; under Outrider that horse escapes.

**Split counterattack** (user decision 2026-10-04; `rules/battle.rs::split_counterattacks`): a
defender (a unit, or a city's melee defense) fights back once per turn. In the first movement
stage in which it is attacked in melee, its counterattack is computed once from the snapshot (its
retaliation against the strongest melee attacker: highest attack strength, then lowest unit id)
and split evenly by integer division among every melee attacker that hits it in that stage; the
strongest also takes the remainder (21 among three: 7 each; 22: 8, 7, 7). Later stages that turn
take no counterattack from it. A city and its garrison are one defending side: a garrison whose
city was assaulted in an earlier stage does not fight back, and overflow never adds a second
counterattack, except that each supporter of an assaulted city adds its own reply to the
pool (supported defence, §4). Every attacker still deals its full hit; overkill on a unit is wasted. Unchanged:
ranged attacks (no retaliation), the wall strike (once per turn), meeting engagements (each pair
still fights once, and they do not spend the counterattack). The result is the same whatever
order the orders were submitted in.

**Ranged**: no retaliation; range 1-2; the Bomber's +36 vs. cities (`data::siege_city_bonus`, design 20 on the era curve). Resolves entirely before
movement/melee. **A shot fights as the ranged class** (`combat::fight_class`, `fight_class_bonus`, used by
`rules/battle.rs::plan_fight_on` and `forecast.rs::forecast_attack_mode`; mirrored in `app/src/combat.ts`): a
mounted unit firing its bow (the Keshig) gets no class bonus for the shot, a melee-line target gets none against it
(arrows are not a charge), and a mounted target gets no "Mounted against ranged" against any shot, an Archer's included (designer, 2026-11-10; the bonus is for charging). Charging
and defending, the Keshig is mounted. Every other unit fights as its own class, so nothing else changed. At range 2, ground ranged units, including those with the Siege upgrade, need a clear line of sight (`los::ranged_clear_with_tech`), except a unit standing in its own walled city (any wall tier) or one with indirect fire. Aircraft ignore terrain blockers. Adjacent shots always work. Walled city strikes also ignore terrain blockers (§4 City strike), while team visibility still limits every target. On a hostile city's tile the city absorbs the shot while it has HP (below). A shot at a city hits the city while it has HP (`rules/battle.rs::ranged_target`).

**Walls and Siege (siege study 2026-10-18; Siege replaced the Military Engineer, user decision 2026-10-22, decisions log; `docs/design/research/siege-rework-2026-10-18.md`, results `docs/sim-results/siege-engineer/`, `docs/sim-results/siege-upgrade/`).**
- **Walls halve every attack on a city's HP** (`combat::wall_damage_percent`, `WALLS_DAMAGE_PERCENT` 50, ranged and melee alike), except attacks from a unit with Siege, bought or built in. A city's wall tier is `data::wall_tier`: 0 none, 1 Walls (the Swiss Alpine Redoubt too), 2 a Castle, 3 a Star Fort. Hits on a unit, or on a garrison once the city is at 0 HP, are never halved; air strikes are not halved. The Walls' strength and the city strike are unchanged.
  The city-fire forecast applies that same percentage to the attack after the city's shot wounds the attacker (`forecast.rs::city_fire_outcome`); the card and its "or N with Siege" hint use the reduced damage.
- **Siege is built in from the Industrial era** (designer, 2026-10-28; `data::built_in_siege`, `BUILT_IN_SIEGE_ERA` 4, the 0-based `unit_era_tier`). Every land military unit whose own era is Industrial or later (Rifleman, Cavalry, Field Cannon and on; not aircraft) fights walled cities as if Siege covered every tier: no wall strength and full damage, whatever its owner knows (`data::siege_cover` returns `MAX_WALL_TIER` 3). It is never offered: `can_have_siege` is false, `UnitView::siege_offer` is None, and an `UpgradeSiege` order is refused with "Rifleman needs no Siege: it ignores Walls, Castles and Star Forts (Industrial-era guns)" (`BUILT_IN_SIEGE_NOTE`, also `rules.generated.json` `siege.builtInNote`). `Unit::siege` stays the *bought* flag and is always false on those units: an upgrade into an Industrial or later kind clears it (`rules/units.rs`, and the order list's supply check in `rules/mod.rs`), so the unit stops using the Siege Iron. The one helper is `data::siege_cover(kind, bought_with)` (with `data::has_siege(kind, bought)` for the AI): `rules/battle.rs::siege_cover`, `forecast.rs::siege_cover` and `ai/siege_buy.rs` all call it. Walls keep their strength against everything else and the city strike; air strikes unchanged; Mongol rules unchanged.
- **Siege is a unit upgrade bought with Gold** (`rules/siege.rs`, `Order::UpgradeSiege {unit}`, `Unit::siege`, `Event::SiegeBought`). Any land military unit except a Scout, before the Industrial era (`data::can_have_siege`), once its owner knows Mathematics (`data::siege_tier` > 0), in its owner's own territory, for `data::siege_gold(kind)`: `SIEGE_BASE_GOLD` 160 Gold (the Engineer's old 80 Production at the upgrade rate of 2 Gold a Production) scaled by the unit's era on the units' own curve (`era_scaled`: about 160 through the Medieval era, 185 Renaissance, 222 Industrial, 285 Modern, 383 Future), halved by Military Engineering like every upgrade. One order per unit per turn, and the unit acts next turn. It is permanent and is kept through unit upgrades, until an upgrade into an Industrial or later kind (built in from then); the check is `siege::check` (the same words in the order validation, the resolution and `UnitView::siege_offer {price, blocker}`, which the app prints on the unit card for own units once Mathematics is known).
- **Iron.** Each Siege unit uses 1 Iron supply while it exists, as the Pikeman does (`resources::SupplyUser::Siege`, `siege_need`; a Siege Pikeman uses two): the purchase needs 1 spare Iron (counted with the Pikeman upgrades in an order list, `siege::iron_blocker`), and short of supply the newest users go idle and fight `UNSUPPLIED_STRENGTH_PERCENT` weaker (an idle Siege unit keeps its Siege). **The one switch** is `data::SIEGE_USES_IRON` (the user was "not sure" about a resource): `false` makes Siege cost Gold only, everywhere (core, `rules.generated.json` `siege.usesIron`, the app .
- **What it does** (`rules/battle.rs::siege_cover`, `forecast.rs::siege_cover`, `UnitView::siege`, public): the owner's highest siege technology is the tier (`data::siege_tier`: Mathematics 1, Machinery 2, Metallurgy 3; the tier follows the technology, so nothing is re-bought). Against walls of its tier or lower the unit fights as if the city had no Walls: the tiers' strength is left out (`combat::city_defense_covered`, `city_defense_vs`) and the damage is full. Against higher walls it leaves out the tiers it reaches and the damage is 75% (`SIEGE_PARTIAL_PERCENT`). The forecast carries `BattleForecast::walls` (`WallsForecast {tier, covered, percent, built_in}`); the app's Walls line names Siege (`ui/forecastWarnings.ts::wallsLine`), and for built-in Siege prints "Siege built in: your Rifleman ignores Walls, Castles and Star Forts (Industrial-era guns)." (core's note after the unit's name). Mirrored in `app/src/combat.ts` (`siegeCover`, `data.ts::builtInSiege`). The unit wears siege gear of its own period on the map (`app/src/siegeProps.ts`, rivals see it too): the gear is capped by the unit's own era, whatever the tier (designer, 2026-10-28: an Ancient Archer with Metallurgy wore a bombard): Ancient and Classical ranged units a catapult, Medieval ranged units a catapult or a trebuchet (tier 2 and up), Renaissance ranged units a bombard; every other unit ladders (tier 1) or a siege tower (tiers 2 and 3) through Medieval, and ladders in the Renaissance; Industrial and later units nothing (no gear and no badge: the designer rejected a badge). The prop is cosmetic: the combat tier still comes from the technologies, and the combat animation's shot at a city follows the drawn prop (`combatFx.ts::shotKindFor`). The unit card's siege note reads "Siege built in: ..." for them, with no Siege upgrade row.
- **The Military Engineer retired** (user, 2026-10-22): the unit, its build card, art, upkeep, stacked cover and capture rule are gone, with the AI's Engineer planning.
- **Mongols** (user, 2026-10-19 and 2026-10-22) raise Walls, Castles and Star Forts **from the Medieval era** (the owner's era, `data::MONGOL_WALLS_ERA` 2, through `available_to_player(faction, era, item)` and `faction_excluded_building_at`) and buy Siege from then too (`data::siege_available`); before it neither. Greyed reasons: "The Mongols raise Walls from the Medieval era" and, on the unit card, `MONGOL_SIEGE_REASON` ("No Siege before the Medieval era."). Their old half damage to city HP was removed earlier. Walls are Ancient (Masonry), so the gate is the era, not the technology.
- Roadmap, not built: units laying roads (Engineering) and building Forts (Feudalism).

**Cities shield their garrisons** (Civ 6; user decision 2026-10-03, after playtest 6). One shared
function decides what an attack on a hostile city's tile damages:
`combat::city_tile_defender(city_hp, garrisoned)` (`crates/core/src/combat.rs::city_tile_defender`).
- **City HP > 0:** every attack on the tile (ranged or melee) is aimed at the city's HP, halved by Walls unless the attacker has Siege.
  Melee retaliation uses the city's own strength:
  `combat::city_defense_for_rules` = 10 + 2 × population + Walls (10; Swiss Alpine Redoubt
  15) + Castle 5 + Star Fort 5, each line scaled to its owner's era by `data::era_scaled` (x1.00 Ancient, 1.00 Classical,
  0.96 Classical, 1.00 Medieval, 1.16 Renaissance, 1.39 Industrial, 1.51 Machine Age, 1.78 World War, 2.14 Modern, 2.88 Future (`MELEE_LINE` over `DESIGN_MELEE_LINE`); the argument is the owner's 0-based era,
  public in the view), − (200 − HP)/20, plus the opt-in `frontier-cities`/`wall-masonry` levers. **There
  is no garrison bonus:** the garrison adds nothing to city strength; it is a second layer. A stage
  that takes the city to 0 HP captures only if its overflow also kills the garrison (below).
- **City HP = 0:** ranged fire hits the visible garrison (unit defense: terrain of the city tile,
  fortify, promotion, wounds); a melee attack fights the garrison, and if the garrison dies the
  attacker advances and captures. With no garrison, a melee unit entering captures as before;
  ranged fire at an empty fallen city is held for whoever comes in (next bullet).
- **Held shots at a fallen city** (Sam, 2026-11-21, RULES_VERSION 47, from his turn-67 Geneva
  siege: five Keshig volleys hit the empty 0-HP city and did nothing, then a Swiss Soldier
  slipped in as garrison and only met the charge; his rule: "1) slip into garrison (with
  support) 2) ranged attack 3) melee attack"; `rules/battle.rs::HeldShot`, `held_city`,
  `release_held_shots`, `rules/movement.rs::movement_round`, `reserves`). A ground unit's shot
  at a hostile city that is at 0 HP with no garrison in the ranged-phase snapshot is not fired
  then: the unit spends its turn as usual and the shot waits. In the first movement round in
  which a military unit of the city's side stands on the tile (a unit slipping in under
  "Reinforcing your own city goes first", or one that arrived in an earlier round), every
  held shot on that city strikes it as a ranged hit at the head of that round's stage, before
  the round's melee hits: aimed at the garrison, shared with supporters as in "Supported
  defence", no retaliation. A held shot is itself an attack on the tile, so a unit stepping in
  under it is a reserve (garrison first, then supporters) even when no melee attacker comes.
  The melee of that round then fights what is left; the volley's and the melee's damage land
  together (the stage is one snapshot), so a garrison the volley kills still gives that
  round's melee its counterattack. If nobody comes in all turn, the held shots land on the
  empty city after movement exactly as before (a `Battle` on the city, no damage); a city
  captured or no longer hostile by then takes nothing. A city razed during movement (a Celtic capture) leaves its held shots with no city: they besiege nothing (`movement.rs::reserves` reads the city with `cities.get`, `held_shot_tests::a_held_shot_whose_city_was_razed_does_not_stop_a_later_relief`) and are dropped. A held shooter counts toward its side's
  force in a rival assault. Aircraft never hold (they return to their hangars after the ranged
  phase). Replaying his tape at rules 47, the five volleys kill the Soldier (29 + 32 + 27 + 27
  + 28) and his Keshig takes Geneva. The forecast card does not describe the held shot yet.
  Tests: `rules/held_shot_tests.rs`.
- **Overflow on a city tile is per hit** (user decision 2026-10-04, made per hit by the designer
  2026-11-11, RULES_VERSION 27; `rules/battle.rs::carried_damage`, `combat::carried_share`): the
  hits of one stage (the ranged phase, or one movement round) go one by one in resolution order.
  Each is judged against what it hits: against the city's HP it is the hit's damage to the city
  (Walls and Siege included); a hit that kills the city's HP with damage to spare carries on to the
  garrison only its unused share, `floor(direct × leftover / hit_damage)`, where `direct` is what
  the same attacker, with the same roll, deals to the garrison itself (its defence, fortification,
  class bonuses such as Melee line against mounted; no Walls), all from the stage snapshot (integer
  math). The old rule pooled the stage's damage and converted the excess by `city_defense /
  garrison_defense`; for a city without supporters that changes the garrison's wound only when the
