  hits differ in class or the defences differ from the city's. Later hits in the stage strike
  whatever layer is current (the garrison once the city is at 0 HP), computed against it directly.
  Excess past the garrison's HP is wasted; with supporters, what gets past the city is shared (supported defence, below). A garrison
  stepping out of the city that round is not reached. The garrison's defence takes the same
  terrain, fortification, promotion, wound, phalanx and resource-shortage modifiers as direct
  combat (`rules/battle.rs::unit_defense_on`); a Plains Pikeman without Iron defends at 24.
- **The forecast shows the whole fight** (user, 2026-10-19, T187 Lugano; `forecast/assault.rs::forecast_city_assault`, `CityAssaultForecast`, surfaced through `GameClient.assault` and the wasm/online `assault` call, never TS rules): one card with the city's HP before and after, the garrison's HP after the overflow (with `AssaultGarrison::carried`, the expected HP lost to the carried part of the hit that empties the city, shown as a note on the city's row, "carries −M to the garrison"), the chance the city is captured this turn, and your unit's HP, **pooling every attack of yours queued on the same city** (`AlongsideAttack`). It runs the stages as resolution does (ranged stage, then the melee round: hits one by one on the city HP, the hit that empties it carrying its unused share to the garrison (`CarriedShare`), a fallen city's garrison taking hits directly), with the primary attacker's and defender's independent rolls varied over 80-120% and the other queued attacks at 100%. A melee attacker the city's own shot would destroy is left out of that roll. A shot alone never captures (`can_capture` false). The headline "Decisive" follows the capture chance, not the city's HP alone.
  Attack strength uses the same city modifiers as resolution, including the mounted charge penalty. The melee stage splits one counterattack among its attackers; capture requires at least one melee attacker to survive it (`combat::attack_strength_rules`, `forecast/assault.rs::run`).
- **Phase order:** ranged fire resolves before movement. Its overflow can damage or kill the
  garrison but never captures; a later melee stage can then fight the exposed garrison or walk
  into the emptied city. In a movement round, a combined melee assault can break the city, kill
  the garrison through overflow and capture immediately: the strongest surviving melee attacker
  that fought on that tile this round advances (highest attack strength, then lowest unit id;
  `rules/movement.rs`, "If this very stage broke the city"). A round that starts with the city at
  0 HP hits its garrison directly.
- **Reinforcing your own city goes first** (user decision 2026-10-04, from a turn-107 bounce;
  `rules/movement.rs::reserves`): inside a movement
  round, after steps are declared and classified and before any fight is planned, a plain move
  by a non-ranged military unit into its **owner's** city resolves first when the tile holds no
  military unit and an enemy attack targets that tile this round (a shot held for the fallen
  city counts, "Held shots at a fallen city" above). Per city tile the one that can
  soak the most goes first (`battle::soak`, next bullet; ties to the lowest unit id); the others
  are its supporters (next bullet). The unit moves, pays its
  step, emits `UnitMoved` then `CityReinforced`, and is the garrison for the rest of the round:
  enemy attacks on that tile are reclassified against it. The city's HP still absorbs first and
  overflow reaches the new garrison (it can die, and then the assault captures); at 0 HP the
  attack fights the new garrison as a unit (a melee, not a meeting engagement). Too late if the
  city was captured in an earlier round (the move then fails as before). Owner only: an ally's
  unit gets no priority (the old contest rules apply). No priority for ranged units, for a unit
  an enemy attacks where it stands this round, or when nobody attacks the city (an ordinary
  move). Order-independent: intents are keyed by unit id. UI: the report line "Your Legionary
  reached Keystone Wreck first and held it" (or "...fought as its garrison, but the city fell"),
  `app/src/format.ts`; a tile-card note on your own threatened, ungarrisoned city when a unit is
  selected (`app/src/ui/forecastWarnings.ts::reinforcementNote`). Tests:
  `rules/reinforcement_tests.rs`, parity in `app/src/test/cityStrike.wasm.test.ts`.
- **Supported defence: the garrison and its supporters share the damage and all fight back; a
  city falls only when all of them are dead** (designer, 2026-11-15, replacing "relief chains in"
  of 2026-11-11, RULES_VERSION 37; `rules/movement.rs::reserves`,
  `rules/battle.rs::carried_damage`, `split_counterattacks`, `soak`). His words: the walls absorb
  first, then the damage is spread among the units supporting the city; "it's basically the same
  result as if you put all the attacking units on one hex and all the defending units on one hex".
  - **Who supports.** All of the owner's non-ranged military units stepping into their own city
    (a plain move) in a round in which an enemy attacks that tile. Owner only. A unit an enemy
    attacks where it stands that round gives no support (cut support: pin it). If nobody attacks
    the city, it is an ordinary move. Supporters are never claimants of the tile: an enemy's claim
    never bounces them, and they never bounce the enemy's.
  - **Shared damage.** The city's HP (halved by Walls unless Siege) absorbs first, as above. What
    gets past it in a stage is shared among the living defenders: the garrison and every
    supporter. Per hit, in resolution order: a hit of damage B with P of it past the city (all of
    it for a hit on the garrison or on a city already emptied that stage) gives each of the n
    living defenders P / n by integer division, the remainder to the garrison (without one, the
    lowest unit id). Each takes that share of what the same attacker, with the same roll, deals
    to it directly, `floor(direct × share / B)` (its defence on the city's terrain, class bonuses;
    a supporter is never fortified), so damage is still judged against the unit type it hits.
    Overkill on one defender is wasted; later hits are shared among those still alive. A garrison
    stepping out of a city with HP is not reached; one leaving a fallen city takes its parting hit
    first (G21) and what gets past it is shared among the supporters. With no supporters this is
    the per-hit overflow above, unchanged.
  - **Supporters stay where they are.** They take their share on their own tiles and stay there,
    wounded, with `MoveBlocked` "another of your units holds the city" while the garrison lives.
  - **Backfill.** If the garrison dies (or steps out), the supporter left alive that can soak the
    most enters as the new garrison (`UnitMoved`, `CityReinforced`; enemy claimants get
    `MoveBlocked` "the city was reinforced"). Soak (`battle::soak`, designer 2026-11-15: "a
    warrior in the late game might have the most HP, but they can't absorb it") is the unit's HP
    over the damage the worst of the round's attackers deals it at an average roll, on the city's
    terrain, unfortified; ties to the lowest unit id. A full-HP Spearman loses to a 45-HP Rifleman
    against Riflemen. The same ranking picks which unit enters an empty attacked city first
    (the "goes first" bullet above).
  - **Pooled reply.** Every living defender fights back: the city's melee defence while the city
    has HP (the garrison only once the city is at 0 HP, as before), plus each supporter's own
    counterattack against the same strongest melee attacker with the same defender roll (its
    defence on the city's terrain, unfortified, class bonuses). The replies are added and split
    among the melee attackers as in "Split counterattack" (even, remainder to the strongest).
    Still once per turn per defender; ranged attacks still draw no reply; the wall strike is
    unchanged. Supporters fight no Battle of their own: their replies show in the attackers'
    `damage_to_attacker`.
  - **Capture.** The city is captured that round only when the garrison and every supporter are
    dead and a melee attacker took part (the usual captor choice). A supporter killed this way
    counts as a kill for Spartan healing and Flower Wars by everyone who hit its city's tile that
    round.
  - **Worked example** (`rules/reinforcement_tests.rs::rifleman_worked_example_prints_turn_by_turn`,
    average rolls): a walled size-5 Industrial city with a Rifleman garrison, three enemy
    Riflemen assaulting it. Alone, the garrison falls on turn 2. With three Riflemen supporting
    it every turn, the city holds and the three attackers are dead by turn 3 (under the old rule
    the supporters went in one at a time and the city fell on turn 5). With three more enemy
    Riflemen pinning the supporters, it falls on turn 3.
  - **Forecast.** `forecast/assault.rs::forecast_city_assault_supported` runs the same fight with
    the supporters the caller names (`CityAssaultForecast::supporters`, each `AssaultSupporter`'s
    expected HP and death chance, and `reply`, the attacker's expected share of the pooled
    reply). The attacker cannot see the defender's orders, so the app's card still calls
    `forecast_city_assault` (no supporters) for now.
  Tests: `rules/reinforcement_tests.rs` (`supporters_share_*`, `shared_damage_splits_*`,
  `the_backfill_is_*`, `every_supporter_adds_*`, `a_supporter_attacked_*`, `bukhara_t67_*`),
  `forecast.rs::a_supported_assault_shares_the_damage_and_pools_the_reply_as_resolution_does`.
- **Rival assaults on one city: the stronger side walks in** (designer, 2026-11-11, Lucerne: his
  city at 0/200, two empires at war with each other both ordering units in and bouncing every
  turn; `rules/movement.rs::settle_by_force`, `rules/battle.rs::city_force`). When military
  claimants of a city tile all belong to players hostile to its owner and are not all one
  permanent team (**at war with each other or not**: since 2026-11-14, Tara, India and Sparta
  both at war with the Celts but at Peace with each other bounced off an empty city for three
  turns, because the rule had only run for claimants at war with each other), each unit's **force** is the damage its attack would deal to the city's HP at an
  average (100%) roll, not limited by the HP left (the city may be at 0 HP with no garrison):
  every unit that moved onto the tile this round (melee strength, including a shooter that
  stepped on) and every unit of those players that shot at the tile this turn (ranged
  strength, aircraft included), with the city's current defence, Walls and Siege as in
  resolution. A side is one permanent team (`GameState::same_team`): Alliance partners of other teams and attackers at Peace with each other are separate sides and do not add together, and the stronger walks in. Only a side
  with a melee claimant that can advance (alive, not pinned) can win; a ranged-only side's force
  counts but never captures. It runs after the round's damage, shared with any supporters: it decides
  only who enters once the city is at 0 HP and every defender is dead (or the garrison stepped
  out). The winning side's captor is chosen as for one team; the others stay where they stood
  with `MoveBlocked` "<winner> took the city with force W against your L" (shown even for units
  that fought the garrison). The two attacking sides never fight each other over the city tile
  (the old meeting engagement at an empty 0-HP city between two of its enemies is gone; a unit
  on the owner's side still meets an attacker there as before). An exact tie in force between
  the strongest sides bounces everyone: "<other> brought equal force (N each), so nobody took the
  city". The assault forecast does not show rival force yet. Tests:
  `rules/rival_assault_tests.rs`.
- **Garrison moving out the same turn:** during the ranged phase it is still shielded. In a
  movement round where it steps out while an attacker steps in, a city with HP takes the hit and
  the garrison leaves unharmed (no overflow reaches it); at 0 HP it takes a parting hit without fortification (G21). The
  attacker does not advance that round (the snapshot holder blocks it).
- **Civilians in the city** are never targets; on capture they are captured as before.
- **Visibility:** at 0 HP a shot hits
  the empty-looking city instead (`rules/battle.rs::ranged_target`). Melee in movement uses the
  real state.
- **Non-hostile city:** a hostile unit standing in a city the attacker is not at war with gets no
  shield (it is hit as a field unit).
- **City HP regeneration** (changed 2026-10-06, playtest 8.2: "too slowly") ignores the garrison:
  a city below 200 HP regains `data::CITY_HEAL` = **+20 HP** at the end of a turn (10 turns from
  empty to full), unless it took battle damage that turn (`City.last_attacked_turn == state.turn`,
  set in `rules/battle.rs::apply_stage` when a stage deals it damage; battles resolve before
  healing, so a hit on turn T blocks T's heal and T+1 heals again) or an at-war military unit is
  adjacent (`rules/cities.rs::heal_blocked_by_adjacent_enemy`, `heal_cities`; a barbarian never counts against a Celtic city, `rules::is_threat`). It was +5 with
  only the adjacency test. A 0-HP city is not "damaged" by attacks on its garrison, so it heals
  (+20) unless an enemy is adjacent. A fallen city that regains HP shields its garrison again.
  The `city-heal` rules experiment is gone (this is the default). The city card shows "Heals +20 a
  turn · full in N turns" or "Not healing: an enemy military unit is adjacent"
  (`CityDetail.turns_to_heal`, `heal_blocked`), the health tooltip and Guide state the rule, and the attack forecast on a city
  with HP says damaging it stops its heal. The proxies' campaign forecasts (`proxy_v5/v6
  campaign.rs::needs_rotation`) project a struck city with no heal and an unstruck one with +20.
- **City-under-attack alerts** (2026-10-08, UI only; no rule changed): `CityDetail.attacked_last_turn`
  (owner-only, absent when false; core `view.rs`) is true when `City.last_attacked_turn
  + 1 == state.turn`, i.e. the city took battle damage in the turn that just resolved, and survives a
  save, unlike the turn report. The app's alerts (`app/src/siege.ts`) also read visible at-war military
  units (scouts excluded): beside the city, or ranged within their range, they can strike it next turn.
  The capture forecast uses the same damage table and strengths as combat at an average (100%) roll; it
  models "0 HP then the garrison then captured", so only a melee unit takes a city.
- **Pursuit:** a unit pursuing a quarry that enters its own city with HP stops beside it, since
  it could only hit the city (`rules/movement.rs::movement_round`).
Used by `rules/battle.rs::ranged_target`, the melee step classification in
`rules/movement.rs::movement_round`, and `forecast.rs::forecast_attack_mode`. `BattleForecast.garrison`
(`forecast.rs::GarrisonForecast`, omitted when absent) reports the visible garrison and
`shielded`; `forecast::city_attack_target(view, hex)` answers "what does an
attack on this city tile damage" for AI scoring. Client display uses `app/src/combat.ts::cityTileDefender`; WASM behavior checks in `app/src/test/cityStrike.wasm.test.ts`. UI: the forecast headline says "The city absorbs
damage first. The hit that empties its HP carries on to the garrison with the part of its damage the city did not absorb; later hits strike the garrison in full" (`app/src/ui/forecastWarnings.ts::garrisonHeadline`),
the win line says what a win does (`forecastWinLabel`), and the Guide's combat entry explains it.
Split counterattack and overflow in the UI (`app/src/ui/ForecastCard.tsx::forecastModel`): with
other friendly melee orders on the same defender this turn, the forecast says "2 others also
attacking: you take about −7 instead of −21" and counts the share in HP, loss odds and risk; when
this turn's friendly hits on a garrisoned city exceed its HP (100% rolls) it adds "This attack /
Combined friendly attacks this turn bring the city to 0 HP and reach the garrison." The turn report
(`app/src/format.ts::formatEvents`) gives one line per combined assault, named by place, and only
that line says destroyed: "… was destroyed at Tessitura in a combined assault by your 3
Oathbearers; its counterattack was split, −7 each"; a city taken with its garrison in that round
reads "Tessitura fell to a combined assault by …; its garrison was destroyed and the city
captured."

**City strike** (default on; **walls only** since 2026-10-03, user decision, as in Civ 6): only a
city with Walls fires. Any tier counts: Walls, its Castle and Star Fort upgrades, and the Swiss Alpine
Redoubt (their Walls, `data::wall_tier`). An unwalled city never
fires; its garrison and 200 HP are its only defence, so early rushes can take fresh cities. A
walled city with HP left auto-fires once per ranged phase at the lowest-(HP, id) hostile
military unit within 2 hexes that its team can see (`rules::is_threat`: a **Celtic city never fires at a barbarian**, 2026-11-03; there is no manual city strike, so only the ranged units inside it can shoot one, by order). Forest, Hills and Mountains do not block
the strike; terrain still affects team visibility (fog). It deals no damage to the city and grants
no XP to either side; a 0-HP city stops firing.
One shared function decides it: `combat::city_can_fire(walls, hp)`
(`crates/core/src/combat.rs::city_can_fire`), used by resolution (`rules/battle.rs::resolve_ranged`)
and through `forecast::city_fires` by
`forecast_city_strike` (so `BattleForecast.city_strike` is absent for unwalled cities) and
`city_strike_target`. Unit ranged fire uses `crates/core/src/los.rs::ranged_clear_with_tech`. A ranged unit standing in its own city with any wall tier (`data::wall_tier > 0`) ignores terrain LOS blockers, as the city strike does; it still needs range and team visibility. An unwalled city's garrison and units outside cities use ordinary LOS. City strikes use range and team visibility, with no terrain LOS check. Walls are public in `CityView.walls`. The client uses `app/src/combat.ts::cityCanFire` and `app/src/los.ts::shotBlockReason`.
UI: the map draws a wall overlay on walled towns (the owner's era sprite, `app/src/map/cityArt.ts::wallStem`; the Alpine Redoubt uses the star-fort Renaissance sprite, and a Castle or Star Fort draws as plain Walls); the city panel and tile card say "Fires
at units within 2 tiles: has Walls" or "Cannot fire: no Walls" (`app/src/format.ts::cityFireStatus`);
the attack forecast notes when the target cannot fire back.
**AI and the city shield** (2026-10-03): projections of committed fire and melee use
`analysis::damage_tile_defender` (`crates/ai/src/analysis.rs`: the stage's damage hits the city
first, then each hit's unused share carries to its garrison, estimated by the defense ratio) in `military.rs::melee_forecast`,
`city_assault.rs::city_assault_succeeds` and proxy v6 `after_volley`. Ganging up is cheaper:
`military.rs::queued_split_forecast` divides a melee forecast's counterattack (and recomputes the
loss chance) by the number of this turn's queued friendly melee orders on the same tile, and
`city_assault_succeeds` splits the defending side's counterattack once among the first round's
attackers, with none in later rounds. `military.rs::best_shot`
(used by `ranged_fire` and `fire_from_holds`) shoots garrisoned cities with HP (it no longer skips
them), scores shots at a shielded tile as capture progress against the city's remaining HP
(`tactics.rs::city_shot_value`, see "Ranged fire" in section 8), and wastes no shot past 0 HP. `tactics.rs::city_shield` (Full /
MeleeOnly / None) makes a garrison of a city that cannot be broken this turn safe in
`can_defend_at`, `garrison_doomed` and `unit_exposure_key`; when it can be broken, only melee
reaches the garrison. `purchase_defense.rs::city_risk_with` applies hits to the city first and
models a ranged garrison's own shot; `production.rs::buy_emergency_defender` can buy Rangers
or Marksmen and prefers a shooter while the city has over half its HP, a melee garrison below.
Threatened cities with over half HP prefer a ranged garrison (`military.rs::prefers_ranged_garrison`, used by `defend_cities` and `garrison_cities`). Rush sieges bombard
garrisoned cities and send the capturer only when the volley exposes the garrison and the melee
forecast beats it. `targets()` keeps its force heuristic (garrison + city/4). Proxies v2-v5 are
frozen benchmarks and keep old-rule projections; v6 is rule-aware (exposure, volley scoring,
capture finishing, rotation).

**AI**: all city-fire exposure maths go through `city_fires` (`crates/ai/src/tactics.rs`,
`analysis.rs`, `exploration.rs`, `victory/goals.rs`, proxies v5/v6). Walls are valued more when a
rival with a city within 8 hexes is at war with us, has declared any war, or masses 2+ military
within 6 hexes (`development.rs::aggressive_neighbour`, used in `production.rs` Walls score,
`infrastructure_bonus`, and Masonry research), and for Hard when a watched or top
threat (§10, `threat.rs`) can reach the city. Rush targets prefer unwalled cities: a walled
city counts 6 hexes farther (`openings.rs::WALLED_DETOUR`, `opening_strike_city`, which also drops
a pre-war target that has since built Walls), and scripted campaigns weight walled targets 1.5x
(`military.rs::campaign_target_for`). AI exposure estimates also use the shared LOS function for
city and ranged-unit fire lanes (`crates/ai/src/tactics.rs::exposure_key,enemy_exposure_key`).
Confirmed scripted routes stop before a newly visible hostile unit or city instead of submitting
an invalid path through it (`crates/ai/src/scripted.rs::unit_order`,
`crates/core/src/rules/units.rs::check_known_path`).

**Capture**: a melee unit dropping an ungarrisoned city to 0 HP captures it, as does a melee unit
that destroys the garrison of a city already at 0 HP (above); ranged alone cannot. Capture halves
the city's population, rounded down (min 1; rules 31, designer 2026-11-13; a city coming home to its founder keeps it all), clears production, loses capital status, starts 6 turns of zero
output. Celts is the exception: capture sacks and burns the city (**unless the Celtic captor founded it: a city you founded comes home intact when you retake it, through the ordinary capture path with its full population, no sack Gold, no camp and no `CityRazed`; designer, 2026-11-14, Celts game turn 156: "if i take back my own city it shouldn't be razed". A city founded by anyone else, a teammate included, is still sacked.** `battle.rs::capture_city` checks `c.founder == captor`; `forecast.rs` shows no `sack_gold` for it; `CityView::founder` is public and the AI's `sack_gold_value`/`Planner::sacks_city` value such a retake as a kept city), releases its owned tiles, and transfers
**60 Gold per population of the city, nothing else** to the Celts (`economy::SACK_GOLD_PER_POPULATION`,
`economy::celts_sack_gold(population)`; the victim's treasury is not touched; designer, 2026-11-03: "simplify the gold
capture... that explanation in the rules is too complicated"), and raises a warband camp: one rite camp
(§7, wild camps) on a free tile within 3 of the ruins, first in the territory of a Celtic city with no camp yet, then
nearest (`barbarians::raise_sack_camp`). A sack takes no technology (the 2026-10-23 steal was removed 2026-11-03: it
fired in 0 of 2 sacks in the designer's 8.9.25 game and 12% of AI Celtic sacks). The 60 Gold value doubles the previous fixed sack payout (designer review, 2026-11-06).
`CityRazed` carries `sack_gold` and `camp`, both public: `forecast.rs::forecast_attack_mode` shows the exact amount
(`ForecastCard`: "Sack: +N Gold (60 per population)"), and `event_visibility.rs` no longer strips `sack_gold` (the
ordered Raze's 10 per population is public math too); there is no optional sack order. Capture through a garrison uses the same path, so the sack, Swiss bounty and resistance all apply unchanged. An
Sparta capture fully heals its military within two hexes (`crates/core/src/rules/battle.rs::capture_city`).

**Greece Captive (Athens, 2026-11-06):** when an empire captures a city Athens owns at that instant, the captor gains one free technology to choose. The free-tech prompt names the captured city as its source (`Player::free_techs_captured`, `FreeTechsView::captured`; a broadcast clears it). No Lyceum is required and even a Celtic capture that immediately sacks the city earns it. The captor can earn this once for that city; `City::greece_captive_paid` records paid empires across later changes of hands. Athens and its teammates gain nothing for taking it. The grant uses `Player::free_techs_pending += 1` and `free_techs_from = Athens`; `Action::ChooseFreeTechs` spends it under the same current-era gate and persistent prompt as Share the Learning (`rules/actions.rs::unknown_techs`, `eras::learn`). A fully researched captor receives no unspendable pick. `AthensCityCaptured` is public and the report names the city and free technology. `CityView::greece_captive_tech` tells a rival whether its capture still pays; Athens sees the risk on its own city card. `FactionRule::AthensGreeceCaptive` turns the reward off for ablation. Hard’s `greece_captive_attack` and `greece_captive_defend` genes value the research gain in city targeting, campaigns, and Athens’s hold decision (`ai/victory.rs`, `plan.rs`, `army_commit.rs`).

**Original-capital VP is held (Sam, 2026-11-22, via the Fable review; rules 50; replaces the 2026-11-01 permanent point):** an empire scores `scoring::CAPITAL_VP` (1) for each rival original capital it **holds now**: a city with `City::original_capital` whose founder is on another team (`scoring::capitals_held`, read by `scoring::vp` as `VpSource::CapitalTaken { capital }`; the variant keeps its old name). The point moves with the city exactly like a world wonder's: whoever owns the city that turn has it, a recapture by anyone (a third empire, the founder or a teammate of the founder) takes it away the same turn, and a razed or sacked capital scores for nobody. Taking it again scores it again. The founder and its teammates never score their own capital. Capturing does not score in `rules/battle.rs::capture_city` any more: end-of-turn scoring compares the holders at the start of the turn (`objectives::CaptureVpSnapshot::capitals`, from `scoring::capital_holders`; barbarians never hold one) with the holders now and reports `Event::CapitalLost { player, capital, name }` for the old holder, then `Event::CapitalTaken { captor, capital, name }` for the new one (`scoring::check_capitals`, run after the world wonders and before `check_victory`), both public. Because team VP is counted live, losing the point can break a victory hold the same turn (`update_vp_holds`), exactly as losing a wonder does. **The Celts' sack is the one exception (pillar 8, the Celts' signature kept):** a Celtic sack of a rival's original capital (founder on another team) burns it and keeps its 1 VP for the rest of the game, since nobody can take the city back. `rules/battle.rs::capture_city`'s sack path pushes `(city, name)` onto `Player::capitals_sacked` (serde default, skipped when empty; only that path adds to it), emits public `Event::CapitalSacked { captor, capital, name }` after `CityRazed`, and `scoring::vp` counts each as `VpSource::CapitalSacked { capital }` (1). `PlayerPublic::capitals_sacked` shows them; the ledger lists them as "(sacked)". A teammate's capital sacked scores nothing. The previous holder still loses its held point (`CapitalLost`), and the capital counts as fallen for the capitals victory. **An ordered Raze** of a held capital gives the point up. `PlayerPublic::capitals_taken` is the list of capitals held now (names included), so the ledger and the map star (hollow while you hold it) read it directly. The capitals victory (`holds_own_capital`) is unchanged. Hard: `victory.rs::capital_capture_vp` reads the held point, and the raze decisions count the held point they would give up (`raze.rs::own_capital_vp`, added to the keep value in `raze_by_value` and `production.rs`'s pressure raze).

**Raze order (2026-10-25 playtest 8.9.11; 2026-10-31 rule):** any empire may order `Raze` on a city it holds but did not found, beginning with the planning turn after capture. It is one city order for the turn and resolves before movement (`orders.rs::Raze`, `rules/cities.rs::check_order,apply_order`); other city orders for the same city then fail. It burns the city, releases all its tiles and clears their improvements and landmarks, as the Celtic burn does, and pays the razing empire population × 10 Gold. World wonders and faction goal buildings can be destroyed this way, along with their held VP. Aircraft based there are destroyed; tile units follow normal movement rules. Original capitals can be razed like any other city (designer ruling 2026-11-01, "let's make all cities razable"): the captor scores its first-capture point before it can raze, and razing denies rivals theirs. The owner's `raze_blocker` has only the founder and first-turn refusals. The owner view provides `CityDetail::captured` and the exact `raze_blocker` so the city panel can explain unavailable orders and forecast the Gold. `CityRazed { ordered: true }` records the result for the report, chronicle and map; Gold stays private to the former owner and razer through `event_visibility.rs`. An ordered raze does not count as another capture for objectives (`objectives::record_fights`) or postgame taken count. Hard AI (`production::plan_raze`, `raze::held_vp_at_city`) includes the VP a former holder could regain, and for an original capital the point a rival could still score by taking it (`raze::rival_capital_vp`, 1 VP while some empire off our team and not the founder has not scored it; razing denies it), when it prices razing and keeping a captured city, with value scoring under gene `raze_value` 1 (§10, "Raze and buys"). A city with a pending `Raze` order is never put in the Choose production queue (`app/src/ui/forecastWarnings.ts::idleCitiesNeedingProduction` skips it; 8.9.29 turn 102, 2026-11-08), so Ready is not blocked on a city that burns at the turn boundary. The Celts keep their automatic capture sack of population × 30 Gold and a camp.

**Capture ends the orders aimed at the city (2026-10-07, playtest 8.4 turn 31).** When a city
changes hands (not sacked), every unit of the captor's team or an ally whose goto or confirmed route
targets that city tile, other than units standing on it, loses goto, route, `path_stop_on_block`,
`blocked_turns` and `attack_target`, and gets `needs_orders`, so it returns to the order cycle with no
"move into this city" arrow. This happens at the moment of capture, so a unit still marching in a
later round of the same turn stops too. Rivals at War with the captor keep their orders (the city
is still hostile to them). A unit's attack order ends the same way when its target is gone
(destroyed, or captured by its own side): `end_pursuit` plus `needs_orders` (`movement.rs::void_pursuit`).
An attack on a Peace unit still just becomes an ordinary move (it is reported, not voided). Code:
`rules/battle.rs::end_orders_into_city` .

**Fortify/heal**: +3 fortify/turn to +6 max, cleared by any move/attack. Heal: 20 HP/turn in own
territory, 10 in the field; barbarians never heal. Spartan units rest-heal at these normal rates too (`rules/units.rs::resting_heal`; designer, 2026-11-06). Closed foreign land heals 0.
Destroying enemy military (including Scouts and barbarians) instead restores up to 100
HP shared evenly by the Sparta units that damaged the victim during this turn. Integer
remainders go to lower unit IDs; healing above max HP is redistributed to wounded participants.
All stage damage and deaths resolve before this healing, so mutual kills revive nobody
(`rules/battle.rs::apply_stage,heal_sparta_killers`, `rules/units.rs::resting_heal`).
A garrison killed by overflow damage (user decision 2026-10-04) counts as a kill: every Sparta
unit with a damaging Battle against the victim's own city tile this turn (ranged or melee, any
stage) joins the sharing, as if it had damaged the garrison itself.

**Promotions**: automatic, **no choice of tree** (`rules/promotion.rs`). Ordinarily 5 levels,
each +10% of base melee/ranged strength; XP from fights (ratio-based, capped at 8) and city
attacks (3, or 10 for the kill). All Sparta military spawn at level 1, gain XP 50% faster
(rounded up after the ordinary XP calculation), and alone may reach level 6, Iron Guard,
at 318 XP. This applies to Oathbearers once, not as a second strength bonus
(`game.rs::spawn_unit`, `rules/promotion.rs::threshold_for,combat_xp,level_for`).
**Aircraft promote the same way (RULES_VERSION 27, user 2026-11-10):** `Aircraft::xp` (serde default 0, omitted at 0, so old saves load) rides on the temporary map unit that stands in for a plane during the ranged phase (`air::ghost`, written back with its HP), so the ground-unit code runs unchanged: a striker earns the ranged-shot XP (or the city-attack XP, 3, or 10 for the blow that drops the city to 0), an interception is a melee exchange that gives the striker the attacker XP and the guard the defender XP, and the level adds the same 10% of base strength per level to the strike and interception strength as its own breakdown line ("Level N: +X%") in the forecast (`forecast_air_strike` reads `AircraftView::level`) and the resolved Battle alike. XP and level survive the Stealth upgrade and any rebase. Sparta's rule reads on "military", so Spartan aircraft start at level 1 (`promotion::starting_xp`, shared with `unit_with_id`), earn XP +50% and may reach level 6; the War Shrine start bonus is not implemented in code for any unit. A promotion is a `UnitPromoted` event (the aircraft's id) like a ground unit's, and `army::aircraft_value` (Strongest Army) counts the level. A Bomber levels risk-free when nothing intercepts it (accepted by the designer, no limiter). Only the enemy/own-strength term of fight XP doubles on a kill (`unit_fight_xp`). Fights with barbarians award XP only until the first promotion, at the first threshold (`barbarians::BARBARIAN_XP_MAX_LEVEL`, `cap_barbarian_xp`).

**Zone of control**: no named mechanic; pathing avoids hostiles when routing.

## 5. Diplomacy

**Peace, Alliance or War** (since 2026-10-03, user decision; Pacts, minimum terms, exit notice
periods, trust and call commitments were removed; Call to Arms was removed 2026-10-18 for shared wars, below). `RelationState`: `Peace | War | Alliance`
(`diplomacy.rs`); all start at Peace. `TreatyKind`: `Peace | Alliance`.

| | Cost (Gold) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Alliance upkeep | 1 per owned city per turn for each empire on both teams (minimum 1); none on a turn either ally is at War with a team close to winning (rules 50) | `TreatyKind::upkeep_for_cities` in `economy::treaty_upkeep`; a "Treaty upkeep: Alliance with X" line in the Gold breakdown, and a waiver line beside it |
| Breaking inside the protected window | 120 | first 10 turns (`ALLIANCE_PROTECTED_TURNS`, `leave_cost`) |
| Peace (ending a war) | free | 10 turns of guaranteed Peace (`PEACE_TRUCE_TURNS`); if Peace begins on turn T, no War may take effect through T+9, and War is possible from T+10. |

The truce belongs only to its two signers, so a war with an ally continues. It blocks direct declarations (including orders queued on the last protected turn), surprise wars, defensive Alliance joins, accepted calls to arms, and an Alliance signing that would join that protected pair in war (`diplomacy::check_order`, `join_blocked`, `call_join_blocked`, `check_alliance`). A surprise declaration's defensive joins are checked against the current resolution, not the next boundary. Signing or leaving an Alliance with the former enemy does not erase the truce's recorded deadline. The UI displays the end turn in the empire row and Declare War refusal; the AI prices the ten-turn loss of its best available war plan when considering Peace (`crates/ai/src/plan.rs::plan_candidates`).

Alliances cost per turn only (designer decision 2026-10-31). Each empire on both teams pays the per-city upkeep after activation; the 120 Gold early-exit cost remains. Offers and acceptance require no treasury balance, and `DiplomaticAction.upkeep` quotes the ongoing cost (`diplomacy.rs::check_order,view,resolve_boundary`; `economy.rs::treaty_upkeep`).

**Alliance upkeep waiver (Sam, 2026-11-22, via the Fable review; rules 50).** An Alliance pays no upkeep, for both allies, on any turn in which either allied team is at War with a third team that is **close to winning**: alive and on a victory hold (`GameState::vp_holds`) or within `scoring::NEAR_VICTORY_VP` (2) VP of its target (`scoring::near_victory`; the same line as the app's public near-victory warning, `celebrate/alarms.ts` `victoryNear`). `diplomacy::upkeep_waived_by(state, a, b)` names the lowest such team id. It is judged when Gold is paid (the income snapshot at the start of `process_cities`), with VP counted live and holds as of the last turn end. The Gold breakdown keeps the "Treaty upkeep: Alliance with X" line and adds "Treaty upkeep: Alliance with X (free while at War with Y, close to winning)" for the same amount back (`economy::treaty_upkeep`), so nothing is hidden. `DiplomaticAction.upkeep` still quotes the ordinary price. Hard's `diplomacy.rs::treaty_cost` prices what the Alliance really costs now: `treaty_upkeep` is 0 while the waiver applies, read from public data by `PlayerView::alliance_upkeep_waived_by(a, b)` (standings, holds and public wars; mirrors the core rule).

**An Alliance** means: the two teams cannot attack each other (not at War), share vision, may pass through each other's territory (`GameState::passage_allowed`), and defend each other or answer calls to arms (next section). Allied city passage is transit only; see §1 Movement. A coalition does not change the Alliance price.

### Defenders join at once; an ally's own war is a call to arms (user, 2026-10-23)

"Allies auto join defensive wars but they get prompted for whether they want to join offensive wars. It should be possible to ally someone, they declare war on someone, and you say you don't want to join, and you still stay allied." This replaces "allies fight their wars together" (2026-10-18, `share_wars`) for every new game. Guide sentence: **"Allies defend each other, and join wars by choice."**

- **Calls are the only treaty rule.** `DiplomacyState::calls: Vec<WarCall>` is present in every game; there are no free Alliances or automatic offensive wars (`diplomacy.rs::DiplomacyState`, `new`, `resolve_boundary`, `signing_fee`). `start-met` still makes teams start in contact for treaty fixtures (`game.rs::blank_game`).
- **Ally** means a direct Alliance (`DiplomacyState::partners`). Teams joined through chains are still one side (`side_of`) for `declare_conflict` and `alliance_conflict`, but a Peace binds only its two named teams; nothing joins or is called through a chain.
- **Defence is automatic, in the same turn as the declaration.** When team D declares War on A, every direct ally X of A that is alive, is not D, is not on D's side through any Alliance and is not already at War with D is at War with D at once: a surprise declaration brings the defenders in at the start of resolution, before ranged fire; a war on an ally (`breach`) takes effect next turn for all. The joined pair copies the war's `war_id` and has `declarers = [D]`, and emits `Event::WarJoined{team: X, ally: A}` (`diplomacy::start_war_effects`, `DiplomacyState::declare_wars`). No cascade: X's own allies do not join. A team that is allied to both D and A is never dragged in: it gets the call below, and Join is blocked for it. `DiplomaticAction.new_wars` on a declaration lists the defenders (team = X, enemy = D); a guaranteed Peace between X and D refuses the declaration (`join_blocked`: "X would have to join the war on D, but their peace is guaranteed through turn N").
- **Offensive wars are a call to arms.** Every direct ally Y of D that is alive, is not the target and is not already at War with it gets a `WarCall { id, team: Y, ally: D, target: A, created_turn, respond_by, alliance_version, war_version }` (`raise_call`, `Event::CallToArms`, visible to the two teams; the id comes from the proposal counter). **Timing:** the call is created at the boundary that begins D's war and first appears in the planning turn after the declaration (`created_turn`); it stays open `CALL_WINDOW_TURNS` = 2 planning turns (`respond_by = created_turn + 1`). The answer is an order, `Order::RespondToProposal { proposal: <call id>, accept }` (Join = accept, Decline), one diplomatic order per other team per turn like any (keyed to the calling ally). It resolves at the turn boundary like a declaration that does not start at once: **Join puts Y at War with A from the next turn** (`declarers = [Y]`, the ally's `war_id`, `Event::CallAnswered{joined: true}` and `WarJoined{team: Y, ally: D}`), and A's allies then defend against Y at once (`start_war_effects` without calls: joining raises no calls of its own). **Decline** keeps the Alliance and the Peace with A: no betrayal record, no Gold, no cooldown, nothing remembered (`CallAnswered{joined: false}`); an unanswered call lapses at the end of its window and counts as a decline (`CallLapsed`). A call also lapses when a team is eliminated, when the Alliance with the caller ended or changed (`alliance_version`), when the caller's war ended or changed (`war_version`) or when Y is already at War with A (`call_void_reason`). Checks (`check_call_answer`): only the called team, inside the window, call not void; Join is refused when A is also Y's ally ("leave that Alliance first, or decline"), a Peace guarantee with A holds, or a defender A would bring in holds guaranteed Peace with Y (`call_join_blocked`, `join_blocked`), and a closed country (Sakoku) cannot Join (`closed_refusal`); Decline is always legal. Thus accepting a call respects every truce the resulting wars would break. `DiplomaticAction.calls` on a declaration lists the allies who would be asked (team = Y, enemy = A).
- **Signing during a war** (`DiplomacyState::alliance_wars`, `alliance_effects`). For each war a partner fights: where the partner is the **defender** (`defender_in`: its enemy declared it, the partner did not) the new ally is at War with that enemy at once, same `war_id` (`new_wars`); where the partner declared or joined the war, the new ally gets a call (`calls`), both ways. Refusals as before: the two are already one side, the Alliance would make an empire fight its own ally (`alliance_conflict`), or a defender join would break a guaranteed Peace (`join_blocked`).
- **Peace is between two teams only** (`DiplomacyState::peace_parties`, `required_teams`). The proposer and recipient must agree (`awaiting`, `PeaceProposal.consents`); `make_peace` ends only their pairwise War and gives that pair a 10-turn guarantee. Both teams keep their Alliances, and every war either team or its allies has with anyone else continues. A separate Peace is no betrayal. A permanent teammate shares the same TeamId and is bound with its team. A later call to arms against the former enemy cannot be joined during this pair's truce; another pair's truce is independent.
- **UI:** a call is a decision in the diplomacy inbox: `CallCard` in `app/src/ui/DiplomacyPanel.tsx` (headline "X, your ally, is at War with Y and calls you to arms", Join the war on Y as the one primary action, Decline beside it, "Declining keeps your Alliance and your Peace", a For-you list with the War it starts, the defenders it brings against you and the note that declining is no betrayal). A call is a pending decision (`turnFlow.pendingDecisions`, "Answer call to arms" in the seat bar) answered with A and D; answering is queued and confirmed with Undo like an offer. The report says "came to the defence of", "joined X's war", "called to arms", "declined" and why a call ended (`format.ts`, `CallToArms`/`CallAnswered`/`CallLapsed`), and an attacked ally raises the "Your ally is attacked" alarm. Covered by WASM behavior checks in `app/src/test/diplomacy.wasm.test.ts`.
- **AI:** answers each call by value, `Planner::call_value` = `join_war_value(target)` (the war's price: the leader or a team counting down +, the odds, our other wars, danger at home, a hopeless or unreachable war, the army at our gates) + `CALL_SHARED_ENEMY_BONUS` (10) per team of the target's side it already fights (up to 3); Join at 0 or more, Decline otherwise, and never when another answer to the same ally uses the turn's one order. Alliance scoring counts the wars a new ally brings in at once (`new_wars`) and notes the calls (we may decline); it no longer prices wars we would be asked to join. The human-seat proxies join a call when the target is at 80% victory progress or a campaign against it is affordable and reachable (`proxy_v4.rs`).
- **Kept as they were:** leaving (protected window, Gold, betrayal records), war on an ally as a betrayal, the permanent human team (same `TeamId`, never at War, shares everything), first contact, Sakoku (a closed country cannot sign an Alliance, so it never has allies to defend).
- **Tests:** `crates/core/tests/diplomacy.rs` (defenders join at once, the call and its answers, lapse, void calls, signing in wartime, guarantees, Peace without the decliner, chains), `crates/ai/tests/proxy_alliances.rs`, WASM behavior checks in `app/src/test/diplomacy.wasm.test.ts`, the card in `app/src/ui/AlliesFight.test.tsx`.

**Offers end when answered or overtaken (2026-10-17, playtest 8.9.2 T20 loop).** An offer (`PeaceProposal`, any kind) records
the pair's `relation_version`; `resolve_boundary` drops it when declined (`ProposalRejected`, 5-turn cooldown), withdrawn,
accepted, expired, or when the pair's relation changed this turn: `ProposalInvalidated` with reason `diplomacy::WAR_ENDS_OFFERS`
("War was declared") when the pair is now at War (a surprise war at the start of resolution or a boundary declaration), else
"Relationship changed" (an Alliance left, bought peace). So **declaring War on an empire withdraws every pending offer between
the two**, and a declined offer never survives the turn. Core takes **one diplomatic order per other team per turn**
(`validate_orders`: "One diplomatic order per other team per turn"; `resolve_boundary` keeps the first by PlayerId), so a
decline plus a war toward the same empire is invalid: the war alone settles the offer. The client mirrors this: `queueDiplomacy`
replaces the order toward that team, except that declining an offer from a team the plan already declares War on, leaves or pays
tribute to is a no-op (`endsOffers`), and `turnFlow.pendingDecisions` counts an offer as settled by a reply or by such an order
toward its sender (`offerSettledBy`), so it is no longer "Respond to offer" and its inbox card reads "Ended by your War on X".

**A closed country (Japan's Sakoku vow, §8):** a closed empire cannot declare war, and no Alliance can be proposed or signed by a team with a closed member or with such a team (`diplomacy::closed_refusal` in `check_order`; the reason reads "X has closed the country (Sakoku): ..."), Peace is unaffected, and `passage_allowed` lets nobody but the closed empire's own team into its land at Peace (no Alliance passage or open road).

**Leaving** (`BreakTreaty`) takes effect next turn. Inside the protected window it costs the breach
Gold and is a **betrayal**; after it, leaving is free. **War on an ally** needs `DeclareWar{breach:
true}`, takes effect next turn, and is always a betrayal (plus the Gold inside the window).
An existing unit inside former allied land is not expelled when the Alliance ends (`rules/mod.rs` keeps
its position and clears fortification). At Peace in closed foreign land it cannot heal, fortify,
shoot, or attack from there; it may leave. A later declaration makes that land open as wartime
territory, so the staged unit may attack (`rules/movement.rs`, `rules/battle.rs`,
`core/tests/diplomacy.rs::closed_borders_require_progress_to_exit_and_forbid_healing_fortifying_or_attacking`).
This passage and the 10-turn free exit are costs in AI Alliance scoring.

A betrayal is one visible record, `Betrayal{by,against,turn,until = turn + 20}`
(`BETRAYAL_TURNS`, `DiplomacyState::betrayals`, public in `DiplomacyView.betrayals`, event
`AllianceBetrayed`): while it lasts the betrayer cannot propose an Alliance to its victim ("X won't
ally with Y until turn N: betrayed on turn M"); the victim may still offer one. There is no hidden
trust stat.
When both allies declare a breach in the same turn, each pays the protected-window cost and gets
their own betrayal record; the shared Alliance ends once (`diplomacy::resolve_boundary`).


**First contact (2026-10-03)**: an Alliance can be neither offered nor accepted until the two
teams have **met**: one team's own units or cities have seen a unit, city or owned tile of the
other. Contact is mutual and permanent; only native sight counts (an ally's borrowed sight
introduces nobody) and barbarians never count. Peace and War don't
need it. Recorded at game start and at the end of every resolution
(`crates/core/src/visibility.rs::contact_pairs,update_explored`) into `DiplomacyState::contacts`;
checked in `crates/core/src/diplomacy.rs::check_order` (ProposeTreaty and accepting
RespondToProposal, error `diplomacy::NOT_MET`) via `DiplomacyState::met`; views list met teams in
`DiplomacyView::met`. Saves from before the rule have no record (`contacts: None`) and treat every
pair as met; tapes before version 5 replay without it (`wasm/src/tape.rs::initial_state`).
 the UI states "You haven't met the X yet" once on that empire's row.

**War**: declaring against a team at plain Peace is a **surprise war** — effective immediately,
same turn, before ranged fire/movement; a unit can be ordered into enemy territory with the
declaration in one submission (G20). **At Peace a unit cannot enter the other empire's territory**
(except Mongols's Scouts and civilians, §8); allies may, but not into a closed country (§8, Sakoku). Only an Alliance shares vision.
The AI uses that same opening turn for ranged attacks and eligible melee attacks. Its combat
forecast projects valid surprise declarations before planning the assault
(`crates/ai/src/scripted.rs::attack_view`, `city_assault.rs::city_assault_succeeds`,
`military.rs::press_strike`). The aircraft planner uses the projected view after diplomacy
orders are chosen (`scripted.rs::projected_attack_view`, `air.rs::air_orders`). Decision gene
`declare_and_strike` defaults to 1; setting it to 0
restores the former one-turn attack delay for comparison. A declaration that breaches an Alliance
still takes effect next turn and is never projected as a surprise war.

**Special units (designer, 2026-11-14: "every civ has a special unit"; `RULES_VERSION` 34; `data::special_unit`, `unit_stats_made_by_rules`, `unit_name_for`, `combat.rs`, `resources.rs`).** Every faction fields one unit that replaces a plain unit for its people only; each has a `FactionRule` ablation switch (`aztecs_eagle_warrior`, `celts_gaesatae`, `athens_peltast`, `india_war_elephant`, `swiss_reislaufer`, `germany_panzer`). The numbers are design numbers and ride the era curve like every faction edge; every one shows as a line of the unit's strength breakdown or its stat line. A special unit upgrades like the plain unit it replaces, into the plain next unit of the line (a Spearman-line piece into the Phalangite, a Reisläufer into the Musketman): the Panzer, a Tank, upgrades into an ordinary Main Battle Tank (rules 35), which loses its extra move. The Mongols' Keshig, the Aztecs' Eagle Warrior, the Celtic Gaesatae, the Peltast, the War Elephant and the Tagmatic Soldier are all before the Modern era and need nothing from it; no special unit exists in the Modern era.
| Faction | Unit (replaces) | What changes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Aztecs | Eagle Warrior (Spearman, `Soldier`) | +4 strength, +10 Production |
| Celts | Gaesatae (Phalangite) | +8 strength attacking and in a meeting engagement ("Gaesatae: furious charge"), -5 defending ("Gaesatae: no shield wall"); `combat::gaesatae_attack`/`gaesatae_defense`, era-scaled |
| Athens | Peltast (Composite Archer) | 3 moves instead of 2, the same shot |
| India | War Elephant (Knight) | +8 strength, 3 moves instead of 4, and the mounted -33% charging a city does not apply (`combat::mounted_city_penalty_rules`); it is still mounted for the class triangle |
| Switzerland | Reisläufer (Pikeman, `Trooper`) | the Pikeman's own stats, plus +50% of its strength against mounted, a separate line ("Reisläufer pikes against mounted (+50%)") on top of the melee line's +40%, only where that bonus applies (`combat::fight_reislaufer_bonus_for_rules`); read as 50% of its own strength, not of the usual bonus |
| Germany | Panzer (Tank) | +1 move (`data::PANZER_MOVES`); it uses 1 Oil like the Tank (rules 37; it used 2 before) |
| Sparta, Mongols, Byzantium | Spartan Hoplite, Keshig, Tagmatic Soldier | unchanged (§8) |
Upgrading a special unit gives the ordinary next unit. A Company hired from a seller is that seller's version (below). Art: `app/src/unitArt.ts` keys the painted figure by faction and kind to the slugs `eagle-warrior`, `gaesatae`, `peltast`, `war-elephant`, `reislaufer`, `panzer`.

**Mercenary Companies (Byzantium / Byzantium only; user, 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-22: "let's do mercenary companies and replace bought peace").** Bought peace (`PayTribute`, `diplomacy/tribute.rs`) and Hire an army are deleted (old tapes drop `PayTribute` on load, `wasm/src/history.rs`; the unknown `tributes` list in a saved diplomacy state is ignored). The Byzantium's field army is hired: `Order::HireCompany { city, unit, seller }` (a city order beside `BuyItem`, `rules/cities.rs`; rules in `crates/core/src/company.rs`, which holds the numbers and the doc comment).
- **Who sells and what** (`company::sellers`, `seller_can_field`, `offers`): any alive empire on another team that the Byzantium has met and is **not at War with** (Peace or Alliance). **Each seller offers exactly one unit: its own special unit** (`data::special_unit(faction)`; the Spartan Hoplite, Keshig, Eagle Warrior, Gaesatae, Peltast, War Elephant, Reisläufer, Panzer, and Byzantium's Tagmatic Soldier), never a plain unit (designer, 2026-11-14; `company::sold_by`, `offers`, and `blocker` refuses anything else: "Sparta sells only its own Spartan Hoplite"). It can be hired when the seller knows the unit's tech and, when the unit needs a strategic resource (`resources::unit_need`), owns an improved deposit of it (`company::seller_can_field` / `seller_refusal`), even if all supply slots are in use. A seller that cannot field it yet is still listed with `Offer::unavailable` ("Panzer needs Armoured Warfare"), which the shop shows as one muted line and which can never be hired. Nothing is on offer before first contact. Published to the viewer as `PlayerView.company_offers` (private, Byzantium only).
- **Price and Gold** (`company::price`, `HIRE_PERCENT` 100): the Production cost of the **seller's version** of the unit (`economy::version_unit_cost`, less the buyer's own Military Science discount), in Gold (everyone else buys at 200%). The Gold goes to the **seller in full** the moment the order is applied (no veto: the seller need do nothing). A hire takes the city's one purchase for the turn (`last_purchase_turn`), needs a free spawn tile, no resistance, and the treasury (`company::blocker`).
- **The Company** (`company::hire`): appears beside the city next turn (moves 0 on the hire turn), promotion level 1 (the Spartan spawn rule), `Unit::contract_until = turn + COMPANY_TURNS` (**30**; user: 20 "feels short") and `Unit::origin = Some(seller faction)` (both public on `UnitView`). It fights, earns XP and **can capture cities**. **A Company is the seller's own version of the unit** (designer, 2026-11-14): its stats, name, picture and the rules of the unit itself follow `Unit::origin` (`GameState::made_by`, `PlayerView::made_by`, `data::unit_stats_made_by_rules`): the Hoplite's phalanx, the Keshig's bow, the Gaesatae's charge, the Reisläufer's bonus, the Panzer's move. The seller's faction-wide rules do not travel and the buyer's do: Mongol +1 move, Spartan ignored wounds, terrain defence, veterans. Combat takes both factions (`combat::unit_attack_strength_for_rules(off, faction, made_by, ..)`).
- **Outside the levy and the supply** (`economy::levy_count`, `resources::current_users` skip Companies): a Company does not count toward thin levies and uses none of Byzantium's strategic supply, so the shop is how Byzantium borrows a rival's era and deposits (era resources: exempt by design; the seller's improved deposit is the check, and the Company reserves no supply slot).
- **Upkeep:** `company::UPKEEP` **2** Gold a turn per Company, its own line "Mercenary Companies" in `economy::upkeep_breakdown`, outside the two free slots. Ordinary military upkeep counts only upkeep-paying land units (`economy::upkeep_unit_count`), not aircraft or Companies.
- **Expiry** (`company::expire`, after `process_cities` in `resolve_turn`): a Company whose `contract_until <= turn` is removed after its last turn's upkeep, wherever it stands (`Event::CompanyDeparted`, visible to its owner's team and anyone seeing the tile). A Company inside a captured city leaves it empty.
- **Unpaid companies turn** (`rules/cities.rs::pay_gold`, `bankruptcy_victim`): on bankruptcy a Company is taken before any levy unit and, instead of disbanding, becomes a barbarian unit where it stands (`company::turn_barbarian`, `Event::CompanyTurned`). With Barbarians Off, mutiny creates the barbarian controller so the loose unit can plan and act on following turns; natural camps remain off (`rules/barbarians.rs::plan_turn,spawn_camps`).
- **Upgrades:** refused for Companies (`rules/units.rs::check_upgrade`: hire the next one instead). A Company can be disbanded (no refund).
- **Events:** `CompanyHired { city, owner, seller, unit, gold, until }` (the buyer's and seller's teams), `CompanyDeparted`, `CompanyTurned`. The numbers are exported as `RULES.company { turns, upkeep, hirePercent }` (`app/src/rules.generated.json`, from `crates/ai/tests/ui_rules.rs`).
- **Tests:** `crates/core/tests/company.rs` (shop, hire and payment, levy and one-purchase rules, War and faction blocks, expiry and upgrade ban, unpaid turning, supply exemption); AI hiring in `crates/ai/src/company.rs`.
- **Measured** (2,048 balanced 8-empire Hard games, seed 9210001; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/mercenary-companies/results.md`): bought peace carried only about 3 pp of Byzantium's 29-30% (27.0% with it switched off); the Heir goal carried the rest (13.0% with the Heir unavailable and bought peace on).

**Coalitions** (engine and AI only; the player UI no longer shows the word, 2026-11-11: it says plain facts instead, see below) are a derived warning, not a mechanic: flags when one team's VP pressure (and
optionally city count) pulls far enough ahead, and lists Alliances signed against it
(`coalition_target`). A team holding its VP target (counting down, §6) is always the target: it
outranks every non-holder even on equal progress; of two holders the one with fewer turns left
(then higher pressure) is, and holders tied on both leave no target (`coalition::select`;
`pressure_view` adds `HOLD_PRESSURE` 1000 minus turns left). The view still carries `DiplomacyView::coalition` and `SecurityTreaty::coalition_target`, which nothing in the app reads. No coalition order exists, and the target changes no Alliance price (`diplomacy/coalition.rs`). Separately, on Hard,
`Planner::containment_target` can name a conqueror from public conquest evidence (captures,
eliminations, wars, armies at the border, momentum) well before VP or city-count would trigger a
formal coalition, and that target feeds the same Alliance-forming logic below
(`ai::diplomacy::containment_target`).

**AI** (`crates/ai/src/diplomacy.rs`): `peace_pair_scope` (default 1, all difficulties) prices the named enemy's danger rather than danger from every war, gives a small value to peeling one member off a hostile Alliance while the ally's war continues, and keeps `plan.rs::peace_would_be_broken` pair-specific. Alliances are value-based: score = −10 + 25 shared enemy +
25 shared coalition target + 15 when losing a war + the army it keeps off us (`alliance_security`:
their public army against ours, ×reach by distance between nearest known cities or units: ≤6 hexes
100%, ≤10 60%, ≤14 30%, beyond 0) − the wars it forecloses (`alliance_opportunity`: aggression and
army edge, same reach) − 80 if they are the leader − upkeep (`treaty_cost`: 10 turns of upkeep, one point per 10 Gold, `TREATY_GOLD_PER_POINT`; none in an old save) − 6 per Alliance already
held, accepted this turn or offered (`TREATY_HELD_PENALTY`, `TreatyBudget`) − 60 per team the Alliance adds to our bloc beyond a pair
(gene `bloc_size_penalty`, `bloc_size_cost`: free Alliances had produced blocs of 5 to 6 of 8 empires) + the wars a new ally brings in at once
(`alliance_war_value` over `DiplomaticAction.new_wars`: a war we would join costs `join_war_value`; an ally who would join our war is worth
`10 + min(20, edge/5)` (+10 endangered), scaled by how far it can reach the enemy, `helper_reach_percent`: all within 8 hexes, none past 16).
Offers to us are answered best first against the budget earlier acceptances leave. Refused when the partner is at
≥60% victory progress, has an active betrayal against us, or the treasury cannot cover
20 turns of any deficit the upkeep leaves (`TreatyCost::affordable`). **Against a shared
leader (Hard, gene `coalition_alliance`, 2026-10-07, playtest 8.4)**: when both teams are at war
with a team counting down, with our diplomatic target, or with the strict public VP leader at 50%
or more (`diplomacy.rs::shared_leader_war`), the 20-turn treasury test is relaxed for a shared leader (a bankrupt treasury still refuses), the score gains
`leader_war_value` ((leader progress − 40)/2, 0..30), and a partner past 60% is still accepted while
the leader is 10 points further ahead and the partner under 70%. The AI leaves an Alliance
only when leaving is free: partner ≥70% progress, a growing aggressor, a lapsed coalition reason,
or a forecast bankruptcy. War on an ally is reserved for a coalition leader at ≥90%. An AI waits
`ALLIANCE_ACQUAINTANCE_TURNS` (15) before offering; human recipients need no signing treasury (`crates/ai/src/diplomacy.rs::alliance_acquainted,treaty_cost`). **Calls to arms (2026-10-23):** an AI answers each call by
`Planner::call_value` (§5, "Defenders join at once"): `join_war_value(target)` plus 10 per team of the target's side it already fights. `join_war_value` also charges the front the war
would open at home (`inherited_front_cost`: up to 40 for the share of the army near our cities that would be theirs, and 20 or more more when a campaign against someone else is under way). The shared settlement scorer discounts a colony's
site value by 40% per travel turn after the first city, up from 10%, so both UI advice and AI
value reaching a city before rival borders close (`core::settlement::analyze_sites`,
`ai::civilians::choose_site`).

## 6. Victory

### Victory points (the current rule set)

Code: `core::scoring` (decadence, target, the public `army_strength` / `economy_value` / `advancement` numbers), `core::objectives` (World Ages), `economy`
(decadence in the yield breakdown), `view` (`eras`, `objective_progress`, `PlayerPublic.army` / `economy` / `advancement`). AI in `ai::objective_pursuit`
(objective strategy).

**The three crowns are gone (designer, 2026-11-11).** Largest Economy, Strongest Army and Most Advanced no longer score VP: games ended near turn 150 against a target of 250, and the crowns were surplus VP outside the target formula. `GameState::titles`, `Title`, `VpSource::Title`, `Event::TitleChanged`, `Chronicle::Crown`, `PlayerView::titles` and `PlayerPublic::crowns` are deleted, with every AI valuation that counted a crown (title investments, title denial, army-title guards, Most Advanced research denial, the crown signs in the runaway and hollow reads, the crown input of the value network and macro model). VP now come only from World Age cards, held original capitals, world wonders and faction goals; the 10 VP target is unchanged. The public army, economy and advancement numbers stay on `PlayerPublic` because the AI reads them as public facts (rival armies, rival treasuries, the win-chance features), and `army::breakdown` stays for the army forecasts; none of them scores. Era numbers and counts come from `data::era_index` / `era_count` / `ERA_NAMES`
(the nine eras of `core::eras`; **VP numbers eras 1-based**, 1 Ancient .. 6 Machine Age .. 7 World War .. 8 Modern .. 9 Future, `objectives::era_of` = `eras::current_era` + 1). World Ages cards (`age_pool`) cover eras 2..9.

**Near-victory warning and rival actions (UI only, 2026-11-11):** `celebrate/alarms.ts` raises `victoryNear` (ranked just below `victoryHold`) once when a team's VP crosses from below `target - 2` to `target - 2` or more, below the target, never on the turn its hold starts and never without a before view. The alarm, the hold alarm for a rival and the race panel's alerts (`nearestRival`) share `rivalWin.ts` / `ui/RivalWin.tsx`: "<People> are N VP from winning.", "At war with them: ...", **Declare war on <People>** (the existing declaration dialog, offered only when `warDeclaration` is legal; a block shows its reason; hidden when already at War or allied) as the alarm's one primary, else "See the race"; **Offer Alliance to X** for each empire already at War with them (existing `ProposeTreaty`, cost in red); and "Show what holds their VP" (the race panel). The turn report leads with a red (green for your own team) line for it. There is no coalition UI.

**World Ages are the only era scoring (owner, 2026-11-22: "world ages and then we'll iterate on world ages from there"; rules 50).** Built 2026-11-11 as the `objectives=deck` experiment; the experiment switch, the lobby's "Rule experiments" section, the `objectives=era` arm (three objectives per era: Build, Expand, Fight, scored once you entered the era) and everything only that arm used (the Techs, Captures, CapturesFromAhead, CapturesFromLeader, Kills and BreakLeaderHold kinds, `FightStats` captures/kills/leader counters, `Levels::techs`, `era_pool`, `GameState::capitals_taken`'s era use) were removed on 2026-11-22. `RuleExperiments` has no objectives field (a save or tape that still carries `objectives_deck` parses and ignores it); the WASM staging room's `rule_experiments` is an empty struct so an older room still parses. `objective-scale=N` remains (it scales the peaceful cards).
*Rule as the player reads it:* "When the first empire reaches a new era, a new Age begins for the whole world and one card, drawn at random, is revealed to everyone. Any empire may complete it for 1 permanent VP, whenever it can; the card never expires. Only what you do after the reveal counts." (designer, 2026-11-11: the 30-turn window was rejected, "otherwise there might not be a path to 10 points".)
`objectives::age_pool(era)` holds six
cards for eras 2-3 and seven from era 4 (Renaissance); the seeded `drawn_from_pool` (`Rng::derive(seed, [era, OBJECTIVES, 97])`,
one pick) draws ONE card per Age (user, 2026-11-11: "only one, randomly decided option per age, not 3 to choose from"), the same for every empire; it may be a Fight card, which a faction that cannot fight for it simply cannot score that Age (three of six, or three of seven from the Renaissance, are peaceful). At the end of the turn in which any living empire first reaches an era, `objectives::update` gives
every living empire an `EraRecord` for it (`entered` = the reveal turn, `base` and `levels` = its fight counters and holdings
at that moment), so a laggard can score an Age its technologies have not reached; it just cannot use the units or buildings it has not
researched. `objectives::revealed_era` is the newest Age any empire has a record for; `PlayerView.eras`, `objective_progress`
and `objective_accounting` stop there for every viewer, the AI included, so an unrevealed Age's cards are in no view (pillar 6).
`EraView.entered_by` still means "reached the era by technology". Scoring is public (`EraObjectiveScored` is visible to all).
An empire scores each Age's card once and at most one Age per turn, the oldest first (R2); after any score `restart_counting` re-snapshots
the Ages still open (R1), so what scored one Age never scores another. Counters count only what happens after the record's snapshot.
*The cards* (Tribute, a Gold payment, and Monument, a Triumphal Arch build, were cut in rules 31, designer 2026-11-13; Demobilise, disbanding 2 costly units, was cut in rules 37, designer 2026-11-15: "it could have just been gold ... it needs to be something that causes the player to take an action they otherwise wouldn't necessarily take"; the three peaceful cards were added in rules 50, 2026-11-22, so every faction has a card it can reach without war; every card is something done in play, none is bought):
- **Veterans** (`Veterans { n: 4 }`, every Age): your units earn 4 promotions. A promotion is one level gained by any of the empire's units through XP after the reveal, counted in `FightStats::promotions` at the one place a level is gained (`rules::promotion::grant_xp`, `after - before` levels). Levels a unit starts with (Sparta's veterans are created at level 1, a Company hired at level 1) or has at the reveal never count, a unit bought or built at a level counts nothing, and `restart_counting` re-snapshots it like the other counters. The meter reads "Promotions earned since the Age began: 2 of 4".
- **Humble the mighty** (`HumbleTheMighty`): capture a city from an empire ahead of the taker on VP (the turn-start `CaptureVpSnapshot`), counted in `FightStats::humbled`.
- **Breach** (`Breach`): capture a city with Walls of any tier, counted in `FightStats::walled`.
- **Gain 3 cities** (`Cities`, Renaissance on): net cities gained by founding or capture since the snapshot. Not scaled.
- **Patrons** (`Buildings { building, cities }`, every Age, Build family): "Patrons: add a <building> to N more cities", counting cities that hold the building or a later step (`objectives::Levels::buildings`), net of the snapshot. An idle building does not count. Building and count by era (8-empire counts): 2 Library 4, 3 Library 4, 4 University 4, 5 University 3, 6 Bank 3, 7 Bank 3, 8-9 Research Lab 3 (`Laboratory`, `College`, `Clearinghouse`, `InferenceCore`). Chosen so every faction can build it: no faction replaces these lines except by a renamed equivalent, which the card names (`app/src/data.ts` faction objective text).
- **Prosperity** (`Population { n }`, every Age, Expand family): "Prosperity: grow your population by N": 2: 50, 3: 42, 4: 34, 5: 25, 6-7: 23, 8-9: 25.
- **Frontier** (`Territory { n }`, every Age, Expand family): "Frontier: gain N tiles of territory": 2: 80, 3: 62, 4: 49, 5: 35, 6-7: 31, 8-9: 33.
The three peaceful counts reuse the old era objectives' Build and Expand thresholds (near the 70th percentile of what entrants gained per era, `docs/sim-results/era-objectives/step1.md`) and are raised 12% per empire below eight and multiplied by `objective-scale` (`Objective::scaled_for`, `scaled_pool`); the draw index is taken over the scaled pool, so a lobby size never changes which card is drawn. They are a first pass to iterate on (owner, 2026-11-22).
A capital or wonder city (`objectives::prize_city`: an original capital or a city with a Great Work or faction landmark) never counts for Humble the mighty or Breach, because it already pays VP. A Celtic sack carries `prize` and `walled` on `CityRazed` so a sacked walled city still counts for Breach. Camps and ordered Razes do not count.
The AI has no paid cards: every card goes through `objective_pursuit::chosen_objective` (the `ai::objective_deck` module and the `ClaimObjective` action were removed in rules 40). Veterans is a fought card: Hard prices it as 25 turns per promotion still needed, only at war with an army (`legacy_objective_effort`), with the same cheapest-effort choice as the other fought cards; it adds no gene. The peaceful cards use the existing Cities/Population/Territory/Buildings effort estimates. Cards of an Age that has not begun are not in its view, so no term for them exists yet.
App: `AgeBanner` announces a new Age with its card; "How you win" shows the cards with progress (`app/src/ageCards.ts`). There is no claim button: a card scores at turn end when met. The sidebar's "How you win" line follows the oldest open Age (the one that scores first): "Age of Classical · 1 VP", the card and your count, clickable to open the ledger. The era-up takeover's first panel is "Victory points open now": one block per Age you have not scored, with your progress and who has scored it. A rival scoring an Age card is one turn-report line naming the empire.
There is no capture requirement for winning by VP
  (designer, 2026-10-31: "you shouldn't be able to win with zero conquest" is to be reached by point
  design and measured, not enforced): the 10-turn VP hold is the ordinary VP win rule. Holding the last original capital also wins (rules 37, below). Turn-limit
  ranking applies only if a game explicitly configures one; the 1,200-turn simulation safety cap
  does not award a winner (designer, 2026-11-01: no turn-limit backstop for late stalemates).
  `tools/sim/objective-deck-report.py` (written for the deck-against-era cohorts) tracks winners with zero captured rival cities.
- **Target:** the solo target per team member (a pair needs two members' worth), held 10 turns at Standard
  (hold rules below unchanged). The solo target uses the earnable era, goal and world wonder points plus one, at most 10
  (`RuleExperiments::solo_vp_target`): with nine eras (as with seven or eight) that is 10 solo and 20 for a pair, the user's rule
  (Age cards 6 + goal 2 + world wonder 2 = 10, so a solo winner reaches it exactly, but the world wonder and some goals can change hands). On the
  old four eras the same formula gave 8 because 10 stalled 75% of 4-empire games; with seven eras no game stalled
  (256 Hard + Search games per lobby, longest 460 turns). Knob: `vp-target=N`.
- **Rival capitals (held):** 1 VP for each rival original capital an empire holds now, lost the turn anyone retakes it or it is razed; a Celtic sack keeps its point for good (`VpSource::CapitalSacked`) (rules 50; `scoring::capitals_held`, `VpSource::CapitalTaken`, end-of-turn `Event::CapitalTaken` / `Event::CapitalLost`). It stacks with the Fight cards; the 10/20 target and 10-turn hold are unchanged, and losing the point can break a hold.
- **Public treasuries (designer, 2026-11-13, rules 31):** every empire's current Gold is public to every viewer, alive or not (0 once dead): `PlayerPublic::gold` (`view.rs`), shown with a Gold icon on each empire's row in the race panel. The Hard search's determinized rival states read it instead of estimating a treasury from the public economy figure (`ai/src/search/determinize.rs`); the hidden-state tests no longer vary a rival's Gold.
- **Public strength numbers (2026-11-11, replacing the crowns):** `scoring::army_strength` (the points of every ground military unit and hangar aircraft in a city, Scouts included, civilians excluded: the higher era-scaled melee or ranged strength plus its promotion bonus minus its supply penalty, squared over 20, times HP / 100, truncated at each step), `scoring::economy_value` (city Gold + Production) and `scoring::advancement` (furthest era entered x 1000 + technologies known) are public on every `PlayerPublic` and score nothing. The AI reads them for rival armies, rival treasuries and the win-chance features. The wound-strength penalty, terrain, fortification, phalanx and class bonuses are situational and are not in the army number.
- **Decadence is off in an ordinary game (designer, 2026-10-31).** `RuleExperiments::decadence_slope` defaults to zero, and `scoring::decadence_percent_now` returns zero at that setting. No city yield, growth or Swiss interest is cut, and the UI shows no decadence line or Power explanation (`crates/core/src/experiments.rs::DEFAULT_DECADENCE_SLOPE`, `scoring.rs::decadence_percent_now`, `economy.rs::yields_for_item`, `app/src/ui/GuideContent.tsx`, `VpHoldings.tsx`, `TopBar.tsx`). Bankruptcy still disbands a unit through its ordinary upkeep rule; the separate overreach warning and reason are gone (`rules/cities.rs::pay_gold`, `view.rs::EconomyView`).
  - **One optional experiment:** `decadence-slope=c` turns on the proportional rule, with no saturation curve or Gold surcharge. Power is the trailing 50-turn average of city Food, Production, Gold and Science yields; lead is Power above the mean of living empires, including the leader. After a full window, the rate is `round(lead_percent * c / 100)`, zero at or below the mean. It cuts each city's Production, Gold and Science and Swiss treasury interest by that rate, and raises growth cost so population grows more slowly; Food itself is unchanged. Lines appear only when a cut is nonzero (`scoring.rs::power`, `mean_power`, `refresh_decadence`; `economy.rs::decadence_cut`, `decadence_growth`, `swiss_interest_cut`). Power stays in the public view because Hard's economic threat estimate reads its lead even with decadence off (`view.rs::PlayerPublic`, `crates/ai/src/threat.rs::economic_signs`).
  - The AI reads the actual rate of zero for target strain and does no marginal decadence calculation when the slope is zero (`crates/ai/src/objective_pursuit.rs::burden`, `crates/ai/src/raze.rs::raze_value`); win chance uses observed economic output and has no decadence assumption (`crates/ai/src/winchance.rs::WinFacts`).
- **Counting (R1, R2; user 2026-10-20: "progress counts from the last era score"; built 2026-10-21, kept for World Ages).** Each Age record keeps two
  snapshots from when counting began: `EraRecord.base` (`FightStats`: humbled, walled, promotions, filled from the turn's events) and `EraRecord.levels`
  (`objectives::Levels`: cities, population, tiles and, for each building, the cities holding it or a later step). A card's progress is the empire's count now minus the snapshot, never below zero, so losing a city
  is not a gain and a city, building or fight that was already there never counts. Whenever an empire scores an Age,
  `restart_counting` takes a fresh snapshot for every Age it has not scored, so what scored one Age never
  scores another and an older open Age needs fresh work.
  The snapshot is taken after the reveal turn's gains, so a city founded that turn is already in the baseline. `EraRecord::counting_since` records that turn, and the view exposes the baseline and current total for each gain count. All captures in a turn are judged against the same pre-resolution VP snapshot, before any capital point or held wonder transfers (`rules::resolve_turn`, `objectives::CaptureVpSnapshot`). A Celtic automatic sack carries the removed city's `original_capital`, `prize` and `walled` status in `CityRazed`, so it counts for the Fight cards even though the city no longer exists at objective scoring (`rules/battle.rs::capture_city`, `objectives::record_fights`). An ordered Raze is not a new capture.
  **At most one Age scores per empire per turn, the oldest first (R2)** (`objectives::update`).
