  Events: `EraEntered {player, era (1-based), first}` (emitted by `eras::learn`, not by `objectives::update`; when several empires enter an era that had no first on the same turn, `eras::draw_same_turn_firsts`, right after `process_research`, draws the first with `purpose::ERA_FIRST` and rewrites `GameState::era_firsts` and the events' `first` flags, rules 50), `EraObjectiveScored`, and `AgeBegan {era (1-based), first}` (public, emitted by `objectives::update` the turn an Age's card opens, once per Age; the turn report shows it as one line that replaces the first empire's `EraEntered` line and opens the score ledger). The VP target (10 solo, 20 for a pair) and the 10-turn hold are unchanged. The world wonder
  (RelayUplink, 2 VP) stays, and so do the faction goals (2 VP each; Egypt and Rome have none).
- On nine eras the eight earnable Age points plus a 2 VP faction goal (for a faction with one) and a held 2 VP world wonder can reach 12 (11 on eight eras, 10 on the old seven); the world wonder points now transfer with their tile (see Wonder tiles above).

**Winning by VP means holding the target** (user decision 2026-10-06). A team wins after staying at
or above its target for **10 consecutive turn ends** (`data::VP_HOLD_TURNS`).
- At the turn-end check (`scoring::update_vp_holds`) a team at/above target with no hold starts one
  with count **1** (the turn it reaches the target is the first turn held; event
  `VictoryHoldStarted`, public to everyone), and gains +1 at every later turn end. It wins at the
  turn end where the count reaches the needed number, so at Standard it holds the target on 10
  consecutive turn-end checks and wins at the end of the tenth.
- Dropping below the target (a title lost, a city captured, a goal broken) deletes the hold:
  count back to zero, `VictoryHoldBroken { turns_held, .. }`. Regaining the target starts a new
  hold from 1. An eliminated team also loses its hold.
- State: `GameState.vp_holds: BTreeMap<TeamId, u32>`, skipped when empty. Public view: `TeamStanding.victory_hold: Option<{turns_held, turns_needed}>`.
- Several teams completing on the same turn end: highest VP, then population, then a draw (the old
  tie-break order, applied to the completers only). A team that completes first wins even if another
  holder has more VP.

**Winning by capitals (designer, 2026-11-15, rules 37: "hunting every last city and Settler is tedious").** A team wins at the end of the turn in which it is the only team that still holds an original capital of its own, in a game with more than one team (`scoring::holds_own_capital`, run in `scoring::check_victory` after last-team-standing and before the VP holds; reason "{team} holds the last capital: every rival's capital has fallen"). A team holds one while any member's original capital (`City::original_capital`, a first-founded capital, never a replacement) stands and is owned by a member of the same team. It has fallen when another team owns it (an occupation lock included: the captor owns the city) or it is gone (ordered Raze, Celtic sack); retaking it puts the team back in. Every empire has its original capital from turn 1. Barbarians are no team. If no team holds its own, or two or more do, the game goes on; a team whose capital is razed can never win this way but still wins by VP. A victory by capitals is a victory, not a draw, even with VP below the target. Public: `PlayerPublic::capital_standing` (see the race panel, §10).

Other game-ending rules are unchanged: one team alive wins outright and instantly (also mid-hold);
at a configured turn limit the highest VP wins (tie = draw; checked after the holds, so a hold that
completes on that turn wins first).
Because a sacrificed VP source can no longer win on the spot, rivals always get N turns to exploit it.
AI response: every AI treats a holder as the top threat (`coalition::select` ranks holders first;
AI war, peace and targeting in `ai::diplomacy`, `ai::plan`).

### Game history (post-game report data, 2026-10-17)

- `GameState.history: history::GameHistory`, skipped while empty. `history::record(prev, &mut s, &events)` runs in `rules::resolve_turn` just before
  `s.turn += 1`, after scoring, so a winning turn's `GameWon` is recorded; `resolve_tactical_turn` does not
  record. Integer data only, no map iteration order.
- `GameHistory { first_turn, empires, chronicle }`. `empires[p]` (indexed by player id, barbarians excluded)
  holds one entry per resolved turn, entry `i` being the state after turn `first_turn + i`: `vp`
  (`scoring::vp_total`), `cities`, `population`, `science` (the turn's `science_history.last()`), `gold`,
  `army` (`scoring::title_metric` StrongestArmy), `techs`, `era` (`objectives::era_of`, 1-based).
  `first_turn` is 1 for a game recorded from its start. History that no longer lines up with the turn
  (series length or empire count) restarts at the current turn instead of failing.
- `chronicle` is a list of `history::Chronicle` entries (tag `kind`, each with `turn`) mapped from the turn's
  events in `chronicle_entry`: `Founded`, `Captured`, `Razed`, `War` (`by` set when one team declared),
  `Peace`, `Era`, `GreatWork`, `Wonder`, `Objective`, `Goal` (first completion
  only), `Eliminated`, `HoldStarted`, `HoldBroken`, `Won`, `Drawn`. Events of barbarians are skipped, except
  a capture or razing where only one side is a barbarian.
- **Hidden until the game ends:** `PlayerView.history` is `Some` only when `state.finished` and the view is
  `ViewDetail::Full` (`view::player_view_cached`); it is `None` for the Decisions and Map views and during
  play, so rivals' numbers stay private. The online host (in the host's tab) sends views as they are.
- **No backfill:** a save from another rules version is refused outright (`wasm::json::parse_state`), so history is never reconstructed from telemetry. A compatible archive is kept when its last tape hash matches the loaded state (`wasm::Session::from_save_json`: "saved archive does not match game state" otherwise); its tape hashes include the history.

## 7. Map and exploration

### Natural wonders (2026-10-10)

Each map seeds distinct wonders from eight real landmarks after resources and starts are fixed: Mount Fuji, Victoria Falls, the Grand Canyon, the Giant's Causeway, Uluru, Ha Long Bay, Iguazu Falls and the Great Barrier Reef (`wonders.rs::place`). At the Standard map size there are six; the count rounds with map area and caps at eight. The derived RNG makes placement stable for a seed without changing terrain, starts, ruins or resources. Wonders use matching terrain (Mount Fuji on impassable Mountains; the Grand Canyon and Uluru on Desert; Victoria Falls, the Giant's Causeway and Ha Long Bay on Hills; Iguazu Falls on Forest; the Great Barrier Reef on Coast), cannot share a ruin or resource, start at least four hexes from every starting capital site and five from another wonder. A wonder occupies one tile; a city cannot be founded on it (`rules/cities.rs::check_found_city`, `settlement.rs::known_legal_site`), and it cannot be improved.

Territory ownership controls a wonder. Its owning city receives **+25% of one city yield per turn**, rounded up from positive output before wonder bonuses: Science for Mount Fuji, the Grand Canyon and the Great Barrier Reef; Production for the Giant's Causeway; Food for Victoria Falls, Ha Long Bay and Iguazu Falls; Gold for Uluru. Multiple wonders each use the same pre-wonder base, so they do not compound one another. The bonus follows a tile transfer and needs no worker assignment or radius limit (`economy.rs::yields_base`). The first empire whose own unit or city sees a wonder receives one reward globally: 50 Science or 80 Gold; simultaneous sightings go to the lower player id. Starting sight is paid with the first turn's report so its notice is visible. Discovery persists across saves and is announced to the discovering empire's team (`wonders.rs::discover`, `event_visibility.rs`). Wonders give no VP. The view carries `TileView.wonder_found` (the discovery reward has been paid to some empire, public like the wonder itself; `view.rs`) so the tile card can show the reward while it is open. A city's yield breakdown lists the bonus as its own source, "<Wonder> (+25%)" (`economy.rs::yields_base`), which the app (`app/src/wonders.ts::wonderOfLabel`) turns into a named line, a city-panel row and a Natural wonders row in the Science hover. Settlement advice values an unclaimed wonder within three hexes (`settlement.rs::site_wonders`); border growth favors it (`rules/cities.rs::claim_score`).

Presets (`crates/core/tests/map_sizes.rs`, `SIZES`): **Small 40×26, Standard 48×32 (default), Large
60×40**, each validated 2-8 players. Generation builds one main continent (~45% land) holding ≥85%
of land, ridge mountains (~6% of land), climate-derived terrain. Ruins ≥4 tiles apart, ~1/60 land
tiles. Resources placed after start sites for fairness. No continent/island choice; only minor
unlandable islands.
The quality-banded start search requires two units of Production above the Plains baseline within
radius 2: two Forest or Hills tiles each provide one, or a visible bonus can make one tile equivalent.
Only passable tiles count. Since
resources are placed after starts, selection relies on terrain. The first 64 map attempts require
the full floor; only after repeated failures does the search retry with one and then zero production
tiles, retaining the quality and spacing rules. The floor is judged on the terrain as generated, before
`balance_founding_sites` rewrites ring 1 (below), so it now mostly screens ring 2.
Quality values Food and Production together but subtracts half their absolute difference, so a
one-yield site ranks lower. The search uses the same 100-point minimum and 24-point maximum spread.

**Balanced founding sites (designer, 2026-11-07; RULES_VERSION 24).** Every empire's founding territory, the start tile plus ring 1 (the seven tiles a new city owns and works), totals exactly **8 Food and 8 Production** (`mapgen.rs::START_FOOD`, `START_PRODUCTION`, counted as terrain yields before the city center's own bonus). After the starts are picked (and India's seat chosen), `balance_founding_sites` sculpts ring 1: water never changes, land becomes Grassland, Plains, Forest or Hills (a Mountains, Desert or Tundra tile in ring 1 is replaced too; no mountain is ever added), and the start tile itself only flips between Grassland and Plains. Among the exact solutions it picks the one that changes the fewest tiles, ties broken by a seeded draw (`LAYER_BALANCE` 8). Bonus resources stay out of ring 1: the two guaranteed Food/Production bonuses go exactly two hexes out (`resources/placement.rs`), the guaranteed Copper is two out, Iron three to five, and every other deposit is beyond three, so the ring's count never changes when a resource is revealed. A start with more than two water tiles in ring 1 is not a candidate (`START_MAX_RING_WATER`; with three the target is unreachable because water yields no Production). `validate` rejects a map where any start's ring differs from the target or holds a resource. Everything beyond ring 1 stays as generated, including strategic deposits and radius-2 production (the 2-unit floor above still applies to radius 2). The search's `site_quality` treats the founding territory as its fixed totals, so quality bands and spread are judged on what lies beyond it.
After selection, India's seat takes the picked start with the most passable land within radius 8,
with a deterministic seat tie-break. Other picked starts are swapped, not regenerated, and the final
assignment still meets the quality spread and team spacing rules
(`crates/core/src/mapgen.rs::pick_starts,assign_india_starts`). No other faction has a start
preference: a lean toward each faction's style was measured and left out
(`docs/sim-results/start-lean-2026-11-02.md`). This changes generated maps for a seed; rules version
10 rejects old saves without migration.

**Ruins**: trigger on landing for empires, never barbarians (`crates/core/src/rules/units.rs::explore_ruin`).
The 24 equally likely roll slots pay Gold (9 slots, 40), Science (9 slots, 25), +1 population
(2 slots, if a city exists), or a free Scout (4 slots, if a spawn tile exists); unavailable
population or Scout rewards fall back to Gold (`crates/core/src/rules/units.rs::explore_ruin`).
Athens receive the same reward as everyone else, plus 20 stored Science on every ruin
(`crates/core/src/rules/units.rs::explore_ruin`).
The scripted AI sends a nearby idle military unit to a known safe ruin when its `ruin_bonus`
outweighs travel, lost fortification, injury and the value of its current city post; emergency
defence and attacks take priority. Scouts still rank ruins with frontier exploration. A Settler
with no worthwhile founding site may take a nearby own-border ruin on a short, safe route; a
threatened city garrison retains its high defensive value (`crates/ai/src/military.rs::collect_ruins`,
`crates/ai/src/civilians.rs::idle_settler_ruin`, `crates/ai/src/civilians.rs::choose_frontier`).

**Barbarians** (toggle, default **on**): camps from turn `FIRST_CAMP_TURN` = 10 (a third of the target at once, then one more every 12 turns; one camp per 120 land tiles), at least 6 hexes from cities and other camps;
each has a guard, a scout, up to `MAX_RAIDERS` = 4 raiders. A scout that sees a city and gets home alive triggers a 3-unit raid (melee, ranged, melee, one mustered per 2 turns) prowling
2 tiles from the city for 12 turns, then a 30-turn cooldown. Raiders capture lone civilians, fight units in the open (never a Celtic unit or civilian: the Celts are spared, below), but
**never assault/capture a city, never heal** (explicit "this game has no pillage" comment). Ranged
raiders also require the shared terrain LOS check before ordering a shot
(`crates/core/src/rules/barbarians.rs::raider_plan`, `crates/core/src/los.rs::ranged_clear`). Clearing
a camp pays `CAMP_CLEAR_GOLD` = 50 Gold.
Raid prowl, scout-return and random roam destinations must be free of units; the camp's
stationary guard is not a valid endpoint (`barbarians.rs::beside,random_tile,raider_plan`).
Movement events store axial `Hex { q, r }`; the stopped-move sentence prints odd-row offset
`column,row` from `Hex::to_offset` (`rules/movement.rs::stop_blocked_moves`).
**Wild camps (Celts, Call the Wild)** are ordinary camps with `Camp::rite = Some(raiser)`: each raise
(`barbarians::call_the_wild`, from `Action::CallTheWild`) puts one only in each Celtic city lacking a camp (`barbarians::rite_plan`)
(`tile.city == city`), on the farthest passable free tile (ties: the lowest hex; never the city's own tile, so
`RITE_MIN_CITY_DISTANCE` = 1, which keeps a young city's one ring usable) and `RITE_MIN_CAMP_DISTANCE` = 2 from any camp, with a guard
and a scout like any camp (`rite_plan` previews the exact tiles; a city with no valid tile gets none, and
the call is refused when no city has one). They raid every empire but the Celts (barbarians never attack them, below), and deny the
tiles under them and their units (above). Differences from natural camps: borders never disperse them,
they are outside the natural camp target (`BarbarianState::natural_camps`), and the raiser's team
gets 0 Gold for clearing one. With barbarians off the rite creates the camp state itself and natural
camps stay off (`spawn_camps` needs `setup.barbarians`). **A Celtic sack raises one too (2026-10-23):**
`barbarians::raise_sack_camp` puts one rite camp, with its guard and scout, on a free tile within
`SACK_CAMP_RADIUS` (3) of the ruins (§4, "Capture"); it follows every rule above and counts for Raise the
Warbands when it lands in the territory of a Celtic city that has no camp yet.
**Barbarians never attack the Celts (designer, 2026-11-02; `RULES_VERSION` 11).** "They view them as neutral or allied", all game, for every camp, natural or Celtic-raised.
`rules::barbarians::spares(state, owner)` (the owner is a Celtic empire; `PlayerView::barbarians_spare_me` for the AI) is checked in
every barbarian decision: `raider_plan` ignores Celtic units (no melee target, no ranged shot, no captive), `scout_goto` never reports a
Celtic city (so no raid is launched at one) and clears a report on a city that has become Celtic, and the raid march ignores a Celtic city. At resolution,
`movement.rs::movement_round` blocks a barbarian step into a tile holding a Celtic unit or city ("barbarians never attack the Celts") and drops
a barbarian step onto a tile a Celtic unit also steps onto that round, so no meeting engagement is ever the barbarians' doing. The rule is one-sided:
the Celts can still attack barbarian units and camps, and clear them (a natural camp pays the usual 50 Gold, a rite camp pays the Celts nothing),
a camp's tile still yields nothing to whoever works it, and rivals clear camps as before, which breaks Raise the Warbands. Scope: the Celtic empire
only. A teammate or ally of the Celts gets no protection (an ambiguity of team play, resolved as "the Celts' own empire only"). Barbarians off changes nothing.
**Barbarians are no threat to the Celts either (designer, 2026-11-03, playtest 8.9.25).** The sparing was one-directional at first: a Celtic city shot at a barbarian beside it and Celtic sleepers woke for one, because `hostile`/`at_war` still read barbarians as enemies. One shared predicate now separates *hostile* (attack orders and targets: the Celts may still fight barbarians) from *threat* (everything automatic or advisory): `rules::is_threat(state, observer, other)` and `PlayerView::is_threat(observer, other)` = at war and not (a barbarian toward the Celts). It decides: a walled city's shot (`battle.rs::resolve_ranged`, `forecast::forecast_city_strike`, `city_strike_target`), the Sleep and Heal wake-ups, the garrison auto-fire target, city heal blocking (`heal_blocked_by_adjacent_enemy`), `settlement::known_threats` (the Threat lens and Settle advice), and in the AI `Ctx::threat_units` (danger, Settler danger, remembered sightings, scout exploration stops, home-defence and escort checks, `purchase_defense::city_attacks`, modernization foes, hollow march). `Ctx::enemy_units` stays the list of attack targets. In the app, `diplomacy.ts::isThreat` / `barbariansSpareMe` drive the city-under-attack alarms and map rings (`siege.ts`), route danger (`enemyReach.ts`), wounded-near-enemies, reinforcement and aftermath-risk notes, the "rival in sight" hint, turn-feedback warnings and the air-wing decision prompt (`airWing.ts::airState`; the Strike picker still lists barbarians). Attack and targeting code still reads `atWar` (`commands.ts`, `unitEra.ts`, `MapCanvas.tsx`, `path.ts` route blocking). No `RULES_VERSION` change (no save field moved).
**Barbarians deny yields** (Civ 6; user 2026-10-06): a tile holding a barbarian camp or a barbarian unit yields nothing to the city that works it, shown in the city breakdown as "Barbarians hold the tile" (`economy.rs::denied_near`, shared with the Byzantium salvage camp ; `app/src/ui/TileCard.tsx`).


**Vision**: unit sight is 2 by default, city sight 3; Hills give either source +1 sight. The Mongol
Scout no longer sees further (the Mongol Outrider and its sight 3 were removed with the Keshig, 2026-10-17):
every unit sees `data::UNIT_SIGHT` (2). No technology extends sight (Salvage Protocols was removed).
Each team always sees every tile owned by any of its members and every tile within one hex of that
land, regardless of terrain, line of sight or distance (`visibility.rs::native_visible_tiles`,
`add_territory_sight`). This applies to AI views and shared team sight too. Outside that territory
visibility, a tile within the radius is visible only when its hex line is unblocked. The blocking
tile itself remains visible, and every
tile adjacent to the sight source is always visible. This governs fog, including which targets a walled city may strike; a visible target within two tiles needs no separate line of sight for the city shot (§4 City strike). Mountains block everyone; Forest and Hills
block beyond themselves from low ground; a Hills viewer sees over Forest and Hills. Edge-aligned
lines are blocked only when both edge tiles block. The line uses integer cube-coordinate
interpolation and opposite fixed tie-break nudges (`crates/core/src/los.rs::blocker,rounded_step`,
`visibility.rs::native_visible_tiles`, `data.rs::UNIT_SIGHT`, `CITY_SIGHT`, `view.rs::unit_view`). On every explored
tile, `view.rs::player_view_detail` shows current territory ownership and current city existence,
owner and name: founding appears, capture changes the banner and razing removes it even under fog.
`CityView.great_works` also lists the city's completed world wonders on visible and explored city tiles,
current through fog; `CityView.detail.buildings` remains private to the owner. The map draws each
painted landmark on its chosen, stored wonder tile within the city's three-hex work radius. A built
landmark and that tile's owner are public even if the tile is unexplored (`view.rs::player_view_detail`,
`app/src/map/landmarks.ts::landmarksOf`). Other unexplored tiles disclose neither borders nor cities.
`CityView.construction` publicly names a queued wonder's chosen tile, building and progress stage,
including through fog once its city is explored; the owner's private production detail still carries
the exact cost and stored production (`view.rs::player_view_detail`, `app/src/map/constructionSites.ts`).
`TileMemory` retains last-seen private facts
(improvements and city population, HP and defenses); a newly public city with no observation
uses a neutral 1-population, full-HP estimate until sighted. Units remain sight-limited. Fogged city
banners and cards omit population, defenses, HP and production (`app/src/map/render.ts::cityBanners`,
`app/src/ui/CityPanel.tsx`, `app/src/ui/TileCard.tsx`).
`visibility.rs::contact_pairs` still uses native sight only: public changes under fog never create
contact. The AI reads this same `PlayerView`.

**Aircraft sight (2026-11-15):** every aircraft in a hangar sees `data::AIRCRAFT_SIGHT` = 4 tiles around its city, terrain ignored (`visibility::add_air_sight`), and an ordered Recon adds `data::RECON_RADIUS` = 2 tiles around its target for one turn (`add_recon_sight`; the air layer, §Eras).

**Shared sight API:**
`los::visible(from: Hex, to: Hex, terrain: impl FnMut(Hex) -> Option<Terrain>) -> bool`
checks geometry only; it does not check range, team or fog. Unknown terrain
is opaque. Callers still check range and target visibility. `visibility::native_visible_tiles(&GameState,
TeamId) -> BitSet` unions the team's own sight sources with their radii and hill bonuses.
`visibility::visible_tiles(&GameState, TeamId) -> BitSet` also includes active allies' native
sight, without forwarding borrowed sight. Native sight includes owned territory and its adjacent
ring. Bits are indexed by `state.map.index(hex)`.
Target selection requires the current tile bit; explored memory alone never reveals a unit. Every
unit on a tile a team sees is seen by that team; no unit is hidden.


**Ordinary attackers** are revealed to the attacked empire and its permanent teammate through the
next planning view, even if they stand outside sight (`Unit::attack_revealed_turn`,
`visibility.rs::attack_revealed_to`). Their battle report retains the attacker position when that unit
is revealed (`event_visibility.rs::visible_events`).

## 8. Factions

### Rome (2026-11-15; increment 1)

"Settle the frontier with the soldiers who earned it." Rome cannot train or buy Settlers (`data::available_to_faction_rules`); captured Settlers use ordinary founding. A full-health melee unit of level 1 or higher may use the existing `FoundCity` order. Founding consumes that unit without paying Gold or advancing `Player::settlers_trained`; ordinary Settler training and buying keep their own price counter. Roman military foundations do not consume a city's population. Every normal site, ownership, spacing and simultaneous conflict rule still applies (`rules/cities.rs::check_found_city,found_city`, `rules/units.rs::found_city_candidates`). In phase 5 of `rules/mod.rs::resolve_turn`, after ranged fire and movement combat but before healing, the resolver checks the founder's current HP, level and site. If it was wounded below 100 HP or a condition fails, the order fails without consuming the unit. Eligible simultaneous orders within either city's spacing limit all fail; surviving non-conflicting orders resolve in unit ID order, each checking its own eligibility. A Legionary is the Roman Phalangite replacement. Only a unit currently in that slot starts its founded city at population 2, with the corresponding population land claim (`cities::settle_territory`). A Castrum is the Roman Walls replacement. In phase 1, upgrading a Roman melee unit standing in its own city with a Castrum restores 50 HP, capped at 100, once per upgrade order even when it skips several intermediate kinds (`rules/units.rs::apply_unit_orders`). Rome has no faction goal in increment 1 (`GoalRule::None`, zero VP). The sheet uses Rome's own painted portrait, seal, glyph, Legionary and Castrum art; Byzantium's Tagmatic Soldier has its own icon.

Hard Rome uses gene `roman_founders` (on by default for Hard): it trains melee replacements, searches the nearby frontier and visible camps for safe first-promotion fights, and weighs nearby legal city sites more heavily for a healthy spare veteran (`crates/ai/src/production.rs,civilians.rs,military.rs`). It keeps the last defender of a threatened city. Four further Hard genes (on by default; 2026-11-21): `roman_hunt_radius` (8) keeps the promotion hunt within 8 hexes of an own city, drops the frontier reconnaissance and leaves recruits to a planned campaign so a strike force can gather; `roman_soldier_route` lets a veteran founder walk to its site as a soldier, without the Settler's capture-danger detour or trip-risk price (the site's own danger gates stay); `roman_veteran_retreat` steps a wounded threatened veteran out of reach to heal, since it can found only at full health, unless it is a threatened city's last defender; `roman_promotion_value` (100) adds the first promotions a siege should earn (3 XP per city attack a turn, 10 for the capture, 16 for level 1) to strike gains and campaign plans, each priced as a new city (`civilians.rs::roman_siege_promotions`). Two more Hard genes (on by default; 2026-11-21): `roman_early_war` lets Rome without a level-1 melee veteran nominate a conquest city with one city (the shared `conquest_min_cities` is 2), so the ordinary campaign planner can price an early war on the softest reachable neighbour (`conquest.rs::conquest_min_cities`); `roman_found_first` keeps the peace-time supported-defence staging from recalling this turn's veteran founder, which used to overwrite its FoundCity (`hard_defense.rs::stage_supported_defence`). `roman_finish_siege` (on by default; 2026-11-21) finishes the siege those promotions come from: while a Roman campaign is live the one-city conquest gate stays open after the first promotion, and while the campaign's capture forecast holds (`campaign_can_take` or `grind_forecast` on its committed units, or a visible 0-HP target with a committed melee unit) those units are exempt from the wounded-veteran heal and retreat, and a committed veteran founds only if the capture still forecasts without it (`civilians.rs::roman_live_siege`). The planner reads only `PlayerView`, never hidden game state. Other factions' planning stays on their existing paths.

### Aztecs (2026-10-31)

- **Flower Wars:** when a surviving Aztec melee unit helps kill an enemy empire's military unit, the nearest Aztec city gains 1 population, excluding barbarians and Scouts. Melee-only (designer, 2026-11-01): the helper must be `UnitClass::Melee` (Spearman through Hunter-Killer), so ranged, mounted and air units never earn it, though a melee unit's shared or defensive kill does. No prisoners are taken; the victim is simply destroyed (`rules/battle.rs::flower_wars`).
- **No horses:** the Aztecs cannot train Horsemen, Cataphracts, Knights, Cuirassiers or Cavalry; Early Tanks, Tanks and Autonomous Armour are allowed (an Aztec army has no Cavalry to upgrade, so its first armour is a newly built Early Tank); horse-only research is a stepping stone and its unavailable upgrade effects are hidden.
- **The New Fire:** offer 30 population in one rite for 2 VP kept for good, taking from the biggest cities first and leaving each city at least 1.

Flower Wars uses `rules/battle.rs::kill_participants`, shared with Spartan healing: positive damage against the victim, defensive kills, and damage to its city's tile for garrison overflow count; dead participants are removed first. Each qualifying Aztec owner gains once per victim, regardless of the number of its participants. The nearest city is measured from the victim's tile, ties use lower city ID, and an empire without cities gets nothing. Population immediately follows `cities::settle_territory`, with no Food cost or change to stored Food. Economic benefits belong to the owner, not a hired Company's origin. `Event::FlowerWars` is visible to the beneficiary's team and observers of the receiving city, and produces a population map popup.

`data::faction_unavailable_reason` supplies the no-horses reason for production and purchases; blocked horse units remain greyed in the city build list once their technology is known. The New Fire reuses `GoalRule::Swarm`, `Action::Swarm` and `goals::swarm_plan`, preserving the save/action IDs, refusal of a repeated rite, city-by-city loss preview and border release; the presentation is torches, never blood. No unique unit or building is added.

The AI restores the former Swarm timing: gain VP net of lost titles, take a winning opportunity with time to hold or bank a net gain after 75% of the game, and avoid shrinking beside an enemy army until the last 5%. Gene `aztecs_playbook` (default 0, §10) adds the designer's Aztec plan: frontier Walls first, the rite as the closer beside an army at a city it doesn't shrink, and a lean war army. Rivals value the smaller post-rite cities. Flower Wars values melee kills by the nearest city's Food growth cost, discounted by kill and survival chances; the `flower_wars_value` gene also values melee production near rivals and ranged units/Walls near Aztecs. Core availability filters keep horse units out of plans; Tanks are available normally.

`GameSetup::standard`, app co-op setup and online random seats exclude explicit picks; AI seats rotate through the remaining factions. App lobby Random seats remain seeded draws without replacement. There are still at most eight seats, with eleven factions available; simulation rosters and proxy schedules distinguish roster count from seat count. The seeded core AI setup keeps its original nine-faction pool until explicitly given Egypt or Rome, preserving existing pins.

Display names are Switzerland (`Switzerland`), Mongols (`Mongols`), Sparta (`Sparta`), Germany (`Germany`), India (`India`, with `Japan` as a save alias), Byzantium (`Byzantium`), Celts (`Celts`), Athens (`Athens`), Aztecs (`Aztecs`), Egypt (`Egypt`) and Rome (`Rome`). Parenthesized names are stable internal IDs in code and saves; faction-specific display names are listed in `docs/design/historical-factions/rename-map.md`.

| Faction | Strength | Drawback | Unique | Faction goal (2 VP except Egypt and Rome) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Switzerland (`Switzerland`) | +1 Gold per 2 population in each city ("tolls", `data::SWISS_TOLL_POPULATION`); +1 Gold a turn in interest per 100 Gold in the treasury, no maximum (`data::SWISS_INTEREST_THRESHOLD`); buys ordinary buildings and units at 175% instead of 200% (`economy.rs::buy_percent`, `buy_quote`) | 3 Gold per military unit beyond your first two, Scouts excepted; captured cities transfer a population share of treasury, doubled for capital (`data.rs::paid_military_upkeep`, `economy.rs::swiss_capture_bounty`) | Alpine Redoubt replaces Walls: 120 Production (Walls +20), +15 city strength; it must be built with Production and cannot be bought with Gold | **The Reserve (live):** hold ≥5,000 Gold (`goals::SWISS_HOARD_GOLD`), after upkeep; +2 VP only while held, lost the turn the treasury falls below it (`FactionGoalLost`), regained when it recovers (`FactionGoalCompleted { again }`). Capturing a Swiss city pays the captor a treasury share and can drop the owner below the reserve |
| India (`India`; the Rust identifier is India) | +1 Food/city; +7% Food and Production per other Indian city within 5 hexes; cities may be founded 3 hexes apart (`economy.rs::city_yields_with_research`, `data::min_city_gap`) | Beyond radius 4 from its capital, corruption removes 10% of all four city yields per extra hex, capped at 100%, with final Production at least 3 (`data.rs::india_distance_loss_percent`, `economy.rs::city_yields_with_research`). Non-capital cities remit no Gold to the treasury until they have a Revenue Office, then half gross Gold rounded down; turn resolution credits this net income (`economy.rs::player_income`, `rules/cities.rs::process_cities`) | Revenue Office replaces the Market at Currency, keeps its 60 Production cost and +3 Gold. The Rice Storehouse is unavailable until Japan returns | **The Imperial Diwan (held):** after every owned city has a Revenue Office, build the 300 Production Diwan in one city. Its city holder scores 2 VP; a capture transfers the points (`economy.rs::imperial_diwan_blocker`, `goals.rs::diwan_holder`) |
| Byzantium (`Byzantium`) | **Mercenary Companies (2026-10-22; only each seller's special unit since 2026-11-14):** it hires the special unit of met empires it is not at War with, as that empire's version, 100% of the Production cost in Gold paid to the seller, 30-turn contracts, upkeep 2, outside the levy cap and the strategic supply, capturing allowed; an unpaid Company turns barbarian (`company.rs`, §5). Bought peace and Hire an army are deleted. Researches normally | **Thin levies (2026-10-10):** at most one armed unit per city, plus one (`data::levy_cap`, `economy::levy`; Scouts, Settlers and the Heir excepted). Training or buying past the cap is blocked, "Levies full: 5 of 5" (`economy::production_check`, also at completion); losing a city never disbands units, it only blocks new ones until the empire is back under the cap; the count and cap are public (`PlayerPublic.levy`). The interim 120% building cost and its `byzantium-building-percent` experiment are deleted | The Pikeman is the Tagmatic Soldier, and it and every later melee-line unit get +4 design strength (era-scaled) and +10 Production (`data::unit_stats_for`). Byzantium builds an ordinary Library, with ordinary name and art (Scriptorium removed 2026-11-06) | **The Heir (held):** train the Heir (below) and hold him; whoever owns the unit scores the 2 VP, so a rival that captures him takes the points and retaking him returns them |
| Germany (`Germany`) | Ruhr Foundry +6 Production (Forge +3); strategic deposits supply twice as much; continuous Industry projects turn city Production into Gold (50%) or Science (25%), rounded down each turn | Each Production building in a German city costs 1 Food | Ruhr Foundry replaces Forge (Iron Working); Factory is ordinary (+5) | **Five Ruhr Foundries (live):** own five cities with a Ruhr Foundry; +2 VP only while the count stays at five or more |
| Celts (`Celts`) | **A sack carries off 60 Gold per population and raises a warband camp** (§4, "Capture"). **barbarians never attack the Celts, all game (designer, 2026-11-02, `RULES_VERSION` 11; §7, "Barbarians never attack the Celts")**. Celtic land no longer wounds intruders and no unit is hidden (both deleted 2026-10-23, user: "does not appear to be meaningful") | Celts can't keep cities they capture: they sack them, burning the city (Gold and a camp are the take, §4). Their only drawback now. | No unique unit; Celts build the ordinary Archer and Ranger line | **Raise the Warbands (live, a repeatable action):** `Action::CallTheWild`, once per turn, raises a barbarian camp only in each Celtic city lacking one (below). +2 VP only while every city owned has a camp in its territory (`goals::wild_camp_cities`; no minimum city count, a Celtic faction with no city never holds it); lost the turn a camp is cleared (`FactionGoalLost`), regained when every city has one again (`FactionGoalCompleted { again }`). The Celts get no Gold for clearing a camp their own action raised; a rival clearing one takes the usual Gold |
| Mongols (`Mongols`) | All military +1 move; a Market 8 or more hexes from the capital gives +2 Gold ("Mongols: long-road commerce", `economy.rs::city_yields_with_research`, `FactionRule::MongolsCommerce`); foreign borders at Peace use the ordinary rule (the Scout and civilian passage was cut 2026-11-06) | No terrain defense bonus for military units; no Walls, Castles or Star Forts and no Siege before the Medieval era | **Keshig** replaces the Cataphract, the Mongols' Classical mounted unit (user, 2026-10-17; moved out of the Ancient era the same day: "the Mongol horde was in the 1200s... premature to give it right at the start"): `data::KESHIG_KIND`, unlocked by Iron Working; the Cataphract's 33 melee (Classical melee line) and mounted moves (5 with the Mongol move) plus a ranged attack, 17 at range 2 (design 18 on the era curve; a Composite Archer has 22), for 164 Production (Cataphract 150; `data::KESHIG_COST`, `KESHIG_RANGED`, `KESHIG_RANGE`, `unit_stats_for`). In the Ancient era the Mongols build ordinary Horsemen, which upgrade into Keshigs. Each turn it charges or shoots, never both (§1); its shot fights as ranged (§4); only its charge takes a city. Upgrades to the ordinary Knight (no bow; `upgrade_cost_for` is floored at 0). Scouts are ordinary (the Outrider is gone). No new order type | **The Yam Network (held):** build the Yam Relay Station (below) on a chosen tile; whoever holds its tile has 2 VP and every empire crosses all of that owner's land at Peace |
| Sparta (`Sparta`) | All military start level 1, XP +50%, level 6 Spartan Veteran; infantry ignore wounds; military kills restore up to 100 HP shared by surviving attackers; captures heal all own military within 2 hexes | Every Settler and non-military build +50% except war buildings (Walls family, Castle, Agoge) (no other drawback; desertion was dropped 2026-11-07, RULES_VERSION 23) | **Spartan Hoplite phalanx (2026-10-17):** the Hoplite is the Soldier kind (Spearman) under a Spartan owner (`data::is_hoplite`) and gets `data::HOPLITE_PHALANX_STRENGTH` = +5 strength, attacking and defending, while another Hoplite of the same empire stands on an adjacent tile in the fight's stage snapshot (`rules::battle::has_phalanx_neighbour`; one neighbour is enough, a line does not stack; a rival's, a teammate's or a Phalangite does not count). It is its own breakdown line "Phalanx: next to another Hoplite" (`combat::add_phalanx`) in every Hoplite fight: melee, meeting, a city's shot at it, a garrison Hoplite's overflow share, and the forecast (`forecast.rs`, which counts only neighbours the viewer can see). The bonus is lost when the Hoplite upgrades (the upgraded unit is a Phalangite). Starting at level 1 is every Spartan unit's rule, not the Hoplite's; Agoge raises trained military by one promotion level; AI: gene `phalanx_formation` (`military.rs::phalanx_spot_among`) ranks campaign staging and assault spots next to a reserved or standing Hoplite first | **Honour the Veterans (permanent, three retirements):** `Action::RetireVeteran{unit}` retires a military unit (not a Scout) of promotion level ≥ `goals::SPARTA_RETIRE_LEVEL` = 3 (designer, 2026-11-06: level 4 was rarely reached; `actions::veteran_level`) standing in an own city with an Agoge (`actions::retire_blocker`). The unit is removed at once (a disband, not a kill: no healing for the others); `Player::veterans_retired` counts them (kept, partial progress stays); the third (`goals::SPARTA_RETIRE_COUNT`) completes the goal for good (+2 VP, `VeteranRetired` is team-only, `FactionGoalCompleted` public). `validate_actions` refuses retirements beyond the ones still owed. The view's `actions.retire` lists the Agoge cities and every unit at the retire level with its `blocker` (the action is offered only where it is None) |
| Athens (`Athens`) | Lyceum +6 Science (Library +3) and costs 70 (Library 80); 20 Science on every ruin, on top of its normal reward | Markets give +1 Gold instead of +3 (`data.rs::building_stats_for`); a rival that captures an Athenian city gains one free technology, once per city for that captor (`battle.rs::capture_city`); the broadcast's free techs for rivals are the goal's price | Lyceum replaces the Library (internal kind `Laboratory`, displayed "Library", `data::building_name`) | **Share the Learning (permanent, one action):** `Action::Broadcast` gives every living empire outside the broadcaster's team `Player::free_techs_pending` = 3 (or all unknown technologies if fewer than three remain across all eras), with `free_techs_from` (`rules/actions.rs::unknown_techs`). Picks left after finishing an era carry into the next. A receiver spends them with `Action::ChooseFreeTechs` (1 to pending unknown techs, each with its prerequisites known or listed before it; they arrive at resolution, with `TechResearched` and `FreeTechsChosen`). The prompt persists across turns; nothing auto-picks for anyone. +2 VP for good (`SignalBroadcast` is public) |
| Egypt (`Egypt`) | **The Nile (rules 48):** Food doubled in every city; the surplus Food above 2 per population builds whatever the city is building (units, Settlers, buildings, wonders, improvements), 1 progress per Food (`economy.rs::nile`) | No Production at all; a city that is building does not grow, so it must switch to the Grow project to grow; the Workshop line is unavailable (it would do nothing) | Medjay replaces Spearman, +1 sight (`visibility.rs::sight_sources`, `view.rs::unit_view`); Royal Granary replaces Granary, +3 Food total before doubling (`data.rs::building_stats_for_rules`) | None in increment 1 (`goals::GoalRule::None`, 0 VP) |

**Egypt: the Nile (designer, 2026-11-21; rules 48; `FactionRule::EgyptNile`).** It replaced Food-funded construction (below), which now applies only with `--rule-off egypt_nile` (`RuleExperiments::egypt_construction`), kept as the comparison arm. It all happens in the city's yields (`economy.rs::nile`, after Germany's projects and before decadence), so turn resolution, forecasts, turns-to-complete and the AI read it without special cases: the Food total is doubled (`egypt-nile-food`, percent, default 200), all Production is removed with its own breakdown line, and while anything but Grow is in production the surplus Food (total minus 2 per population, when positive) is removed from Food and added as Production, one for one (`egypt-nile-build`, percent, default 100, rounded down when below; the designer chose 1:1 on 2026-11-21: 17.5% of eight-seat Hard games against an even 12.5%, 85 gave 10.5%). A building city therefore ends each turn with exactly its upkeep Food: it neither grows nor starves, and its stored Food stays put; a deficit is never converted, so it still starves. `IndustryProject::Grow` (Egypt only, `data::available_to_faction_rules`) is a project like Germany's: it never completes, banks nothing, and with it (or with nothing in production) the doubled surplus grows the city as usual. Switching costs nothing: the item keeps its bank, and since the Food-to-progress trade is linear (bar rounding), alternating only splits the surplus. Gold buys as usual. The Workshop line (Workshop, Forge, Factory) is unavailable to Egypt, so Bronze Working gives it nothing and Egypt skips it (rules 49: known from the start, see "Skipped technologies" in §Faction-accurate unlocks); Industrialization still reveals Coal, which counts as a benefit (`data::tech_has_benefit` counts strategic resource reveals). Egypt's cards leave out the effect lines that only add Production (`CardEffect::Production`: Deep Quarry, +1 Production per Workshop family, Railways' Mines, Factories +2, Fusion's +5, Recursive AI's +20%). City panels and AI views derive each build's yields from the build-independent base (`yields_cache::CityCache::base`), so the Nile, decadence and Science conversion apply once. Growth claims tiles with the Growth focus (`cities::next_claim_among`). **Per-tile numbers are not Nile-adjusted (2026-11-22):** the Nile lives on city totals, so `economy::owner_tile_yields`, the view's `next_claim.yields` and `improvement_preview.gain` stay the plain tile yields (the AI reads them); the app applies the Nile on top for display (`app/src/resources.ts::nileYields`: double Food, no Production) so Egypt never sees Production on the map, tile card, claim preview or improvement gains. **Quarry is not offered (view only):** `view.rs::city_detail` leaves `Improvement::Quarry` out of an Egyptian city's `options` while `egypt_nile` is on (a Quarry only adds Production); the order itself stays legal (`improvements::blocker` has no Egypt rule) and `RULES_VERSION` is unchanged (test `improvements/tests.rs::egypt_is_not_offered_a_quarry_but_may_still_order_one`). Mines stay offered for strategic supply and Copper's Gold.

**Egypt construction (2026-11-15; rules 41; replaced by the Nile at rules 48, still reachable with `--rule-off egypt_nile`).** Only `ProductionItem::Building` uses Food: not units, Settlers, improvements, projects, or tile/world wonders. A city with an eligible building under construction diverts the positive part of its current turn's Food minus 2 per population, at two progress per Food. Stored Food never pays construction. Food reserves remain unchanged on a construction turn with nonnegative surplus; a deficit still drains reserves and may starve the city. The same surplus never grows population and constructs. Each building retains its own progress on a switch; starting one discards unassigned Production, and Production earned during construction is discarded. Food-funded overflow is discarded on completion. If the city's population falls below the building's minimum, construction pauses with its progress in place and ordinary growth resumes until the gate is met. The city uses one yield snapshot at phase 6 of `rules::resolve_turn`: purchases and combat have already resolved, newly completed buildings affect yields from the next turn. Captured cities obey ordinary resistance and occupation rules. Two players contest a world wonder under the ordinary post-combat completion order; Egypt's ordinary buildings never enter a shared world race. The owner view quotes Food-funded turns and Gold's refusal, while the city panel shows diverted Food, ignored Production and bank/overflow behavior. The Medjay sight applies whenever visibility is recomputed, including after simultaneous movement; it has no combat or supply override. Rival units entering the same newly visible tile follow ordinary simultaneous movement and combat rules, and no new tie breaker is introduced.
| Rome (`Rome`) | A full-health melee veteran at level 1 or higher founds a city by consuming the unit, with no Gold cost or price escalation | Cannot train or buy Settlers; captured Settlers work normally | Legionary replaces Phalangite: its founded city starts at population 2. Castrum replaces Walls: upgrading a melee unit in that city heals 50 HP once per order | **None in increment 1:** no faction goal or faction-goal VP |

The Workshop line includes Workshop, Forge and Factory; `CityView::has_workshop` preserves the last visible observation for reconstructing a city under fog.

**Goal actions in the UI (2026-11-01, merged from `astra/goal-action-visible`; no rule changed).** The goals that need an action (the Aztecs' New Fire, Japan's Sakoku, Athens' Share the Learning, the Celts' Raise the Warbands, Sparta's Honour the Veterans) keep their button in the sidebar all game instead of hiding inside a disclosure: `ui/FactionGoalProgress.tsx::SidebarFactionGoal` renders `GoalActionRow` whenever `view.actions` holds `swarm`, `sakoku`, `broadcast`, `wild` or `retire`, and a blocked action shows disabled with its reason (`ui/GoalActions.tsx`, key `G`; Sparta's row has "Retire this veteran", which selects the first veteran in place). The first turn an action is available it is also offered once as a pending decision of kind `goal` (Raise the Warbands only once its VP would bring the team within 1 VP of its target, `goalDecision.ts::WILD_OFFER_SLACK`, the Hard Celts' calling window; Share the Learning only once its VP would reach the target, `BROADCAST_OFFER_SLACK` 0, the Hard AI's `rite_pays` crossing; before that only the sidebar button offers them, since 2026-11-21: on turn 1 the dock told new Celtic and Athenian players to act at the worst time), "X is ready: ... for +N VP", ranked after sieges and before free technologies (`goalDecision.ts::readyGoalAction`, `turnFlow.ts::pendingDecisions,nextDecision`); opening the action or ending that turn marks it seen (`store.goalDecisionSeen`, `seenGoalsAfterTurn`), and from then on only the sidebar button remains. A broken order still comes before a newly ready goal.

Sparta city capture has no faction Gold payout; the Swiss capture bounty still applies to a Swiss city (`rules/battle.rs::capture_city`).

The scripted Sparta AI trains a larger field army with ranged support before committing
its two goal Walls after the first-third opening window, chooses a known nearby rival
on a projected war route and prefers unwalled cities in the opening. On Hard and Hard + Search (plain Hard since
2026-10-11, `sparta_search_only` = 0), it trains a modest assault
wing once a reachable unwalled rival city is known, opens a feasible Campaign even when
the short Search forecast favors Economy, credits capture healing in siege losses, and
continues to the next exposed city after a capture. Its wounded field units may recover at the normal 10/20 HP rate. These choices are AI policy only. Until 2026-10-11 plain Hard kept its earlier
thresholds and plan valuation; with them it never declared on a one-city Germany with five
Spearmen (war scenario Sparta's First Blood, 0/64 → 64/64 once plain Hard plays the policy). The tactical attack forecast still applies. The veteran objective starts at most one
war; an additional front needs the ordinary public-victory containment checks
(`crates/ai/src/diplomacy.rs::Planner::plan_diplomacy`). The military planner gives extra value to
captures that can heal nearby regiments and stages the opening assault after emergency
home defense but before routine garrisons; rival AI values Walls against this rush. Sparta uses the ordinary recovery policy (`tactics.rs::recover_wounded`) and still values kill and capture healing during attacks.
A wounded Spartan unit in safe land heals at the normal rate; Scouts use the shared exploration recovery pass (`crates/ai/src/exploration.rs`).
India's distance penalty is linear corruption: scoring and AI building valuation use the same capped linear rule (`crates/core/src/economy.rs::city_yields_with_research`,
`settlement.rs::analyze_sites`, `crates/ai/src/victory.rs::building_gain`).
Swiss AI keeps its hoard once held (`victory.rs::hoard_held`: the ordinary Gold reserve is the reserve itself, so it does not spend below it), and rivals value knocking it down (`hoard_denial_value`: capturing a Swiss city drops the treasury through the public bounty and takes the VP away). It reserves Gold for its treasury goal, including any projected deficit before the
same-turn award (`crates/ai/src/victory.rs::plan_victory`, `production.rs::plan_buy`). World wonders cannot be bought; Swiss routine purchases use the same legal item list as every empire.
While saving for the hoard (`victory.rs::saving_for_goal`: income would reach 5,000 within
the game's remaining turns, which is nearly always true for a Swiss with two cities and positive
income) the ordinary reserve is the whole 5,000, so routine buys and the frontline defender buy
nothing below it; door defense (`door.rs::door_gold_floor`) and forecast rescues still spend it.
The 2026-11-06 paired ablation found that the 2 VP goal reduces Swiss wins because its full
reserve keeps Gold out of routine purchases. Reducing that reserve broadly also loses interest
income and worsens Swiss wins; see `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/swiss-hoard-1106/README.md`
for the census, failed prototype genes, and human-seat gates. The rule remains unchanged.
Gene `swiss_hoard_any_war` (shared default 0, Swiss Hard builtin 1 since 2026-11-06) releases the unfinished Reserve for
ordinary purchases during an actual war even if the routed plan is Peace. A held Reserve and a
Reserve that would win the game stay protected; the deficit runway still applies. Its eight-Hard
AI-only paired Swiss wins rose 388 to 404 on seed 9311003 and 374 to 422 held out on 8241001;
the human-seat gate was noninferior for both seat and rivals.
Gene `hoard_at_war` (default 1 since 2026-11-06; added 2026-10-30, playtest 8.9.17) releases it while the routed plan
fights a war we are in, the hoard is not held and is not what wins: the ordinary reserve drops to the defence or deficit floor (`scripted.rs::hoard_spent_at_war`).
Gene `decisive_buy` (default 1 since 2026-11-03) releases the reserve for one purchase a turn with
decisive value, whatever the plan is called (`decisive_buy.rs::buy_decisive`, first in `plan_buy`): for each enemy city at war whose capture brings VP (an original capital, a live
goal, a Fight objective, a title), the siege forecast of the field force that can reach it within
`march_turns`, our Bombers striking in the volley (`opportunity.rs::forecast_siege_air`), runs without
and with the bought unit; the gain is the added capture odds times that VP, summed over targets, less
the VP the Gold puts at risk (a Swiss hoard still to bank arrives later by the income turns the purchase
costs, out of the turns left; a held hoard is never spent). It must reach `decisive_buy_min` (20 VP
percent), leave 100 Gold or the deficit reserve, and keep upkeep payable; a Bomber is bought in the
qualifying city whose range holds the most of the war (`strike_reach`). 1: Bombers only (they strike
from the next turn; not on a city whose owner has reached the Flight era); 2: land units too. At Peace
nothing qualifies, so routine buys keep the reserve.
The frontline defender (`production.rs::buy_frontline_defender`: visible enemy military within
`frontline_purchase_radius` of a city held by fewer than two units or outmatched) buys the best
strength-per-Gold unit above the reserve; when a threatened city can't yet afford one, the rest of
`plan_buy` keeps the reserve plus that city's cheapest military quote (`Frontline::Save`) instead
of stopping. A threatened city that already has this turn's purchase, or can buy no unit, holds
nothing back (before 2026-10-30 any threatened city ended every routine purchase that turn)
(`crates/ai/src/scripted.rs::ordinary_gold_reserve`, `production.rs::plan_buy`). Optional readiness purchases wait until the treasury goal is
banked; a forecast rescue remains available before it. India AI chooses linked sites
from the settlement forecast (`crates/ai/src/civilians.rs::choose_site`). Rival AI uses the public
city bounty for war and campaign targets, and nearby India-city links in campaign target value
(`crates/ai/src/diplomacy.rs`, `military.rs`).

**Borders at Peace (designer, 2026-11-06).** Mongol Scouts and civilians follow the ordinary closed-border rule. Every empire crosses its own team’s land, an enemy’s land at War, an ally’s land, and land owned by a Yam Relay Station holder. `GameState::passage_allowed` and `PlayerView::passage_allowed` answer this in core; `app/src/diplomacy.ts::canEnter` mirrors it. Units stranded when a border closes must leave by a legal route. The AI uses the same passability table for military units, Scouts and civilians (`crates/ai/src/analysis.rs::Ctx::move_cost`).
The scripted AI values distant frontier exploration, uses normal settlement scoring, raids exposed civilians, and avoids remote field battles (`crates/ai/src/civilians.rs::choose_frontier`, `military.rs::attack_targets`).
The existing +1 military move, no military terrain defense and distant-Market Gold
retain their preceding values (the Wayfinder/Outrider Scout was replaced by the Keshig, 2026-10-17)
(`crates/core/src/data.rs:unit_stats_for`, `economy.rs:city_yields_with_research`;
`app/src/data.ts:unitStatsFor`). The two new default
movement flags are exported through `crates/ai/tests/ui_rules.rs` to
`app/src/rules.generated.json`, with values defined in `crates/core/src/data.rs`.

**Germany rework (2026-11-05 decision; Industry Food updated 2026-11-15, rules 43).** `BuildingKind::MachineShop` is Germany's Ruhr Foundry. It replaces the Forge at Iron Working, costs the same 320 Production, needs a Workshop, population 4 and one Iron, and yields +6 Production instead of a Forge's +3. Each Production building's own stat line costs a German city 1 Food, including Workshop, Ruhr Foundry, Factory and Pyramids. Upgraded tiers retain earlier tiers, so Workshop plus Foundry costs 2 Food; the Foundry no longer has its own Food stat. The faction-wide drawback has its own `FactionRule` switch and follows captured cities while German. `BuildingKind::Autoworks` is an ordinary +5 Production Factory for Germany again; it keeps the Foundry's Iron need and adds Coal. Every German strategic deposit supplies double the ordinary amount (4, or 6 improved; `resources::deposit_supply`), with its own switch. German units have ordinary movement. The 10% first-building-of-each-kind discount was removed after its 2026-11-05 ablation measured −0.38 pp [−1.34, +0.57]. Industry projects remain. The Foundry is the building counted by the live Five Ruhr Foundries goal (`goals::factory_cities`).

An opening AI may plan a direct attack under a same-turn declaration, but a confirmed multi-step route stops at the last tile passable under the current border state (`crates/ai/src/openings.rs:OpeningBrain::orders`; `crates/core/src/rules/units.rs:check_known_path`).

Bundle D originally set Germany's and Mongols's goals to 3 VP. The Germany redesign tunes its goal back to 2 VP; since 2026-10-06 every faction's goal is worth exactly 2 VP (`data::FACTION_GOAL_VP`; `goals.rs::spec_for`). The old +5 Sparta Settler surcharge was removed in the iron-price redesign. The Swiss and India redesigns above are the default rules.
The Celts redesign is a default rule too. The Rust AI attacks cities as the Celts like any faction
(the early returns for Celts in `military.rs::targets,campaign_target_for,campaign,open_front,
seize_open_city,rush_siege,occupy_rush_city`, `city_assault.rs::coordinate_city_assaults`,
`plan.rs::campaign_candidates,contain_war`, `campaign.rs`, `victory.rs` and `threat.rs` were deleted on
2026-10-23) and prices every capture as the sack it is (§10, "The Celtic sack, priced");
Goal conditions live in `crates/core/src/goals.rs::reached`.
Celts's damage and HP floor are in `data.rs` and applied by `rules/mod.rs::resolve_turn`; attack forecasts include that damage in `forecast.rs::forecast_attack_mode`. `rules/battle.rs::capture_city` sacks every Celts conquest and pays the per-population Gold described in §4. Celts's old hill-yield, Grassland, and building-price rules are absent from `data.rs::terrain_yields,building_stats_for` and `experiments.rs::RuleExperiments`; site scoring no longer has a Celts hill preference (`settlement.rs::analyze_sites`, `crates/ai/src/civilians.rs::choose_site`).

**Faction-accurate unlocks (2026-10-04).** One shared helper decides what an item or tech gives a
faction: `data::available_to_faction(faction, item)` (buildable and does something under the
faction's rules: projects are Germany-only; a building counts if it has yields, city strength, is the
War Shrine or a world wonder; `data::faction_excluded_building` keeps the War Shrine from everyone else),
`data::tech_card_effects(tech)` and `data::tech_has_benefit(rules, faction, tech)`.
`research::tech_unlocks` lists only available items, under faction names. The generated
`app/src/rules.generated.json` carries `techCardEffects` (per tech since 2026-10-10: the Byzantium's
Science exclusions are gone) and `techBenefit` per faction and `factionNames`; TS mirrors are
`availableToFaction`, `techCardEffects`, `techHasBenefit`, `factionItemName` in `app/src/data.ts`.
With no prerequisites, every technology a faction researches is a gate step: a card with no item for a faction still lists its immediate effects, and the era's last technology is always worth researching. A tech with nothing at all for the faction is skipped (next paragraph), not shown as a card.

**Skipped technologies (rules 49, user 2026-11-22).** A technology with no benefit for a faction (`data::tech_has_benefit` false) is skipped: `game::try_new_game` puts every `data::skipped_techs(rules, faction)` into the player's known techs, with no event, so the era gate needs one fewer and no card ever says "No benefit". Skipped techs give no diffusion discount (`economy::tech_known_by` leaves out a faction that skipped the tech), `ResearchView.skipped` lists them, `era_total` and `era_known` exclude them, and the research screens, era-up card and free-tech picker leave them out (`app/src/ui/eraResearch.ts::researchCatalog`). Today the only skip is Egypt's Bronze Working; `crates/core/tests/factions.rs::no_faction_ever_researches_a_tech_that_gives_it_nothing` pins that list and requires every other faction × tech pair to give something in its own era (`data::tech_has_player_benefit`). The "No benefit for X" stepping-stone card is gone from the app.

**Byzantium goal: the Heir (2026-10-06 decision, replaces the 2026-10-03 cumulative-Science goal).**
The cumulative salvage-Science goal is gone, and since 2026-10-10 so is all salvage Science, squatting and the Salvage Works (Byzantium researches normally and hires Mercenary Companies; see the Byzantium row above and §5).

**Sakoku (reserved for the redesigned Japan; no playable faction uses it).** `Action::Sakoku{close:true}` sets `Player::sakoku_turns = Some(0)` and emits public `SakokuClosed`; `close:false` clears it and emits `SakokuReopened{turns}`. It is refused for any other goal rule, when already in that state, while the team is in an Alliance, and while Japan holds a captured Courier Hall. Closing cannot ride in the same order list with a war declaration, an Alliance proposal or acceptance; a teammate cannot sign an Alliance on the turn Japan closes. While closed, Japan cannot declare war, its team cannot propose or accept Alliances, it receives no tech diffusion, and no foreign unit may enter its land at Peace. Wars others declare are fought normally, Japan may still make Peace, and its permanent teammate's other diplomacy is untouched. The closed borders also block the open road. **Score:** `Player::sakoku_turns` counts turn ends closed (0 on the vow turn); after 30 turn ends (`goals::SAKOKU_TURNS`), Japan holds +2 VP. Enemy military units in Japanese land have no effect on the vow. Reopening loses the VP and resets the count. `FactionGoalCompleted` and `FactionGoalLost` reflect scoring and reopening; `PlayerPublic::sakoku_turns` and the goal status are public, while `ActionsView.sakoku` gives Japan its own close/reopen state and blocker. Old game saves remain compatible because only derived view data and rule evaluation changed; the saved `sakoku_turns` field is unchanged. **AI** (`crates/ai/src/goal_sakoku.rs`): Japan prices the 30-turn closure against lost tech diffusion, wars it cannot start and nearby military danger; `vow_hold` remains off by default and still accepts Peace and keeps city defenders when the vow is near scoring or closes the VP race. Intruder hunting and `vow_denial_percent` are removed. UI keeps the public countdown, closed-country border and close/reopen report lines; no unit rings or intruder warnings remain.

**The Heir (HELD goal, 2 VP).** `UnitKind::Heir` is a Byzantium-only civilian (`data::available_to_faction`, "Only the Byzantium can train an Heir"): `data::HEIR_COST` 800 Production (calibrated 2026-10-07 at 400 half-points), 2 moves, no strength, no tech, available from turn 1. He cannot be bought with Gold (`economy::is_unbuyable` makes `buy_cost_for` None, so `buy_check` reports `NotBuyable`, "Heir cannot be bought"). At most one exists per Byzantium: `economy::heir_check` blocks training while any Heir of this Byzantium exists anywhere, held by anyone ("The Heir still lives; disband him to train another" when the Byzantium holds him, "The Heir lives, held by another; retake him instead" otherwise), and while another of its cities builds or queues one ("An Heir is already being trained in <city>"); `rules/cities.rs::complete_item` refuses a second completion, stamps `Unit::heir_of = Some(owner)` (public as `UnitView::heir_of`) and emits the public **`Event::HeirBorn { city, owner, city_name, at }` to every empire** (user, 2026-10-22: "a clearer announcement that the heir has been born and where the heir is so people can try to snatch it"). **His position is public from then on:** `view::player_view` lists every Heir in `PlayerView.units` whether or not the viewer sees the tile (fog and unexplored included), current each turn, so the AI planners and the map see him wherever he is. Identity is the unit's `heir_of`, not its owner: a civilian is captured in place (owner changes, id and `heir_of` stay: `rules/movement.rs` "Hostile civilians left under an arriving military unit are captured"; a captured city's civilians too), so the captor owns the Heir and `goals::holders` returns `(byzantium, owner)`. Owned by barbarians (they capture lone civilians on raids and walk the captive to their nearest camp), nobody scores; clearing the camp or catching him on the way captures him for the clearer (existing captive rules). The holder may `DisbandUnit` him: the VP is gone and the Byzantium may train another once no Heir of it exists. `scoring::check_held_goal` keeps `Player::goal_holder` and emits `FactionGoalLost { player: old holder }` then `FactionGoalCompleted { player: new holder, faction: Byzantium, again }` on every change of hands; the Byzantium's own `faction_goal_done` means it holds him itself, and every holder's `vp_sources` carries `FactionGoal { of: byzantium }`. The viewer's own `faction_goal` (`goals::view`) stays present for a Held goal: value 1 if the Byzantium itself holds him, target 1, label "The Heir is not trained" / "is yours" / "Barbarians hold the Heir" / "A rival holds the Heir". AI (`crates/ai/src/goal_forge_heir.rs`): the Byzantium trains him once it has three cities, a garrisoned city, time to finish and no threat (`heir_value`), in one city only per planning pass (`production.rs::Tally::heir_pipeline` counts Heirs held, building or already picked this turn, so three cities that saw the same pre-turn availability never all queue him), and never a faction-locked item (`available_to_faction` is re-checked in `choose_item`), walls and a garrison are priced up in the city that shelters him (`production.rs` Walls and military arms via `heir_unguarded`), and `protect_heirs` moves every Heir it holds (its own, or a captured one) into the safest own city (threat, then walls, then garrison) and never anywhere else. Rivals value him: `Target::heir` in `military.rs::attack_targets` strikes a visible rival-held Heir first and on half the usual margin; a city sheltering one adds `heir_capture_value` to its denial value (`victory.rs::hoard_denial_value`); and `diplomacy.rs` adds `held_goal_denial` (public `vp_sources`) to the value of a war against the team that holds a Byzantium's goal.

**Five Ruhr Foundries (LIVE goal, 2 VP; Germany; updated 2026-11-05).** Germany scores while it owns at least five cities with a Ruhr Foundry (`BuildingKind::MachineShop`), including cities in resistance. A city without one does not interrupt the goal. Losing a Foundry city by capture or raze removes the VP when the count drops below five; building or capturing another Foundry restores it (`goals::reached,factory_cities`, `scoring::check_faction_goal`). Progress is the number of owned Foundries out of five (`goals::progress`). Hard prices the faction-wide Food loss on every Production building; goal valuation adds a share of the held 2 VP to needed Foundries, Workshop prerequisites, Iron supply and expansion before five cities (`goal_forge_heir.rs`, `production.rs`). Rival capture denial prices the live VP at risk.

**The Open Road (HELD goal, 2 VP; Mongols).** *Naming and tile update (2026-10-24 and 2026-10-29; the rest of this paragraph predates it and says Hall and city): the player-facing name is the Yam Relay Station (`data::building_name`), and it is built as a `ProductionItem::Wonder` on a chosen owned land tile within three hexes of its city (§3, "Wonder tiles and held VP"; `landmarks::site`). The holder is the owner of that tile (`goals::hall_holder`, falling back to the city's owner), so a border change can move the points without a capture; a released, unclaimed tile must be reclaimed before another is built (`economy::courier_hall_blocker`). The internal kind is still `CourierHall`.* `BuildingKind::CourierHall` is a Mongols-only building (`data::faction_excluded_building`, "Only Mongols can build the Courier Hall"): `data::COURIER_HALL_COST` 600 Production (kept at calibration, 2026-10-07), no yields, no tech (from turn 1), buyable with Gold like any building, its price its own (no Byzantium surcharge). **One per Mongols empire:** `Player::hall_city` records the city it was built in; `goals::hall_city(of)` is that city while it still stands with the Hall in it, so the Hall is destroyed only when the city is (a Celtic sack destroys the city) and only then may the empire rebuild it. While it stands, even in a rival's captured city, `economy::courier_hall_blocker` refuses another ("The Courier Hall already stands in X" / "...stands in X; retake the city instead"), and so does another city building or queuing one ("...is already being built in X"). A captured city keeps its buildings, so a captor simply owns the Hall. **Held:** whoever owns the Hall's city (`goals::hall_holder`) scores the 2 VP (`goals::holders`, `scoring::check_held_goal`, the same `Player::goal_holder` bookkeeping and `FactionGoalLost` then `FactionGoalCompleted { again }` events as the Heir); the Mongols's own `faction_goal_done` means it holds it itself. The viewer's own `faction_goal` (`goals::view`) label is "The Courier Hall is not built" / "stands in X" / "A rival holds the Courier Hall in X". **Roads laid by the Hall (`crates/core/src/roads.rs`):** the first Hall to stand makes `roads::extend` (end of every resolution, after borders) root the network at its city (`Map::road_hub`) and join every city on the map, enemy land included, announcing `Event::RoadsLaid { owner, city, at, linked }` (public; the celebration is the Hall's legendary card, the report line for everyone). Layout (deterministic, integer): cities join one at a time, nearest the Hall first, each by a Dijkstra search over land to the road tile where its new road plus the trip along the existing network to the Hall is cheapest (new tile 64 x terrain cost, network step 48, about 1 per 0.4 tile off the straight line city-to-Hall), so branches share trunks, roads run straight, use passes (Mountains and water are impassable), and never run parallel to a trunk. **Later cities** (founded or captured, any empire) are joined the next resolution while any Hall stands; a second Mongols's Hall city is joined like any city. **Roads persist forever:** a captured or sacked Hall, or a sacked city, removes none (roads are land, not a city's property); with no Hall standing nothing new is laid, and a rebuilt Hall adds links for cities still without one. An unreachable island city stays unlinked. **The open road:** `GameState::passage_allowed` lets every empire's units of every kind, military included, stand in all land owned by a Hall's holder at Peace (`goals::holds_open_road`); a surprise war still takes effect the same turn, so exposure is the Hall's cost. Units standing in land that closes when the Hall changes hands are stranded under the usual closed-land rule. `PlayerView.courier_halls` (public `{of, holder, city}`) lets the UI draw the landmark and the view's passage table agree with the engine. AI (`crates/ai/src/goal_hall.rs`): the Mongols wants the Hall (one `HallPlan` per turn) once it has three cities, expansion is done (45% of the game or no room left), nobody holds a Hall of its own (a captor's counts: it retakes, never rebuilds), and the 2 VP pay by the same `rite_pays` test as the Swarm and Broadcast; the site is the safest city (walled, garrisoned, capital preferred, far from rivals) and it starts only with the garrison ready and no larger neighbouring army; it buys with Gold under the usual reserve. Whoever holds a Hall city (a captor too) guards it first (Soldier priority, Walls, 150% of the visible threat). Rivals fold `hall_in_city_value` (2 VP x 100; the Mongols retaking its own x250; Celts half, since its capture razes the Hall) into `hoard_denial_value`, so conquest and campaign targets see it, and `held_goal_denial` treats a holder as a war target. Staging at Peace needed no new code: the AI reads passage from the view's entry table, which includes the Halls, so armies march to tiles inside the holder's land beside the Hall city and declare war the same turn (tests in `goal_hall_tests.rs`). Placeholders to calibrate: the 2 VP lead, the 60% late cutoff, the 1500 build value, the 250 retake value, the 2-unit garrison. The old "3 Markets 8+ hexes apart" goal and the settler-site "spaced network" score (`settlement.rs`) are gone.


## 9. Systems that do NOT exist (verified absent by code search, re-verified 2026-11-02)

| Civ-6-style system | Status |
|---|---|
| Trade routes / caravans | **Absent** — no match in `crates/` |
| Districts | **Absent** |
| Builders / worker units | **Absent** — improvements are built by cities as production items (§3, Tile improvements) |
| Religion | **Absent** |
| Culture / tourism | **Absent** |
| Great People | **Absent** (world wonders exist, unrelated) |
| Espionage / spies | **Absent** |
| Naval units | **Absent** — Guide: "There is no navy yet" |
| Aircraft on the map / airbases as tiles | **Absent** — aircraft live in a city's hangar (Eras and technologies, "The air layer"); no airfields, carriers, air movement or air superiority over open ground |
| Mounted/ranged/melee class bonus beyond the triangle | **Absent** — only +40% of base strength (`combat::CLASS_BONUS_PERCENT`) melee line vs mounted and mounted vs ranged (in melee only: none when the mounted unit is shot at, designer 2026-11-10), none from the Modern era (`combat::triangle_applies`), and the mounted −33% charging a city; no ranged-vs-melee bonus (the cavalry experiment's +27 backfired). A Keshig's shot fights as ranged (`combat::fight_class`), so it gets no ranged-vs-melee bonus either |
| Loyalty | **Absent** |
| Pillaging | **Absent** — code: "this game has no pillage" |
| Road-laying units / tile-by-tile road building | **Absent** — roads come only from the Yam Relay Station (the Courier Hall, §8) and the Roadworks building (`roads::extend_roadworks`) |
| Military Engineer, Forts | **Removed 2026-10-22** — Siege is a Gold upgrade on an ordinary unit (§4); units laying roads or building Forts are roadmap only |
| City-states | **Absent** |
| Tech trading | **Absent** — only passive diffusion (−10% per other living empire that knows it, max −50%), which a closed Japan (Sakoku, §8) does not get |
| Tech boosts (Eurekas) | **Removed 2026-10-17** — a technology's price depends only on the table and diffusion |
| Endless / repeatable technologies | **Absent** — after the 40th technology Science converts to Gold, Food or Production at 50% (Eras and technologies) |
| Relocating/moving a city | **Absent** |
| Influence (or any culture-like currency) | **Removed 2026-10-03** — borders grow per city (§3); treaties cost Gold (§5) |
| Pacts, notice periods, hidden trust, call commitments | **Removed 2026-10-03** — Peace, Alliance or War; betrayal is one visible record (§5) |
| Alliance signing fee, free shared wars | **Removed** — Alliances cost Gold per turn only (2026-10-31); allies defend at once and answer an ally's offensive war by choice (calls to arms, 2026-10-23, §5) |
| Bought peace / tribute | **Removed 2026-10-22** — Byzantium hires Mercenary Companies instead (§5) |
| Game speeds (Quick) and the opening price ramp | **Removed** (2026-10-10 and 2026-10-22) — one pace, listed prices (§3 Default pacing) |
| Celtic homeland damage and hidden hunters | **Removed 2026-10-23** — every unit on a seen tile is seen (§7) |
| Multi-step/attack-then-move orders | **Absent** — one order/unit/turn; a move onto an enemy fuses into one attack and ends the turn. The Keshig picks a shot or a charge each turn; no shoot-and-move, no hit-and-run |
| Promotion choice/trees | **Absent** — flat automatic bonus only |
| Upgrade choices | **One only** (2026-11-14): a Biplane upgrades to a Fighter or a Bomber, the player's choice; every other unit has one upgrade line |
| Unit-specific Fortify bonuses | **Absent** — every military unit fortifies alike: +`FORTIFY_PER_TURN` 3 defence per full turn a military unit does not act, up to `FORTIFY_MAX` 6 (reset to 0 when it moves, attacks or stands where passage is closed to it, `GameState::unit_in_open_land`), a flat breakdown line "Fortified" not scaled by era (`rules/units.rs::heal_and_fortify`, `combat::unit_defense_strength_for_rules`). Trench Infantry has no bonus of its own (designer, 2026-11-14, final) |
| Sight from an aircraft away from its base | **Absent** — every aircraft sees 4 tiles around its city (`add_air_sight`) and a Recon shows 2 tiles around a tile for one turn (`add_recon_sight`), but there is no sight along a flight, no patrol and no automatic reconnaissance |
| Manual tile-worker assignment | **Absent** — a city works every tile it owns within 3 hexes (§3) |
| Luxury resources / amenities | **Absent** — luxuries are planned as flavour tiles only, like natural wonders (user 2026-10-22); no empire-wide rule |
| Resource trade or stockpile | **Absent** — supply only (§3); resource-specific objectives were rejected (user 2026-10-22) |
| A conquest requirement for winning by VP | **Absent**: no rule demands a captured city for the ten-turn VP hold (§6). A separate win by capitals exists: the only team still holding an original capital of its own wins (rules 37, §6) |
| A turn-limit winner backstop | **Absent by default** — no turn limit; the 1,200-turn simulation safety cap names no winner (§6) |
| Decadence | **Off by default** — only the opt-in `decadence-slope` experiment turns it on (§6) |
| A playable Japan | **Absent** — Sakoku is reserved and `Japan` is only a save alias of India (§8) |

## 10. AI architecture

**Hard threatened-city production (AI only, 2026-11-20; `crates/ai/src/production.rs::ranged_relief_needed`, `item_value`, `plan_emergency_production`; gene `production_threat_agreement` default 1).** When a visible enemy can bombard a city without a friendly ranged reply, or hostile military is within three hexes of a garrisoned city with no shooter nearby, the routine production score values a trainable ranged defender above a routine melee quota. A garrisoned or walled city with threat exposure also values that defender when a visible rival army is within four hexes, including before war.
