The emergency pass uses the same read, but leaves a shooter-only production choice to routine scoring; it still interrupts for a local defence deficit or exposed capture. The ranged value is applied after general production and modernization weights so those weights cannot recreate the pass disagreement, even when its ordinary quota score is zero. Outside an opening, threat exposure plus a visible rival within four hexes also stops routine scoring from resuming a Settler that the threat pass interrupts. Gene 0 restores the earlier behavior. This changes AI choice only; order legality, combat and the rules version are unchanged.

**Hard siege survival clock (AI only, 2026-11-21; `purchase_defense.rs::city_survival_clock`, gene `siege_survival_clock`, default 0 since 2026-11-21 after the held-out Athens and Rome hybrid cells scored 8.6 with it on against 25.6 off; 1 turns it on).** Hard forecasts up to eight turns of attacks on each threatened own city from units in its player view. Each round uses the engine combat forecast against current city HP, Walls and garrison; the projection carries wounded attackers, melee retaliation, the walled city strike, rest healing and visible attackers advancing into range. `DefenseRisk.turns_to_fall` is the first projected capture turn, counted from the current order. If the visible force breaches the HP shield but cannot finish the garrison, its breach turn plus two garrison rounds supplies a provisional production deadline. Hold, rescue, door production, emergency production, purchases, upgrade holds and the Walls deadline read this forecast. An immediate purchase gets rescue priority only when it prevents this turn's fall; otherwise a delay still faces the normal Gold and upgrade value checks. A unit or Walls option that finishes on or after the deadline cannot rescue that city; among timely unit builds, the score credits the turns left for it to defend. A city with no forecast capture within the window retains the previous broader front valuation. The old optional `siege_matchup` pass and gene were removed; its separate six-turn approximation did not share the live hold forecast. Gene 0 (the default) keeps the former one-turn danger read and build timing; gene 1 turns the clock on for paired measurement. The clock is conditional on visible attackers continuing their assault; it does not reveal units in fog or know future orders. On the Sparta 8.9.57 T29 Gergovia state, two reinforcements are still unseen by the Celts, so the legal clock does not predict the T32 capture until they enter view. This changes AI decisions only, not combat, order legality or `RULES_VERSION`.

**Egypt on the Nile, AI (2026-11-21).** All difficulties (`production.rs::egypt_nile_choice`, applied to every `choose_item` result): Settlers, the Heir and combat units pass through; an idle city chooses Grow; otherwise the city chooses Grow when the Food still missing from its growth threshold is repaid by the extra surplus of one more population within `egypt_grow_payback` turns (gene, default 30, capped at a third of the turns left), the extra surplus estimated as the city's doubled Food per worked tile less 2. A city on Grow is reconsidered every turn like any project, so it returns to building once growth stops paying. The increment-1 Hard gene below applies only to the old rule.

**Egypt Hard, increment 1 (2026-11-15).** The `egypt_food_buildings` gene has shared default 0 and Egyptian Hard builtin 1 (`crates/ai/src/params.rs`). Its production picker reads only its own `PlayerView`: building option turns come from current surplus Food rather than Production, a zero-surplus building is rejected or parked so growth can resume, and after the Royal Granary a city below population three trains or expands to resume growth before more optional buildings. Units, improvements and wonders retain their normal Production valuation. The first increment does not yet model deeper multi-city growth opportunity cost or the planned faction goal.

**Campaign supply cap (AI only, 2026-11-15; `crates/ai/src/plan.rs::price_campaign`, gene `supply_race_cap` default 1 for Hard).** A campaign still values a captured deposit using `supply_term` and `supply_shortage_percent`, but the separate supply term cannot exceed the candidate's largest positive capture, victory-race, or denial term. The comparison includes the capture's win-chance change, VP hold closeout, elimination path, coalition city prize, rival objective denial, and breaking a hold; it does not use an unrelated soft-target or war-footing bonus as the ceiling. With no positive race or denial term, supply contributes zero. Setting the gene to 0 restores the uncapped term. The public economy projection, resource supply, city capture rules, and save shape are unchanged.

**Hard named Gold fund (2026-11-15, AI only; `named_fund` default 0 for Hard after the purchase gate failed; 1 turns it on).**
`gold_hold.rs::named_fund` chooses one use from the Hard AI's own `PlayerView` and routed plan:
first a visible at-war army within eight tiles of an own city with fewer than two nearby troops
(two defenders that survive a visible attack, ranked by the purchase scorer when enabled,
released at three tiles),
then the whole next-unit-tech upgrade bill for combat units when research is due within ten turns
(released when known), then a Campaign or Contain army purchase for a known target (the priced
`decisive_buy` candidate when one changes capture odds, released once a field unit reaches
staging range). The one-city
purchase-slot rule still applies: a defensive burst buys in the exposed city and nearby cities
where slots and Gold allow; it can cancel queued Siege refits to free the Iron and Gold for them.
The burst respects the immediate upkeep deficit and a live faction goal floor. Routine Gold buys
and single ground refits wait while the fund is
named. Its quote, not 25 turns of net income, is the hold ceiling; the older income cap still
applies when no fund qualifies. With `hard_purchase_scorer` 0, the affected troop-buy passes put
the newest affordable unit era ahead of strength and price, subject to the generation floor below; with the scorer on, its forecast value
chooses the fund's unit and each purchase (`gold_hold.rs`, `hard_defense.rs::second_troop_buy`,
`production.rs` purchase passes, `modernization.rs::plan_modernization`). This changes no core
combat, save shape or rules version.

**Campaign supply cap (AI only, 2026-11-15; `crates/ai/src/plan.rs::price_campaign`, gene `supply_race_cap` default 1 for Hard).** A campaign still values a captured deposit using `supply_term` and `supply_shortage_percent`, but the separate supply term cannot exceed the candidate's largest positive capture, victory-race, or denial term. The comparison includes the capture's win-chance change, VP hold closeout, elimination path, coalition city prize, rival objective denial, and breaking a hold; it does not use an unrelated soft-target or war-footing bonus as the ceiling. With no positive race or denial term, supply contributes zero. Setting the gene to 0 restores the uncapped term. The public economy projection, resource supply, city capture rules, and save shape are unchanged.

**Hard supported defence (AI only, 2026-11-15; `crates/ai/src/hard_defense.rs::supported_defence_orders`, gene `supported_defence` default 1, 0 restores the former orders).** After ordinary movement reconciliation, Hard checks visible enemy melee units that can reach an own city within two turns, including a rival that could legally declare War. Before a legal declaration, it stages a garrison and nearby melee reserves when at least two of its own non-Scout melee troops are available; a sole escort is left on its existing assignment. During an ongoing war it changes orders only when at least three visible melee attackers can strike immediately and the engine can forecast their pooled assault. In a multi-city empire, this pass preserves field assignments under a routed Campaign or Contain plan, including staging before another rival declares; a last-city Campaign or Contain yields to a visible non-Scout melee attacker, but not to Scouts alone. It leaves existing ranged garrisons to their fire orders. During that imminent assault, a melee garrison ordered out stays unless an adjacent defender has better HP per worst average hit against the visible attackers and can exchange places. Hard prices up to two reachable non-ranged supporters with the core's shared-damage and pooled-counterattack forecast, charging their expected wounds and the damage they would take outside. Ranged units remain available to fire from their existing tile, and enter an empty city only when no melee defender can reach it. This changes AI orders only; supported-defence resolution remains §4 and `RULES_VERSION` stays 40.

**Capitals victory in Hard (AI only, 2026-11-15, rules 37; `crates/ai/src/capitals.rs`; gene `capitals` default 1, 0 switches the guard and target terms off for ablation and old-behaviour tests; constant `winchance::CAPITAL_STAKE`).** Reads only public `PlayerPublic::capital_stands` and the fogged view. (1) Win chance: `TeamFacts::capital` per team; every team still holding a capital shares `CAPITAL_STAKE` 4,000 milli-logits (500 with eight holders, 2,000 with two) and the chance is 10,000 when we are the only holder and 0 when a rival is; `plan.rs` projects a capture of a standing rival capital (drops its flag) or of our team's fallen capital (restores ours) through `project_capital_capture`. (2) Own capital: `capital_pressed` is true while our standing original capital has a known rival military unit (not a Scout, not barbarian; seen at war or under a legal declaration, or remembered) within `CAPITAL_REACH` 6 hexes that can walk there. Then `secure_capital` (first pass in `plan_military`) keeps one troop in it (a shooter first) out of every other pass or calls the nearest troop that can defend it (never a city's only garrison); `garrison_cities` no longer skips it as sheltered or spared; `hold_capital_guard` holds the last troop whatever `capital_guard` says (the gene `capital_guard` itself stays 0); `production.rs` values a missing garrison and Walls there at 4,000, above the garrison floor (2,200) and every other build. (3) Rival capitals: `capital_race_vp` adds, on top of `capital_capture_vp`'s +1, a bonus to campaign, conquest, opportunity and decisive-buy target values (100 per point): 4, 2, 2, 1... as fewer rivals still hold one, 10 for the last standing rival capital while ours stands (the win), 0 without a capital of our own, 10 to retake our team's fallen capital (2 when another member's still stands), 5 to restore a third team's capital when we have none and exactly two teams hold one. `capital_restore_vp` adds the same scale to `rival_capital_vp` in `raze_value`/production's raze test (razing keeps the founder from retaking). Sim: `Outcome::Capitals` / `VictoryType::Capitals` (`stats.rs::winner_holds_last_capital`), and `human-proxy-report.py` counts `capitals`. Not done: defending an ally's or another rival's standing capital (only denial by retaking a fallen third-team capital and by taking the leader's), and `city_lost` does not price losing our own capital.

**Hard military purchases (2026-11-15, gene `hard_purchase_scorer`, default 1).** Every military Gold-buy path uses `production.rs::military_purchase_score`: door, readiness, emergency, frontline, second Settler escort, surplus army, campaign reinforcement and the threat pass. The price includes the researched refit chain a newly bought old unit would still need. Combat value uses visible rival units, the engine's forecast for a legal attack from the purchase city, the combat damage and class-bonus functions for other prospective fights, endurance through two exchanges, and movement. A unit that would die without changing the outcome is therefore a poor bargain even when its listed Gold price is low. The door and second Settler escort passes also wait up to five income turns for a trainable option scoring at least twice as well per Gold when the city survives this turn (`military_purchase_viable`). A city that would fall this turn can still buy the old unit if `defense_risk` says that purchase saves it. A healthy city with strong intrinsic defence can also buy an old garrison against a visible door front arriving next turn, since the city shields that unit through the first exchange. When the current research completes within three forecast turns and upgrades an owned combat unit, optional military buys wait if the city's no-purchase forecast survives; rescue buys that prevent a fall do not wait (`production.rs::military_upgrade_hold`). Gene 0 restores the prior scorer and hold behavior, with the separate generation floor still applying if enabled. The AI reads only its own `PlayerView`; this changes no combat rule, order legality or `RULES_VERSION`.

**Hard purchase generation floor (2026-11-15, AI only; gene `purchase_generation_floor` default 1 for Hard).** Beneath the shared purchase scorer, each military Gold-buy pass can buy only the newest researched unit in the needed melee, ranged or mounted upgrade line that its city can train. If that unit is blocked by strategic supply, only its immediate predecessor on the same line is eligible. If the eligible unit costs more Gold than available, the order is dropped and Gold stays in the treasury. An empty city forecast to fall this turn may instead buy the cheapest affordable unit whose added body prevents the fall. `production.rs::enforce_purchase_generation` checks final orders after every buy pass so a pass with an incomplete shortlist cannot bypass the floor; 0 restores the former orders. When a named fund pays for a recruit, `gold_hold.rs::record_fund_deliveries` records its city, kind and objective; `military.rs::deliver_fund_units` binds the new unit on the next view and routes it to the funded campaign front or threatened city before routine military passes. Funded upgrades also return to their front. The route uses only the own fogged `PlayerView` and saved AI memory, with integer decisions and deterministic tie breaks. No core rule or `RULES_VERSION` changes.

**Threatened-city tactical valuation (AI only, 2026-11-08).** `tactics.rs::enemy_threat` values a visible attacker's expected damage to an own city by how much of the city's remaining HP it removes. If the attacker can capture it this turn, the stake is 400 + 80 per population, the same city value used by assault forecasts. `shot_value` and `focus.rs` price a shot by that threat, so ranged fire goes to attackers as a breach approaches (`enemy_threat` also scales damage by the city's shield: `(max HP + HP) / HP`, so the same hit counts more as the city nears a breach). `door.rs::door_strikes` orders its targets by threat first, then HP. `counterattack_target` ranks favorable melee strikes by the harm they avert (win chance next) and considers adjacent city attackers as well as bombardiers for a sortie, subject to its existing forecast, loss and reply tests. In `door.rs::rotate_exposed_garrisons`, a garrison at risk of falling or losing its city shield can swap with an adjacent line unit when the replacement is forecast to survive and has greater defense value against adjacent attackers; a two-unit friendly swap is legal in the core movement model. `production.rs::buy_ready_defenders` ranks affordable units by the class bonus against visible attackers within three hexes of the city, then by defensive role and strength per Gold. The siege stance, siege-war, campaign and breach passes (`breach.rs`, `siege.rs`, `military.rs`) exclude a unit occupying its own city while a visible enemy military unit is adjacent (`military.rs::last_garrison_under_attack`); tactical shots and favorable sorties remain available. A wounded garrison of a city already at 0 HP shoots an adjacent attacker rather than resting, since that attacker decides the city (exception to the late-garrison-fire rest rule in the 2026-10-24 audit entry; `defense_tests.rs::wounded_garrison_of_a_breached_city_shoots_the_adjacent_attacker_instead_of_resting`). No core combat or purchase rule changes.

**Designer imitation seat (2026-11-09; measurement only, no rules change).** `crates/ai/src/designer.rs::brain` is Hard (`ScriptedBrain` at `AiDifficulty::Hard`) with the gene file `crates/ai/src/designer.json` and two order-level habits applied after Hard's orders (`DesignerBrain::habits`): Walls first in a city without them from turn 25 (`WALLS_FROM_TURN`), and purchases dropped except aircraft and cities with a military unit of a civ at war within 3 hexes (`drop_purchases`, `DANGER_RADIUS`). Only sim examples use it (`scenario_gate --him designer`, `designer_predict`); no app game, difficulty or default does. On the held-out tape it predicts 26% of his decisions against Hard's 23%, and it is about as hard for Hard to beat as Hard is (`docs/sim-results/scenario-gate/README.md`). The paired scenario gate that uses it is `crates/sim/examples/scenario_gate.rs` (`tools/sim/scenario-gate.sh`).

**Mongol horde playbook (2026-11-07; AI only, gene `mongol_horde`, shared default 0 and Mongol Hard builtin 3 after measurement).** Levels 1–3 apply to Hard Mongols only (`crates/ai/src/params.rs`, `production.rs::mongol_horde`). Level 1 raises a war army to at least five units beyond its cities and fills the war quota and early counter with the best available mounted unit; a threatened city's garrison floor still asks for a melee unit. The Rush opening trains mounted troops rather than ranged support, Keshigs get a 100-point unique-unit production term, and Iron Working gains research value after three mounted units (`production.rs::item_base_value,rush_opening_item,uncapped_army,unique_unit_bonus`; `research.rs::tech_base_value`). Level 2 spends available Gold and spare Iron to upgrade Horsemen to Keshigs while keeping promotions; this priority bypasses the ordinary Gold runway and Forge opportunity-cost comparison but still obeys the core supply budget. A wounded Keshig beside an enemy retreats to a safer tile before the assault planner takes it (`modernization.rs::plan_modernization`, `military.rs::hold_or_upgrade,mongol_keshig_upgrade,plan_military`, `supply_value::unit_outbids_industry`). Level 3 treats Keshigs as shooters in a siege of a walled city: they stage at range two and shoot every turn while the city has HP, then charge to capture at zero HP; an unwalled city can be charged directly (`city_assault.rs::coordinate_city_assaults_on,city_assault_succeeds`, `siege.rs::siege_city`, `military.rs::fire_units`). Each unit still has one order per turn and the core's combat and faction rules are unchanged.

The level-3 builtin won 18.38% and 19.68% of 4,096 paired Mongol seats on seeds 9311003 and 8241001, versus 16.87% and 17.48% with the gene off. On the eight-map human-seat gate it raised seat wins from 472 to 502 out of 2,048; enabling it only for rivals changed them to 469. The faction best/worst ratio worsened on both AI-only seeds (2.289 to 2.429; 2.024 to 2.279), and `mongols_keshig` re-ablation at level 3 was inconclusive (18.03% to 17.95%, rule off). See `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/mongol-horde-1107/README.md`. The unit's price or Iron requirement, rather than a bow buff, is the next balance variable if the spread remains high.

**Hard decision audit (2026-11-06).** When requested by `legatum-sim --decision-trace DIR`
or `seat_run --trace DIR`, the live scripted Hard order path records option outcomes per
seat-turn. The observer distinguishes options gated before valuation, compared and rejected,
selected but removed by a later pass, and executed orders. It records plan and production
scores, decisive purchases, Settler sites, garrison/escort reservations and open-city capture
filters (`crates/ai/src/decision_trace.rs`, `scripted.rs::order_diagnosis`,
`crates/sim/src/runner.rs`). `seat_run --trace` also writes the seat's final orders for each
turn (`crates/sim/examples/seat_run.rs`), along with military unit positions, HP, and
Peace diagnostics (desire gate, treaty score and routed plan), so plan labels can be
checked against army movement, shots, casualties and actual treaty orders. The trace
does not change game rules, planning inputs or orders;
ordinary runs allocate no decision records.

- **Sparta's expedition plan (2026-11-03; AI only).** The `sparta_valued_expedition` gene in
  `crates/ai/src/params.rs` suppresses the synthetic value-zero Campaign and its unconditional
  plan override. A Campaign can compel the expedition only when its priced value is positive
  and at least as good as the best competing plan (`plan.rs::plan_candidates,choose_plan`).
  Diplomacy follows that valued Campaign (`diplomacy.rs::sparta_expedition_target`). At zero,
  the legacy forced expedition remains available for a paired comparison.

- **Coalition push (2026-11-01; AI only).** `coalition_push` defaults to 1. In `plan.rs::contain_candidates`, a containment coalition counts field strength only when visible armies approach a known target city; the leader-race value includes an equal-survivor floor when the planner's current solo chance is zero and scales by its own contribution. A held-VP contest likewise scales by the planner's approaching field force. `plan.rs::coalition_fronts` counts distinct threatened cities rather than several allied flags beside one city. `campaign.rs::coalition_city_push` adds a reachable leader-capital or held-VP city prize to campaign, assault and plan scores. Setting `coalition_push=0` restores the prior strength, race, hold, front and city scoring; the older `coalition_front` gene remains independent.

- **India's regional court plan (2026-10-31, AI only).** Hard prices each provincial Revenue Office by the Gold it will remit after completion: half the city's production-focus preview plus the Office's +3 Gold, rounded down (`india_remittance_value` 400 per remitted Gold per turn). A safe provincial city can start this Office before the opening queue; exposed cities keep the defense priority (`india_early_office` 1). From turn 60, `india_office_goal_value` 800 values missing Offices while the Imperial Diwan goal is pending, and normal production scores the Diwan once every owned city has an Office. The extra last-Office bonus and forced Diwan order are available as genes but default off after paired tests found that forcing the chain increased eliminations (`india_last_office_value` 0, `india_diwan_commit` 0). Production-focus funding and runway forecasts count only Gold actually remitted, including zero before an Office (`production.rs::remitted_city_gold`, `funding.rs::fund_production_runway`). A threatened city may train a needed troop despite India's ten-turn runway veto (`india_emergency_defense` 1); Hard also counts an assessed rush-capable neighbour as a funding front before War, so an anticipatory guard can start (`india_prewar_guard` 1). India's Hard personality uses expansion 70, military 60, science 40, economy 70 and aggression 40; it avoids optional city campaigns and second fronts while funding its regional courts (`campaign_contain_only` 1, `second_front_temperament` -60). Containment wars require a funded siege route (`leader_commit` 2); threatened cities use stronger local defense weights (`defend_margin_percent` 250, `door_defense` 150). This changes AI decisions only; the Diwan remains a held, transferable 2 VP goal.
**Recent-city retake commitment (2026-10-31; gene `retake_hold`, default 0 after the seat-only gate was inconclusive (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/retake-hold-1031/README.md`); AI only).** `crates/ai/src/params.rs` defines 0 as off and N as the first N turns of a campaign. `crates/ai/src/campaign.rs::reconcile_campaign` records each city last owned by the AI in saved `CampaignMemory::last_owned`, using its own `PlayerView` only. Against a city it owned within the preceding 20 turns, `InsufficientArmy` does not end the campaign during that window while committed units within four hexes have at least half the combined strength of the city, its visible garrison and a half-city wall allowance. `crates/ai/src/plan.rs::missing_plan_patience` also keeps the routed objective through a missing candidate forecast while that hold applies. A collapse below the floor, a negative campaign value, a new wall before arrival, a funding deadline, peace or a missing target still ends it. The committed force still follows the existing `campaign_persist` reinforcement rule and existing military routing; this gene changes no combat or capture rule. The paired-game diagnostic in `crates/sim/examples/hard_vs_human.rs` reports campaign start/end pairs within three turns and the number of cities recaptured within 20 turns of being lost, per AI and per game.

- **Military exploration and ruins (2026-10-31; AI only, gene `military_scout`, default 0; `crates/ai/src/military.rs::military_scout`).** After combat, emergency defence, garrisons, ruin collection and campaign passes claim units, a healthy uncommitted land military unit may move toward an explored, passable frontier tile that reveals unknown land or holds an unclaimed known ruin. The option compares three points per unknown tile and `ruin_bonus` for a ruin, scaled by `military_scout` percent, against route cost, distance from the nearest own city, lost fortification and missing HP. Routes exclude exposed tiles and recent remembered enemy reach and use `campaign_approach` for a safe waypoint. The gene at 0 leaves the prior military orders unchanged. No core exploration or ruin rule changes; any unit already collects a ruin by entering its tile (§2).
- **Captured-city production alarm (2026-10-31, decision audit round 6; `capture_alarm`, default 0, opt-in; AI only).** In `production.rs::plan_emergency_production`, plain Hard with the gene at 1 does not treat a neighboring city's resistance alone as a reason to interrupt economy with a unit. A neighboring city at half HP or less, within eight hexes, still raises that alarm when a visible non-barbarian enemy military unit is within six hexes of it. Direct local threat, ranged relief, the lone-city front rule, and all other production passes are unchanged. Zero restores the prior resistance-or-enemy condition. No rules, visibility, yields or orders change. Results: `docs/sim-results/ai-war/audit-round6.md`.
- **Decision audit across rule revisions (2026-10-31, offline diagnostics).** `audit_states` exports every recorded position using a historical engine and refuses any tape hash mismatch. `decision_audit --states FILE.jsonl` verifies those positions against every tape hash before using them for shadow observations and branch starts; the branches and one-turn comparisons still resolve under the current engine. This avoids replay drift after a combat rebalance. `--prod-detail` now includes every own city, its incoming production, the planning passes that set production before routine scoring, and local defense evidence. These traces are diagnostic only; the untraced AI records nothing.
- **Battle tactics: focus fire and stragglers (2026-10-30, genes `focus_fire` 100 and `gather_hold` 50, on by default since 2026-10-30 (2,048-game gate neutral, `docs/learnings.md`); AI only, no
  rule change; AI war plan step 2; `crates/ai/src/focus.rs`, measured in
  `docs/sim-results/ai-war/w24-focus/`).** Hard only (`hard_defense`), this turn's view, no memory.
  `focus_fire` runs in `plan_military` after `recover_wounded`, before `ranged_fire`: for each
  visible enemy field unit (not on any city tile; cities stay with the assault coordinator), the
  free shooters in range with a clear line and the chargers at or above `recover_hp` that reach it
  this turn (not a garrison standing in an own city) are ordered shots first (expected damage,
  then id), then charges (forecast with the shots queued, `melee_forecast`); every prefix of up to
  six attackers is priced: kill chance is the exact distribution of the summed per-roll damage
  (`kill_chance`, integer counts over 41^k rolls) against the target's HP less shots already
  ordered; prize = kill chance x (target cost + `enemy_threat` HP x our army's mean cost / 100) x
  `focus_fire`% + the surviving share of damage at half value; price = each charger's forecast
  damage (counterattack split among the plan's chargers, `queued_split_forecast`, plus city fire)
  at its cost, loss chance x cost / 2, and after a kill the extra incoming fire on the target's
  tile over its own; and every attacker's value as a lone hit. The best positive plan over all
  targets is ordered, its units leave the pool, repeat. `gather_hold` runs after `rest`, before
  `fire_from_holds`: a military move (not an attack, retreat, assault group or coordinated
  assault, nor one whose destination is an own city or beside one) whose end tile this turn
  (fogged route, `can_step`) takes an expected share of visible fire (`incoming_at`, divided by
  1 + our units ending beside it) of at least `gather_hold`% of the unit's HP, and more than
  staying would, stops at the last route tile below that share or holds (`SkipUnit`).
- **Defend where the enemy army is (2026-10-27, gene `door_defense`, default 100 since 2026-10-28 (8.9.15), parts mask `door_parts` 29; AI only, no
  rule change; idea #23, AI war plan W2; `crates/ai/src/door.rs`).** Hard only
  (`threat_aware`), from this turn's view, no memory. A *front* is an own city that a visible
  enemy field unit (combat class, not barbarian, not standing in its own city) can strike
  within `DOOR_TURNS` 3 turns of march (`strike_turns`: distance less its range or 1, over its
  moves); each unit threatens its nearest reachable city plus `threat_aim_slack`. Threat is
  the units' Strongest Army value (`army::combat_value`, strength squared, HP-weighted) times
  `door_defense` percent; defence is the same value of own combat units within 2 plus the
  city's own (`CityView.strength` squared, HP-weighted). Only the gap is acted on:
  `plan_door_defense` (in `plan_cities` after the hard-defence rescue purchases, before
  `plan_modernization`) greedily buys, per Gold, the unit option with the most
  `modernization_power` at the city (one purchase per city per turn) or a one-step
  `UpgradeUnit` of a unit within 2 that has no enemy in its strike reach, until each gap is
  closed; it keeps solvency (`reserve_deficit_turns` of deficit, plus ten turns of the next
  unit's upkeep for purchases), a held Swiss hoard and `live_goal_floor`, but not goal or
  siege savings; strategic supply is checked with `supply::within_budget`. A front still
  short builds the strongest unit it can finish within arrival + 3 turns, keeping Walls under
  way and a committed Settler, and its combat build is not reconsidered while the gap stays
  (`Planner::door_cities`). `door_military` (early in `plan_military`, after
  `withdraw_failed_campaign`) then: strikes enemy field units within 3 of an own city, weakest
  first, with shots (resolved before moves) and then melee units whose forecast loss chance is
  at most 30% and who survive the reply, only when expected damage reaches 115% of the
  target's HP and the target's cost times `door_defense`% covers the HP cost to the strikers;
  sends the nearest free unit that reaches an empty threatened city this turn into it (never
  another city's garrison: measured, walking units in over 2-3 turns or emptying quiet cities
  cost Japan T142 84% to 67%); and, with or without a front, assaults
  (`coordinate_city_assaults_at`) an enemy city within 6 of ours at half HP or less whose
  visible garrison our free units within a turn outweigh. Gene `door_parts` (bit mask: 1 buys,
  2 upgrades, 4 production, 8 strikes, 16 garrisons, 32 retake) picks the parts; its default 29
  leaves out upgrades and the retake, which the 2026-10-28 attribution found worth nothing or
  less. Measured: `docs/sim-results/ai-war/door-defense.md`.
- **Army-sized production at the door (AI only, 2026-11-15; gene `army_door_production` default 1 for Hard, 0 restores the older choice; `threat.rs::plan_threat_defense`, `production.rs::choose_item`).** While a visible army of a team at War leaves a door front short, Hard counts the city's Walls and nearby garrison through the existing `door_fronts` forecast, subtracts a defender bought this turn, and assigns line-unit production first in that city and then in reachable own cities within four hexes until the gap is covered. The chosen melee or ranged unit is the newest trainable tier in that city's options, then the strongest against visible foes. An existing line build stays if its tier is at least as new and its power is at least three quarters of that choice, or it can finish before the army arrives and sooner than the replacement. This can replace buildings, wonders, Walls-line construction and obsolete units. `choose_item` keeps a direct front on a defender when assigning a new build. If the current era's unit lacks a required resource, Hard falls back to the newest trainable tier. The AI sees only its own `PlayerView`; core combat, costs and `RULES_VERSION` do not change.
- **Re-aim, don't quit (2026-10-29, gene `war_reaim`, default 1 since 2026-10-29, acting only against a hollow leader (`persist_hollow`); AI only, no rule change; 8.9.15 Swiss review rec 1;
  `plan.rs::reaim_candidates`).** Hard only. When the routed Campaign plan's war on team X is
  still on and its campaign ended this turn (`InsufficientArmy`, `WallsBeforeArrival`,
  `NegativeValue`, `TargetGone`; not after a capture) or its city dropped off the candidates,
  X's other known cities within `march_turns` of our healthy army are ranked by worth
  (100 + 25 a population + 200 for a capital) over defence (`city_defence_weight` plus one
  defender the owner could buy: rival treasuries are public since rules 31, but this pricing still assumes every owner is able to
  buy one unit of the strongest kind it is seen fielding, half of it for a garrisoned city)
  less 100 a turn of march; the best `FRONT_TARGETS` (3) are forecast with the war already
  open (`campaign_forecast_with(open_front)`: no second-front veto, and
  `earliest_winning_march` tries every march window from `approach_turns` to `march_turns`
  and keeps the best capture odds) and priced by `price_campaign` (label "re-aimed after the
  last campaign"). Those under `min_seat_war_odds` are dropped, so the plan stands down to
  Peace only when no city in reach is winnable. Level 2 also forecasts an empty target as
  holding that bought defender (`with_bought_defender`); measured worse (it priced every
  target out). Measured: `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md` W2.2.
- **Peace follows the plan (2026-10-29, gene `peace_own_danger`, default 2 since 2026-10-29, toward a hollow leader only (`persist_hollow`); AI only; 8.9.15 Swiss review rec 3;
  `diplomacy.rs::wants_peace,endangered_by,collapsing`).** In "Hard's plan decides", toward
  the team the routed plan fights, `endangered()` (pressure from any enemy) is replaced by
  `endangered_by(team) || collapsing()`. Level 1: `team`'s units within 5 of a city outweigh
  our units within 2 plus half the city. Level 2: they must outweigh all our combat units
  within 5 plus half the city. A city's low HP is not danger by itself (2026-11-15, rules 35: a
  city just taken and worn down by our own siege used to count as "our city in danger" and
  bought Peace); it only lowers the city's own weight. `collapsing`: a city of ours at half HP
  or less with enemies at war within 2 that outweigh our units within 2. Every other
  path of `wants_peace` is unchanged, and wars the plan does not fight still end as before.
- **Prize in reach (2026-11-15, gene `peace_prize`, default 0; AI only;
  `diplomacy.rs::peace_prize_cost,wants_peace_why,treaty_score`).** At war, the best enemy
  city (a remembered sighting counts; a fogged city is assumed to hold one Soldier) that the
  forces already in place take within the ten-turn truce, by the campaign planner's own
  forecast and price (`campaign_forecast`, `price_campaign`), is a cost of Peace of capture
  value x gene / 250, at most 200, set against the Peace terms (like `peace_siege_cost`, whose
  larger cost it joins). Waived while the enemy army stands within three hexes of our original
  capital.
- **Keep a valuable siege open (2026-11-03, gene `peace_siege_value`, default 0 after an inconclusive rivals-only gate; AI only;
  `diplomacy.rs::peace_siege_cost,wants_peace_why,treaty_score`).** An offered Peace ends
  attacks on that team for the ten-turn truce. Hard compares the Peace terms with the
  best visible wartime capture against that team: the usual committed-force siege
  forecast must take the city within the truce and before the Gold runway expires.
  A visible original capital at one-quarter HP or less also has a capture option
  while a healthy own military unit stands within four hexes, even if the current
  force has no melee capturer. Positive campaign capture value becomes a treaty
  cost, weighted by the gene; an enemy army beside our original capital removes
  that cost so Peace can relieve the capital. Only the AI's `PlayerView` supplies
  cities, units, HP, original ownership and war state. The core treaty and combat
  rules do not change. At weight 100, the Swiss T147 offer is refused on the
  recording tag, but the 2,048-game rivals-only gate was inconclusive (human
  seat 453 to 455 wins); see `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/keep-siege-1103/`.
- **Read the hollow leader (2026-10-29, genes `hollow_read` and `persist_hollow`, both default 1 since 2026-10-29 (AWS gate rivals-only +0.34 pp [-1.38, +2.07] with the persistence genes; `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md` W2.3); AI only;
  8.9.15 Swiss review rec 2; `threat.rs::hollowness,hollow_bonus,runaway_signs`,
  `plan.rs::hollow_leader,persists_against`, `military.rs::campaign_target_for`).** Hard only,
  public numbers only. `hollowness(team)`: 100 minus the team's Strongest Army title value per
  city as a percent of the median other living empire's (0..100). `hollow_bonus`: when
  hollowness is 50 or more, the team holds 2+ cities and shows riches in public (economic signs
  of 8+: a live goal or a Power lead; an era above the median other empire's; or 500+
  Gold of Swiss capture bounty on cities we see), hollowness times its cities (up to 10) / 30.
  With `hollow_read`, `runaway_signs` counts for any team no more than 10 points of target
  progress behind the best other team (not only the strict leader) once its hollow bonus is
  positive, and adds that bonus; the threat race and proximity rules then apply as for
  `runaway_read`. Hysteresis (`ThreatTracker::hollow_teams`, saved in the opponent memory): a team enters the read at hollowness `HOLLOW_ENTER` 50 and keeps it down to `HOLLOW_KEEP` 25, so one bought defender (the 8.9.15 Swiss went 66 to 35 the turn after the Mongols declared) does not end it; the plan, Contain and persistence checks use the same floor. `contain_leader` falls back to `hollow_leader` (the team at 20%+ of its target
  with the highest such signs, 20 or more), and a campaign or Contain march on that team's
  cities counts as `contained` (threshold -20) with 4x its hollow bonus added to the city's
  priority. `persist_hollow` 1: `war_reaim` re-aims and `peace_own_danger` holds Peace only
  against an enemy whose hollow bonus is positive (hollowness alone let Japan T142 refuse Peace to a four-veteran Sparta).
- **Execute a war on the hollow leader (2026-10-30, gene `hollow_march`, a bit mask, default 0;
  AI only; AI war plan W2.4, playtest 8.9.17; `hollow_march.rs`, `production.rs::buy_war_army`).**
  Hard only, public numbers only. `hollow_war_target`: the routed plan's war target (Contain or
  Campaign) while at War and its hollow bonus (with the tracker's floor) is positive. Part 1:
  an own city that is not the capital, guards no Hall or Wonder and has no visible foreign
  military unit within `HOLLOW_GUARD_RADIUS` 6 reachable hexes keeps no standing garrison
  (`garrison_cities`), and its units count toward the march force (`could_strike` otherwise
  leaves every unit standing in an own city out). Part 2: the capital too. Part 4: the war
  army quota (`uncapped_army`) is at least `HOLLOW_WAR_EXTRA` 5 beyond one unit per city
  (the shared `army_war_extra` is 1 since 2026-11-06). Part 8: while `campaign_against` reads every enemy
  city out of reach, buy one military unit a turn (best strength per Gold, nearer the enemy
  first, only shooters while the nearest target is walled and fewer than two shooters stand
  in the field) until the fielded army meets the quota, keeping 150 Gold and non-negative
  income and ignoring the Swiss hoard savings; it runs before `buy_frontline_defender`.
  Door defense still answers visible armies.
  Diagnostics: `Brain::war_diagnosis` (`war_diagnosis.rs`), printed by `war_trace` with
  `WAR_TRACE_DIAG=<seats>`.
- **Army modernization (2026-10-26, AI preferences only).** `modernization_value`,
  `modernization_upgrade` and `modernization_return` each use 0 for the previous policy.
  Defaults are **100 / 100 / 100** since 8.9.14 (8.9.13 shipped 100 / 0 / 0). The
  2026-10-27 frozen-control gate (2,048 games per arm, `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md`)
  measured upgrade plus return at 100 on top of valuation: seat-only +2.4 pp [+0.1, +4.6],
  opponents-only -6.5 pp, war suite 41% to 47%. The value path uses
  `army::effective_strength/full_health_value` from the
  Strongest Army work (strength squared / 20, HP-weighted), with visible class counters
  and an adjacent Spartan Hoplite's +5 phalanx. Enemy references come from the fogged
  view near the front; without contacts, legal own production supplies the reference.
  Production discounts obsolete bodies in its army quota, reconsiders a running military
  build with banked progress credited, and purchases compare combat value per Gold.
  Refits compare researched, supplied upgrade chains with alternative purchases, reserve
  Gold, and buy before optional city spending. Each intermediate step still requires its
  real Gold, supply and action turn; upgrades preserve XP. After tactical orders, a funded
  unit staging outside own territory may replace a hold with a safe path home, charging
  round-trip time and lost combat service. Ready-to-retire veterans and hired companies
  are excluded. No combat, resource, territory, faction or Strongest Army scoring rule
  changes here. `crates/ai/src/modernization.rs`, `production.rs`, `military.rs`;
  evidence and gate decisions: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/army-modernization-1026.md`.

- **Athens war audit decision (2026-10-25; AI only, default 1 after the gate).**
  `capture_vp_accounting` 1 makes `hold.rs::science_loss_on_capture` leave the VP-v2 Most Advanced
  metric unchanged by a capture (era/known techs cannot be unlearned); the old scoring model retained its
  rolling-science estimate. `plan.rs::price_campaign` credits Germany's five-Foundry goal
  progress from a capture when it has fewer than five cities. Private enemy building detail is
  conservatively assumed absent, not read from hidden state. Each gene at 0 restores the old
  decision path. Fixtures and original-engine traces:
  `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/athens-game-1025.md`; default gate:
  `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/gate-genes-1025/README.md`.
- **Air defense and operations (Japan audit and gate, 2026-10-26).** `air_defense` (100 since 2026-10-28; percent weight; 0 restores bomber-count production and the fixed guard value) values Fighters independently of own Bombers using uncovered own cities and troops within eight hexes of known non-allied Modern-or-later cities. It uses public era and known city positions, never enemy hangars. One Fighter covers four hexes and one interception: value is the largest exposed asset, not their sum; existing and queued Fighters reduce production demand, earlier guard assignments reduce overlapping coverage. It can exceed the old empire-wide one-aircraft-per-city quota for uncovered defense, but never the owner’s hangar cap (two, or three after Stealth Technology). `air_commitment` (0) lets a useful aircraft build survive emergency ground-production replacement when the city is not forecast to fall immediately. `air_rebase` (default 1; 0 disables) moves idle aircraft to strictly more useful own bases within ten hexes, reserving hangar slots; it also evacuates a base with an adjacent visible capture unit when city HP is low or public rival bases could bomb it. Fighters can still intercept from their old base before rebasing resolves. The 1,024-game fixed-map gate enabled only rebasing: measured-seat wins +3.7 pp [95% +1.7, +5.8], all-seats +4.0 pp [+2.0, +6.1]. `air_defense` was kept off on 2026-10-26 after measured-seat -4.8 pp [-6.9, -2.5] and all-seats -13.1 pp [-15.4, -10.6], but that gate's rivals were Hard+Search and never bombed; on the fixed gate (plain Hard rivals bomb, 2,048 games, `docs/sim-results/ai-war/baseline.md` W2.0) it reads seat-only +3.2 pp [+1.6, +4.8], rivals-only -1.3 pp [-2.9, +0.3] and all-seats +1.0 pp [-0.6, +2.6], with the war suite unchanged (49% mean goal success, Golden Horde 96%, thin 87%, First Blood 100%), so it defaults on; `air_commitment` remains off because its intervals crossed zero. See `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/air-gold-gate-1026.md`. `crates/ai/src/air.rs`, `air/coverage.rs`, `production.rs`; evidence and fixture interventions: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/japan-air-war-1026.md`. No combat, visibility, resource or order rule changes.
- **Air-stage public read (2026-11-05 India review; AI policy, gene `air_stage_read`, default 1 after its gate).** `view.rs::AirSighting` reveals only the kind and base of aircraft in a currently visible rival city; the city panel shows the same sighting, without HP or supply. Fogged hangars and rival technology remain private. At gene 1, `decisive_buy.rs::interception_risk` prices a Modern rival's unknown Fighter at 25% interception risk rather than treating Modern as certain defense; a Fighter reported in a battle within ten turns raises that estimate to 50% without revealing its base or survival, a remembered sighting of a Fighter base in guard range raises it to 60%, and a Fighter currently seen in guard range raises it to 75%, since it could strike or rebase instead. A Stealth Bomber counts only a seen Stealth Fighter. At War, `air/coverage.rs::prize_guard_value` adds guard value for own exposed capital and Great Work cities when a rival is **known** to have Flight: a seen aircraft or one reported in a battle proves Flight, as does entry into a later era under the strict tech gate. Mere Modern entry is only a possibility and does not trigger this extra value. A Fighter already guarding a prize removes demand for another. The gene changes AI valuation and visible information, not air combat or orders. `visibility::update_explored` remembers aircraft in seen rival hangars; `Event::Battle.air_strike` publicly identifies an air attack without disclosing the striker kind; `rules::remember_air_contacts` records that coarse fact and the exact Fighter kind named in an interception for the participating or sighted teams; `GameState::air_contacts` preserves both in saves and `PlayerView.air_contacts` exposes them to the AI. Unseen battles provide no contact. Gate: 4,096 paired-seed AI-only games per arm had zero panics and no material faction shift; the 2,048-game human-seat rivals-only arm was noninferior to the −1.5 pp floor (strength −0.05 pp, simultaneous interval −0.73 to +0.63 pp). The seat-only arm observed +0.20 pp but was inconclusive from sparse discordance. See `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/air-stage-1105/README.md`.
- **Air and Oil valuation (2026-11-01, AI only).** `supply_value::demand` includes the strength recovered by resupplying idle aircraft and the best aircraft each Flight-era city currently values for production, limited by the empire's unfilled aircraft quota. This value raises the price of Oil deposits in settlement, capture and Oil Well choices. `aircraft_oil_opportunity` prices the best lab or land upgrade displaced by a new plane's Oil slot; `production::item_base_value` subtracts that opportunity at `industry_priority`, with `supply_war_percent` applied to an upgrade at war. This is a score, not a production veto. No core rule changes.
- **Raze and buys (AI decisions only, both on by default since 2026-10-31: `raze_value` 1, `buy_hold` 3; added 2026-10-31; `docs/sim-results/ai-war/raze-buys.md`).** From the decision audit of the designer's wins. `raze_value` 1 (`crates/ai/src/raze.rs`, called from `production::plan_raze`): keep or raze a captured city by value. Keeping is worth its output over 40 turns (less resistance), estimated from our settled cities' yield per population, plus growth and 300 Gold per live "gain cities" objective, minus the extra Decadence its output adds (public Power, `rule_experiments.decadence_percent`); against that, either the Gold to buy enough of our cheapest defender to cover the visible pressure (if affordable within 5 turns of income) or a loss chance (90% when `defense_risk` forecasts the fall this turn, else the unmatched share of pressure x 75% if an attack can arrive now or 40% if not) with the lost city valued at half to its former owner. Razing pays population x 10. The Celtic sack is unchanged. `buy_hold` 1-3 (`crates/ai/src/gold_hold.rs`, `Planner::held_gold`): routine `plan_buy` purchases (buildings, improvements, Settlers, surplus army) spend only Gold above its later uses: the summed upgrade bill through each unit’s newest researched or imminent form (within 10 research turns), plus at war or under threat the cheapest defender quote per threatened city (one at war with none in sight, two at most); 2 also holds it from `buy_ready_defenders` and `buy_frontline_defender` unless `defense_risk` says an attack can reach the city this turn; 3 also banks the dearest buyable unit for each of up to four cities (the army spike). Capped at 25 turns of income. Upgrades, rescues, door, threat, victory, goal and hoard purchases ignore it. Diagnostics: `crates/ai/src/buy_trace.rs` tags every purchase with the planning pass that ordered it (thread-local, off unless a measurement enables it; `crates/sim/examples/raze_buy_census.rs`).
- **Army commitment (AI decisions only, both on by default since 2026-10-31 after the 2,048-game gate `w30-1031-032745`: opponents-only +2.64 pp for `capture_hold`, +3.32 with `strike_kill`; added 2026-10-31; `docs/sim-results/ai-war/army-commit.md`).** From the decision audit's army category (Hard in the designer's seat held back units he kept attacking with). `capture_hold` 1 (`crates/ai/src/army_commit.rs`, `military.rs::hold_captures`): a city in resistance, or damaged with enemies about, takes a holder before the attack passes only when the visible attack would take it empty this turn (`purchase_defense::city_risk_with` with our units removed), a holder would stop that, and the holder has no capture this turn worth more (an ungarrisoned enemy city at war it reaches: population x capture chance, against our population x 90%); every other front city gets its holder from the units the attack passes leave free (`hold_captures_late`, after the campaign pass), and the early anti-Mongol `garrison_cities` pass skips it too. `strike_kill` 1 (`press_strike`): a unit of the opportunity strike's force that can kill a visible enemy field unit alone this turn (forecast kill chance at least 50%, the target's cost times the kill chance above its own cost times its loss chance) is left to the attack passes instead of staging. Diagnostics: `OrderTrace::passes` names, per unit, the planning passes that gave or changed its order (`Planner::pass`, recorded only by `order_diagnosis`); `decision_audit --army-detail` writes one row per unit and turn with both resolutions and those passes.
- **Founding and upgrade timing (AI decisions only, both off by default; added 2026-10-31; `docs/sim-results/ai-war/audit-round3.md`).** From the third decision-audit round; neither moved the quarter gate beyond noise. `found_here` 1 (`crates/ai/src/civilians.rs::found_here_value`, called from `plan_settler`): a Settler standing on a known legal, preferred site (`settlement::settlement_site`, foreign-border risk included; quality at least `settler_min_site_score`, or any positive quality at level 2; within `settler_max_site_distance` of a city) and safe from capture this turn (`settler_capturable_this_turn`: founding resolves after movement and combat, so a Settler an enemy can reach is taken before it founds) founds there when the site's value, less its loss as a fresh city counted on both sides of the swing (`fresh_city_loss`: the at-war military that can reach the tile by next turn against a population-1 city's defence), is at least the value of the site `choose_site` picked, or when it would otherwise retreat from exposure or go home. The site search itself is unchanged: it still drops tiles for hazards to a Settler on a trip (an enemy army within the threat radius, danger, trip risk) and for a foreign border that could hide a city. A found order on such a tile is remembered for a turn (`CampaignMemory::found_tried`), so a found that failed (an unseen city within spacing) falls back to the search and its rescout. `upgrade_wait` 1 (`modernization.rs::upgrade_waits_for_attack`, in `plan_modernization`): a unit with an attack the military passes would take this turn (`best_shot` for a ranged unit, `best_strike` or `counterattack_target` for a melee one) is not upgraded this turn (an upgrade takes all moves). Diagnostics: `ScriptedBrain::settler_diagnosis` (the plan branch, the verdict on the Settler's own tile, the best site of each kind; thread-local, off unless asked) and `decision_audit --settle-detail` (`settle.jsonl`; since round 4 one row per Settler of the seat per turn, with the search's verdict on the tile the tape later founds on with it).
- **Founding now when delay loses the site (AI decisions only, gene `found_now`, default 10 since 2026-11-03; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/fix-found-now-1103/README.md`).** From the decision-diff patterns (`docs/playtests/diffs/patterns-2026-11.md`, fix 1). Hard only (`civilians.rs::found_here_value`, which `plan_settler` calls in its exposed branch and when the site search picks another tile, home or nothing). The Settler's own tile is weighed as `found_here` weighs it, with three changes. (1) The chance it is captured before the found order resolves is priced per foreign military unit that can reach it this turn (`found_now_capture_percent`) instead of ruling the tile out: an empire at war or a barbarian raider 100 (a veto), a barbarian Scout 5 (a camp's Scout only wanders and reports, `rules/barbarians.rs::scout_goto`), a barbarian standing on a known camp 0 (the guard holds the camp tile), a rival at Peace with a legal declaration its `peace_settler_capture_chance` (10 while `peace_settler_capture` is 0); the value is the site's value less twice the chance of losing it, captured now or (`fresh_city_loss`, empires at war only, since barbarian raiders never assault a city) taken as a fresh city, less at war the search's pre-Walls damage charge when `founding_survival_weight` is set (`founding_damage_before_defense`; the archived Sparta 10-25 Kyoto Settler still goes home). (2) The tile must pass the search's border verdicts (`found_now_border_risk`: `remembered_border_risks`, and the visible-border inference, unexplained only with `border_explained`), which keeps the 8.9.24 Swiss T145 rejection. (3) The search's own choice is discounted by the chance its land is gone first (`site_claim_percent`): 50 for a visible foreign Settler that can get within its city gap of the site by arrival, plus for each other empire whose nearest known city lies within twice its city gap plus 2 of the site, N% a turn times `(reach + 1 - d) / reach` over the travel turns (distance over moves, rounded up) plus one, capped at 100; with no alternative (retreat, home) any positive value founds. Measured: at the cited turns the choice changes at 8.3 Tollkeepers T36 and T44 and 8.9.5 Signalwrights T58 (ported to the tags), not at T118 (barbarian raiders in reach) or T137 (border risk, quality 31); Lab `germany-found-by-the-scout` 111 to 200 of 200; benchmark (2,048 games, seed 310001) rivals-only -0.39 pp [-2.00, +1.27], seat-only +0.34 [-0.98, +1.66]. At 0 the prior behaviour (and `found_here`, still off) is unchanged.
- **Settler destinations and shooters' kills (AI decisions only; `border_explained` and `fallback_risk` on by default since 2026-10-31 after the 2,048-game gate `w31-1031-070814`: seat-only +4.35 pp, opponents-only +1.42; `shot_sweep` off; added 2026-10-31; `docs/sim-results/ai-war/audit-round4.md`).** From the fourth decision-audit round. `border_explained` 1 (`crates/ai/src/civilians.rs::unexplained_border_risks`, in `choose_site_scored`; candidates from `settlement::settlement_candidates_with_risky`): the site search also lists legal sites a foreign border puts at risk and drops only those an unexplained border implies an unseen city near; a visible foreign tile within a known city's work radius (3) of a city of the same owner is explained by that city, as `remembered_border_risks` already treats remembered borders. The shared `settlement::border_city_risks` (and the Settle lens) is unchanged; such a site's preferred-quality check reads `SiteBasics::legal_base_score`. `fallback_risk` 1: when every preferred site is out and the search would take a fallback (a non-preferred site, which is never charged trip risk), the fallback's own `settler_trip_risk` is compared with the least risky preferred site dropped for trip risk (`legacy_settler_trip_risk` at or above `settler_risk_avoid`); a fallback no safer loses to that site, its value discounted by risk/250 as for a kept site. The two belong together (each alone read weaker rivals in its quarter gate). `shot_sweep` 1 (`army_commit.rs::shot_sweep`, after `hold_stragglers`, before `fire_from_holds`): a shooter ordered to move, not onto an enemy, that can kill a visible enemy field unit from where it stands given the shots already ordered (kill chance at least 50%, `focus::shot_kill_chance`) fires instead when the target's cost times the kill chance beats its own cost times its loss chance if it stays (`incoming_at` without the target: certain if the rest of the visible enemy would finish it, else half its share of HP), unless it is the holder that keeps an own city from falling this turn (`purchase_defense::city_risk_with`). Measured: site genes, quarter gate pooled over three salt sets (1,536 games) seat-only +5.99 pp, rivals-only +2.21 (stronger AI); full gate (2,048 games per arm, control 364) seat-only +4.35 pp [+2.47, +6.22], opponents-only +1.42 [-0.55, +3.38]; audit settling share halved, whole policy flat; `shot_sweep` neutral (off). Diagnostics: `ScriptedBrain::settler_diagnosis(view, unit, probe)` records the search's verdict on a probe tile ("probe tile ...", with the border evidence for a border verdict).
- **Peace while winning and Settlers in held cities (AI decisions only, both off by default; added 2026-10-31; `docs/sim-results/ai-war/audit-round5.md`).** From the fifth decision-audit round; neither moved the quarter gate beyond noise. `peace_strong` 0-50 (`diplomacy.rs::peace_terms`): when our army is at least (100 + gene) percent of the Peace proposer's (the same public `strength` ratio as the army-advantage term, which is clamped to +-50), the "endangered" term (+30 whenever any own city has visible enemy pressure within 5 above its local defence plus half the city's power, or stands at 100 HP or less with an enemy near) adds nothing to the Peace score; the plan gates in `wants_peace_why`, the other terms and the VP cost are unchanged. `settler_shelter` 1 (`tactics.rs::unit_exposure_key`): a Settler in an own city whose shield is Full (`city_shield`: the worst-case visible fire and charges this turn cannot break it) has no exposure there, so `plan_settler` does not retreat it from the city and `safer_tile` does not avoid such a city; a civilian in a city is captured only with the city (a hostile city's tile is entered only at 0 HP). Measured: `peace_strong` 50 audit whole policy -0.076 cities @20 (Sparta -0.36), VP @50 +0.016; quarter gates pooled (1,536 games) seat-only -0.13 pp, rivals-only -1.04; `settler_shelter` audit -0.002, seat-only -0.98. Diagnostics: `decision_audit --peace-detail` (`peace.jsonl`, with `PeaceDiagnosis::danger`, the cities `endangered` weighs) and `--prod-detail` (`prod.jsonl`, what the routine production scorer valued per city).
- **Expansion: the priced colony, garrison escorts and Settler stalking (AI decisions only, all three on by default since 2026-11-02; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-expansion-1102/README.md`).** From the 8.9.23 Germany coach review. `colony_compound` 100 (`production.rs::colony_compound_value`, Hard only): a Settler that passes its gates also scores a new city's compounding return, priced like an improvement per Production spent: (50 + expansion knob) x points x window / (2 x cost) x (100 + 100/cities)%, where points are 36% of the best reachable preferred site's score (the radius-3 Balanced potential a young city works over the window) less one citizen of the training city, and the window is 40 turns (fewer near a turn limit); off while a conquest target is set. With the gene on, the early `army_ok` gate needs one troop, not two. `settler_escort` 1 (`hard_defense.rs::garrison_can_escort`, the multiplayer-off "duel" guard path that plain Hard uses while `settler_safety` is 0): a unit standing in an own city may escort a threatened Settler when that city has no threat (`ctx.threat`) and no enemy at war or barbarian within 3. `settler_stalk` 3 (`plan.rs::stalk_settlers`, a pass before `plan_scouts`): while no own city is threatened and the plan is not already a Snipe, a Scout or field troop outside a city closes, without declaring, on a visible rival Settler at Peace (outside a city, no owner troop beside it, a legal declaration) that is beyond this turn's moves but within 3 turns, stopping on a free tile beside it; the Snipe plan then prices the war once the capture is in reach. At 0 each restores the prior policy. Measured (2,048 games per arm, seed 310001): rivals with all three cut the designer's seat 513 to 444 wins (-3.37 pp [-5.62, -1.12]), rival cities T50 2.64 to 3.01, Settlers lost per empire-game 0.58 to 0.52; Hard on his 8.9.23 Germany map: 3rd and 4th city T42/T56 to T30/T47, mean cities T81 4.70 to 5.20. `hard_vs_human` arms take `other_params` (the parameters for the seats an arm's mode leaves out) so a rivals-only arm can switch a default-on gene off in the human seat.
- **The Settler contest, both ways, and finishing a siege (AI decisions only, on by default since 2026-11-03; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-strike-escort-1102/README.md`).** From the 8.9.24 Swiss coach review. `settler_escort` 2 (`hard_defense.rs::settler_route_near_rival_city`, `guard_exposed_settlers_duel`): a Settler route within 2 hexes of a city of a rival that can declare a surprise war (not allied, at Peace, a legal declaration) counts as threatened even with no rival unit in sight, and a threatened Settler with no escort in reach this turn but an eligible one within 2 turns' moves waits (`SkipUnit`; not when it is falling back into an own city) where it cannot be taken this turn while that unit comes; 1 is the garrison escort alone. A Settler made to wait because a rival that could declare reaches its next stop, with no escort inside that two-turn reach, also calls the nearest eligible troop within `escort_search_distance` (6) hexes to its tile (2026-11-14, 8.9.37: it used to skip with nobody coming); the Settler steps once the troop stands beside it. `stalk_contact` 1 (with `settler_stalk` on; `plan.rs::snipe_targets`, `stalk_settlers`): the Snipe plan pairs a rival Settler only with a melee-capable unit already beside it (an adjacent pursuer pins its target in movement), the stalk closes every non-adjacent unit within the horizon using this turn's moves (`turn_moves`; the view's `moves_left` is last turn's leftover), picks the free tile beside the Settler farthest from its owner's nearest city, and may use a quiet city's garrison (`hard_defense.rs::quiet_city`); 0 restores the 8.9.23 two-move reach. `finish_kill` 50 (`campaign.rs::finish_kill_force`, `finishing`; `plan.rs::missing_plan_patience`): a campaign whose city is at most this percent of its HP, with a unit of ours still on it, is not ended for an insufficient army while a melee unit (a quiet garrison included) can arrive within `march_turns`; those units join the force and march from their cities, and the Campaign plan waits for them. Measured: Hard in the designer's 8.9.24 seat from T40 (48 runs) wins 2 with the 8.9.24 genes and 20 with the new seat genes (T50 cities 4 against the Mongols' 3, as he had); rivals with both new genes, 2,048 games, seed 310001: the designer's seat -1.46 pp [-3.22, +0.34].
- **A new war is judged by the march, staged at Peace, declared on arrival (AI decision only, gene `march_past_reach`, on by default since 2026-11-03; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-declare-1103/README.md`).** From the 8.9.24 Swiss coach review. Before it, `plan.rs::campaign_forecast_with` judged a city by the units within the two-turn approach whenever any stood there, and a reach force with no melee unit ended the forecast, so shooters walking ahead of the infantry hid every Campaign candidate (Hard Mongols in `swiss-strongbox`: no candidate on any turn, 29 of 200 runs declared). With the gene (2) a forecast with no committed force falls through to the field army that reaches the city within `march_turns` (1: only for a new war); a Campaign plan stages that force toward the city (`stages_campaigns`, as `planned_stage`: at Peace, and at war every free unit marches, even alone), and in the first third of the game against an open city declares only once the force in reach takes it (`musters_campaigns`, as `early_campaign_muster`). Committed campaigns keep the old rule. Measured: `swiss-strongbox` 6% to 85% (200 runs); his game from T58 with his orders and purchases, Lugano falls in 31 of 32 runs (0 of 32); 2,048-game gate rivals-only -2.54 pp [-4.35, -0.68], seat-only +1.46 [-0.05, +3.08]; war suite seat-only: the late Mongol attacks on Solothurn (14% to 0%) and Chur (32% to 23%) lose, re-aiming at walled prizes.
- **Finish a fallen city; attack a walled city together (AI decisions only; genes `finish_fallen`, on at 50 since 2026-11-03, and `joint_assault`, off; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/joint-assault-1103/README.md`).** From the 8.9.25 Celts coach review. `finish_fallen` 50 (`military.rs::finish_fallen_cities`, run at the start of `withdraw_failed_campaign`): a visible enemy city at war at or below 50% of its HP, with a free unit of ours within four hexes, gets the city-assault coordinator (`city_assault.rs::coordinate_city_assaults_on` with any campaign, its multi-turn capture forecast) before any withdrawal takes the units. `joint_assault` 1 (`siege.rs::siege_city`, `joint_entry`; `military.rs::press_strike`): against a city that strikes (Walls, HP left) the planned units within one move of its range step in only when `opportunity.rs::forecast_siege_rounds` of that group on its tiles (our Bombers in reach included, `decisive_buy.rs::planes_in_reach`; `joint_window` rounds, 0 the siege window) takes the city; otherwise every planned unit stages three or four hexes out (a safe tile, else the nearest free one outside every walled city's range); siege tiles one move away are preferred and tiles in another walled city's range refused. 2 also sends the force when every planned unit steps in this turn. Why: in his game 35 of 45 Aztec moves into his cities' range came from the siege pass, whose far units arrived one at a time. Measured: Colony 7 from T93 with Hard in his seat taken 13 to 32 of 32 (his own orders: 0 either way); `joint_assault` 2 cut lone city-strike hits 7.2 to 5.5 (Hard seat) and 20.7 to 12.2 (his orders) but the Aztecs took fewer of his cities against his orders (2.9 to 1.2); war suite: `finish_fallen` neutral, `joint_assault` Mongols vs the Bomber 35% to 57% and Tepid Swiss T220 64% to 40%; 2,048-game gate rivals-only `finish_fallen` +0.68 pp [-0.68, +2.05], `joint_assault` 2 +1.27 [-0.54, +3.03].
- **Breach and siege stance (AI decisions only; genes `breach` (default 0) and `siege_stance` (default 6 since 2026-11-08, see the next entry; its other guards and calibration are described there); added 2026-11-08; `crates/ai/src/breach.rs`, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/hard-breach-1108/README.md`).** Training round 2's battle moves, distilled into plain Hard. `breach` N (a pass right after door defence): on a visible enemy city at war, the shots already ordered at the city count first, then free shooters and aircraft in range are added (ground first) until the city reaches 0 HP 95% of the time. The nearest free melee unit that reaches the city this turn walks in (ranged fire and air strikes resolve before movement), and its route is reserved. The zero chance is exact over the uniform 80-120 rolls. A garrison, or an owner's melee unit that can reinforce first, multiplies it by the walker's win chance on the fallen city. The pass orders the breach when capture × (400 + 80 × population) × N% × hold beats the walker's expected loss. The air planner leaves a plane the ground planner ordered alone. `siege_stance` N (a pass right after the city-assault coordinator): a storm when the strike forecast on the free units (N rounds) takes the city for fewer Production lost than its value (only the units that hit it this turn are ordered); otherwise a siege when the shooters near it and our aircraft zero it within N turns: shooters take firing tiles, and melee hold out of the city's strike but within one move of it. The same test replaces the open-city seizure's wait while a walled city still fires above half HP. Measured at `breach` 100 and `siege_stance` 4: combat lab 29.8% → 34.5%, war suite +5.0, human-seat gate seat +2.05 pp and rivals −1.51, designer seat flat (−0.1); `siege_stance` 4 alone at 96 salts: held out +1.5 [−2.1, +5.1], India 8.9.25 −2.5. At that measurement both stayed off because the designer seat didn't improve; `siege_stance` went on at 6 (with the forecast calibration and two guards, next entry) once that fixed the designer-seat cost, while `breach` stays 0.
- **Siege forecast calibration and stance guards (AI decisions only; on by default since 2026-11-08: `forecast_garrison_holds` 1, `siege_stance` 6, `stance_home_guard` 6, `stance_plan_war` 1; `breach` stays 0; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/siege-forecast-1108/README.md`).** `forecast_garrison_holds` (`opportunity.rs::forecast_siege_rounds`): the besieged city's own garrison no longer charges the weakest attacker every round in the strike forecast; it holds the city and only shoots if it can. Level 1 applies this only in the siege stance's storm test, and level 2 in every siege forecast (level 2 kept a losing war going on India 8.9.25). With level 1, the storm also sends the force's units that can't reach the city this turn to their assault tiles, unless an enemy field unit is within two tiles of them. `stance_home_guard` 6: units within 6 hexes of an own city under real threat (more than half its strength) stay out of the stance, unless the city is also within 6 of that home. `stance_plan_war` 1: the stance only takes on the plan's Campaign or Contain enemy, or the campaign or conquest city. `siege_stance` 6 runs the storm forecast six rounds past arrival (at 4 the calibration does nothing). Measured: combat lab 29.8% → 41.7%, war suite +5.6, designer seat at 200 salts +1.2 [−0.5, +2.9] with held out +0.5 [−2.0, +3.0], human seat seat-only +1.46 and rivals-only −1.86.
- **Capture-tactics genes from the Fable seat (AI decisions only; all default 0; added 2026-11-13; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/capture-tactics-1113/README.md`).** `relief_forecast` 1: `forecast_siege_rounds` and `city_assault.rs::city_assault_turn` model the relief chain as it was before rules 37 (not yet updated for supported defence): the city owner's melee units that can step into the city this turn (`opportunity::relief_units`, healthiest first) are fought one by one once the garrison is gone, and still count as attackers of our units; the coordinator's lone walk-in of an empty fallen city and its trim to one capturer apply only with no relief beside it. `charge_preview` 1: in the storm stance and the coordinator, a melee charge that does not capture this turn is held (`siege_hold`) when `melee_forecast` has it dying more often than not or taking more than it deals; shots are ordered first so the preview counts them. `relief_kill` N: `enemy_threat` adds N% of a city's value (400 + 80 per population), scaled by the share of its HP gone, for a unit that can relieve a city our melee can reach. `capturer_upgrade` N: refits of melee units within 4 hexes of a visible enemy city at war score N% higher, an unaffordable captor refit is still a candidate at its full price, and when the best orderable refit is such a captor's and income covers it within `march_turns`, its cost joins `modernization_savings` and nothing else is refitted that turn. `regen_strike` N: `city_damage_at` charges a tile within 2 (not adjacent) of a walled enemy city at 0 HP with none of our units beside it N% of the strike it makes after healing `CITY_HEAL`. On After the Riflemen (50 runs, seat-only) none beat the 21/50 baseline by more than noise.
- **Refits before Siege (AI decisions only; gene `refit_before_siege`, default 1 since 2026-11-14 (designer approved after the full-game gate, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/refit-gate-1114/`); added 2026-11-13; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/campaign-holds-1113/README.md`).** `refit_before_siege` N (`siege_buy.rs::wall_proof_refits`, read by `plan_siege_buys`). It covers the force near the walled war target. A unit without Siege whose refit gives a kind with Siege built in counts if the refit is orderable now (`upgrade_to`, `upgrade_breakdown`) or unlocked by the technology finishing within N turns (`research.current`, `turns_left`; priced by `rules::units::upgrade_cost_for`, halved after Military Engineering). These refits keep their Gold, cheapest first, as far as Gold plus N turns of income reaches. Siege is bought only beyond that, never for a refitting unit, and no `siege_savings` are set while a refit waits. `plan_modernization` still picks the refit. After the Riflemen (seat-only, 50 runs): 21 → 35 wins, median 801 → 1360. Stop the Snowball, Courier King, two Pikemen T112 and Walled Nicomedia: no run changed.
- **Slow Settlers and the pipeline (AI decision only, gene `settler_slow_turns`, default 0; added 2026-11-02; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-expansion-2-1102/README.md`).** At N > 0, the Settler gate's pipeline count (`production.rs`, `Tally::slow_settlers`) leaves out Settlers that other cities are still N or more turns from finishing; a city's own Settler under way and every purchase decision still count them. At 12 it made rivals weaker in the 2,048-game gate (designer's seat +1.56 pp rivals-only, +1.71 with `settler_pipeline` 3), so it stays off.
- **Land race: contested sites and camps in front of land (AI decisions only, genes `land_contest` and `camp_land`, both default 0; added 2026-11-02; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-land-race-1102/README.md`).** `land_contest` N > 0 (Hard): a preferred Settler site's value gains N% of itself times `(reach + 1 - d) / reach`, where d is the site's distance to the nearest known city of another team's empire and reach is twice that empire's city gap plus 2 (`civilians.rs::land_contest_value`); beyond reach, nothing. `camp_land` N > 0 (Hard): each known barbarian camp's land is the best known legal site score within 3 plus 3 per unexplored tile within 3, times N%; camps are cleared in order of ten times the distance home less that land, and a camp whose land reaches `settler_min_site_score` may be up to 12 hexes from home instead of 8 (`barbarians.rs::camp_land_value`). At 0 both leave the prior behaviour. No rules change.
- **Gold purchase savings (AI decision only, default off, 2026-10-26):** `decision.purchase_savings_turns` is 0 by default (range 0–12). When positive, `crates/ai/src/production.rs::plan_buy` compares a legal building quote blocked only by insufficient Gold with today's affordable ordinary purchases using total `item_value`; Gold price determines affordability and arrival time. A candidate must be reachable at the current positive planned net Gold rate within the gene's turn horizon and have more than that many Production turns left. If an immediate purchase would delay the higher-value building, the routine buy waits; existing emergency defense, faction-goal and Siege passes retain their priority. This gene changes no costs, Gold income, orders or visibility. It does not yet reserve a multi-unit campaign war chest. The baseline (`0`) keeps the affordable-only, value-per-Gold purchase path. `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/gold-usage-1026.md` measures the three human saves behind the proposal.

- **VP race valuation (2026-10-24, AI preferences only):** `crates/ai/src/winchance.rs::WinFacts` keeps the public VP leader and the closest rival fixed while comparing projected plans; `vp_race` (default 100, 0 disables) adds our VP progress minus theirs to the fitted win estimate, with more weight near the target and during a public hold. `victory.rs::capture_goal_swing` projects live goal loss and a captured Heir or Courier Hall transfer separately for the two teams in `plan.rs::price_campaign`; `rival_objective_denial` prices interruption of a publicly committed world wonder and disruption of a city or territory objective without pretending to know a rival's private progress. `vp_denial` (default 100, 0 disables both new city effects) controls these projections. `vp_hold_break` (default 100) adds value only to a campaign forecast that gets a holder below its target within the countdown, to a containment front with force and reach, and to a coalition joining against the holder. `hold.rs::hold_city_points` nominates holder cities whose loss takes a held goal. `vp_peace` (default 100) prices Peace with a holder or a team one VP from holding against the projected loss from continuing the war (`diplomacy.rs::vp_peace_cost,vp_war_loss`), for offers and acceptances; 0 preserves the old hold veto. `vp_two_step` (default 100) makes `objective_pursuit.rs::chosen_objective` count the cheapest different-era objective after the first VP and progress one objective makes toward another, allows conquest as the next Cities step; 0 restores the former one-point choice. All genes are in `params.rs`, are on by default, and change no core rule or information visibility.
- **Revived stale-intel pricing (AI only, idea 24).** `extent_guard` 1 prevents city projections from inventing population and territory shares when those facts are absent; shared `capture_economy_percent` 200 credits public captured output (banked faction overrides can use another value). `stale_intel` 15 clocks the last visible turn of each rival city, ages its forecast under fog, asks Scouts to revisit it and prevents a new declaration on an old picture. `strike_range_exit` 1 withdraws idle units from an enemy city's firing range, except Sparta and Celts. Each gene can be set to 0 for a control arm (`campaign.rs`, `plan.rs`, `civilians.rs`, `military.rs`, `params.rs`).
