A loaded midgame state starts a city's clock on first observation because earlier memory is unavailable.
- **Fresh conquest nominees and targeted re-scouting (AI only, 2026-11-15).** Hard's `stale_nominee` gene defaults to 1 (`conquest.rs`, `intel.rs`, `scripted.rs`): a reachable rival city with current intel is nominated ahead of a stale one; a stale city is nominated only if there is no fresh candidate. When the best nomination under the old value ordering is stale, one idle Scout, or the fastest idle military unit (cheapest on a speed tie) if there is no Scout, moves toward an explored, legal, safe tile within sight of its remembered position. The city's current defenders and ownership stay hidden until ordinary vision reveals them. The existing `stale_intel` declaration gate still applies; `stale_nominee = 0` keeps the previous nomination and unit orders. This changes AI orders only, so `RULES_VERSION` stays 40. A siege forecast with no land force now returns no capture forecast instead of indexing an absent unit (`opportunity.rs::forecast_siege_rounds`).
- **Cheapest open era objective (AI only, 2026-11-03; `crates/ai/src/objective_pursuit.rs::costed_objective_effort`, `objective_build_value`, `production.rs::item_value`).** Hard's `cheapest_objective` gene (default 1 since 2026-11-04; 0 restores the legacy effort and percentage bonus) compares every entered, unscored card's one permanent VP with estimated completion time and diverted production, using only its PlayerView. A Build card counts the missing buildings, each city's visible production, banked progress, prerequisite, population, technology and strategic-supply waits, and how many cities can build in parallel. A territory count prices the remaining tiles over the number of cities; a Fight card prices the nearest visible matching city, the army's march, visible guards, city strength, Walls and a declaration. For the chosen Build card, a city values one still-needed building or its prerequisite as a share of the VP against its normal production options; other cities' queued work consumes the remaining slots. It can outbid a routine peacetime opening or army choice on the same Production value and completion-time discount; a city facing a visible war threat keeps its defender. In 200 paired eight-Hard games (the old era-objective rules), era scores rose 3,772 to 4,002 (Build share 57% to 64%); the 50-run Aztec Lab median rose 770 to 840. In the 2,048-game human-seat benchmark the rivals-only arm changed seat wins 468 to 465, (the interval still allows weaker rivals, so Codex left the gene off); the seat-only arm changed 468 to 486. The lead turned it on by default on 2026-11-04: the bar is rivals not significantly weaker, as for `found_now`, and 465 against 468 is not weaker. No objective threshold, score, order or visibility rule changes.
- **Faction target and defense choices (AI only).** `celt_sack_target` 1 makes the Celtic conquest nominee weigh `sack_gold_value` more and charge more for path length, visible defenders and Walls (`conquest.rs`); `sack_commit` 1 still requires a funded route before a new containment war. `india_network_guard` 1 funds one extra defender per two linked cities (within five hexes of another Indian city) and reserves a city's healthy melee guard against even one arriving attacker; `india_objective_reach` 1 lets a reachable conquest nominee substitute for a missing settlement site when estimating a Cities era objective (`production.rs`, `hard_defense.rs`, `crowns.rs`). All are decisions from `PlayerView`; no engine rules or vision changed.
- **Diplomacy and war audit pricing (2026-10-24, AI only):** `second_front_siege_price` (default 1) replaces the veto on a fully walled top threat with the least costly reachable siege estimate and the VP denied by opening another front. At 0 the soft-city veto remains. `rival_redeclare_percent` (100) weighs a rival's public declarations, its declaration on us and its army at our border when scoring a Peace plan, offer or acceptance; direct pressure on a home city offsets that risk, and a threatened home discounts it in Peace scoring. `peace_withdraw_price` (1) values healing the withdrawn army in place of a flat +100 Peace bonus. At 0 each retains its old path. The six older commitment genes remain at their defaults pending the climb.
- **Alliance exposure and war participants:** `ally_defense_percent` (100) preserves Walls investment when a stronger allied army masses near a city. `ally_vision_cost` (10), `ally_passage_cost` (15) and `ally_exit_discount_percent` (50) price vision, passage and the ability to leave free after ten turns in an Alliance. `ally_commitment_model` (1) uses actual war participants for ongoing wars, direct allies as automatic defenders of a new war and only the gene `ally_offensive_join_percent` (50%) of a direct ally's strength for a new offensive call. At `ally_commitment_model=0` the old whole-Alliance strength estimate returns; the current war rule always uses actual participants.
- **Pre-hold Peace:** `leader_peace_horizon` (1) prices the ongoing campaign and an attainable siege against a strict rival leader one point from its victory target before its hold starts. Peace can still win when survival or healing is worth more. At 0 only the old active-hold protection and other existing peace valuation apply.

- **Audit tactics and forecast corrections (2026-10-24, AI only):** The AI compares a ranged shot with the unit's recoverable HP and safer reachable tiles before committing, and late garrison fire preserves a more valuable rest (`ranged_survival_weight`, 0 restores fire-first). Melee counterattack and ordinary strikes price the unfortified post-kill tile and a nearby pursuit endpoint; a city still shielding its garrison gives city progress rather than garrison damage, and an opportunity strike waits for its melee wing when one early charge does not pay (`melee_response_weight`, 0 restores immediate-duel scoring). Non-Spartan siege forecasts divide the city's one counterattack among simultaneous melee attackers (`split_counterattack`, 0 restores the Sparta-only forecast). Aircraft allocate strikes against HP remaining after committed ground fire and earlier aircraft (`air_marginal_strikes`, 0 restores independent picks). These are forecasts and order values, not combat-rule changes.
- **Audit race and resource corrections (2026-10-24, AI only):** The final technology of the current era receives the era-entry bonus and public neighbor eras are compared one-based (`era_transition_scoring`, 0 restores the old comparison). Siege cover in a forecast uses Gold available now and adds an upgrade turn (`siege_timeline`, 0 restores the six-turn advance). Settlement supply values only newly claimed deposits, with outer-ring deposits discounted until border growth (`settlement_claim_timeline`, 0 restores full-radius supply). An Heir refuge's score includes enemy reach along its route (`heir_route_risk_weight`, 0 restores destination-only scoring). No core supply or movement rules changed.
- **Audit gate defaults (2026-10-25):** `era_transition_scoring` and the settlement group (`settlement_claim_timeline`) default to 0 after the paired AI gate; their code remains available through decision parameters. Ranged survival, melee response, aircraft allocation and siege timeline retain their enabled defaults.
- **New Fire planning (2026-10-31):** Swarm action planning and post-rite city weakness valuation are restored for the Aztecs (see §8). Keshig dual-role combat remains active for Mongols; no tribute planner remains in the current AI.
- **Peaceful army exposure (AI only, 2026-10-24):** `tactics.rs::enemy_exposure_key` prices visible military of a peaceful rival as potential incoming attacks without making those units legal targets. `peace_declaration_percent` uses the rival's public declaration record, distance to our cities, local force advantage, how far the force stands from its own cities, top-threat status, and alliance/Peace guarantees. `hard_defense.rs::prepare_hard_defense` applies the same weighted risk to city pressure. Gene `peace_exposure_percent` defaults to 0 after the AI gate, retaining war-only exposure and pressure; 100 enables peaceful-army exposure. No diplomacy or combat rule changed.
- **Arrival-based threat horizon (AI only, 2026-10-24):** `hard_defense.rs::declaration_arrival_turns` walks known passable tiles and roads with each unit's movement allowance, treating unseen terrain as cheap for risk assessment, including a possible declaration into our territory. At `threat_horizon_turns=2`, Hard's city pressure counts an army that can arrive within two turns; the default 0 uses the old five-hex trigger. At `assessment_interval=1`, `opponents.rs::update_with_risk` refreshes a public rival assessment every turn when its visible army is within two turns of a city; the default 0 retains the five-turn cadence. Distant rivals retain the five-turn assessment interval.
- **Fogged army rollouts (AI only, 2026-10-24):** `search/determinize.rs::determinize_with` preserves visible units and remembered sightings exactly, then allocates `fog_army_forward_percent` (default 70; 0 restores city garrisons only) of the unaccounted public Strongest Army value to unseen, passable tiles near the front of a rival fighting us or another empire, or the tracked top threat. Remaining public army strength is placed around its cities. Candidate plans in Search share the same seeded samples and receive no hidden army positions.
- **Placement by neighbour (AI decisions only, gene `site_denial` 100; the aggressor-risk and backfill genes were measured and removed; 2026-11-07; `crates/ai/src/site_strategy.rs`, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/settle-strategy-1107/README.md`).** From the designer's placement strategy (`docs/playtests/human-reasoning.md`). Hard only, not the first city; the term adjusts a preferred site's value in `civilians.rs::choose_site_scored` after `land_contest_value`.
  - `site_denial` N: inside the nearest known rival city's Settler reach (2 x its city gap + 2), the site gains N% of its value, times closeness `(reach + 1 - d) / reach`, times a denial share. The share is the open, unowned, explored land within 2 city gaps that is farther from that rival city than the site and nearer the site than the rival (18 tiles = 100%), plus 30% per unowned strategic deposit within the work radius (at most 2), capped at 150%.
  - No rule changes; Walls stay unbuyable.

- **Settler capture priced by what the AI can see (AI decisions only, 2026-11-03; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/settler-safety-1103/README.md`).** From the 8.9.25 India coach review (Germany and the Celts handed him two Settlers and Leipzig in T16-T22). Three genes; `snipe_read` 90 and `escort_buy` 90 are on, `capital_guard` is off by default. `snipe_read` 90 (`civilians.rs::recent_surprise_war`, `peace_settler_capture_chance`, `snipe_fog_risk`; `hard_defense.rs::guard_exposed_settlers_duel`, `troops_in_reach`): a rival whose team alone declared a war still running that took effect within the last 20 turns (public relations) is a known Settler sniper; its surprise-war chance becomes 90 (founding in place, trip risk) and its Scouts mark settler danger. On plain Hard's escort path (`settler_safety` 0): a trip with an unseen tile within 2 hexes of its route counts as threatened while such a rival is not known to be far (none of its known cities and units within 12 hexes; one never seen counts); an escort that cannot reach the Settler's stopping tile makes the Settler wait (`SkipUnit`) for it, unless it is falling back into a city; two or more rival troops (not Scouts) that can stand on an escorted Settler's stopping tile next turn send it back to a city it reaches this turn; and a Settler falling back into a city keeps its escort beside it even from a tile a rival could strike. `capital_guard` 1 (`hard_defense.rs::capital_in_rival_reach`, `hold_capital_guard`, `keep_settler_home`): while two or more troops of one rival at war or at Peace with a legal declaration, or one of a rival at war or with a recent surprise war, stand within 3 hexes of the capital (barbarians excluded), a lone defender in the capital does not escort and a threatened Settler without an escort stays in or returns to a city it reaches this turn; an empty capital calls Settlers within 4 hexes home, and the troop beside one that gets home this turn goes with it. `escort_buy` 90 (`hard_defense.rs::second_troop_buy`, called from `production.rs::plan_buy` after the frontline defender): Hard with at most one troop, a Settler out or in production, and a rival troop (not a Scout, not a spared barbarian) at war or able to declare within 6 hexes of it or its city buys the cheapest melee troop whose quote is at most 90% of the Settler's quote, before routine purchases, from the city nearest the Settler; if it is at most 5 turns of income away, routine purchases leave its price. Measured: fixtures `crates/ai/src/india_settler_tests.rs` (his recorded seat views, `fixtures/india-1103/`); his own orders from T12 with the genes on in every AI: the German and Celtic Settlers and Leipzig stay out of his hands (his T37 1 city and 14 Production instead of 4 and 59, Germany alive; deterministic, his tape breaks at T15); benchmark (2,048 games, seed 310001) rivals-only all three +1.17 pp [-0.98, +3.27] (softer), seat-only -0.29 [-2.00, +1.42]; `snipe_read` alone rivals-only +0.63 [-1.51, +2.69], T100 cities 5.35 to 5.28. `snipe_read` ships on anyway (lead decision 2026-11-03): the benchmark's Hard seat rarely snipes, and on his orders from T12 it alone keeps both Settlers (his T37 4 cities and 46 Production to 1 and 14); `capital_guard` alone leaves his game unchanged. The flat pair (`settler_safety` 1, `peace_settler_capture` 100), re-gated on the same games: rivals-only +3.12 pp [+0.83, +5.42] (softer, more Settlers lost 0.48 to 0.55 a game, T100 cities 5.35 to 5.13), seat-only -4.39 [-6.35, -2.54].
- **Fog risk for Settler escorts (AI only, 2026-10-24):** `civilians.rs::fog_route_risk` prices unseen tiles beside the intended route, with a larger interception prior where the route approaches a known rival border; a rival's public rush speed and declarations raise that prior. It feeds both the legacy Hard escort path and `settler_safety`'s route valuation. If the risk warrants an escort and none is available, the legacy Hard path waits on a currently safe tile. `fog_escort_percent` defaults to 0 after the AI gate, keeping risk from visible units, sightings and camps only; 85 enables the unseen-route risk. The AI does not infer the location of a hidden unit.
- **Quiet border buildup (AI only, 2026-10-24):** `threat.rs::ThreatTracker::observe` scores a rival's visible army within the configured movement-turn horizon of our cities against our own field strength, including its positive trend and excess public Strongest Army value. The term is uncapped, so a peaceful rival massing an overwhelming army can become the top threat before any declaration, capture or VP lead. Gene `army_threat_weight` defaults to 0 after the AI gate, keeping the capped 20-point army term plus its five-point trend bonus; 100 enables the uncapped term. Gene `threat_horizon_turns` at 0 retains the old six-hex sighting radius.
- **A neighbour falls (AI only, 2026-11-15):** Hard gene `neighbour_fall` defaults to 100 (percent); 0 restores the previous orders. `ThreatTracker::observe` remembers a city seen changing owner within eighteen hexes of ours, or a known nearby city of an eliminated empire attributed to its public war partner. When the fallen city is still fogged, a public elimination by a rival whose unit was recently seen near us gives a weaker local approach warning without revealing the city. A fresh brain does not date a seeded old elimination as a new capture. `Planner::neighbour_fall_power` weighs the conqueror's public Strongest Army against our own, distance from that fall or sighting to each own city, and age; an army below one and a half times ours is not a credible conqueror warning. It contributes to the existing city threat and hold terms, so cities on that approach value Walls, a standing defender and Gold reserved for one defender before any declaration on us. The same threat raises ordinary Alliance value for another nearby empire wary of the conqueror, but an under-garrisoned empire prices the risk of being pulled into that ally's next war before its city defenders are ready. Once a defended city can support a viable second front against the leader, the extra city threat from the warning yields to that plan; other defender and reserve valuation stays. When the conqueror's actual army is visible within six hexes of any own city, live hold forecasts and tactical defence take over from the remembered approach warning. The warning fades if its army turns away and ends when its public wars end.
- **The conqueror alarm (AI only, 2026-11-20):** Hard gene `neighbour_alarm` defaults to 1; 0 restores the previous production. `threat.rs::conqueror_alarm` is a second reading of the neighbour-fall warning used only by production (`production.rs::choose_item_inner`, the walled-city pass in `threat.rs`): it also starts from a capture known only from public city counts when one of the conqueror's military units was seen near us in the last 20 turns (no elimination needed), multiplies the warning's strength by (2 + his captures in the last 20 turns, at most 4) / 2 with a cap of 240, and stays on when his army is within 6 hexes of our cities. A city whose alarm exceeds its local defence gets the anticipatory-defence branch (shooter or Walls first, as before; then, instead of an economy build, the melee or ranged unit with the most strength per turn of production). Unit positioning, the Gold reserve, alliance value and containment wars still read `neighbour_fall_power` unchanged. Own `PlayerView` only, integer maths; no core rule changes.
- **A second front (AI only, 2026-11-15):** Hard gene `busy_leader_front` defaults to 1; 0 restores the funded siege gate. A new Contain candidate on a strict public leader already at war elsewhere may pass `contain_commit`/`leader_commit` without `contain_min_odds` when a known city is reachable, at least half its public army is visibly eight hexes away from that city or it captured a city during the last twelve turns of its other war, no visible enemy army threatens our cities, there is at least one military unit per own city with local defenders present, and a troop within eight hexes of the target outweighs its visible nearby guards. That locally superior force values the exposed city in the ordinary Contain terms; war-footing and retaliation costs still apply. Scout valuation first follows the observed army approach, then any explored leader-owned border, to discover a legal city route without reading fogged ownership. `reachable_wars` still filters the chosen plan. The same opening raises the value of answering a call to arms against that leader. Combat, diplomacy legality and `RULES_VERSION` do not change.
- **FieldStrike plan (AI only, 2026-10-24):** a visible rival army massing near an own city can be a plan objective before war starts. Hard forecasts an opening field attack on a prospective war view, scores the chance of preventing a city loss and inflicting attrition against retaliation and the ordinary new-war costs, and declares only when that plan wins the comparison. The plan issues its chosen field attack on the declaration turn. Gene `field_strike` defaults to 0 after the AI gate, preserving the old city-only declaration plans; 100 enables the candidate. Combat and declaration rules are unchanged.
- **Scout capture threat (AI only, 2026-10-24):** Hard counts visible enemy Scouts in city pressure, front guards and retaliation by their low `threat_power`, instead of treating their unit kind as harmless. A Scout can still capture an ungarrisoned city at zero HP or take a civilian, so that small positive pressure can bring a free melee defender into a broken city. Gene `scout_threat` defaults to 0 after the AI gate, keeping the old exclusions; 100 enables and scales that pressure. Scout combat strength and movement rules are unchanged.
- **Defence after Walls (AI only, 2026-10-24):** a walled city whose visible siege forecast still predicts capture remains eligible for military production even without a tracked top-threat label. The AI compares available melee and ranged defenders by the city's keep value, the forecast after substituting that defender, unit strength, build time and cost; the ordinary idle-city production path uses the same forecast gate. Gene `walled_defense_percent` defaults to 0 after the AI gate, keeping the old stop at Walls; 100 enables and scales the saved-city term. Walls still reduce damage in the combat forecast.
- **Information and early warning (AI only, 2026-10-24):** `information_value` prices the public strength of a rival army and the age of its most recent visible or remembered sighting. This value enters Scout production and sentry placement. Scouts watch the known approach between a strong rival's city and our city, favoring Hills for the core's extra sight tile, and move toward stale last-known army positions to regain vision; an exposed Scout retreats before taking a sentry post, and the first sentry waypoint must be safe. Neither a memory nor public army title becomes a live combat target. A newly visible force within four movement turns of a city adds early pressure, moving free defenders and starting Walls or a ranged unit before the two-turn emergency window. Visible nearby class composition adds production value for the class triangle's counter (mounted versus ranged, melee versus mounted, ranged versus melee). Genes `intel_value`, `sentry_posts`, and `counter_composition` default to 0 after the AI gate, keeping their prior valuations; 100 enables each new valuation and the original `sentry` gene remains available. No core sight, combat or production rule changed.
- **Explore before sentry (AI only, 2026-11-21; gene `sentry_needs_sites`, default 1; Mongols 8.9.73.1 review):** a Hard empire with fewer than four cities, an unexplored frontier and no known good city site (`count_good_sites`) does not post Scouts as sentries or approach watchers (`civilians.rs::plan_scouts`, `scouts_needed_for_sites`), so they explore. The sentry posts resume once a good site is known.

- **Threat-model gate defaults (2026-10-25):** Border threat (`peace_exposure_percent`, `threat_horizon_turns`, `assessment_interval`, `army_threat_weight`, `field_strike`), city defence (`scout_threat`, `walled_defense_percent`, `counter_composition`), fog escort (`fog_escort_percent`), and intel sentry (`intel_value`, `sentry_posts`) default to 0 after the paired AI gate. Their code remains selectable through decision parameters. Fog army placement remains at 70, and Heir route risk remains at 500.

- **Climb transfer objective (offline only):** `crates/sim/examples/climb_arena.rs` can place the climbed faction in a rival seat while a master Hard AI plays the observed seat, paired by seed with a master control arm. It records the climbed seat's declarations (`relation.declarers`) and captures, including Celtic sacks. `tools/evolve/climb_program.py` uses half as many opponents games per arm and penalizes increased observed-seat wins, plus lost declarations or captures for Mongols, Sparta, Germany and Celts. This changes training and candidate selection, not live AI defaults or game rules; the arena, human-seat and war-suite banking gates still decide whether a candidate lands.
- **Siege Iron budgeting (2026-10-24, AI only):** `crates/ai/src/siege_buy.rs::siege_iron_spare` reads `SupplyView.spare`, which already subtracts Iron held by buildings or units in progress (`resources::supply_views`, `resources::reserved`). `crates/ai/src/supply.rs::within_budget` counts every planned `UpgradeSiege` alongside unit upgrades before city starts, so a later city order cannot spend the same spare Iron. This follows core's `siege::iron_blocker` and `resources::shortage_after_orders`; no resource rule changed.
- **Hand-built AI test fixtures:** `crates/ai/src/fixture.rs::{TEST_KNOBS, planner, brain}` pins shared decision defaults and fixed knobs for tests of general AI behavior. Faction bank changes must be tested through explicit faction tests or real-game fixtures, not silently inherited by unrelated unit tests.
- **Per-faction decision genes** (`crates/ai/src/params.rs`, `scripted.rs`): `AiParams.decisions` supplies shared decision defaults; optional partial `faction_overrides` tables replace only the listed genes for that faction when a `ScriptedBrain` is built. Missing entries retain the shared value, and an empty table leaves built-in play unchanged. The override affects AI preferences only, not game rules. The evolution tooling can route decision genes into one selected faction's table with `milestone_climb.py --faction <Faction>`.
- **Built-in per-faction decision defaults and the stage-1 gates (2026-10-23, AI only):** `params.rs::BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` gives a faction its own value of a decision gene unless a parameter file overrides it. Stage 1 of the climb program moved the AI's faction-keyed gates (then including no conquest nominee for Germany, the old siege exclusions for Germany and Sparta, Sparta's lone-city strike, early prize opening and army quota, Switzerland banking, per-faction scout and strike eagerness tables) and its literal thresholds (turn windows, reach radii, alliance progress limits, plan margins) into genes; the list is in `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/climb-program/gates.md`. The EU4 follow-up below changed the default siege exclusions. With the 2026-11-05 Germany rework and ordinary German movement, Germany's old gates (no conquest nominee, siege exemption, campaign only to contain, no scout front, no border raid, its strike table) are gone and it plays the shared defaults for those genes (`conquest_enabled`, `campaign_contain_only`, `scout_front`, `border_raid`, `strike_eagerness`, `siege_exempt`). It still has a banked tuned table of its own in `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` (climb round 1, 2026-10-24, shared with Athens, Byzantium and Switzerland: for example `conquest_min_cities` 3, `campaign_commit` 714, `war_siege` 218, `industry_tech_bonus` 100, `bronze_bias` 100), and the table also carries the playbook genes `swiss_playbook` 3, `swiss_hoard_any_war` 1, `celts_playbook` 2, `aztecs_playbook` 7 and `india_playbook` 2, plus the Mongols' `mongol_horde` 3 and `courier_raid_only` 1. AI preferences only: no engine rule changed by these defaults.

- **The Keshig in the AI (2026-10-17, AI only; claude/keshig-ai):** the live AI reads a unit by what it can do, not by "ranged == 0". `crates/ai/src/composition.rs::charges` (core `data::attacks_in_melee`: closes in, captures, counts in the melee wing, threatens a charge), `shooter_only` (has a bow and never closes in: stays back, never the capturer) and `dual` (both: the Keshig). For every other unit `charges` is exactly the old "ranged == 0" and `shooter_only` the old "ranged > 0", so their behaviour is unchanged. Where the AI plans an army (city assaults `city_assault.rs`, opportunity strikes `opportunity.rs::strike_force,forecast_siege`, campaign and conquest readiness, rush sieges, escorts, staging) the Keshig is the melee wing, as the Cataphract it replaces (a Cataphract's role); where a question is "can it shoot" (best shots, `fire_from_holds`, ranged garrisons and city holders, answering bombardment, purchases that need a shooter) it counts as a shooter, so a Keshig holding a city or a staging tile fires instead of idling. **Own Keshig, shoot or charge by value** (`crates/ai/src/dual.rs`, gene `decision.dual_attack_choice`, default 1): in `military.rs::dual_fire`, which runs after the campaign and staging passes and just before `rest` (so the plan's march, staging and assault orders take the Keshig first; `ranged_fire`'s early volley skips it: firing first kept it shooting Scouts instead of marching with the siege, Golden Horde 44% to 33%), and before a garrison sortie ( `tactics.rs::sortie_garrisons`) it compares its best shot (`best_shot`'s target) with its best charge this turn, both in HP: what the attack takes (expected damage dealt, capped at the target's HP, plus the victim's next-turn harm `tactics.rs::enemy_threat` at the kill chance; a fallen city it rides into is worth twice a city's HP; a shot at a city with HP is its capture progress, `city_shot_value`), raised by a victory denial as `best_shot` is, minus the HP it costs: the charge's expected retaliation and city fire (`melee_forecast`, `attack_city_damage`), then the expected fire where it ends the turn (`dual.rs::incoming_at`: the victim's tile on a win, its own tile otherwise; a shot pays the fire on its own tile). A charge the forecast calls suicidal (`too_risky`) or at a city with HP (the city assault's call) is never considered. So it charges an exposed Archer (mounted against ranged) and a fallen city, and shoots a fortified Spearman (no spear bonus against arrows, no retaliation). Gene 0: the bow wins whenever it has a target. A free Keshig with nothing to shoot falls through to the ordinary melee passes (`counterattack`, `attack_targets`, `strike`). **Rivals' threat estimates follow the fight** (`tactics.rs::fight_bonuses`, core `combat::fight_class_bonus`): `enemy_threat`, `defensive_exchange`, `garrison_doomed` and our counterfire take each enemy's charge and shot separately with that fight's class bonus (an enemy Keshig's shot gives our spear no bonus; its charge does), its charge counts as a capture threat (`enemy_threat`'s city stake, `city_shield`, `purchase_defense.rs::city_attacks`: a Keshig that can arrive is scored as the charge), `city_assault.rs::city_assault_succeeds` lets an enemy Keshig shoot or charge, not both, and `opportunity.rs::forecast_siege` takes its more damaging attack. **Production and upgrades:** `production.rs::composition_bonus` values a unit with a bow as mounted that the melee line no longer counters (its arrows answer spears as an Archer's do); emergency purchases treat it as fitting either garrison role. `military.rs::hold_or_upgrade` upgrades it to the bowless Knight only when `composition::worth` (its stronger attack plus half the other) rises: Keshig 33 + 17/2 = 41 against Knight 42, so it takes the step once the Knight is available (Chivalry); and once the Knight is itself outdated (Metallurgy known) it takes any step, so it is never stranded. **Frozen proxies** (`proxy*.rs`, `proxy_v*/`) are unchanged and still read "ranged > 0" as a shooter: they keep a Keshig back as an archer, shoot with it, and never use it to capture. Tests: `crates/ai/src/dual_tests.rs`.
- **Strategic supply in the AI (2026-10-20, `crates/ai/src/supply.rs`, `supply_value.rs`; gene `decision.supply_value_percent`, default 100):** every brain sees only production it has supply for (options with a `resource_blocker` are removed) and `supply::within_budget` drops orders that would start more users than the view's `spare` (unit upgrades first, then Siege purchases, then city orders in list order; replacing a held build frees its supply). **Value:** a deposit is worth the Production its supply would put to work: `supply_value::unmet` = idle users (`SupplyView.idle`) + own cities ready for the user building (Forge: population 4, a Workshop, no Forge; Factory: population 6, a Forge, no Factory; half when the technology is only queued or one era away) − spare; `own_gain(r, n)` = min(n, unmet) × the tier's Production (+3 Forge, +5 Factory). That Production lands in the win-chance model's public economy term: a Hard **campaign** adds `own_gain` of the target city's deposits (tiles its owner holds nearer it than any other of its cities, +1 if mined) to the captured economy, and the owner loses `rival_loss` (its public cities big enough for the building, once its era has the technology, at half, capped by the supply lost) (`plan.rs::price_campaign`); a **city site** adds 3 points per Production of the unowned deposits in its work radius (`civilians.rs`); a **Mine** on Iron or Coal adds 3 points per Production of its +1 supply (`production.rs::improvement_value`). Only public facts: deposits appear in the view once teched, rivals' buildings are never read. The AI's tactical strength estimates apply the unsupplied penalty. **Industry (2026-10-21, `claude/ai-supply-value`; genes `industry_priority` 300, `supply_war_percent` 800, `supply_term` 400, `supply_shortage_percent` 300):** a Forge or Factory the city can start (so its supply is spare) gets `industry_priority` × its Production on top of its payback value (`production.rs`, not while threatened), so it outranks Markets and Colleges but not the due-turn ladder (Laboratory 1400, Granary 1100); a Mine on a deposit that caps an industry (`unmet` ≥ 1) adds half that priority for the building its +1 supply unlocks (`improvement_value`); where every spare Iron is already wanted by a Forge-ready city or an idle Forge (`supply_value::contested`) a Pikeman, and the Phalangite→Pikeman upgrade (`military.rs::upgrade_supply_worth`), takes it only if its strength premium over a Phalangite (a share of the cheaper unit's cost over 30 turns, ×`supply_war_percent`/100 at war) beats the Forge's Production per Iron (`unit_outbids_industry`). **Deposit cities as war targets:** `plan.rs::price_campaign` adds a "supply" term of its own, (the Production the city's deposits would put to work for us + half the owner's loss) × `supply_term` (400, win-chance points per Production per turn) × the capture odds, times `supply_shortage_percent` (300) when the seat is short (`supply_value::short`: an idle Forge or Factory, or ready cities with nothing spare). The public economy share the older `supply_value_percent` term feeds is 1 to 3 points against capture terms of 1,000 to 5,000, so it never moved a target. Expansion toward deposits is the older `site_gain` site-score term, which grows as industry makes supply the binding limit. Switching all of it off for a control arm: `docs/sim-results/strategic-supply/arms/off.json` (`industry_priority`, `supply_war_percent`, `supply_term` 0).
- **Faction goals in a game with no limit, and a Courier Hall that wins the game (2026-10-23, `claude/ai-stalls`; genes `goal_open_end` and `hall_decisive`, both default 1; `goal_actions.rs::last_turn`/`is_late`, `goal_hall.rs`, `victory/goals.rs::plan_faction_goal`):** a game with no turn limit (`view.turn_limit` None) has no last turn: `last_turn` is `u32::MAX`, so a goal whose build or hold needs more turns than are "left" is never too late (it was the expected length, or a tenth of it ahead once past it, so past turn 200 no Hall of 45 turns paid); `is_late(percent)` is still measured against the expected length (the limit when there is one), so the rites that wait for a late game still wait. Applies to the Swarm, broadcast, Sparta retire, Celts Wild, Sakoku and Hall windows (`rite_pays`, `wild_window`, `retire_window`, `sakoku_gain`, `hall_pays`). **Hall:** when the Hall's 2 VP take the team to its target (`hall_decides_the_game`: vp < target <= vp + 2) the Hall is ready without the garrison and `!hall_outmatched` checks, `hall_value` is 4,000 per goal VP instead of 1,500 (`HALL_DECISIVE_VALUE_PER_VP`: a 45-turn build is discounted over a 60-turn horizon against a five-turn garrison unit), and `hall_takes_over` lets `plan_faction_goal` replace what the site city is producing (a city repeats the unit it builds, so no city was ever idle for the goal pass). Other brains (Proxy, Opening) read the gene's default (`DecisionParams::default`). Measured: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/stalls-1023/README.md` (games over 600 turns 22 to 8 in 6,144, over 800 turns 5 to 0; Mongols wins 11.9% to 17.0% of all-AI games). Genes at 0 reproduce master bit for bit.
- **Movement preflight: movers that claim one tile take turns (2026-10-23, `claude/ai-movement-wait`; gene `movement_wait`, default 1; `movement.rs::movement_conflict`):** the preflight no longer returns the lower-priority mover when two of our movers enter one tile in the same simulated round; it does what the resolver's step 5 does (same-owner claimants of a tile advance by unit id, the others wait a round with their moves): the higher id waits, a mover entering a waiter's tile waits too, each wait adds a simulated round (at most double). A mover that meets a unit that is not moving (parked or standing) is still a conflict, and the end checks for escapes and assault groups are unchanged. Measured: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/movement-wait-1023/README.md`. At 0 the old cancellation is restored.

- **Tactical arena study (2026-10-10, offline only):** `ScriptedBrain::tactical_orders` runs the existing `Planner::plan_military` on a fogged `PlayerView` without production, research or settler orders. A scenario can nominate a city already present in that view as its tactical objective; it supplies no hidden city or unit state. `rules::resolve_tactical_turn` executes the ordinary unit-order, ranged, movement, combat, healing and fortification phases but skips economy, diplomacy updates, elimination and scoring. These entry points support `crates/sim/examples/combat_scenarios.rs`; the live AI decision path and game rules are unchanged. A cityless arena can expose a Hard tactical refusal to engage without requiring a fake home city.

- **Combat lab probe (2026-10-17, offline only, no decision changed):** `ScriptedBrain::probe_city_assault(view, city)` (`crates/ai/src/combat_probe.rs`, a child of `military.rs`) asks the planner what Hard thinks of a visible enemy city: the strike force, the `forecast_siege` capture turn and losses, whether `opportunity_strike` names the city, and how many orders `coordinate_city_assaults_at` issues. `crates/sim/examples/combat_lab.rs` calls it to calibrate those forecasts against real fights (`docs/sim-results/combat-lab/`).

- **Benchmark-only behavior switches (2026-10-10):** `DecisionParams::settler_safety` enables the Hard multiplayer route exposure, intercept, escort and recall policy in `civilians.rs` and `hard_defense.rs`; 0 keeps the earlier policy. The Sparta war genes in `params.rs` enable its new expedition, forced feasible Campaign and capture-heal valuation; `sparta_search_only=1` confines that policy to Hard + Search and its rollout copies; its default is 0 since 2026-10-11, so plain Hard plays it too (human-seat benchmark seat-only +2.93 pp [+0.20, +5.66], opponents-only 0.00). The human-seat benchmark assigns candidate or baseline `AiParams` per seat, while each seat retains its own difficulty and Search setting (`crates/sim/examples/hard_vs_human.rs::play`). These are AI decisions only; game orders and rules are unchanged.
- **Swiss Market priority (default 300, 2026-10-11; AI only):** while its treasury goal is pending, a Hard Swiss adds 300 to the production value of Markets (`crates/ai/src/production.rs::swiss_market_priority`, configured by `DecisionParams::swiss_market_priority` in `params.rs`). Normal difficulty and other factions receive no added value. This changes AI production scoring only; Market rules, costs and yields are unchanged.
- **The designer's Swiss playbook (gene `swiss_playbook`, shared default 0, Hard Switzerland plays 3 by default via `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` since 2026-11-07; AI only, no rule change; Hard Switzerland only; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/swiss-playbook-1105/README.md`).** From his 8.9.24 win: he walled every founded city with a Redoubt before its Market and kept 2 to 5 troops across 4 to 22 cities, buying defenders with Gold when attacked. 1: a city with a Redoubt standing, under way or buildable needs no troop of its own: the garrison floor (`item_base_value`, 2,200 a missing troop; every Hard faction plays this part since 2026-11-06 through `walls_garrison`), the peacetime `army_banker` quota and the threatened-empire quota (`uncapped_army`) count only the other cities (`troop_garrison_cities`), never below the two free units; a founded (non-capital) city's Redoubt is valued at that same 2,200 tier (the `Walls` arm); a border-threatened city raises its Redoubt before a shooter (`choose_item_inner`); Masonry gets the foundation value 100 inside the Swiss military research weight (`research.rs`). 2: also lets the Swiss expansion genes the climb set below the shared defaults (Settler chain priority, site floor, Settler weight) play the defaults (`params.rs::DecisionParams::for_faction`). Measured: AI-only Swiss win rate per seat 6.37% → 10.22% (1) and 11.58% (2) over 4,096 eight-empire games (paired +3.85 and +5.21 pp); human-seat gate noninferior at both (seat-only +1.07 and +0.73 pp, rivals-only −0.15 and −0.98). Masonry first outright, his whole research opening and a walled city of 3 counting as developed were tried and removed (no gain AI-only).
- **Swiss expansion valuation (gene `swiss_playbook` 3, Hard Swiss default since 2026-11-07; AI only; `params.rs::DecisionParams::for_faction`).** Hard Switzerland's quiet-opening Settler chain priority is at least 1,500 (level 2: 500) and its minimum site score is at most 40 (level 2: 60). The general Settler production weight stays at 100. This prices earlier colonies with Redoubts and Market tolls. On 4,096 eight-player Hard games per seed, Swiss share rose 6.84% to 7.87% on 9311003 and 8.05% to 8.19% on 8241001, while best/worst fell 2.48 to 2.16 and 2.12 to 2.06. The human-seat benchmark seat-only was noninferior (472 vs 474 of 2,048); rivals-only was inconclusive and trended softer (497 vs 474 human wins). These results predate rules v20 and the Mongol horde AI default. No game rule changes. The prior opt-in war-quota candidate was removed after it caused a T50 takeover loss on the designer's 8.9.24 seat (8/32 versus 15/32 control) despite a tiny census gain.
- **Walls are the garrison for every Hard faction (gene `walls_garrison`, shared default 1 since 2026-11-06; AI only, no rule change; Hard only; `docs/sim-results/strategy-gap-1105/README.md`).** The `swiss_playbook` 1 rule without its faction test (`production.rs::redoubt_garrison` is true for any Hard seat): a city with Walls standing, under way or buildable needs no troop of its own, so the garrison floor counts only the other cities (`troop_garrison_cities`, never below the two free units), a founded (non-capital) city's Walls are valued at the garrison floor's 2,200 tier, Walls come before the anticipatory shooter, and Masonry gets the foundation research value. The Hard peacetime quota still counts every city (`garrisoned_city_count`) outside the Swiss `army_banker` path; gene `walls_peace_quota` (default 0, an untested-then-measured follow-up) would count only `troop_garrison_cities` there. Measured (4,096 eight-empire Hard AI-only games per arm, seed 9311003): on one faction at a time +1.7 to +5.0 pp of win rate per seat for each of the eight factions it reaches (Athens +5.0, Mongols +4.5, India +4.4, Aztecs +4.2, Byzantium +4.0, Sparta +3.1, Germany +3.0, Celts +1.7); shared, cities lost per seat-game 1.83 → 1.66 and eliminations 11.9% → 10.4%, held out on 8241001; human-seat gate rivals arm −3.03 pp [−5.37, −0.78] (rivals harder to beat), seat arm +1.90 [−0.20, +4.10].
- **Shared Walls-as-garrison experiment (2026-11-05, AI gene `walls_garrison_value`, shared default 0).** With the gene on, `production.rs::walls_garrison_ratio` prices four expected city attacks saved by Walls' strength and HP protection, plus two city shots, against a troop's HP and counterattack. It divides each by its Production cost and credits the troop with projected damage against a current Campaign, Contain, Field Strike or Snipe target in reach; a Rush or military posture gives it full attack service, and without a plan its faction aggression weights a known nearby rival city. Only visible hostile units, known rival cities, own plans and the public rule experiment enter the comparison. A city with Walls standing, under way or buildable leaves the garrison troop minimum only when its Walls win that comparison; the two free troops remain the floor. The Walls production option receives the corresponding garrison value. `RuleExperiments::walls_cost` (`--rules-experiment walls-cost=100` or `120`) changes Walls' base Production price through `base_production_cost`, retaining faction adjustments, so engine costs and city view options use the same price; `None` keeps 60 for ordinary Walls (`experiments.rs`, `economy.rs`, `view.rs`). This opt-in study leaves combat, default cost and default AI behavior unchanged. On top of the shared `walls_garrison` 1 default (4,096 eight-empire Hard games, seed 9311003, `docs/sim-results/walls-garrison-value-1106/`): the gene 100 with the blunt rule on changes no faction outside its interval and leaves cities lost and eliminations flat; the gene 100 replacing the blunt rule (`walls_garrison` 0) loses cities (1.615 to 1.746 per seat-game) and eliminates more seats (9.72% to 11.04%), so the blunt rule stays the default and the gene stays 0.
- **A lean war army for every faction (gene `army_war_extra`, shared default 1 since 2026-11-06, was 5; Athens keeps its built-in 0; the Aztec playbook's cap is 1 as well). Hard only: Easy and Normal use the old 5 (`production.rs::EASIER_WAR_EXTRA`), since the lean army was measured on Hard alone.** A conquest or containment war wants one unit per city plus `army_war_extra` (`production.rs::uncapped_army`; the hollow-leader march still raises it to `HOLLOW_WAR_EXTRA` 5). The gene is read at every difficulty. Measured on one faction at a time: Byzantium +3.2 pp, Celts +2.5, Mongols +1.6, Sparta and India null, upkeep 100 to 200 Gold a game lower; together with `walls_garrison` the human-seat gate's rivals arm read −4.15 pp [−6.35, −1.90].
- **The designer's Aztec playbook (gene `aztecs_playbook`, a bit mask, shared default 0, Hard Aztecs play 7 via `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` since 2026-11-05; AI only, no rule change; Hard Aztecs only; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/aztecs-playbook-1105/README.md`).** From his two 8.9.22 wins: Walls first in every new city, a small current-era army (4 to 9 units for up to 21 cities), the New Fire as the closer. 1: an Aztec city without Walls that sees an unallied rival empire's city within `open_prize_radius` builds Walls first unless a Settler is under way (`production.rs::aztec_frontier_walls`, before every other production branch), and the anticipatory defense puts Walls before the shooter unless a visible army arrives first, from turn one (`threat.rs::walls_beat_arrival`; the stock rule also assumes an army from the rival's nearest city, distance / 2 + 3 turns, and waits for the opening's hand-off). 2: when the New Fire starts the VP hold, only the cities it shrinks must be clear of an enemy army within three hexes (`goal_actions.rs::swarm_now`, `cities_under_threat`). 4: a conquest or containment war wants one unit per city plus 1 instead of plus `army_war_extra` (5 then; the shared default is 1 since 2026-11-06, so bit 4 now matters only in a hollow-leader war, where it still caps the `HOLLOW_WAR_EXTRA` 5) (`production.rs::uncapped_army`, `AZTEC_WAR_EXTRA`). Measured (4,096 eight-empire Hard AI-only games, held-out seed 8241001): Aztec win rate per seat 13.9% → 16.5% at 7 (paired +2.66 pp [+1.32, +4.00]); early military share 42% → 34%, cities lost 1.89 → 1.72, New Fire 17% → 21% of seats, final VP 4.50 → 5.02. Screening seed 9311003: bit 4 alone +2.87, bit 1 +1.02, bit 2 +0.11; Sparta's early-prize conquest opening on the Aztecs (his Spearmen-first rush) -1.32, target choice, Settler weight, site floor, pipeline and a lower aggression knob all null. No Aztec seat on the human-seat benchmark maps: the gate is identical by construction (470/470/470).
- **The designer's India playbook (gene `india_playbook`, bit flags, shared default 0, Hard India plays 2 via `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` since 2026-11-06; AI only; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/india-playbook-1105/`).** From his 8.9.25 win: Walls protected 16 of 21 founded cities while five upgraded ground units covered as many as twenty cities. Bit 1 (Walls as an Indian city's garrison) is retired: it was the shared `walls_garrison` rule, which every Hard faction plays since 2026-11-06. Bit 2 removes India's extra network guard and returns its defensive margin and door defense to the shared 200 and 100 (`params.rs::DecisionParams::for_faction`, `production.rs::desired_army`, `hard_defense.rs`). Bit 4 would refuse Peace while India is at War with the strict VP leader at 60% of its target (`diplomacy.rs::wants_peace_why`); it remains off after measuring −0.05 pp AI only. Measured on the pre-`walls_garrison` AI, bits 1+2 gained +6.03 pp [+4.79, +7.27] India win share in 4,096 paired eight-empire games and +5.27 [+4.03, +6.52] on held-out seed 8241001 (bit 1 alone +4.39, bit 2 alone +1.06 [−0.01, +2.13]). On top of the shared default, bit 2 alone (the merged branch against master) gains +2.43 pp [+1.20, +3.57] on seed 9311003 and +2.58 [+1.37, +3.82] on 8241001, 9.82% to 12.25% and 10.30% to 12.88%. A gene-off control in a params file needs `faction_overrides.India.india_playbook = 0`, because built-in faction decisions replace shared-default gene values.
- **Mongols and Siege (2026-10-22, replaces the Engineer drawback of 2026-10-18):** the Mongols' half damage to city HP is gone, and every empire's forecast prices Walls (`forecast::forecast_attack_mode`, `ai/opportunity.rs::forecast_siege`). Their drawback is that they cannot raise Walls or buy Siege before the Medieval era (§4, "Walls and Siege"). They skip Wall-directed threat and border research, garrison a ranged unit first, then train or buy another local defender at threatened open cities (`ai/threat.rs`, `production.rs`). Rivals' opponent assessments score its observed unwalled cities under the usual open-target rule (`ai/opponents.rs`).
- **Walls are a price the AI pays in Gold (Siege, 2026-10-22; replaces the Engineer logic of 2026-10-20, genes `walls_as_cost`, `engineer_for_walls`, `sally_engineer` deleted; `crates/ai/src/siege_buy.rs`, `opportunity.rs::forecast_siege`, `city_assault.rs`, `campaign.rs`, `adaptation.rs`).** Walls halve every attack of a unit without Siege on a city's HP. Gene `siege_for_walls` (default 1; 0 returns to Walls as a veto for a small economy):
  - **Forecasts count Siege.** `add_siege_cover` counts the force units that have Siege, bought or built in (`data::has_siege`: Industrial and later units are wall-proof and never bought Siege, 2026-10-28), and the units at home it could buy Siege for within `siege_budget` (current treasury above the reserve, Iron permitting; gene `siege_timeline` restores the former six-turn future-income forecast at 0). The cost counts in the capture price at the upgrade rate, and buying delays that force one turn.
  - **The army buys it before it leaves.** `plan_siege_buys` buys Siege for the force near the walled war target, charging units first and units at home first (Siege is bought in own land and the unit rests a turn), keeping the reserve; what it cannot pay yet is saved for (`siege_savings`), never more than six turns of income away. `walls_are_a_price` (Siege units, or two it could buy) lifts the early-game rule and the `WallsBeforeArrival` campaign end.
  - **Measured** (2026-10-22, Siege at 160 Gold with 1 Iron each; human-seat benchmark, Hard, 8 maps x 128 salts, all seats): 42.8% on master, 45.1% with Siege (+2.3 points, inside the noise; seat-off and opponents-off arms 46.1% and 43.8%). The war suite's attack check `war-golden-horde-thin` reads 54% with Siege against 65% on master (64 runs each, floor 30%); before the Alliance pricing fix it read 0% on both, at every price from 20 to 160 Gold and with Iron on or off.

- **No Peace the next campaign would break (2026-10-21, from the coach's review of the user's 8.9.5 Athens loss; `crates/ai/src/plan.rs::peace_would_be_broken`, `diplomacy.rs::wants_peace`, gene `peace_redeclare`, default 2).** Hard's plan fights one war and ends the others, and while the army holds a city it just captured no campaign is priced, so the quiet plan won by default: Sparta signed Peace after every capture and declared again a few turns later (Nagoya changed hands 11 times). `wants_peace` now prices both sides before it offers or accepts a Peace with a team at war, unless the empire is `endangered()`:
  - **Ending the war costs** what declaring again costs: aggressor risk, second front, broken peace (at full weight, as just after a signing) and the `peace_credibility` charge, all as `plan.rs::new_war_terms` and `peace_credibility_term` price a new war.
  - **Keeping it costs** the retaliation the team can bring (`retaliation_permille` times the win-chance a lost city takes).
  - **When it applies:** while the army holds a captured city (`campaigns.held_capture`), and (gene value 2) for a war whose units fought the enemy within the last 8 turns (`idle_war_turns`). Value 1 is the hold alone (a Celtic army never holds a captured city, so only value 2 applies to it).
  - **Measured** (2026-10-21, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-fixes-3/results.md`): in 256 eight-player Hard games, wars per game 47.2 -> 36.3, declarations within 10 turns of a Peace with the same target 13.4 -> 8.1 per game (28.4% -> 22.3% of wars; Sparta 30.3% -> 22.7%), captures per war 0.57 -> 0.68, captures per game 26.9 -> 24.7; human-seat benchmark seat-only -0.6 pp [-3.2, +2.1], opponents-only -1.2 [-4.5, +2.1]; war suite 47% -> 48%, thin attack check 67% -> 85%. Cities retaken within 10 turns by their old owner are unchanged (25%): the Nagoya churn is not only peace-then-war.

- **End a war nobody fights (2026-10-31, gene `dead_war_peace`, on (12) by default since 2026-10-31; AI only; 8.9.17 Mongols review rec 2; `diplomacy.rs::dead_war,rival_leader_pull,peace_terms,wants_peace_why`).** Hard only (needs the routed plan). A war with `team` is *dead* when it is at least the gene's value in turns old, the plan's `war_target()` is not `team`, no active campaign or `strike_against` aims at `team`, none of our military units stands within 2 hexes of a `team` city, `endangered_by(team)` and `collapsing()` are false, `team` is neither `leader_war` (a holder, the contained leader, our diplomatic target) nor a hollow leader (`hollow_bonus_from` above 0, so W2.3's persistence stands), and `team` is not a co-belligerent (itself at war with the plan's war target, the strict VP leader from 30% or our diplomatic target: Peace with it frees nothing of ours and spends its one diplomatic order a turn). For a dead war: the `peace_would_be_broken` veto in `wants_peace` is skipped (no plan would declare again), the Peace score's "truce forgoes pressing" term is 0, and it adds "dead war" (turns past the gene's age, up to 30) and "rival leader" (20 when a strict VP leader from 30% of its target, our diplomatic target or a hollow leader other than `team`, not our ally, has a city within 12 hexes of ours; 10 if farther; 0 if none). The Peace score is now a list of named terms (`peace_terms`), same arithmetic as before. Related: `peace_follows_plan` 2 (default stays 1) gives plain Hard's own `Peace { with }` plan the zero floor on the Peace score that only a searched plan had. Diagnostics: `ScriptedBrain::peace_diagnosis` and `crates/sim/examples/peace_trace.rs` (gate, score terms, idle turns, per war per turn); census `tools/sim/dead-war-census.sh` with `war_peace_metrics`.

- **A capture that completes a count is a VP (2026-10-21, from the coach's review of the user's 8.9.6 Swiss win; `crates/ai/src/objective_pursuit.rs::objective_capture_value`, gene `capture_completes_count`, default 1).** Japan sat at 9 of 10 VP for T199-T202, 2 tiles short of the Industrial territory count (141/143) and 11 of 12 cities, with the strongest army, and no capture counted as its closer. `objective_capture_value` (which feeds `conquest_victory_value`, so campaign and conquest target value, the strike value, the `endgame_push` one-short target list and the VP prize in `price_campaign`) now also counts, like a Fight objective:
  - a live **Cities** objective with exactly one city to go (any capture is the next city; Celts's razing excluded as before);
  - a live **Territory** objective whose remaining tiles the captured city's ring covers (the city's owned tiles within one hex, a floor: the whole border comes with the city).
  The weight is `objective_bonus_percent / 20`, the same as `Captures`. For India with `india_objective_reach` 1, `objective_pursuit.rs::objective_effort` also counts a reachable conquest nominee when free settlement sites run out, so the production and `chosen_objective` side can pursue the Cities objective through capture.
  - **Measured** (2026-10-21, `ai-fixes-3/results.md`): human-seat benchmark (1,024 games per arm) seat-only -0.4 pp [-1.0, +0.1], opponents-only -1.0 [-1.9, -0.1] (the AIs convert a little better); 8.9.6 Swiss branches from T160/T175 with every AI on the gene: the user's seat wins 46/60 -> 0 and 50/60 -> 0; Japan alone, from T190/T196: no change (a closed Japan's army stays home, 0.1 captures); war suite 47% -> 47%; Scenario Lab `japan-big-empire`, `byzantium-last-point`, `athens-next-points`: no change (those positions are 30 turns long and none offers a count-completing capture inside the window).

  - **accepts and offers Peace with anyone at war** (`vow_at_stake`: closed, and the vow holds or scores within 15 turns, with the game not nearly over). A closed country cannot start a replacement war, so peace can end an ongoing cost; the rule adds 60 to its Peace score (`treaty_score`) and overrides the plan's "end every war but the one it fights" rule.
  - **keeps two defenders per city** (`desired_army`) while the vow takes it to, or one point from, its VP target (`vow_closes_the_race`).
  - **Why off:** the Swiss branches from T160 to T190 show the intended effect (Japan makes 10 to 11 peaces instead of 4 to 5, loses 1.3 to 1.6 cities instead of 2.0, peaks at 10 VP instead of 8), but the user's seat and the end VP barely move, and in 768 all-AI games Japan wins 24 -> 21 and cities lost while closed 1,232 -> 1,164 (-5.5%): noise. `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-fixes-3/results.md`.

- **Relative win value (2026-10-08, AI only):** `WinFacts` prices plan outcomes and Hard + Search rollout leaves as a relative VP race, using a softmax over every surviving team's public score. This means weakening a likely winner raises our estimated chance more than the same damage to a distant rival. The `relative_vp_percent`, `relative_city_percent`, and `relative_economy_percent` decision genes scale VP, city/population, and public Largest Economy score gaps (`crates/ai/src/winchance.rs::estimate_with`, `projected_vp`; `crates/ai/src/plan.rs::plan_candidates`; `crates/ai/src/search/determinize.rs::chance_in_with`). Largest Economy publicly combines Production and Gold; the AI cannot infer their separate rival totals from fog. Tactical material remains a short-horizon combat score, while the strategic rollout leaf scores the complete relative race (`crates/ai/src/search/mod.rs::material_weighted`, `rollout_relative`). No engine rule or public information changed.
- **Distilled macro value (off by default, `crates/ai/src/learned_macro.rs`, `plan.rs::search_decision_with`, `search/mod.rs::choose_opening`):** The Hard+Search decision gene `learned_macro` is 0 off, 1 for plans plus openings, 2 for plans only or 3 for openings only. It acts only with four or more players, the trained lobby sizes; two-player duels retain ordinary search. It replaces Economy/Peace, Campaign and Contain plan rollouts or turn-one opening rollouts at review points with an integer linear value estimate. A review that includes Snipe or Hold keeps normal search. The model reads only the acting player's `PlayerView`: own economy, army, cities and VP, visible or remembered targets, distance, Walls and visible defenders. It scores only plans already admitted by the normal plan candidate generator, preserving feasibility and abort rules. `crates/sim/examples/macro_labels.rs` produces offline 64-sample, 40/80-turn rollout values from benchmark-map and simulation positions; `tools/sim/train-macro.py` fits pairwise values and quantizes weights. This changes AI choice only; orders, turn resolution and game rules are unchanged. The gene remains 0 until full-game benchmarks justify enabling it.
- **Learned value network (2026-10-31, off by default, `crates/ai/src/value_net.rs`, `winchance.rs::estimate_inner`; AI war plan step 3):** decision gene `learned_value` (0 off, 1 on) makes plain Hard's plan estimate (`WinFacts::with_relative`, so every `plan.rs` and `diplomacy.rs` projection that goes through it) score each team with a small integer MLP (19 inputs per team, 32 and 16 ReLU units, one milli-logit output) instead of the fitted linear `WEIGHTS`. The inputs come only from `WinFacts` (this seat's `PlayerView`): the nine linear features, whether the team is this seat, phase, team count, public hold state and margin, and gaps to the best other team on VP progress, cities, economy and army. The rule-based parts are unchanged: the VP hold overlay (a completing hold wins outright) and the `vp_race` term. `estimate_with`, `search_chance` (Search rollout leaves) and other callers without `with_relative` keep the hand formula. Weights are compiled in from `crates/ai/data/value_net.json` (trained offline by `tools/sim/train-value.py` on Hard self-play games from `crates/sim/examples/value_data.rs`); inference is i64 with truncating division, so native and WASM agree, and a stale or missing file falls back to the hand formula. AI choice only; no rule or visibility changed.

- **Offline deep-search experiment (`crates/ai/src/search/mod.rs::OracleNode`, `crates/sim/examples/deep_search.rs`, 2026-10-07):** `OracleNode::new` determinizes from the searching seat's `PlayerView` with the same prior-map seed stream as plan search. A branch forces an existing Hard plan for ten turns while it remains feasible, preserves simulated brains' observation memory, and advances through `resolve_turn`. A named opening can continue across ten-turn intervals until its normal handoff or a branch ends it. Rivals can run scripted Hard, as in existing plan rollouts, or Hard + Search. `from_recorded_state` replays a line selected on determinized samples on the actual map as a held-out test; no search choice receives its hidden state. The oracle exposes exact T50/T60/T62/T100/T123/T150 snapshots. This is an experiment API; `SearchConfig::in_app` and the shipped decision path are unchanged.
- **Storm ring for a fallen city (AI only, no gene, always on; 2026-11-14, his 8.9.36 Celts game):** `safe_campaign_tile` rejects every tile beside a garrisoned city, so before this a healthy unit could never stage next to a 0-HP city that its owner kept re-garrisoning. `military.rs::storm_ring_spot` gives a charging unit whose own charge passes `too_risky` the nearest free tile beside a visible 0-HP enemy city (route length through friends, which units may pass through; then least exposed). It is the fallback in `military.rs::strike` when `campaign_staging_spot(.., 1)` finds nothing, and in `city_assault.rs::coordinate_city_assaults_on` for a charging arrival that cannot reach a tile beside the city this turn (instead of parking two tiles out). A city with HP is unaffected. Wounded units that hold off still hold their tile, which keeps the city from healing. Test: `crates/ai/tests/siege_ring.rs`.
- **Committed operation executor (gene `operations`, default 0, offline only except the second-front strikes of `runaway_coalition` bit 2 (on for Hard by default), 2026-11-07; `crates/ai/src/operation.rs`, `search/mod.rs::OracleNode::advance_committed`, `crates/sim/examples/operation_search.rs --commit`):** an `Operation` (a strike on one enemy city, optionally bought, or a pinned plan) is stored in `campaign.rs::CampaignMemory.operation` (serde-skipped when None). With the gene on, `operation.rs::execute_operation` orders its roster (own land military units within `--radius` of the target at the start, plus units built or bought for it) before every other pass in `military.rs`; `choose_plan` (`plan.rs`) injects the operation's plan ahead of the plan layer and faction rules such as Sparta's campaign rule; `campaign.rs` reconcile skips the strike's campaign; `diplomacy.rs::wants_peace_why` refuses Peace with the target's team (`operation_refuses_peace`); a bought strike's `operation_reserve` keeps the treasury (`scripted.rs` gold reserve). Phases: assemble on a ring 3 tiles from the target, strike (declaring War) when two thirds of the roster is near it or 10 turns pass, storm when the city's HP is 0. Only `review_operation` ends it: turn budget, capture, target gone or taken by a third team, Peace after the strike began, roster lost, War still illegal 5 turns after assembly. A home city actually at War-attacked releases roster units within 4 tiles to its defence (logged). In live play only bit 2 of `runaway_coalition` (on for Hard by default) creates operations: its second-front strikes (next entry), for which the executor is honoured even with `operations` 0. No rule changes and `RULES_VERSION` is unchanged; results: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/operation-search-1107/` round 4.
- **Told scenario objective (simulator only, `legatum-sim scenario --tell-objective`, 2026-11-10; `crates/sim/src/scenario_told.rs`):** a measurement arm, never built by the app or a normal game. The scenario seat's scripted brain is wrapped and, before each turn, handed the card's win condition as a pinned operation (`OperationKind::Pin`, honoured by setting the seat's gene `operations` to 1): a Campaign on the first `OwnCity` target it doesn't own yet, against the owner's team, or, for a card whose only goal is `HoldStartingCities`, the quiet plan (`PlanKind::Economy`: no Campaign, Contain war or plan-war siege stance). The operation's budget ends with the card's window. Every executor is Hard's own and the brain reads only its `PlayerView`. `--tell-hold-city` tells a hold card as `PlanKind::Hold` on the own city with the most visible at-war military within 3 tiles instead: Hold Biel 6 of 50 before the `execute_hold` swap fix, 50 of 50 after it. The same command also takes `--params-seat-only` (`--ai-params` for the scenario seat only) and `--trace-run N` (that run's orders with the planning passes that set them, and the plan options with their gates, in `<out>/<id>-trace-run<N>.json`); every run's JSON now lists the cities the seat lost or took and its plan switches by turn. Results: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-objective-1110/`.
- **Capture horizon (gene `capture_horizon`, Hard, default 0 = off, 2026-11-10; `crates/ai/src/plan.rs::capture_turns`, `price_campaign`; Lab card Ride to Dyrrachium):** a Campaign candidate is priced by when its capture lands. With the gene at N > 0, a siege of a standing walled city whose forecast force has no Siege and no Siege on offer (gene `siege_for_walls`) counts its siege turns after the march at N percent in `capture_odds`, and the `supply` term counts only the share of the 10-turn plan horizon left after that capture turn (`(10 - turns) / 10`, at least 0). Open cities and forces with Siege keep the forecast's turns; with the gene off both are as before. Measured seat-only at 200: Ride to Dyrrachium 2 to 48 of 50 (each part alone stays at 2), Hold Biel and Two Cities unchanged; not yet measured on the designer seat, war suite or censuses, so it stays off.
- **Short-horizon objective (gene `short_objective`, Hard, default 0 = off, 2026-11-10; `crates/ai/src/short_objective.rs`, called in `scripted.rs::planner_through_plan` after the second front and before the operation review):** each turn, from the seat's own view, it nominates (1) every enemy city of a team at war with us, or of the team the routed plan is about to declare on (fresh sighting, legal declaration), within 15 tiles of an own military unit and not given up in the last 5 turns, whose campaign forecast (`plan.rs::campaign_forecast`, the committed force first) captures it within 5 turns, and (2) every own city a visible at-war army within 5 tiles is forecast to take within 5 turns (`opportunity.rs::forecast_siege` played from the attacker's side, the team with the most units at the door, each unit sent to the nearest tile beside the city). It prices in Production, not the plan layer's win chance: a city is worth 400 + 80 per population; a capture is worth its city at its `capture_odds` (forecast turns under `capture_horizon` 200, or the gene's own value) less the forecast's expected Production lost, and less the sum of every endangered home city's fall odds times its worth. It holds the best through a pinned operation (`OperationKind::Pin`, honoured with this gene on): a Campaign with odds of at least 15% and positive value, or, if none, a Hold on the most endangered city (`plan.rs::execute_hold`), but a Hold only when the routed plan takes the army away (a Campaign, a Hold, none yet, or Contain on a team not at our door; not the quiet plan, Peace or Contain on the attacker). The held objective is kept unless another beats it by `switch_margin` (a Hold yields to any positive offence), it is met, its city is gone, or its capture odds stay under 15% two turns in a row (then that city is left alone for 5 turns). A running operation it doesn't own (a second front, a told objective) is left alone. It starts no war and never reads a scenario's win condition.
- **Strike force keeps its moves (gene `strike_upgrade_wait`, Hard, default 0 = off, 2026-11-10; `modernization.rs::in_strike`, `upgrade_waits_for_attack`):** a unit the running strike counts on doesn't upgrade: the active campaign's committed force, a running operation's roster, or a unit that reaches an assault tile of the routed Campaign's city within `march_turns` (`opportunity.rs::strike_force_within`). A unit within 4 tiles of an own city that has an at-war enemy unit within 4 tiles still upgrades (it is defending).
- **Hold plan swaps in the stronger defender (2026-11-10, a bug fix; `plan.rs::execute_hold`, `door.rs::garrison_swap`):** when the Hold plan's city already has a unit, the Hold first makes the swap `door.rs::rotate_exposed_garrisons` would make (an adjacent free melee unit forecast to hold in its place, when the one inside would let the city fall, lose its garrison or have its shield broken) and only otherwise holds or upgrades the unit inside. Before, the Hold held the unit inside first, which used its move and blocked the swap. Only a Hold plan reaches this (Hard+Search holds, a told scenario hold, the short-horizon objective); a pinned `PlanKind::Hold` operation is now accepted while the city is ours (`operation.rs::operation_candidate`).
- **Measurement hooks (simulator only, 2026-11-10):** `war_suite` takes `WAR_PLAN_TRACE=<run,run,...>` (a run list; `1` alone still means runs 0 to 2), printing the seat's plan, campaign, short objective and running operation per turn (`WAR_PLAN_TRACE_ORDERS` adds its orders), and `WAR_SUPPLY_LOG=1`, which logs the seat's strategic supply in use and idle each turn and every plan switch to a Campaign with its `supply` term (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-objective-1110/supply_report.py`). `tools/decision-audit/designer_seat.py --seat-params FILE` plays the commit's Hard and Hard with that `AiParams` in the designer's seat on the same branch turns, salts and dice (`seat_run --continue hard,params:FILE`) and writes both results without touching the history; `--jobs N` caps the tapes run at once.
- **Runaway coalition (gene `runaway_coalition`, Hard, bit flags, default 55 (bits 1, 2, 4, 16, 32; bit 8 off) since 2026-11-08, bits 1 and 2 since 2026-11-07; `crates/ai/src/runaway.rs`; designer's Horde scenario and the 8.9.26 reviews):** the runaway is the strict VP leader (or a team counting down), read by its score on public signs and acted on by a seeded draw whose percent chance is that score (no cutoff since 2026-11-08: `runaway_read` is the score, `runaway` the draw, held 8 turns): 15 per VP of lead (up to 4), a quarter of its army's excess over the next team's (up to +25), 10 per city captured recently (up to 4), 20 per empire eliminated (up to 2), 60 for a hold; never our own team. Bit 1 (diplomacy): `treaty_score` adds `runaway_alliance_value` to an Alliance with a team at war with the runaway (15 plus up to 50 for the share of the runaway's public army the pair can bring, each side's army weighted by `helper_reach_percent`); `join_war_value` (calls to arms, wars an Alliance brings) adds 40 against the runaway; `wants_peace_why` refuses Peace with it (gate "runaway leader", checked after India's leader bit) unless one of our cities is about to fall (`collapsing`), even when the runaway endangers our home (the ordinary gates' escape). Bit 2 (second front): `start_second_front` runs before the operation review each turn when no operation runs (5 turns after the last one ended); if we are at War with the runaway, or allied with a team that is and may declare, it picks the runaway's known city where at least half its public army (Strongest Army values of its visible units) stands 8+ tiles away, none of its units stands within 6 of our cities, the city or a tile 3 from it is reachable from one of our cities within 12, and our land units within 12 have threat power at least 1.5 times the city's (2.5 times if walled) plus 1.5 times its visible defenders (an unseen city is credited its share of the army not seen away). A rich but army-less runaway therefore never starts one: no city passes the army-away test, and nobody near it is at War with it (Swiss hold probe, 64 runs: 0 second fronts). It commits an `Operation` strike (15 turns, roster radius 12, no purchases) that the executor honours under bit 2 even with `operations` 0 (`operation.rs::operation`). AI decisions only: no rule changes, `RULES_VERSION` unchanged.
- **Wariness, the standing bloc and all-in coalition wars (gene `runaway_coalition` bits 4, 16, 32 on by default (55 with bits 1 and 2), bit 8 off; `claude/coalition-bloc`, 2026-11-08, designer's revision of the EU4 memo `docs/design/ai-strategy/eu4-coalitions-2026-11-08.md`; AI decisions only, no rule changes):** with any of these bits on, a Hard AI works out a hidden **wariness** toward every other empire when it decides (`runaway.rs::wariness`, never shown to the player; nothing is stored, and with the bits off it is never read), from public information only: cities that empire captured recently (0, 3, 8, 30, 45, 55 for 0 to 5: one or two barely register, three to five are a roll-over), 20 per empire it eliminated (up to 2), a fifth of its visible army's excess over ours (up to 30), 6 per VP of lead over us (up to 24), scaled from 25% to 100% by how near it is (`helper_reach_percent`: the next target is the one in its path). There is no threshold; every yes/no choice is a seeded draw (`wary_draw`, stream per purpose, partner and 8-turn bucket) with the wariness as its percent. **Bit 4:** `treaty_score` adds `runaway_bloc_value` to an Alliance, at Peace included, with a partner wary of the same empire as we are: 40% of the weaker wariness, 30% of the share of that empire's public army the pair can bring (reach-weighted), 10 for a partner next door; the offer and the acceptance both go through that draw. `bloc_size_cost` is waived, by a draw at the weakest member's wariness, for a bloc whose members are all wary of one empire outside it (no cap on its size: dogpiles are fine, designer 2026-11-08). Our Alliance rule makes the bloc a coalition: an attack on one member brings the rest in at once. **Bit 16 (all in):** at War with an empire that a team outside its side is also at war with, and by a draw at wariness squared over 100 percent (a mild worry almost never; the first version, every war all-in, cost the Hard seat 3.6 pp), `start_second_front` commits a strike on its weakest reachable city with no army-away test and a roster radius of 20 (`ALL_IN_RADIUS`), and the roster keeps one defender in each city of ours with an enemy unit within 8 tiles (`operation.rs`: a member must not empty a city the enemy takes next turn). **Bit 8 (strike a hollow runaway):** a runaway counting down, or read as hollow (`hollow_bonus_from`), is struck on any weakly held reachable city with no army-away test and no war under way; the operation declares when assembled (radius 20). **Bit 32 (wary guard):** `desired_army` adds one troop per city of ours within 10 tiles of the empire we fear most, by the same draw. Bits 8 and 16 are honoured by the operation executor like bit 2 (`operation.rs::operation`). The runaway score (above) still names the runaway for bits 1, 2 and 8. No hard line remains in this path: the runaway, the bloc waiver and the all-in strike are draws on a score (all-in at wariness squared over 100 percent), and one or two captures give at most 8 wariness.

- **Roadworks production value** (`crates/ai/src/production.rs::roadworks_value`): all factions score 30 + 10 per other own city within eight hexes (capped at six) + their military knob. Sparta add 1,000 plus 100 per visible enemy city in range; Mongols add 240 plus 40 per visible enemy city in range (each enemy count capped at four). Any active campaign adds 300 in a Roadworks city within eight hexes of its committed target. Sparta's higher value compensates for its 50% non-war building production premium. This only changes AI production choices; Roadworks' game effects and costs are unchanged.

- **Opponent assessment** (`crates/ai/src/opponents.rs`, `adaptation.rs`): scripted and opening
  brains retain per-rival observations from `PlayerView` (persisted in session saves), refreshed every five turns and on first
  physical contact. Public faction unit data supplies rush priors; known city walls, visible
  garrisons, known land routes, public city counts/declarations, treasury capture bounties and
  India links inform targets and border defense. Army changes are sighting deltas, and fogged
  garrisons remain unknown. A remote scout does not make a rival a homeland neighbor.
  A rush-capable neighbour within 10 hexes raises the military knob (`DefendedExpand`); only
  observed aggression (2+ Soldiers' strength at our border, a visible buildup or any war it
  declared: `OpponentAssessment::observed_aggression`) makes exposed cities research legal Walls
  prerequisites and build a garrisoned shooter, then Walls (`production.rs::choose_item`,
  `adaptation.rs::assessed_border_threat`); a faction prior alone never walls (units counter
  rushes before Walls).
  Early rush/conquest plans with known but unsuitable targets pivot to expansion; city combat
  forecasts still govern tactical execution. early walled campaigns are avoided except
  finishing an already badly damaged city. Strategy switches record player, turn, rival, old/new
  plan and reason through `Brain::strategy_switches`. The shared Hard conquest nomination
  gates army funding, staging and war persistence (`conquest.rs::assessed_conquest_allowed`):
  only early walled cities are ruled out (`adaptation.rs::assessed_campaign_allowed` likewise);
  Celtic land is no cost or ban any more (the +120 was deleted with the homeland damage on 2026-10-23); a strong or unseen garrison is a cost
  in `choose_conquest_city`'s score, not a ban (the earlier viable-garrison gate removed most
  AI-vs-AI captures). The weakened-city exception for those requires a visible target, an
